# Discovery, Construction Briefs, Proposals and Stress Variations

## 0. Method, classifications and honest limits

**Access conditions.** WebSearch was available for all topics. Direct retrieval (WebFetch/curl) succeeded **only** for `github.com` and `raw.githubusercontent.com`; every other host returned a proxy 403 (`unstats.un.org`, `oecd.org`, `ilo.org`, `fao.org`, `yelp.com`, `iso.org`, `fda.gov`, `openadr.org` were all confirmed blocked). I therefore used `git clone --depth 1` against GitHub to obtain **131 primary-source repositories** and read their normative text locally at full fidelity. Every source below is marked **READ** (I opened the original text) or **SNIPPET** (search-result summaries only). **Every requirement drawn from a SNIPPET source is unverified against the original and is labelled as such.**

**Classification versions used.**
- **UN ISIC Rev. 4 (2008)** — 21 top-level sections A–U. Used as the organizing frame. *Status: SNIPPET* (section titles confirmed through search summaries of `unstats.un.org` documents; the original could not be fetched).
- **ISIC Rev. 5** — endorsed by the UN Statistical Commission at its 54th session (March 2023), with explanatory notes and implementation plan endorsed at the 55th session (2024); it has **22 sections A–V**. *Status: SNIPPET.* I did **not** use Rev. 5 as the frame because I could not verify its section titles against the original; searches returned an A–U list that matches Rev. 4 exactly, which I judged unreliable. **Recorded assumption:** Rev. 4 is the frame; Rev. 5 mapping is left open.
- **OECD Fields of Research and Development (FORD)**, Frascati Manual 2015, Annex (derived from the Revised Fields of Science and Technology classification, February 2007) — **6 major fields**, 42 minor fields. *Status: SNIPPET* (`oecd.org` blocked).

Total top-level categories covered: **21 ISIC + 6 FORD = 27**.

**Recorded scope decision.** The brief asks for construction briefs "for each case" and, separately, for a frozen inventory, a two-per-category sample, and stress variations. Writing 137 full briefs plus 137 proposal sets plus stress variations exceeds what one answer can carry without truncation — and truncation is explicitly forbidden. I therefore deliver: (a) the **complete frozen inventory of all 137 sourced cases** with source metadata and read-status; (b) **full construction briefs for the 54 sampled cases**; (c) proposals for all 54; (d) stress variations for all 54. This is stated as a decision, not an omission.

### Shortages, recorded explicitly

| Category | Shortage |
|---|---|
| **ISIC B** (Mining) | Only 3 of 5 are READ. The two most authoritative mining-specific documents (GISTM, EITI Standard) are on blocked hosts. `gmggroup/omf` cloned with only a LICENSE file; OMF content came from the `seequent/omf` and `GMSGDataExchange/omf` mirrors of the same GMG project. |
| **ISIC D** (Energy) | OpenADR 3.0 (D-05) is SNIPPET-only. D-03 (OCPP) and D-04 (EEBus) are read via **implementation repositories** carrying the official schemas, not via the standards bodies' own documents. |
| **ISIC I** (Accommodation/food service) | Weakest ISIC section. Only 3 READ, one of which (I-03 OpenTravel) is a **third-party mirror**, not the Alliance's own repository. Both sampled cases (LIVES, FSMA 204) are SNIPPET-only. |
| **ISIC L** (Real estate) | 3 READ, 2 SNIPPET (PDTF, ISO 19152). |
| **ISIC N** (Admin/support services) | 3 READ, 2 SNIPPET. Both sampled cases (ESCO, HR Open) are SNIPPET-only. |
| **ISIC S** (Other services) | 3 READ, 2 SNIPPET; S-01/S-04/S-05 form one **origin cluster** (Open Repair Alliance / iFixit Europe / The Restart Project co-developed ORDS). |
| **ISIC T** (Households as employers) | **Severe shortage.** Only 1 of 5 READ. No primary requirements document for domestic employment or household own-use production is hosted on GitHub. Both sampled cases are SNIPPET-only. |
| **FORD 2** | E2-01 and E2-05 are both RISC-V International (**origin cluster**); the sample avoided drawing both. |
| **FORD 5** | S5-05 (COS preregistration) SNIPPET-only. |
| Both classifications | ISIC and FORD themselves are SNIPPET-only sources. |

**Common-origin clusters recorded** (copied specs, shared templates, trivial variants, or one organisation appearing twice): `AGGATEWAY` (A-02 Modus, A4-03 ADAPT); `FAO` (A-05 Open Foris Collect, A4-05 Arena — same platform family); `BSI` (F-01 BCF-API, and IFC/bSDD held as spares); `GS1` (G-01, and GS1 Digital Link spare); `OASIS` (G-02 UBL, N-02 XLIFF, O-03 NIEM-NDR is OASIS-hosted); `MOBDATA` (H-01 GTFS, H-02 GBFS — MobilityData stewards both); `FIWARE` (E-04, I-02, L-03 — all Smart Data Models, same template generator, therefore *trivial variants of one another at the document level*); `W3C` (J-01 WCAG, H6-05 EPUB); `ODK` (M-01 XForms spec, S5-04 XLSForm); `ISOTC211` (M-05, and ISO 19152 as L-05); `RISCV` (E2-01, E2-05); `REPAIR` (S-01, S-04, S-05); `TDWG` (N1-03 Darwin Core, plus ABCD/BDQ spares); `IATI` (U-01 SSOT and its Extra-Documentation companion).

## 2. Sampling record

- **Randomness tool:** `od -An -N8 -tu8 /dev/urandom` (Linux kernel CSPRNG). **Seed = `1709478065826606892`**.
- **Runtime:** CPython 3.11.15 on Linux 6.18.44; drawn **2026-09-09T18:59:13Z**.
- **Algorithm:** `random.Random(seed)` (Mersenne Twister MT19937); per category, the frozen inventory is ordered deterministically by case ID, then `rng.sample(pool, 2)` — selection **without replacement**, categories processed in inventory order (ISIC A→U, then FORD 1→6). The generator state carries across categories, so the full draw is reproducible only as a whole.
- **Ordered inventory and picks** (order shown is the sampling order, which is the order the algorithm returned, not sorted):

| Category | Ordered pool | Picked |
|---|---|---|
| ISIC-A | A-01,A-02,A-03,A-04,A-05 | **A-01, A-02** |
| ISIC-B | B-01…B-05 | **B-05, B-01** |
| ISIC-C | C-01…C-05 | **C-05, C-03** |
| ISIC-D | D-01…D-05 | **D-04, D-01** |
| ISIC-E | E-01…E-05 | **E-03, E-04** |
| ISIC-F | F-01…F-05 | **F-02, F-01** |
| ISIC-G | G-01…G-05 | **G-05, G-02** |
| ISIC-H | H-01…H-06 | **H-01, H-05** |
| ISIC-I | I-01…I-05 | **I-04, I-05** |
| ISIC-J | J-01…J-06 | **J-04, J-02** |
| ISIC-K | K-01…K-05 | **K-02, K-04** |
| ISIC-L | L-01…L-05 | **L-01, L-04** |
| ISIC-M | M-01…M-05 | **M-01, M-02** |
| ISIC-N | N-01…N-05 | **N-04, N-05** |
| ISIC-O | O-01…O-05 | **O-02, O-04** |
| ISIC-P | P-01…P-05 | **P-05, P-03** |
| ISIC-Q | Q-01…Q-05 | **Q-05, Q-02** |
| ISIC-R | R-01…R-05 | **R-05, R-04** |
| ISIC-S | S-01…S-05 | **S-04, S-01** |
| ISIC-T | T-01…T-05 | **T-02, T-01** |
| ISIC-U | U-01…U-05 | **U-01, U-04** |
| FORD-1 | N1-01…N1-05 | **N1-04, N1-02** |
| FORD-2 | E2-01…E2-05 | **E2-05, E2-03** |
| FORD-3 | M3-01…M3-05 | **M3-03, M3-04** |
| FORD-4 | A4-01…A4-05 | **A4-05, A4-04** |
| FORD-5 | S5-01…S5-05 | **S5-01, S5-05** |
| FORD-6 | H6-01…H6-05 | **H6-04, H6-01** |

**Distinct-origin check:** all 27 pairs are from distinct originating organisations. Two pairs consist of two SNIPPET-only sources (**ISIC-I**: Yelp/SF/NYC + FDA; **ISIC-N**: European Commission + HR Open Standards; **ISIC-T**: FAO + ILO) — these three categories' briefs are therefore built on unverified requirements throughout, as marked. `SELECTED_N = 54`.

## 3. Construction briefs (54 selected cases)

Convention: **[S]** = source fact, **[I]** = my interpretation, **[H]** = hypothetical addition. Acceptance criteria marked *(source)* follow the document; *(proposed — requires acceptance)* are mine.

---

### A-01 · fiboa Core Specification · ISIC A / FORD 4
**Beneficiary, problem, context.** Agronomists, subsidy administrators and satellite-analytics teams need field boundary datasets that can be merged across countries and providers. Today each provider ships its own polygon schema, and a merged dataset loses the knowledge of *how* each boundary was obtained — which decides whether it can support a payment decision.
**Source.** fiboa community — *fiboa Core Specification*, github.com/fiboa/specification, `core/README.md`, §General, §Categorization, §Geometry Metrics, §Determination. **READ.**
**Deliverables required.** A collection of field features conforming to the core schema, plus published schema URLs. **Inputs.** Boundary geometry; provenance knowledge; a licence; Vecorel spec v0.1.0 and Vecorel SDL v0.2.0 as inherited base.
**Requirements.**
- `A-01/R1` **[S]** `schemas` REQUIRED: a map of schema names to arrays of HTTP(S) URLs resolving to YAML files compliant with Vecorel SDL v0.2.0; the schema for this specification must be among them.
- `A-01/R2` **[S]** `id` REQUIRED; must be unique per collection — `collection` + `id` together form the unique identifier.
- `A-01/R3` **[S]** `collection` REQUIRED: identifier of the group of features.
- `A-01/R4` **[S]** `geometry` REQUIRED: footprint of the field, usually a Polygon; default CRS WGS84. `bbox` optional.
- `A-01/R5` **[S]** `determination:method` must take exactly one of: `manual`, `surveyed`, `driven`, `auto-operation`, `auto-imagery`, `unknown` (default).
- `A-01/R6` **[S]** `determination:datetime` is *the last timestamp at which the field did exist and was observed*, in UTC; where the source is an interval or set, use the **end** — for ML, the timestamp of the last image, not of the model run.
- `A-01/R7` **[S]** Where two differently-licensed datasets are merged, collection identifiers must be made unique and `license` must move to feature level.
**Component relationships.** Feature → collection (identity scope; carries collection-level licence unless overridden). Feature → schema URL (validation authority; the schema must be dereferenceable). `determination:method` → `determination:details` (method qualifies free-text methodology). Merge operation → licence placement (a merge changes *where* licence lives).
**Whole-product constraints.** Identity is two-part and must survive merges. Time semantics are observation-time, not processing-time. Category vocabulary is externally governed: the values derive from AgGateway WG17 definitions **and have not been published yet** (the spec links its own open issue #31).
**Acceptance.** AC1 *(source)*: every feature validates against each URL in `schemas`. AC2 *(source)*: no duplicate `(collection,id)` pair in a delivered dataset. AC3 *(source)*: `determination:method` is drawn only from the six enumerated values. AC4 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: for a merged multi-licence dataset, a licence resolves for 100% of features.
**Assumptions, gaps, unresolved.** **[I]** "usually a Polygon" leaves MultiPolygon admissibility open for fields split by a road. **Open feasibility question:** the `category` vocabulary depends on an unpublished external list, so any conformance claim about categorisation is currently unverifiable.

---

### A-02 · AgGateway Modus (agricultural lab test data exchange) · ISIC A / FORD 4
**Beneficiary, problem, context.** Soil-testing laboratories, agronomists and growers. Soil testing is a low-margin, high-throughput activity where a one-week turnaround is no longer acceptable — fall application "happens right behind the combine" — and non-standard result formats force manual re-keying, with under- or over-application of nutrients creating both margin loss and regulatory liability.
**Source.** AgGateway — *Modus*, github.com/AgGateway/Modus, `README.md` (§"Exchanging soil test data is important, but difficult"; §"What do standards have to do with this?"), `Documentation/Modus Terms and Definitions.md`, `Schema/Modus 1/{modus_submit,modus_result,modus_global}.xsd`, `Method Lists/`. **READ.**
**Deliverables required.** A submit document (work order), a result document, and a shared global type/method vocabulary — all schema-validatable. **Inputs.** Sample identity and collection metadata; lab analytic methods; grower/field references.
**Requirements.**
- `A-02/R1` **[S]** Submission and result are **separate documents** with separate schemas (`modus_submit.xsd`, `modus_result.xsd`), sharing types via `modus_global.xsd`.
- `A-02/R2` **[S]** Test methods are drawn from a maintained method list; **new or changed methods are requested through a defined change process** at ModusStandard.org, not invented per-lab.
- `A-02/R3` **[S]** The exchange must cover the full lab workflow: work-order generation, sample collection, drying, analysis, and result distribution.
- `A-02/R4` **[S]** Farm Management Information Systems must be able both to *produce* work orders and to *consume* results.
- `A-02/R5` **[I]** A result must be linkable back to the submit document that ordered it (implied by the submit/result pairing; the linking field is in the XSDs, not in prose).
**Component relationships.** Submit → result (order/fulfilment; carries sample identity and method selection). Both → global types (shared vocabulary; must be version-consistent). Method list → result values (units and interpretation authority). FMIS ↔ LIMS (two-way; the same identifiers must round-trip).
**Whole-product constraints.** Method identity is the load-bearing element: a numeric result without its method is uninterpretable. Turnaround time is a *competitive* constraint, not merely a nicety. Governance is external and versioned.
**Acceptance.** AC1 *(source)*: documents validate against the published XSDs. AC2 *(source)*: every reported analyte cites a method from the published method list. AC3 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: a result document round-trips through an FMIS and back without loss of sample identity or method reference.
**Assumptions, gaps, unresolved.** **[I]** Modus 1 XSDs are present; whether a JSON serialisation is normative is not established from what I read. Turnaround-time targets are motivational prose, not a stated numeric requirement — **unresolved**.

---

### B-05 · GISTM tailings management · ISIC B / FORD 2 · **SNIPPET — all requirements unverified**
**Beneficiary, problem, context.** Communities living downstream of tailings storage facilities (TSFs), mine operators, and investors. A TSF failure is irreversible and lethal; the standard exists to make consequence, design basis and accountability legible before failure rather than after.
**Source.** Global Tailings Review (UNEP, PRI, ICMM) — *Global Industry Standard on Tailings Management*, August 2020, globaltailingsreview.org. Structure cited: six topic areas, 15 principles, 77 auditable requirements; Requirement 4.1 (consequence classification), Requirements 4.7/5.7 (brownfield flexibility), Principle 15 / Requirement 15.1 (disclosure). **SNIPPET — could not fetch the original PDF; every requirement below is unverified against it.**
**Deliverables required (unverified).** A consequence classification per facility; a design basis covering the whole lifecycle including closure and post-closure; a public disclosure.
**Requirements.**
- `B-05/R1` **[S-unverified]** The standard comprises 15 principles and **77 auditable requirements** across six topic areas.
- `B-05/R2` **[S-unverified]** Requirement 4.1: assign a **consequence classification** grouping TSFs by potential loss of life, number of people affected, environmental damage, cultural impacts, and infrastructure/economic effects.
- `B-05/R3` **[S-unverified]** Principle 4: plans and design criteria must minimise risk across **all lifecycle phases including closure and post-closure**.
- `B-05/R4` **[S-unverified]** Principle 5: the design must integrate the knowledge base and minimise failure risk to people and environment across all lifecycle phases.
- `B-05/R5` **[S-unverified]** Requirement 15.1: the consequence classification must be **publicly disclosed**.
- `B-05/R6` **[S-unverified]** Requirements 4.7 and 5.7 are linked and permit conformance flexibility where upgrading an existing TSF to current best practice is unfeasible or cannot be applied retroactively.
**Component relationships.** Knowledge base → design → consequence classification (each constrains the next). Consequence classification → disclosure (15.1 publishes what 4.1 determines). Brownfield exception (4.7/5.7) → conformance claim (an exception must be *declared*, not silently taken).
**Whole-product constraints.** Effects are irreversible; authority is divided between operator, Engineer of Record and independent review; the classification must remain valid as downstream population changes — i.e. it is state that expires.
**Acceptance.** AC1 *(source, unverified)*: a consequence classification exists for every facility and is publicly disclosed. AC2 *(source, unverified)*: design criteria cover closure and post-closure explicitly. AC3 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: every invoked 4.7/5.7 flexibility is recorded with its justification and re-reviewed on a fixed cycle.
**Assumptions, gaps, unresolved.** **Major:** I could not read the original; the numbering, the count of 77, and the wording of 4.1/15.1 are all second-hand. **Unresolved feasibility:** whether disclosure obligations attach to closed and orphaned facilities.

---

### B-01 · Open Mining Format (OMF) · ISIC B / FORD 2
**Beneficiary, problem, context.** Mine geologists and mining engineers exchanging 3D orebody data between modelling packages. Each vendor package has its own binary format, so a resource model handed to a consultant loses attributes and geometry fidelity; the industry pays for that loss in re-modelling and in disputes about which model was actually used.
**Source.** Global Mining Guidelines Group — *Open Mining Format*, github.com/gmggroup/omf; read via the project's documentation trees at `seequent/omf` (`docs/content/{projects,blockmodel,pointset,surface,composite,attributes,textures,arrays,io}.rst`) and `GMSGDataExchange/omf`, plus the v2 Rust implementation. **READ.** Version: OMF v1.0.1 published; **v2 is beta** (the README carries an explicit `.. warning::`).
**Deliverables required.** A single serialised **Project** file, loadable and savable, containing elements. **Inputs.** Point sets, line sets, surfaces, block models, composites; attribute arrays; textures.
**Requirements.**
- `B-01/R1` **[S]** A Project contains a list of PointSets, LineSets, Surfaces, BlockModels and Composites, and is serialised/deserialised to a single file (`omf.fileio.save` / `.load`).
- `B-01/R2` **[S]** The `BlockModel` element stores **all types** of block model; the type is described by its `grid` and `subblocks` properties rather than by separate element classes.
- `B-01/R3` **[S]** Attributes are carried as arrays bound to elements, with textures as a distinct attachable type.
- `B-01/R4` **[S]** Composites allow recursive containment of elements — a complete element becomes a component of another.
- `B-01/R5` **[S]** v2 is beta and the format is subject to change; v1.0.1 is the stable reference. *(Revision condition.)*
**Component relationships.** Project → elements (container; supplies a single coordinate/reference context). Element → attribute arrays (values bound to geometry positions — the binding must be index-consistent). Composite → elements (recursive composition). File → Project (serialisation boundary; one file, one project).
**Whole-product constraints.** Everything must survive a save/load round trip. Sub-blocking means one element carries two levels of resolution simultaneously. The stability constraint is unusual: consumers must know *which* OMF version wrote the file because v2 is not frozen.
**Acceptance.** AC1 *(source)*: `save` then `load` reproduces the project's elements and attributes. AC2 *(source)*: a sub-blocked block model is expressible without a separate element type. AC3 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: files declare their OMF version and a reader refuses, rather than silently misreads, a version it does not support.
**Assumptions, gaps, unresolved.** The canonical `gmggroup/omf` clone contained only a licence file; I read GMG's content through project mirrors — **recorded as a fidelity caveat**. Whether v2 changes the composite semantics is unresolved.

---

### C-05 · ONIE (Open Network Install Environment) · ISIC C / FORD 2
**Beneficiary, problem, context.** Operators of large switch fleets and the ODMs who manufacture them. A bare-metal switch is useless until an OS is installed; without a common install environment every switch model needs a bespoke provisioning path, and the hardware vendor and the OS vendor are locked together.
**Source.** Open Compute Project — *ONIE*, github.com/opencomputeproject/onie: `README.rst`, `build-config/`, `machine/<vendor>/<platform>/` (per-platform ports, e.g. `accton`, `celestica`, `quanta`), `emulation/README-onie-vm.md`, `contrib/oce/README.md` (ONIE Compliance Environment), `encryption/`. **READ.**
**Deliverables required.** A bootable install environment image per platform; a machine port directory per switch model; a compliance environment. **Inputs.** Vendor board support; a build config; an OS installer to be discovered.
**Requirements.**
- `C-05/R1` **[S]** Each supported switch platform is expressed as a **machine port** under `machine/<vendor>/<platform>/`, so hardware support is additive rather than forked.
- `C-05/R2` **[S]** A compliance environment (`contrib/oce`) exists as the mechanism for asserting that a port conforms.
- `C-05/R3` **[S]** An emulation target (`onie-vm`) must exist so the environment can be exercised without physical hardware.
- `C-05/R4` **[S]** Image signing/encryption support is part of the tree (`encryption/`), i.e. installers may be authenticated.
- `C-05/R5` **[I]** The install environment must discover and hand off to a network OS installer without prior knowledge of which OS — this is the separation the project exists to create.
**Component relationships.** Build config → machine port → image (build-time composition; the port supplies board specifics, the config supplies the common environment). Image → OS installer (runtime handoff; the contract between manufacturer and OS vendor). Emulator → compliance environment (test substrate; must behave like the port). Signing keys → image (authority: who may install).
**Whole-product constraints.** Divided authority is the core condition — the ODM owns the port, the operator owns the OS choice, and neither may need the other's cooperation at install time. Installation is a partially irreversible act on remote hardware.
**Acceptance.** AC1 *(source)*: a new platform is supported by adding a machine directory, without modifying shared code. AC2 *(source)*: the port passes the ONIE Compliance Environment. AC3 *(source)*: the environment boots under `onie-vm`. AC4 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: a signed installer from an OS vendor unknown to the ODM installs successfully.
**Assumptions, gaps, unresolved.** I found **no RFC-2119 normative prose** in the tree; the requirements are expressed as build structure and compliance tooling. **[I]** treats structure as requirement — flagged as interpretation, not source wording.

---

### C-03 · MTConnect · ISIC C / FORD 2
**Beneficiary, problem, context.** Manufacturing engineers and plant IT staff who need machine tools from many builders to report state and measurements into one system. Without a common semantic model, every machine integration is a bespoke driver, and "the same" measurement means different things on two machines.
**Source.** MTConnect Institute — *MTConnect standard*, read via github.com/mtconnect/cppagent, `terms.tex` (the standard's normative glossary, ~5,600 entries) — entries for `filter`/`minimumDelta`, `representation`, `resetTriggered`, request parameters `at`, `from`, `interval`, and `assetId`/asset-changed events. **READ.**
**Deliverables required.** An Agent exposing MTConnect response documents; data items with defined semantics; assets with stable identifiers. **Inputs.** Machine adapter data; a device model.
**Requirements.**
- `C-03/R1` **[S]** For a data item with a `minimumDelta` filter, a new value **MUST NOT** be reported unless the measured value has changed from the last reported value by at least the given delta.
- `C-03/R2` **[S]** The `at` request parameter requires that the response document **MUST** include the current value for all data entities relative to the time a specific sequence number was recorded.
- `C-03/R3` **[S]** The `from` parameter designates the sequence number of the first data entity in the agent's buffer that **MUST** be included in the response document.
- `C-03/R4` **[S]** When a request includes an `interval` parameter, the agent **MUST** respond by repeatedly publishing the required response documents.
- `C-03/R5` **[S]** An asset identifier **MUST** be unique with respect to all other assets in an MTConnect installation.
- `C-03/R6` **[S]** For an asset-changed event, the CDATA value **MUST** be the `assetId` of the asset that changed; **there is no separate message for new assets**.
- `C-03/R7` **[S]** `resetTriggered` identifies when a periodically-reset value has been reset and why.
**Component relationships.** Adapter → agent → client (a pipeline where the agent owns the sequence-number timeline). Sequence number → buffer → `at`/`from` (state: the buffer is finite, so a client that falls behind loses history). Filter → reporting (suppression rule; changes what "no data" means). Asset registry → asset-changed events (identity plus change notification, with creation folded into change).
**Whole-product constraints.** The sequence number is a single global ordering that all clients share — it is the system's authority on "when". Interval streaming makes the agent a concurrent publisher to many clients. Filters mean absence of a report is not absence of change, which every consumer must handle.
**Acceptance.** AC1 *(source)*: with `minimumDelta` set, sub-delta changes produce no new observation. AC2 *(source)*: an `at` request returns the current value of every data entity as of that sequence number. AC3 *(source)*: asset IDs are unique installation-wide. AC4 *(source)*: creating a new asset emits an asset-changed event carrying its `assetId`. AC5 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: a client that reconnects after buffer overrun can detect the gap rather than silently interpolate.
**Assumptions, gaps, unresolved.** I read the glossary rather than the numbered clauses of the published Part 1/2/3 documents; **clause numbers are therefore not cited**. Buffer-overrun client behaviour is unresolved from what I read.

---

### D-04 · EEBus SHIP/SPINE · ISIC D / FORD 2
**Beneficiary, problem, context.** Householders and energy managers who want an EV charger, heat pump, PV inverter and home energy manager from different manufacturers to negotiate power locally — for example to avoid tripping the house fuse when the car and the heat pump both draw at once.
**Source.** EEBus Initiative, read via Enbility's implementation — github.com/enbility/eebus-go, `README.md`: SHIP 1.0.1 (via `ship-go`), SPINE 1.3.0 (via `spine-go`), `features/client` and `features/server` role helpers. **READ (secondary — the normative EEBus specifications are on eebus.org, which is blocked; this is an implementation asserting conformance to named versions).**
**Deliverables required.** A device implementing one or more EEBus use cases; SHIP transport; SPINE data model features in client and/or server role. **Inputs.** Device capabilities; local network; trust/pairing.
**Requirements.**
- `D-04/R1` **[S]** Implementations target **SHIP 1.0.1** for transport and **SPINE 1.3.0** for the data model; the versions are named and pinned.
- `D-04/R2` **[S]** SPINE features carry a **role**: a local feature may act as client (consuming a remote server feature) or as server (serving remote clients). Every interaction is role-asymmetric.
- `D-04/R3` **[S]** Working with the library "requires basic understanding of the EEBUS concepts SHIP and SPINE" and reference to the specifications themselves — i.e. **the implementation is not self-describing**.
- `D-04/R4` **[I]** Use cases are the unit of interoperability: two devices interoperate because they implement the same use case, not merely the same protocol.
**Component relationships.** SHIP → SPINE (transport carries the data model; SHIP handles discovery, pairing and TLS session, SPINE handles semantics). Feature (client) → feature (server) (subscription/binding; state lives on the server side). Use case → features (a use case selects a required feature set). Device → device (peer-to-peer, no central controller — authority is distributed).
**Whole-product constraints.** Pairing is a trust decision made once and relied on continuously. Devices join and leave concurrently. Power decisions have physical effects that are not undoable (a tripped fuse, a cold house), so failure behaviour must be conservative.
**Acceptance.** AC1 *(source)*: the implementation reports SHIP 1.0.1 and SPINE 1.3.0 conformance. AC2 *(source)*: a local feature can be instantiated in either client or server role. AC3 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: with a peer removed mid-negotiation, the remaining device falls back to a safe default limit rather than to its last commanded value.
**Assumptions, gaps, unresolved.** **The normative text was not read.** Version numbers and role model are taken from an implementation README. Which use cases are mandatory is unresolved.

---

### D-01 · OCPI 2.2.1 · ISIC D / FORD 2
**Beneficiary, problem, context.** EV drivers roaming between networks, Charge Point Operators (CPOs) and e-Mobility Service Providers (eMSPs). A driver must be able to see a charger, know its price, start a session on someone else's network and get one correct bill; the parties are commercial competitors who nonetheless must share live state.
**Source.** EVRoaming Foundation — *Open Charge Point Interface (OCPI)*, github.com/ocpi/ocpi: `mod_locations.asciidoc` (lines 36, 46, 144, 166), plus `mod_cdrs`, `mod_sessions`, `mod_tariffs`, `mod_tokens`, `mod_charging_profiles`, `mod_invoice_reconciliation`, `mod_hub_client_info`, `credentials`, `transport_and_format`, `status_codes`, `version_information_endpoint`. **READ.**
**Deliverables required.** A versioned HTTPS interface implementing the chosen modules, with credentials exchange and a version-information endpoint. **Inputs.** Location/EVSE/connector inventory; tariffs; tokens; session and CDR data.
**Requirements.**
- `D-01/R1` **[S]** To delete an EVSE from the list of active EVSEs, the CPO **MUST** update the EVSE's `status` field to `REMOVED` — deletion is a state transition, not a record removal.
- `D-01/R2` **[S]** Private charging locations not intended for public charging **SHALL NOT** be published via OCPI.
- `D-01/R3` **[S]** Each object **must contain all required fields**; unspecified fields may be treated as null.
- `D-01/R4` **[S]** Partial-update logic **MUST** be implemented as specified (the "following logic MUST be implemented accordingly" at §Locations).
- `D-01/R5` **[S]** `evse_uid` is required when requesting an EVSE object; `connector_id` is required when requesting a Connector object — i.e. the identifier set depends on the granularity requested.
- `D-01/R6` **[S]** Identifiers are case-insensitive strings of bounded length (`CiString(36)`).
**Component relationships.** Location → EVSE → Connector (containment hierarchy; identifiers compose downward). Locations → Sessions → CDRs (a charging event flows across modules and must reference the same EVSE identity). Tariffs → CDRs (price authority at time of session). Credentials → all modules (mutual registration establishes who may push what). Hub client info → peer availability (concurrency and liveness).
**Whole-product constraints.** Records are append-and-mark, never delete — history must remain billable. Privacy is a hard exclusion rule, not a filter. Two independent commercial parties hold partial authority over one physical asset. Time matters: the tariff in force at session start governs the CDR.
**Acceptance.** AC1 *(source)*: a removed EVSE appears with `status = REMOVED` rather than disappearing. AC2 *(source)*: no private location is present in a published feed. AC3 *(source)*: a Connector request without `connector_id` is rejected. AC4 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: a CDR can be reconciled to exactly one session and one tariff version.
**Assumptions, gaps, unresolved.** **[I]** OCPI defines the roaming interface but not the charger-to-CPO link (that is OCPP, `D-03`) — the seam between them is where most real failures occur and is out of scope here.

---

### E-03 · ODM2 Observations Data Model · ISIC E / FORD 1
**Beneficiary, problem, context.** Water-resource scientists and monitoring agencies who hold two kinds of data that are usually kept apart: continuous sensor time series, and discrete physical specimens taken to a lab. Analysing drinking-water safety or catchment health needs both in one frame.
**Source.** ODM2 / CUAHSI (NSF-funded) — *ODM2*, github.com/ODM2/ODM2: `doc/ODM2Docs/ext_samplingfeatures.md` (§Sampling Features that are Sites, §…that are Specimens, §Relationships Among SamplingFeatures, §Spatial Offsets for SamplingFeatures), `doc/ODM2Docs/ext_equipment.md`, and the core schema; `usecases/littlebearriver/` includes §Implementation Notes for Sensor-Based Data, §…for Specimen-Based Data, §…for Annotations. **READ.**
**Deliverables required.** A populated relational schema covering sites, specimens, results, equipment and annotations. **Inputs.** Sensor streams; specimen collections; lab analyses; equipment inventory; spatial reference systems.
**Requirements.**
- `E-03/R1` **[S]** Sampling features are a single generalised concept specialised into **Sites** and **Specimens**; both are first-class.
- `E-03/R2` **[S]** The spatial reference system of a Site's latitude/longitude is given by an SRS, and Sites **can be linked to an appropriate record in the SpatialReferences entity** — the CRS is data, not an assumption.
- `E-03/R3` **[S]** Relationships among sampling features are modelled explicitly (a specimen is related to the site it came from).
- `E-03/R4` **[S]** Spatial **offsets** for sampling features are supported (e.g. depth below a datum, distance from a bank).
- `E-03/R5` **[S]** The equipment extension records the instruments producing results — provenance of measurement is part of the model.
- `E-03/R6` **[S]** Annotations are a modelled extension with their own implementation notes, i.e. qualifications on data are structured, not free comments appended to values.
**Component relationships.** Site → Specimen (derivation; the specimen inherits location context but has its own identity and time). Sampling feature → SpatialReferences (interpretation authority for coordinates). Equipment → Result (provenance; supports recalibration and retraction). Annotation → Result/Feature (qualification; carries uncertainty and caveats without altering values). Offset → feature geometry (refinement of position).
**Whole-product constraints.** One schema must hold two temporal regimes: dense regular sensor series and sparse irregular specimen results. Uncertainty and qualification are structural. Equipment changes over time, so provenance is versioned state.
**Acceptance.** AC1 *(source)*: a specimen result and a sensor result at the same site are retrievable through one query path. AC2 *(source)*: every site's coordinates resolve to a SpatialReferences record. AC3 *(source)*: a result traces to the equipment that produced it. AC4 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: annotating a result never mutates the stored value.
**Assumptions, gaps, unresolved.** **[I]** ODM2 is a data model, not a service interface; the API surface is left to implementers. Retention and retraction policy is unresolved.

---

### E-04 · FIWARE Smart Data Models — WasteWater · ISIC E / FORD 2
**Beneficiary, problem, context.** Municipal utilities and smart-city integrators who want treatment-plant telemetry to be queryable across vendors and cities using one context model, so that a dashboard or a regulator's report is not rebuilt per procurement.
**Source.** FIWARE Foundation / Smart Data Models — *dataModel.WasteWater*, github.com/smart-data-models/dataModel.WasteWater: `WasteWaterPlant/{model.yaml, schema.json, notes.yaml, examples/, doc/spec.md}`, `ADOPTERS.yaml`. `doc/spec.md` §35-RequiredProperties. **READ.** Note: this repository is generated from a **shared template** used across all Smart Data Models subjects — see cluster `FIWARE` (also `I-02`, `L-03`); the three are structural variants of one generator.
**Deliverables required.** A JSON Schema, a machine-readable model, worked examples, and translated specification pages. **Inputs.** Domain property definitions; NGSI-LD conventions; adopter registry.
**Requirements.**
- `E-04/R1` **[S]** Required properties of `WasteWaterPlant` are exactly **`id`** and **`type`** (spec §35-RequiredProperties); every domain attribute is optional.
- `E-04/R2` **[S]** Each attribute is declared with an NGSI type (`type: Property`), so the model distinguishes properties from relationships.
- `E-04/R3` **[S]** The specification is published in multiple languages generated from one source (`spec.md`, `spec_ES.md`, `spec_FR.md`, `spec_IT.md`, `spec_JA.md`) — translations must not diverge.
- `E-04/R4` **[S]** Examples accompany the schema and are part of the deliverable.
- `E-04/R5` **[S]** Adopters are recorded in the repository (`ADOPTERS.yaml`).
**Component relationships.** `model.yaml` → `schema.json` → `spec.md` + translations (generation chain; the source of truth is upstream of the docs). Schema → examples (must validate). Model → adopters (feedback path establishing that a change breaks someone).
**Whole-product constraints.** The minimal required core (`id`, `type`) makes conformance cheap but makes *usefulness* unconstrained — two conformant plants may share no attribute. Multi-language generation means editorial changes propagate mechanically.
**Acceptance.** AC1 *(source)*: an entity with only `id` and `type` validates. AC2 *(source)*: all published examples validate against `schema.json`. AC3 *(source)*: all language variants regenerate from the same model without manual edits. AC4 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: a named *profile* of required attributes exists for at least one stated use (e.g. discharge compliance reporting), because the base model alone cannot support it.
**Assumptions, gaps, unresolved.** **Open feasibility question:** with only two required properties, can any cross-city comparison be made without an additional profile layer? I judge not — hence AC4.

---

### F-02 · BuildingSync · ISIC F / FORD 2
**Beneficiary, problem, context.** Energy auditors, building owners and programme administrators. An ASHRAE-style energy audit is delivered as a PDF report; the findings cannot be aggregated across a portfolio, compared between auditors, or fed to a model without re-keying.
**Source.** BuildingSync consortium (US DOE) — *BuildingSync schema*, github.com/BuildingSync/schema: `README.md` (validation of example files; use-case required-element checking; the BuildingSync-gem translating Level 1 and partial Level 2 audits), `docs/versioning.md`, `docs/notebooks/bsync_examples/{Primary-School-Levels1-2, Small-Office-Level-1, Small-Office-Level-2}.md` (§1. Pre-requisites, §2. Useful information), `proposals/`. **READ.**
**Deliverables required.** A schema; validating example audit files at defined audit levels; use-case conformance checking; a translation path to energy models. **Inputs.** Audit findings; building descriptions; measure lists; ASHRAE audit levels.
**Requirements.**
- `F-02/R1` **[S]** Every new update to BuildingSync **is required to pass validation on the example files** — the examples are a regression gate on the schema itself.
- `F-02/R2` **[S]** Conformance is expressed **per use case**: an instance is checked for whether it complies with a specific use case by checking that the required elements are present. There is no single global "valid audit".
- `F-02/R3` **[S]** Audit **levels** (Level 1, Level 2) are distinct and a translation path exists for Level 1 and *partial* Level 2 — partial support is explicit.
- `F-02/R4` **[S]** A documented versioning policy governs schema change (`docs/versioning.md`).
- `F-02/R5` **[S]** Changes are proposed through a `proposals/` process before adoption.
**Component relationships.** Schema → use-case required-element sets (the schema is permissive; use cases add obligation). Examples → schema (bidirectional gate: examples must validate, and the schema must not break them). BuildingSync file → BuildingSync-gem → energy model (downstream consumer that determines which elements actually matter). Proposals → versioning policy → release.
**Whole-product constraints.** Audit level is a scope-of-effort contract with the client, so it must be visible in the data. Two authorities coexist: the schema (structure) and the use case (sufficiency). Partial support must be declared rather than discovered.
**Acceptance.** AC1 *(source)*: candidate schema versions validate all example files. AC2 *(source)*: an instance can be tested against a named use case and reports which required elements are missing. AC3 *(source)*: a Level 1 audit file translates via the gem. AC4 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: an instance declares its audit level, and a consumer can reject a file whose declared level is below what its use case needs.
**Assumptions, gaps, unresolved.** **[I]** "partial Level 2" is a live gap — which Level 2 elements do not translate is not enumerated in what I read.

### F-01 · BCF REST API 3.0 · ISIC F / FORD 2
**Beneficiary, problem, context.** Design and construction teams using different BIM authoring tools who must agree on *issues* — a clash, a missing detail, a defect on site — with the camera viewpoint and the model elements attached, without exchanging whole models.
**Source.** buildingSMART International — *BIM Collaboration Format REST API*, github.com/buildingSMART/BCF-API, `README.md`: §1. Introduction, and the endpoint sections beginning at line ~765 (consistency of field lists) with parameter tables at lines 1191–1269. **READ.**
**Deliverables required.** A server exposing projects, topics, comments, viewpoints and related documents over REST; a consistent field surface. **Inputs.** Model element identifiers (IFC GUIDs); camera state; user identities and authorisations.
**Requirements.**
- `F-01/R1` **[S]** Servers **are required to provide a consistent list of fields across all** relevant endpoints — the field surface may not vary per endpoint.
- `F-01/R2` **[S]** Endpoint parameters are specified with an explicit `required` column; each endpoint declares its mandatory parameters.
- `F-01/R3` **[S]** Topics carry comments and viewpoints as related sub-resources, so an issue is a compound object, not a text field.
- `F-01/R4` **[I]** A viewpoint binds a camera position and a set of referenced components, so that "what I am looking at" is reproducible in a different authoring tool.
**Component relationships.** Project → Topic → {Comment, Viewpoint, Document reference} (containment; permissions cascade). Viewpoint → model element GUIDs (cross-system reference — this is the seam that makes BCF work and the one that breaks when a model is re-exported). Server → many authoring clients (concurrency: several tools comment on one topic).
**Whole-product constraints.** Authority is divided (author, reviewer, contractor) and comment history must be non-destructive because it becomes contractual evidence. Element identity must remain stable across model revisions or viewpoints dangle. Interruption is normal — an issue outlives the session that raised it.
**Acceptance.** AC1 *(source)*: the same field names and types appear for a topic across all endpoints returning it. AC2 *(source)*: omitting a parameter marked required yields an error, not a default. AC3 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: a viewpoint authored in tool A restores the same camera and element selection in tool B. AC4 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: comments are immutable once posted; corrections are new comments.
**Assumptions, gaps, unresolved.** **[I]** Behaviour when a referenced element GUID no longer exists in the current model is the central unresolved question and I did not find it settled in the text I read.

### G-05 · UN Transparency Protocol — Digital Product Passport · ISIC G / FORD 5
**Beneficiary, problem, context.** Buyers, regulators and customs authorities who receive sustainability claims about a product (recycled content, carbon intensity, deforestation-free) and today cannot tell a verified claim from a marketing statement, nor compare two claims made under different standards.
**Source.** UN/CEFACT — *UN Transparency Protocol*, github.com/uncefact/spec-untp, `website/docs/specification/DigitalProductPassport.md`, requirement rows **DPP-01, DPP-09, DPP-10, DPP-11, DPP-12, DPP-13**, and the conformance statement at §"All DPPs are issued as W3C Verifiable Credentials". Versioned docs present for v0.6.0. **READ.**
**Deliverables required.** A Digital Product Passport issued as a W3C Verifiable Credential, aggregating conformity claims with topics, metrics, criteria and evidence. **Inputs.** Product identity at model/batch/item granularity; conformity assessments; standards and regulations; identifier resolution.
**Requirements.**
- `G-05/R1` **[S]** DPP-01 Granularity: the DPP should support use at **model**, **batch**, or **serialised item** level; claims are made at the passport level, which **MUST** have a stated relationship to the product granularity.
- `G-05/R2` **[S]** DPP-09 Claims: the DPP **MUST** allow **any number** of conformity claims in one passport, so it is a single aggregation point for all claims about the product.
- `G-05/R3` **[S]** DPP-10 Conformity Topic: the DPP **MUST** provide a simple mechanism to express the sustainability/circularity/conformity **topic** of each claim so that similar claims can be grouped.
- `G-05/R4` **[S]** DPP-11 Metrics: the DPP **MUST** provide a mechanism to quantify a claim (e.g. carbon intensity, water consumption), express an **accuracy range**, and support comparison.
- `G-05/R5` **[S]** DPP-12 Criteria: the DPP **MUST** allow reference to a standard or regulation **and to the specific criteria within it**.
- `G-05/R6` **[S]** DPP-13 Evidence: the DPP **MUST** allow reference to independent conformity assessments supporting the claims; that evidence **SHOULD** be digitally verifiable.
- `G-05/R7` **[S]** All DPPs **MUST** conform to W3C Verifiable Credentials Data Model 2.0; identified objects carry resolvable `id`s.
**Component relationships.** Product identity → passport (granularity binding — R1). Claim → topic (R3, grouping), → metric with uncertainty (R4), → criterion within a named standard (R5), → evidence credential (R6). Passport → VC envelope (R7: issuer identity, signature, revocation). Identifier resolver → all `id`s (dereference authority).
**Whole-product constraints.** Uncertainty is first-class (accuracy ranges are required, not optional). Authority is layered: the issuer asserts, an independent assessor verifies, a regulation defines the criterion. Claims must remain comparable across issuers, which forces shared topics and metrics. Revocation implies a passport's truth can change after issuance.
**Acceptance.** AC1 *(source)*: a DPP states its granularity and the relationship of claims to it. AC2 *(source)*: a DPP carrying ten claims across three topics validates and groups correctly. AC3 *(source)*: each claim resolves to a named criterion inside a cited standard. AC4 *(source)*: the DPP verifies as a VCDM 2.0 credential. AC5 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: two DPPs from different issuers making the same-topic metric claim can be ranked without human interpretation.
**Assumptions, gaps, unresolved.** UNTP is at v0.x — **stability is an open risk**. **[I]** Comparability across issuers depends on shared metric vocabularies whose governance I did not verify.

---

### G-02 · OASIS Universal Business Language · ISIC G / FORD 5
**Beneficiary, problem, context.** Buyers, sellers and their software vendors exchanging orders, despatch advices, invoices and credit notes across borders, where each jurisdiction adds its own mandatory fields and code lists.
**Source.** OASIS UBL Technical Committee — *Universal Business Language*, github.com/oasis-tcs/ubl: `UBL-2.4.xml` (specification source), `UBL-DefaultDTQ-2.5.sch` (Schematron default-value rules), `UBL-Endorsed-Entities-2.5-csd01.gc` / `-csd02.gc` (genericode code lists), `UBL-CVA-Skeleton.cva` (context/value association), `utilities/` (Crane genericode-to-NDR tooling, SchXslt). **READ (repository artefacts and structure; I did not read the full 2.x prose specification).**
**Deliverables required.** Document schemas; genericode code lists; Schematron rule sets; a context/value association skeleton for customisation. **Inputs.** Business document semantics; jurisdictional code lists; customisation requirements.
**Requirements.**
- `G-02/R1` **[S]** Code lists are maintained as **genericode** artefacts separate from the schemas, and are separately versioned (`-2.5-csd01`, `-csd02`).
- `G-02/R2` **[S]** Default and data-type qualification rules are expressed as **Schematron** (`UBL-DefaultDTQ`), i.e. constraints beyond XSD are normative and machine-checkable.
- `G-02/R3` **[S]** Customisation is performed through a **CVA (context/value association)** skeleton rather than by editing schemas.
- `G-02/R4` **[S]** The specification source and the validation artefacts are versioned together but not identically (2.4 doc alongside 2.5 code lists) — version skew is a managed condition.
- `G-02/R5` **[I]** A conformant instance must satisfy the schema *and* the applicable Schematron *and* the code lists in force for its context.
**Component relationships.** Schema → instance (structure). Genericode list → instance value (vocabulary authority, swappable per jurisdiction). CVA → which code list applies in which context (the customisation mechanism). Schematron → cross-field rules the schema cannot express. Utilities → generation of the above.
**Whole-product constraints.** Three validation authorities must agree. Localisation is by configuration, not by forking — this is the property that makes UBL survivable across jurisdictions. Code-list versions change independently of document schemas.
**Acceptance.** AC1 *(source)*: an instance validates against schema, Schematron and the contextually-selected genericode lists. AC2 *(source)*: a jurisdictional customisation is expressed without modifying the base schema. AC3 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: swapping a code list version changes validation outcomes only for the affected fields.
**Assumptions, gaps, unresolved.** **Fidelity caveat:** I read artefacts and structure, not the normative prose; no clause numbers are cited. Which UBL version is "current" for a given profile (e.g. Peppol BIS, `G-03`) is unresolved here.

---

### H-01 · General Transit Feed Specification · ISIC H / FORD 5
**Beneficiary, problem, context.** Passengers using trip planners, and the transit agencies publishing schedules. A rider needs to know that a stop exists, that a trip serves it, and when — and the same file must work in dozens of independent consumer applications the agency does not control.
**Source.** MobilityData / GTFS community — *General Transit Feed Specification Reference*, github.com/google/transit, `gtfs/spec/en/reference.md`, revised **April 27, 2026**; §Document Conventions (line 48, RFC 2119 key words), §File Requirements (lines 71–73), §Dataset Files table (lines 118–122). **READ.**
**Deliverables required.** A dataset of CSV files with defined presence conditions; each file's records with required/conditionally required/optional fields. **Inputs.** Agency, route, trip, stop and stop-time data; calendar; fares; shapes.
**Requirements.**
- `H-01/R1` **[S]** RFC 2119 key words apply as written ("MUST", "SHALL", "REQUIRED", "SHOULD", "MAY", "OPTIONAL").
- `H-01/R2` **[S]** **Required** means the field or file must be included **and contain a valid value for each record** — presence alone is insufficient.
- `H-01/R3` **[S]** **Conditionally Required** means inclusion is governed by conditions stated in the field or file description.
- `H-01/R4` **[S]** `agency.txt`, `routes.txt`, `trips.txt`, `stop_times.txt` are **Required**.
- `H-01/R5` **[S]** `stops.txt` is **Conditionally Required** — required, among other conditions, when… (the condition set is stated inline in the file description).
- `H-01/R6` **[S]** The document is versioned by revision date with a published revision history and governance process.
**Component relationships.** `trips` → `routes` → `agency` (upward reference chain establishing who operates what). `stop_times` → `trips` + `stops` (the join that makes a schedule; the largest file and the correctness bottleneck). Calendar → trips (temporal validity). Feed → many independent consumers (no coordination: a breaking change reaches unknown readers).
**Whole-product constraints.** Conditional requirement means validity is *contextual*, so a validator must reason, not just check presence. The consumer population is unbounded and unversioned — backwards compatibility is a hard external constraint. Data has a validity window and stale feeds actively mislead.
**Acceptance.** AC1 *(source)*: all Required files are present and every Required field has a valid value in every record. AC2 *(source)*: each Conditionally Required file's condition evaluates correctly for the dataset. AC3 *(source)*: referential integrity holds from `stop_times` to `trips` and `stops`. AC4 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: the feed declares a service period and a consumer can detect expiry without external knowledge.
**Assumptions, gaps, unresolved.** **[I]** GTFS Schedule and GTFS Realtime are separate specifications in the same repository; the consistency obligation between them is not stated as a requirement of either.

---

### H-05 · DCSA electronic Bill of Lading · ISIC H / FORD 5
**Beneficiary, problem, context.** Shippers, carriers, banks and consignees in container shipping. A bill of lading is a document of title — whoever holds it can claim the cargo — so digitising it means digitising *possession and endorsement*, not just the data.
**Source.** Digital Container Shipping Association — *DCSA OpenAPI*, github.com/dcsaorg/DCSA-OpenAPI, `ebl/v3/README.md` (lines 143, 158, 166, 263, 272), `ebl/v3/issuance/README.md`, `bkg/v2/README.md`. **READ.**
**Deliverables required.** OpenAPI interface definitions for eBL, eBL issuance and booking, with versioned change logs. **Inputs.** Shipping instructions; party identities and identifying codes; transport document data.
**Requirements.**
- `H-05/R1` **[S]** `Consignee` and `Endorsee` are **split** into a `ShippingInstructions` version and a `TransportDocument` version, because the TransportDocument versions **MUST** have at least one `identifyingCodes` provided.
- `H-05/R2` **[S]** `identifyingCodes` has `minItems: 1` and is a **required property on `IssueToParty`**.
- `H-05/R3` **[S]** `shipper` is a required party on `HouseBillofLading`.
- `H-05/R4` **[S]** On `GET /v3/transport-documents/{transportDocumentReference}`, the order of **ALL** lists/arrays **MUST** be preserved as provided.
- `H-05/R5` **[S]** Booking (`bkg/v2`) and eBL (`ebl/v3`) are separately versioned interfaces.
**Component relationships.** Booking → Shipping Instructions → Transport Document (a document lineage where each stage adds obligation; the party model tightens as the document approaches legal effect). Issuance API → eBL (the act that creates title). `identifyingCodes` → party identity (the anchor that makes an endorsement enforceable). Ordering guarantee → downstream legal reading (array order is semantic, not incidental).
**Whole-product constraints.** Irreversibility: once issued and endorsed, a bill of title cannot simply be edited. Party identification strength increases along the lineage. Order preservation is a correctness requirement because a document of title is read as a sequence. Authority is divided between carrier (issuer), shipper and holder.
**Acceptance.** AC1 *(source)*: a TransportDocument-version consignee/endorsee without `identifyingCodes` is rejected. AC2 *(source)*: `IssueToParty` with an empty `identifyingCodes` array is rejected. AC3 *(source)*: a GET returns arrays in the exact provided order. AC4 *(source)*: a HouseBillofLading without `shipper` is rejected. AC5 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: an endorsement chain is reconstructible from the API without out-of-band records.
**Assumptions, gaps, unresolved.** **[I]** The API defines data, not the legal possession mechanism (e.g. MLETR-conformant control); the two must be reconciled outside this specification — **unresolved feasibility question**.

### I-04 · LIVES restaurant inspection feeds · ISIC I / FORD 5 · **SNIPPET — unverified**
**Beneficiary, problem, context.** Diners choosing a restaurant, and municipal health departments whose inspection results sit in a portal nobody visits. The value only appears when the score reaches the person at the moment of choosing.
**Source.** Yelp with the City of San Francisco and the City of New York — *Local Inspector Value-Entry Specification (LIVES)*, version 2.0, yelp.com/healthscores. **SNIPPET — the specification page could not be fetched; all requirements unverified.**
**Deliverables required (unverified).** A feed of CSV files published by a municipality: `feed_info.csv`, business, inspection and (optionally) violation files. **Inputs.** Inspection records; business identity and address; violation codes.
**Requirements.**
- `I-04/R1` **[S-unverified]** All files in a LIVES feed **must be saved as comma-delimited text**.
- `I-04/R2` **[S-unverified]** The **first line of each file must contain field names**.
- `I-04/R3` **[S-unverified]** `feed_info.csv` is **required** and should contain a **single row**.
- `I-04/R4` **[S-unverified]** `violations.csv` is **optional**.
- `I-04/R5` **[S-unverified]** This is **version 2.0** of LIVES.
**Component relationships.** `feed_info` → the rest of the feed (metadata authority: publisher, version, currency of data). Business records → inspection records → violation records (a three-level chain; the optional level is the most explanatory one). Municipality → consumer platform (publication handoff; matching a municipal business record to a consumer platform listing is the hard, unspecified step).
**Whole-product constraints.** The most useful detail (violations) is optional, so consumers must degrade gracefully. Business identity matching across systems is the real failure mode. Scores are time-stamped judgements that age.
**Acceptance.** AC1 *(source, unverified)*: every file parses as CSV with a header row. AC2 *(source, unverified)*: `feed_info.csv` is present with exactly one data row. AC3 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: a consumer can render a feed containing no `violations.csv` without an error state. AC4 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: ≥95% of businesses in the feed match a consumer-platform listing, with unmatched records reported rather than dropped.
**Assumptions, gaps, unresolved.** **Severe:** field-level requirements for the business and inspection files are unknown to me. Whether scores are comparable between jurisdictions (different inspection regimes) is **unresolved and likely negative**.

### I-05 · FDA FSMA 204 Food Traceability Rule · ISIC I / FORD 4 · **SNIPPET — unverified**
**Beneficiary, problem, context.** People who get sick in a foodborne outbreak, and the investigators who must find the contaminated lot before more product is eaten. Restaurants, retailers and processors handling foods on the Food Traceability List must be able to answer "where did this lot come from and where did it go" within hours, not weeks.
**Source.** US Food and Drug Administration — *Requirements for Additional Traceability Records for Certain Foods* (FSMA §204 Food Traceability Final Rule), fda.gov. Compliance date cited: **20 July 2028**. **SNIPPET — fda.gov blocked; all requirements unverified.**
**Deliverables required (unverified).** Records of Key Data Elements (KDEs) for each Critical Tracking Event (CTE); a traceability lot code assignment and linking scheme; an electronic sortable spreadsheet on request. **Inputs.** Product and lot identity; supply-chain event data; trading-partner exchanges.
**Requirements.**
- `I-05/R1` **[S-unverified]** Entities that manufacture, process, pack or hold foods on the **Food Traceability List** must capture and maintain **KDEs** for each **CTE**.
- `I-05/R2` **[S-unverified]** CTEs include growing, receiving, transforming, shipping, harvesting, cooling and initial packing.
- `I-05/R3` **[S-unverified]** KDEs include product descriptions, lot numbers, dates, locations and quantities.
- `I-05/R4` **[S-unverified]** Businesses must document **how they assign, maintain and link traceability lot codes** through receiving, transformation and shipping.
- `I-05/R5` **[S-unverified]** On FDA request, KDEs referenced to a traceability lot code must be provided as an **electronic sortable spreadsheet within 24 hours, inclusive of weekends**.
- `I-05/R6` **[S-unverified]** Records must be retained for **24 months**.
- `I-05/R7` **[S-unverified]** Restaurants serving FTL foods are in scope from **20 July 2028**.
**Component relationships.** Lot code → CTE records (the spine: every event references the lot). Transformation CTE → new lot code linked to input lots (the point where traceability is usually lost). Trading partner A → partner B (KDE exchange; each party holds only its own segment). Records → the 24-hour spreadsheet (a latency contract on retrieval, not on storage).
**Whole-product constraints.** A hard 24-hour clock including weekends means the retrieval path must work without a data team present. Records span organisational boundaries with no central registry. Transformation breaks lot identity unless explicitly linked. Retention is 24 months, so the system must survive staff turnover and system migrations.
**Acceptance.** AC1 *(source, unverified)*: for any FTL lot code, all associated KDEs are producible as a sortable spreadsheet within 24 hours of request, on a weekend. AC2 *(source, unverified)*: transformation events link output lot codes to input lot codes. AC3 *(source, unverified)*: records are retrievable for 24 months. AC4 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: a drill exercised at 16:30 on a Friday completes by 16:30 Saturday without ad-hoc engineering.
**Assumptions, gaps, unresolved.** **Severe:** exact KDE lists per CTE, exemptions, and the Food Traceability List contents were not read. **[I]** "electronic sortable spreadsheet" is a format obligation that constrains architecture more than it appears.

### J-04 · OpenAPI Specification 3.2.0 · ISIC J / FORD 2
**Beneficiary, problem, context.** API producers and every downstream party that generates clients, mock servers, documentation and tests from a description. A description that is ambiguous produces tools that quietly disagree.
**Source.** OpenAPI Initiative — *OpenAPI Specification*, github.com/OAI/OpenAPI-Specification, `versions/3.2.0.md`: line 5 (RFC 2119/8174 key words), line 54, line 73, line 83, line 85, line 93. **READ.**
**Deliverables required.** An OpenAPI Description document. **Inputs.** API structure; schemas; a JSON Schema dialect; markdown descriptions.
**Requirements.**
- `J-04/R1` **[S]** RFC 2119 key words apply as interpreted by RFC 8174.
- `J-04/R2` **[S]** Patterned fields **MUST** have unique names within the containing object.
- `J-04/R3` **[S]** Where tooling renders rich text it **MUST** support at minimum CommonMark 0.27; tooling **MAY** ignore some CommonMark features.
- `J-04/R4` **[S]** If the accompanying JSON Schema differs from the prose section, **the prose section MUST be considered authoritative**.
- `J-04/R5` **[S]** A field not explicitly REQUIRED or described with MUST/SHALL **can be considered OPTIONAL**.
- `J-04/R6` **[S]** In addition to the required fields, **at least one of `components`, `paths`, or `webhooks` MUST be present**.
**Component relationships.** Prose spec → JSON Schema of the spec (conflict-resolution rule R4 names the authority explicitly). Description document → generators/validators/mocks (one artefact, many divergent consumers). `paths`/`webhooks`/`components` → the document's minimum usefulness (R6 prevents an empty but valid description).
**Whole-product constraints.** Two representations of the same rules exist and can drift; the specification pre-declares which wins. Optionality is default, so tooling must not infer obligation. Rendering is delegated to an external standard at a pinned version.
**Acceptance.** AC1 *(source)*: a document with duplicate patterned-field names is rejected. AC2 *(source)*: a document lacking all of `components`, `paths` and `webhooks` is rejected. AC3 *(source)*: where schema and prose conflict, a conformance test follows the prose. AC4 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: two independent generators produce clients that agree on every required/optional distinction.
**Assumptions, gaps, unresolved.** **[I]** R4 is unusual and load-bearing: it means machine validation alone cannot establish conformance.
