### J-02 · OpenTelemetry Metrics SDK · ISIC J / FORD 2
**Beneficiary, problem, context.** Operators diagnosing production incidents across services written in many languages. Metrics must mean the same thing regardless of which language SDK emitted them, or cross-service dashboards lie.
**Source.** CNCF OpenTelemetry — *OpenTelemetry Specification*, github.com/open-telemetry/opentelemetry-specification, `specification/metrics/sdk.md` §Metrics SDK, §MeterProvider (lines 106, 112, 127, 129, 134, 138); document status markers (`Development`, per `document-status.md`); also `specification/metrics/data-model.md`, `oteps/`. **READ.**
**Deliverables required.** A conforming SDK per language, providing a MeterProvider and Meters. **Inputs.** Resource attributes; instrumentation scope; exporters; the API layer.
**Requirements.**
- `J-02/R1` **[S]** **All language implementations of OpenTelemetry MUST provide an SDK.**
- `J-02/R2` **[S]** A `MeterProvider` **MUST** provide a way to allow a `Resource` to be associated with it.
- `J-02/R3` **[S]** The `MeterProvider` **MUST** implement the "Get a Meter" API.
- `J-02/R4` **[S]** The input provided by the user **MUST** be used to create the Meter.
- `J-02/R5` **[S]** A working Meter **MUST** be returned as a fallback rather than returning null or raising — degraded operation, never failure.
- `J-02/R6` **[S]** Some MeterProvider requirements carry **Status: Development**, meaning they may change. *(Revision condition.)*
**Component relationships.** API → SDK (the API is what instrumented code calls; the SDK is what an operator configures — the separation lets libraries instrument without choosing a backend). MeterProvider → Meter → instruments (creation chain carrying scope identity). Resource → MeterProvider (identity of the emitting entity; joined at export). Views/exporters → aggregation (where cardinality is controlled).
**Whole-product constraints.** Never break the application: telemetry failure must degrade, not propagate (R5). Requirements carry maturity status, so conformance is version- and status-scoped. Cross-language semantic equivalence is the actual product.
**Acceptance.** AC1 *(source)*: requesting a Meter with invalid input still returns a working Meter. AC2 *(source)*: a Resource associated with the MeterProvider appears on exported metrics. AC3 *(source)*: the same instrumentation in two languages produces metrics that join on resource and scope. AC4 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: a conformance report states which Development-status requirements were implemented.
**Assumptions, gaps, unresolved.** **[I]** "Development" status means a passing conformance run can be invalidated by a spec revision — consumers need the status set recorded alongside results.

---

### K-02 · FINOS FDC3 Desktop Agent API · ISIC K / FORD 5
**Beneficiary, problem, context.** Traders and analysts running many vendor applications on one desktop. Clicking an instrument in a chart should populate the order ticket and the news panel — today this is solved with bespoke bilateral integrations that break at every vendor upgrade.
**Source.** FINOS — *FDC3*, github.com/finos/FDC3, `website/docs/api/spec.md` (lines 45, 98, 123, 124, 130, 132), `website/docs/api/ref/DesktopAgent.md`, `website/docs/agent-bridging/spec.md`. **READ.**
**Deliverables required.** A Desktop Agent implementing the FDC3 interfaces; intent resolution; channel membership; app lifecycle control. **Inputs.** App directory registrations; context objects; user channel state.
**Requirements.**
- `K-02/R1` **[S]** The API consists of interfaces; each Desktop Agent is expected to implement them, and a Desktop Agent **MUST** provide implementations for the listed interfaces.
- `K-02/R2` **[S]** A compliant Desktop Agent implementation **MUST** satisfy an enumerated obligation list (§"An FDC3 Standard compliant Desktop Agent implementation MUST").
- `K-02/R3` **[S]** Intent resolution **MUST** take into account any specified **input or return context types**.
- `K-02/R4` **[S]** Requests for resolution to apps returning a channel **MUST** include apps registered as returning a channel of a specific type.
- `K-02/R5` **[S]** After applying a User channel membership change, the agent dispatches a `userChannelChanged` event to apps with a matching listener — including when the app itself initiated the change.
- `K-02/R6` **[S]** `fdc3.close()` allows an app to request closure of its own window or frame; on success the app **MUST** be closed.
**Component relationships.** App → Desktop Agent → App (brokered; no app knows another's identity). App directory → intent resolution (registration is the authority on capability). User channel → context broadcast (shared state across apps; membership is per-app). Agent bridging → other agents (federation across desktops). Close → lifecycle (an irreversible act on a running app).
**Whole-product constraints.** Concurrency: many apps listen to one channel. Divided authority: the user picks the channel, the directory declares capability, the agent arbitrates. Self-initiated changes still produce events, so apps must be idempotent about their own actions. Closing is irreversible and must not lose in-flight results.
**Acceptance.** AC1 *(source)*: intent resolution excludes apps whose declared input/return context types do not match. AC2 *(source)*: an app that changes its own channel receives `userChannelChanged`. AC3 *(source)*: `fdc3.close()` closes the calling app's window. AC4 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: with three apps on one channel, a broadcast reaches all three without ordering assumptions.
**Assumptions, gaps, unresolved.** **[I]** Behaviour when an intent resolves to an app that is not running (launch-then-deliver) has timing implications I did not confirm in the text.

---

### K-04 · FIX Orchestra · ISIC K / FORD 5
**Beneficiary, problem, context.** Trading venues and their members. A FIX "rules of engagement" document is prose: which fields a venue actually requires, which values it accepts, and what workflow follows are discovered through integration testing that takes weeks per counterparty.
**Source.** FIX Trading Community — *FIX Orchestra*, github.com/FIXTradingCommunity/fix-orchestra: `repository/README.md` (XML schema for Orchestra conveying message structures, components, and FIX application behaviours; users may express workflow), `dsl-antlr/` (a grammar for the conditional expression language), `message-model/`, `orchestra2doc/`, `repository2010/`, `interfaces/`. Normative schema documentation is referenced to `fix-orchestra-spec` v1-0-STANDARD. **READ.**
**Deliverables required.** A machine-readable Orchestra repository file per venue: message structures, components, code sets, scenarios, and behavioural rules; generated documentation and bindings. **Inputs.** Existing FIX message definitions; venue-specific rules of engagement; workflow definitions.
**Requirements.**
- `K-04/R1` **[S]** The Orchestra repository is defined by an **XML schema** that conveys message structures and their components **as well as FIX application behaviours**.
- `K-04/R2` **[S]** Users **may express workflow** in the repository — behaviour is in scope, not only structure.
- `K-04/R3` **[S]** Conditional logic is expressed in a defined DSL with an ANTLR grammar (`dsl-antlr`) — rules are parseable, not prose.
- `K-04/R4` **[S]** Java bindings are generated from the schema so applications manipulate a repository as objects rather than raw XML.
- `K-04/R5` **[S]** A documentation generator (`orchestra2doc`) produces human-readable output from the same source.
- `K-04/R6` **[S]** A migration path exists from the older repository format (`repository2010`).
**Component relationships.** Orchestra repository → generated docs (`orchestra2doc`) and → generated code (bindings): **one source, two audiences**. Scenario → message structure (the same message type has different required fields in different scenarios — this is the core insight). DSL expressions → field presence/values (conditional obligation). `repository2010` → current (migration; old rules must not be lost).
**Whole-product constraints.** The same document must be simultaneously legible to a compliance officer and executable by a conformance test. Rules are conditional on scenario and state. Venues change rules on notice, so the artefact is versioned and dated.
**Acceptance.** AC1 *(source)*: a repository file validates against the Orchestra schema. AC2 *(source)*: DSL expressions parse under the published grammar. AC3 *(source)*: documentation and code bindings are both generated from the one file. AC4 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: a conformance suite generated from a venue's Orchestra file detects a message that violates a scenario-conditional rule.
**Assumptions, gaps, unresolved.** **Fidelity caveat:** the normative Orchestra specification lives in a separate repository I did not clone; I read the reference implementation's schema modules and README. **[I]** Whether venues publish Orchestra files in practice — the adoption question — is unresolved.

---

### L-01 · RESO Web API Core · ISIC L / FORD 5
**Beneficiary, problem, context.** Home buyers and the brokerages, portals and appraisers who consume Multiple Listing Service data. Every MLS historically exposed a different feed, so a national portal maintained hundreds of ingestion pipelines and the data meant different things in each.
**Source.** RESO (Real Estate Standards Organization) — *RESO Web API Core Specification*, github.com/RESOStandards/transport, `proposals/web-api-core.md` (line 39 RFC 2119; lines 44, 45, 62, 70, 71, 75) and `proposals/data-dictionary.md`; Data Dictionary 1.7 references at `dd.reso.org`. **READ.**
**Deliverables required.** An OData-based Web API server exposing standard resources with server-driven paging and expansion. **Inputs.** MLS listing data; Data Dictionary definitions; lookup/enumeration values.
**Requirements.**
- `L-01/R1` **[S]** RFC 2119 key words apply.
- `L-01/R2` **[S]** The **Property, Member, Office, Field and Lookup resources MUST be available at the top level**; they MAY additionally be expanded into other resources (e.g. Member into Property as ListAgent).
- `L-01/R3` **[S]** Providers **MUST support server-driven paging** using `@odata.nextLink`.
- `L-01/R4` **[S]** Support for expanded data elements using OData `$expand` is required.
- `L-01/R5` **[S]** **Expanded properties MUST use the definitions in the RESO Data Dictionary** where applicable.
- `L-01/R6` **[S]** As of Data Dictionary 1.7, standard names exist for related standard data elements (e.g. `Media` in the Property resource).
**Component relationships.** Web API (transport) → Data Dictionary (semantics): two separately versioned standards that must be cited together for any conformance claim. Property ↔ Member/Office (expansion relationships representing the human parties to a listing). Lookup resource → enumerated values (vocabulary authority). Paging → consumer replication (the mechanism by which a consumer stays in sync).
**Whole-product constraints.** Two-standard versioning (transport + dictionary) means "RESO certified" is meaningless without both version numbers. Server-driven paging shifts control of batch size to the provider — consumers cannot assume stability across pages. Listing data is time-sensitive and legally consequential (fair-housing constraints on what may be published).
**Acceptance.** AC1 *(source)*: all five named resources are retrievable at top level. AC2 *(source)*: a large result set is traversable purely by following `@odata.nextLink`. AC3 *(source)*: expanded fields resolve to Data Dictionary definitions. AC4 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: a full replication followed by incremental sync converges to the same record set as a fresh full pull.
**Assumptions, gaps, unresolved.** **[I]** `proposals/` is where these documents live in the repository; the ratified artefacts are published at transport.reso.org, which I could not fetch — **status of the exact file read as "ratified" vs "proposal" is unresolved**.

---

### L-04 · Property Data Trust Framework · ISIC L / FORD 5 · **SNIPPET — unverified**
**Beneficiary, problem, context.** Home buyers and sellers in England and Wales, plus conveyancers, lenders and estate agents. A property transaction stalls because the same facts (title, EPC, searches, seller's disclosures) are re-collected by each party with no shared record of who verified what — buyers and sellers describe the process as one in which they feel helpless.
**Source.** Property Data Trust Framework (developed by ~150 Home Buying and Selling Group members) — *PDTF schemas*, propdata.org.uk; github.com/Property-Data-Trust-Framework/schemas. **SNIPPET — I confirmed the repository exists via search but did not read it; all requirements unverified.**
**Deliverables required (unverified).** JSON Schema definitions for residential property data; an OpenAPI-described REST interface; verified claims with provenance. **Inputs.** BASPI dataset; Title Deed; EPC; property searches; form layouts.
**Requirements.**
- `L-04/R1` **[S-unverified]** The schema incorporates the existing **BASPI** dataset and additionally Title Deed, EPC and Property Searches data.
- `L-04/R2` **[S-unverified]** The schema contains **labels that allow referencing a single piece of data by where it appears on each form**, so data maps to and from digital versions of forms.
- `L-04/R3` **[S-unverified]** **Verified claims** package property data with verification evidence, enabling traceability back to authoritative sources.
- `L-04/R4` **[S-unverified]** A common API uses an **Open API Standard following REST principles**.
- `L-04/R5` **[S-unverified]** The next version will migrate to **W3C Verifiable Credentials** for selective disclosure and granular permissions. *(Revision condition.)*
**Component relationships.** Claim → evidence → authoritative source (provenance chain). Data item → form label (bidirectional mapping so legacy paper forms and digital records stay reconcilable). Participants (agent, conveyancer, lender, buyer, seller) → shared property record (divided authority over one object). Current schema → future VC model (planned migration).
**Whole-product constraints.** Trust is the product: an unverified field and a verified field must be visibly different. Consent and selective disclosure matter because the record contains personal data about the seller. The record outlives any one transaction and is re-used at the next sale.
**Acceptance.** AC1 *(source, unverified)*: any data item resolves to its position on each relevant form. AC2 *(source, unverified)*: a claim carries evidence traceable to an authoritative source. AC3 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: a buyer can see, per field, who asserted it and who verified it. AC4 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: migration to VCs preserves every existing claim's provenance.
**Assumptions, gaps, unresolved.** **Severe:** schema contents unverified. **Unresolved:** liability for a wrong verified claim — a governance question the framework must answer for the product to be usable.

### M-01 · ODK XForms Specification · ISIC M / FORD 5
**Beneficiary, problem, context.** Field enumerators collecting survey and monitoring data offline in low-connectivity settings, and the researchers who design their forms. A form must enforce skip logic, validation and required-answer rules on a phone with no server, and must produce data that means the same thing whichever client rendered it.
**Source.** ODK — *ODK XForms Specification*, github.com/getodk/xforms-spec, `_sections/30-bindings.md` (lines 27, 32, 36, 39, 44, 45), `_sections/40-body.md`, `_sections/120-entities.md`. **READ.**
**Deliverables required.** A form definition with a model (bindings) and a body (presentation), rendering identically across conforming clients. **Inputs.** Question definitions; XPath expressions; translations; entity definitions.
**Requirements.**
- `M-01/R1` **[S]** On `<bind>` nodes, **only the `nodeset` attribute is required**; all other binding attributes are optional.
- `M-01/R2` **[S]** `nodeset` specifies the XPath path to the instance node or attribute, as in XForms 1.0.
- `M-01/R3` **[S]** `required` specifies whether the question requires a non-empty value, using a boolean **expression** (not a literal flag) — requiredness can depend on other answers.
- `M-01/R4` **[S]** `jr:requiredMsg` and `jr:constraintMsg` specify custom messages shown when `required` or `constraint` is violated; each value may be a string literal **or a translation function**.
- `M-01/R5` **[S]** The model (bindings) and the body (presentation) are separate sections of the specification and of the form.
**Component relationships.** Body → binding → instance node (three-layer separation: what is shown, what is enforced, what is stored). Binding expression → other instance nodes (cross-field dependency; the dependency graph determines evaluation order). Message → translation catalogue (localisation of an error, not just of a label). Entities → data reuse across forms (state carried between submissions).
**Whole-product constraints.** All logic must evaluate **offline on-device**. Requiredness and validity are dynamic, so a form's obligations change as it is filled — interruption and resumption must preserve that state. Localisation extends to error messages, which are the moments where comprehension matters most.
**Acceptance.** AC1 *(source)*: a `<bind>` with only `nodeset` is valid. AC2 *(source)*: a `required` expression referencing another field toggles requiredness as that field changes. AC3 *(source)*: a violated constraint displays the localised `jr:constraintMsg`. AC4 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: an interrupted form resumes with all computed states (relevance, requiredness) correctly re-derived.
**Assumptions, gaps, unresolved.** **[I]** Evaluation-order guarantees for mutually dependent expressions are not fully pinned in the section I read.

---

### M-02 · RO-Crate · ISIC M / FORD 5
**Beneficiary, problem, context.** Researchers, data stewards and repositories who must hand over "the whole thing" — data files, scripts, workflows, licences, people and their roles — as one package that a machine can understand years later, without a bespoke deposit format per repository.
**Source.** Research Object community — *RO-Crate*, github.com/ResearchObject/ro-crate, `docs/_specification/1.1/` (and `1.0/`, `0.2/`, plus appendices), `docs/pages/community/outreach.md`. **READ.**
**Deliverables required.** An RO-Crate: a directory or archive with a root data entity and an `ro-crate-metadata.json` descriptor using schema.org terms in JSON-LD. **Inputs.** Files and directories; contextual entities (people, organisations, licences, workflows); external identifiers.
**Requirements.**
- `M-02/R1` **[S]** The specification is versioned as distinct, separately published documents (0.2, 1.0, 1.1, …); a crate is conformant **to a named version**.
- `M-02/R2` **[S]** Metadata is expressed as JSON-LD using schema.org vocabulary, in a single descriptor file at the crate root.
- `M-02/R3` **[S]** Data entities (files, directories) and contextual entities (people, licences, organisations) are distinguished, and both are described in the same graph.
- `M-02/R4` **[I]** The crate must be self-describing: a consumer with only the archive must be able to identify the root entity, its licence and its parts.
**Component relationships.** Root data entity → parts (files/directories) → contextual entities (authorship, licence, provenance). Descriptor → external identifiers (ORCID, DOI, ontology terms) — dereferenceable but the crate must remain interpretable if they are unreachable. Crate → repository deposit (handoff), and crate → crate (a crate may be a component of a larger crate: **recursive composition**).
**Whole-product constraints.** Longevity: the package must be readable without the tooling that produced it. Licence and authorship travel with the data or the package is unusable. Profiles specialise the base for domains (e.g. workflows), so conformance is base + profile.
**Acceptance.** AC1 *(source)*: `ro-crate-metadata.json` exists at the root and declares the conformed-to specification version. AC2 *(source)*: every file in the crate is either described in the graph or explicitly out of scope. AC3 *(source)*: the root entity has a licence. AC4 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: a crate opened with no network access still yields author, licence and file inventory.
**Assumptions, gaps, unresolved.** **[I]** The precise 1.1 MUST-list was not transcribed line by line; I read the specification tree and appendices. Profile conformance semantics are unresolved here.

### N-04 · ESCO classification · ISIC N / FORD 5 · **SNIPPET — unverified**
**Beneficiary, problem, context.** Jobseekers, employment services and employers across EU member states, where the same occupation is named differently in 27 labour markets and skills stated in a CV cannot be matched to a vacancy across a language border.
**Source.** European Commission — *ESCO (European Skills, Competences, Qualifications and Occupations)*, esco.ec.europa.eu. **SNIPPET.** Corroborating search results reference a taxonomy with relationships between **2,942 occupations and 13,485 skills/competences**, an ESCO API, and third-party extraction tools. **All requirements unverified.**
**Deliverables required (unverified).** A multilingual classification of occupations, skills/competences and qualifications, with relationships between them, published via an API and downloadable datasets.
**Requirements.**
- `N-04/R1` **[S-unverified]** The classification relates occupations to skills/competences (order of magnitude: ~2,900 occupations, ~13,500 skills).
- `N-04/R2` **[S-unverified]** It is multilingual across EU languages, with concepts stable across translations.
- `N-04/R3` **[S-unverified]** It is exposed through an API usable for matching and for building derived artefacts (e.g. open badges keyed to ESCO skills).
- `N-04/R4` **[I]** Concept identity must be stable across releases, or every downstream CV, vacancy and badge referencing a skill breaks.
**Component relationships.** Occupation ↔ skill (essential/optional relations). Concept URI → all downstream references (identity anchor). ESCO → national classifications (mapping; ESCO does not replace them). Extraction tools → free-text CVs and vacancies (the practical entry point).
**Whole-product constraints.** Language neutrality with legal and cultural variation between member states. Version stability against continuous labour-market change — an inherent tension. Matching decisions affect individuals' access to work, so false negatives are consequential.
**Acceptance.** AC1 *(source, unverified)*: a skill concept resolves in every supported language to the same URI. AC2 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: a vacancy and a CV described independently in two languages match on shared skill URIs. AC3 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: a release provides a machine-readable diff of added, deprecated and merged concepts.
**Assumptions, gaps, unresolved.** **Severe:** the actual data model, licence and versioning policy were not read. ESCO version (v1.1/v1.2) unconfirmed.

### N-05 · HR Open Standards 4.1 recruiting/screening · ISIC N / FORD 5 · **SNIPPET — unverified**
**Beneficiary, problem, context.** Employers, staffing agencies, assessment vendors and applicant-tracking systems. A single hire touches sourcing, screening, assessment, interviewing and onboarding across several vendors; each integration is bespoke, and candidate data is re-entered at each hop.
**Source.** HR Open Standards Consortium — *HR Open 4.1 / 4.2 data exchange specifications*, hropenstandards.org. **SNIPPET.** Cited structure: JSON specifications for Assessments, Benefits, Compensation, Interviewing, Recruiting, Screening, Timecard and Wellness; each repository holds a WIP folder, a JSON folder and an XML folder; each schema folder holds noun and reusable schemas, a top-level schema listing all domain nouns, and a sample folder of domain instances. **All requirements unverified.**
**Deliverables required (unverified).** Per-domain JSON (and XML) schemas, code lists, and sample instances, downloadable free of charge.
**Requirements.**
- `N-05/R1` **[S-unverified]** Domains are separately specified: Assessments, Benefits, Compensation, Interviewing, Recruiting, Screening, Timecard, Wellness.
- `N-05/R2` **[S-unverified]** Each domain provides **noun schemas plus reusable schemas**, a **top-level schema listing all domain nouns**, and **sample instances**.
- `N-05/R3` **[S-unverified]** JSON and XML representations are maintained in parallel folders for the same domain.
- `N-05/R4` **[S-unverified]** The Recruiting specification includes a **Candidate** schema covering education, professional experience and contact information.
- `N-05/R5` **[S-unverified]** Schemas, instances and code lists are free to download.
**Component relationships.** Candidate → assessment result → interview record → offer (a chain crossing vendor boundaries, all keyed to one person). Noun schema → reusable schema (shared types across domains). JSON ↔ XML (parallel representations that must not diverge). Code lists → field values.
**Whole-product constraints.** The data is personal and often special-category (assessment results, background screening), so retention and consent govern the whole chain. Vendors join and leave mid-process. Two serialisations double the drift risk.
**Acceptance.** AC1 *(source, unverified)*: each domain publishes a top-level schema enumerating its nouns. AC2 *(source, unverified)*: sample instances validate against their schemas. AC3 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: the same candidate record round-trips JSON→XML→JSON without loss. AC4 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: a candidate's screening result can be deleted on request without breaking referential integrity of the hire record.
**Assumptions, gaps, unresolved.** **Severe:** no schema was read. Version currency (4.1 vs 4.2) unconfirmed.

### O-02 · Open Contracting Data Standard 1.2 · ISIC O / FORD 5
**Beneficiary, problem, context.** Citizens, journalists, auditors and competing suppliers who want to see how public money is spent. Procurement information is scattered across planning notices, tenders, awards and contracts published at different times by different offices, so nobody can follow one process end to end.
**Source.** Open Contracting Partnership — *Open Contracting Data Standard*, github.com/open-contracting/standard, `docs/schema/reference.md` (§Release Reference, lines 5, 7, 33, 84, 225, 251, 516), `docs/guidance/map/framework_agreements.md`, `docs/governance/index.md`. **READ.**
**Deliverables required.** Releases and release packages; records; a consistent `ocid` per contracting process. **Inputs.** Planning, tender, award, contract and implementation data; organisation registries.
**Requirements.**
- `O-02/R1` **[S]** Releases are **immutable**: they present information about a particular event in the lifetime of a contracting (or planning) process. **Publishers must not edit a release after publication**; a new release is issued instead.
- `O-02/R2` **[S]** Releases **must be published within a release package**.
- `O-02/R3` **[S]** To **remove** rather than update a previously-set value, the field **must be set to `null`** (per the merging rules) — absence and deletion are distinguished.
- `O-02/R4` **[S]** **Each organisation referenced in a release must be included in the `parties` section.**
- `O-02/R5` **[S]** **Every contract must have a related award, linked via `awardID`** — because supplier information is carried on the award.
- `O-02/R6` **[S]** A date-time **must include a time and a timezone/offset**.
- `O-02/R7` **[S]** Releases may contain all or a subset of awards; a related award block is required where awards are referenced.
**Component relationships.** `ocid` → all releases for one process (the identity thread; without it nothing joins). Release → release package (publication envelope). Releases → record (compiled view produced by merging under the null-deletion rule). Award → contract (`awardID`, carrying supplier identity). Party references → `parties` (single place where each organisation is described).
**Whole-product constraints.** Append-only history: corrections are new events, so the audit trail is the product. Time zones are mandatory because processes cross jurisdictions and deadlines are legally binding. Partial publication is expected, so consumers must handle incomplete processes. Divided authority: many buying entities publish into one standard.
**Acceptance.** AC1 *(source)*: no published release is ever modified in place; corrections appear as later releases sharing the `ocid`. AC2 *(source)*: every organisation mentioned appears in `parties`. AC3 *(source)*: every contract resolves an `awardID` to an award in the same process. AC4 *(source)*: all date-times carry a timezone. AC5 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: merging all releases for an `ocid` yields a record identical to the publisher's own compiled record.
**Assumptions, gaps, unresolved.** **[I]** Framework agreements are handled by separate mapping guidance — a sign that the base model does not cover multi-stage purchasing cleanly. **Unresolved:** how a publisher retracts a release published in error, given immutability.

---

### O-04 · U.S. Web Design System · ISIC O / FORD 2
**Beneficiary, problem, context.** Members of the public using federal government websites — including people with disabilities, people on old devices, people in a hurry and under stress — and the many small agency teams building those sites without design or accessibility specialists.
**Source.** US General Services Administration — *U.S. Web Design System*, github.com/uswds/uswds, `COMMUNITY.md` (§Roles & responsibilities; lines 109, 115, 143, 161), plus the component library and `.agents/skills/uswds-code-review/references/{gates,calibration}.md`. **READ.**
**Deliverables required.** A component and pattern library with code, plus a governance process controlling what enters it. **Inputs.** Agency requirements; accessibility standards; contributed components.
**Requirements.**
- `O-04/R1` **[S]** A change requires that **at least one current USWDS Maintainer approve the PR, including their reasoning for that decision** — approval must be justified, not merely granted.
- `O-04/R2` **[S]** For certain changes the **USWDS Product Owner / Lead must then approve the PR, including their reasoning**.
- `O-04/R3` **[S]** For some categories, **at least one current Maintainer or Internal Team Member must approve and merge**.
- `O-04/R4` **[S]** A Maintainer must meet all the responsibilities and requirements of a Contributor, **plus** additional ones — roles are cumulative and explicit.
- `O-04/R5` **[S]** The project maintains an accessibility strategy and code-review gates as first-class artefacts.
**Component relationships.** Contributor → Maintainer → Product Owner (an escalating authority ladder, with reasoning recorded at each rung). Component → consuming agency sites (one change propagates to many sites the team does not control). Review gates → merge (automated plus human). Accessibility strategy → component acceptance.
**Whole-product constraints.** **Divided authority with recorded rationale** is the distinctive constraint: the decision record, not just the decision, is a deliverable. Downstream consumers are numerous and cannot be coordinated, so backwards compatibility and deprecation windows govern. Accessibility is a legal obligation, not a quality goal.
**Acceptance.** AC1 *(source)*: no PR merges without the required approvals for its category. AC2 *(source)*: each approval carries recorded reasoning. AC3 *(source)*: components pass the accessibility gates. AC4 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: a deprecation is announced with a stated support window before removal.
**Assumptions, gaps, unresolved.** **[I]** The mapping from change type to required approval level is described in COMMUNITY.md prose; whether it is enforced automatically is unresolved.

### P-05 · LRMI learning resource metadata · ISIC P / FORD 5
**Beneficiary, problem, context.** Teachers and learners looking for material that fits a specific learning need, and publishers who want their resources found. Search alone is insufficient: as the source itself argues, *"Discovery of new content alone is not enough to drive improvement in learning."*
**Source.** DCMI — *Learning Resource Metadata Initiative*, github.com/dublincore/lrmi, `docs/what-is-lrmi-metadata-for.md` (line 13), `lrmi_vocabs/learningResourceType/learningResourceType.md`, `docs/OLD-smart-publisher-guide.md`. **READ.**
**Deliverables required.** Metadata terms and controlled vocabularies for describing learning resources, aligned with schema.org. **Inputs.** Resource descriptions; learning outcomes; learner information; vocabulary terms.
**Requirements.**
- `P-05/R1` **[S]** LRMI is explicitly **not just about search**: collections of data about learning resources, learning outcomes and learner information are the intended purpose.
- `P-05/R2` **[S]** Controlled vocabularies are published as separate versioned artefacts (e.g. `learningResourceType`), not embedded in the term definitions.
- `P-05/R3` **[I]** Terms are designed to be used within schema.org markup, so a learning resource description is simultaneously web-discoverable and pedagogically meaningful.
- `P-05/R4` **[I]** Alignment to learning outcomes requires an external competency framework; LRMI provides the linkage point, not the framework.
**Component relationships.** Resource description → `learningResourceType` vocabulary (kind of thing: worksheet, assessment, simulation). Resource → learning outcome/competency (alignment, requiring an external framework such as a national curriculum). Resource → learner information (fit: age range, reading level). LRMI terms → schema.org (host vocabulary; adoption depends on the host's release cycle).
**Whole-product constraints.** Usefulness depends on a *third* artefact (a competency framework) that LRMI does not supply. Vocabulary terms must remain stable because they are embedded in published web pages that are never updated. Educational appropriateness is contextual and contested.
**Acceptance.** AC1 *(source)*: resource descriptions use published `learningResourceType` terms. AC2 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: a resource aligned to a competency framework can be retrieved by that competency, not only by keyword. AC3 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: vocabulary changes are additive; retired terms resolve rather than 404.
**Assumptions, gaps, unresolved.** **[I]** Some documents in the repository are explicitly marked "OLD STUFF BELOW HERE" — **currency of individual documents is unresolved** and I did not establish which are normative today.

### P-03 · Ed-Fi Data Standard · ISIC P / FORD 5
**Beneficiary, problem, context.** School districts and state education agencies, and ultimately teachers who need one view of a student across the assessment system, the attendance system, the gradebook and the intervention programme — each supplied by a different vendor.
**Source.** Ed-Fi Alliance — *Ed-Fi Data Standard*, github.com/Ed-Fi-Alliance-OSS/Ed-Fi-Standard: `Models/*.mmd` (entity-relationship models, e.g. `StudentAssessment.mmd`, `Assessment-AssessmentMetaData.mmd`, `AlternativeAndSupplementalServices-FederalPrograms.mmd`, `BellSchedule.mmd`), `Schemas/{Bulk,JSON}`, `Descriptors/`, `Samples/`, `eng/xml-validator/`, `eng/prepare-openapi/`. **READ.**
**Deliverables required.** A domain model expressed as diagrams plus machine-readable schemas (bulk XML and JSON/OpenAPI), a descriptor (controlled vocabulary) set, and validation tooling. **Inputs.** Domain entity definitions; local vocabularies; vendor systems.
**Requirements.**
- `P-03/R1` **[S]** The standard is decomposed into **named domains** (Assessment, Alternative and Supplemental Services, Bell Schedule, …), each with its own model file — extension and change are domain-scoped.
- `P-03/R2` **[S]** Both **bulk (XML)** and **API (JSON/OpenAPI)** representations are produced from the standard; an XML validator and an OpenAPI preparation step are maintained in-tree.
- `P-03/R3` **[S]** **Descriptors** provide the controlled-vocabulary layer, distinct from the entity model, allowing local value sets without model change.
- `P-03/R4` **[S]** Sample instances are maintained as part of the standard.
- `P-03/R5` **[I]** Interoperability rests on a shared student identity across domains; each domain model references the same student entity.
**Component relationships.** Domain model → schemas → API surface (generation chain). Descriptors → field values (local extensibility point — this is what lets a state add its own programme codes without forking). Bulk load ↔ API (two ingestion paths that must converge on the same data). Samples → validators (regression material).
**Whole-product constraints.** Student data is legally protected (FERPA-type constraints), so identity linkage is both the core requirement and the core risk. Local extensibility must not break national comparability. Two ingestion modes create reconciliation duties.
**Acceptance.** AC1 *(source)*: an instance validates under the XML validator and against the generated OpenAPI. AC2 *(source)*: a locally-defined descriptor value is accepted without model modification. AC3 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: the same student loaded by bulk and by API resolves to one identity. AC4 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: a domain can be versioned without forcing revalidation of unrelated domains.
**Assumptions, gaps, unresolved.** **[I]** The `Models/*.mmd` files are Mermaid diagrams — human-readable models whose relationship to the normative schemas I did not verify line by line.

---

### Q-05 · OpenHIE Architecture Specification · ISIC Q / FORD 3
**Beneficiary, problem, context.** Ministries of health in low- and middle-income countries assembling a national health information exchange from separately procured components — a client registry, a facility registry, a shared health record, a terminology service — often funded by different donors on different timelines.
**Source.** OpenHIE — *OpenHIE Architecture Specification*, github.com/openhie/openhie-specification: `introduction/shared-health-record/save-patient-level-clinical-data-workflow.md` (§Interaction Description; §Assumptions and Prerequisites), `introduction/alerting-sending-reminders-or-information/{send-health-worker-alert-workflow, send-client-alert-workflow}.md`. **READ.**
**Deliverables required.** Workflow specifications: for each workflow, an interaction description and an explicit statement of assumptions and prerequisites. **Inputs.** Component registries; an interoperability layer; point-of-service systems.
**Requirements.**
- `Q-05/R1` **[S]** Each workflow is specified with an explicit **"Assumptions and Prerequisites"** section — what must already be true before the workflow can run is part of the specification.
- `Q-05/R2` **[S]** Each workflow has an **Interaction Description** enumerating the ordered exchanges between components.
- `Q-05/R3` **[S]** Workflows are defined per purpose (save patient-level clinical data; send health-worker alert; send client alert), not per component.
- `Q-05/R4` **[I]** Components are replaceable: the architecture specifies the interactions, so any conformant client registry may be substituted.
**Component relationships.** Point-of-service system → interoperability layer → registries + shared health record (mediated; the layer holds routing and identity resolution authority). Client registry → patient identity (the prerequisite most workflows assume). Terminology service → coded values. Alerting workflows → health worker vs client (different recipients, different consent and privacy conditions).
**Whole-product constraints.** Components arrive at different times under different funders — so a workflow must state what it presumes rather than assume a complete platform. Patient identity resolution is probabilistic and consequential. Alerts reach people directly, making privacy and message content sensitive. Connectivity is intermittent.
**Acceptance.** AC1 *(source)*: every workflow document states its assumptions and prerequisites. AC2 *(source)*: the interaction sequence is reproducible against conformant components. AC3 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: substituting one conformant client registry for another leaves all workflows passing. AC4 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: a workflow whose prerequisites are unmet fails visibly at the prerequisite, not midway.
**Assumptions, gaps, unresolved.** **[I]** The specification is architecture-level; wire-level conformance is delegated to profiles (IHE, FHIR). Which profile version is required is unresolved in what I read.

---

### Q-02 · OMOP Common Data Model · ISIC Q / FORD 3
**Beneficiary, problem, context.** Researchers running the same study across many hospitals' and insurers' databases without moving patient data. Each source database has its own schema and vocabulary; without a common model, a multi-site study means re-writing the analysis for every site.
**Source.** OHDSI — *OMOP Common Data Model*, github.com/OHDSI/CommonDataModel: `inst/csv/OMOP_CDMv5.4_Field_Level.csv` (the field-level normative table: `cdmTableName, cdmFieldName, isRequired, cdmDatatype, userGuidance, etlConventions, isPrimaryKey, isForeignKey, fkTableName, fkFieldName, fkDomain, fkClass`), `inst/ddl/{5.3,5.4,5.5}`, `extras/migration/v54_to_v55/`. **READ.**
**Deliverables required.** DDL for the CDM tables plus a field-level specification carrying, per field, its requiredness, datatype, user guidance and **ETL conventions**. **Inputs.** Source EHR/claims data; OHDSI standardised vocabularies; an ETL.
**Requirements.**
- `Q-02/R1` **[S]** In `PERSON`, `person_id`, `gender_concept_id`, `year_of_birth`, `race_concept_id` and `ethnicity_concept_id` are `isRequired = TRUE`; `month_of_birth`, `day_of_birth`, `birth_datetime`, `location_id`, `provider_id`, `care_site_id` are `isRequired = FALSE`.
- `Q-02/R2` **[S]** Each field carries **`etlConventions`** — normative instructions on how to populate it from source data (e.g. "for data sources with date of birth, the year should be extracted"; if no year of birth is available, guidance applies).
- `Q-02/R3` **[S]** `gender_concept_id` captures **biological sex at birth** and *"should not be used to study gender identity"* — a stated scope restriction on the field's meaning.
- `Q-02/R4` **[S]** `location_id` captures the **last known** location; `provider_id` the **last known** primary care provider — these are current-state, not historical, fields.
- `Q-02/R5` **[S]** Foreign keys are declared per field (`isForeignKey`, `fkTableName`, `fkFieldName`, `fkDomain`, `fkClass`), including the vocabulary **domain and class** a concept must belong to.
- `Q-02/R6` **[S]** Versions 5.3, 5.4 and 5.5 coexist with published migration scripts. *(Revision condition.)*
- `Q-02/R7` **[S]** `birth_datetime` is "not required but highly encouraged".
**Component relationships.** Source data → ETL (governed by `etlConventions`) → CDM tables. CDM field → vocabulary concept, constrained by `fkDomain`/`fkClass` (semantic typing beyond referential integrity). CDM version → analysis code (an analysis written for 5.4 must be migrated for 5.5). Person → all clinical event tables (identity spine).
**Whole-product constraints.** The model is the contract for **federated** analysis: code travels, data does not, so any site-specific deviation silently corrupts multi-site results. Field semantics carry explicit epistemic limits (R3) that analysts must respect. "Last known" fields mean the model is not fully historical — a real analytic constraint.
**Acceptance.** AC1 *(source)*: no NULLs in fields marked `isRequired = TRUE`. AC2 *(source)*: every `*_concept_id` resolves to a concept in the declared domain and class. AC3 *(source)*: a 5.4 instance migrates to 5.5 using the published scripts. AC4 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: the same study package executed at two sites returns results whose differences are attributable to population, not to ETL variance.
**Assumptions, gaps, unresolved.** **[I]** `etlConventions` is prose embedded in a CSV — machine-checkable conformance to it is not possible, which is the model's main quality gap. **Unresolved:** how a site declares deliberate deviations.

---

### R-05 · OpenActive Modelling Opportunity Data · ISIC R / FORD 5
**Beneficiary, problem, context.** People trying to find a swimming session, a walking group or a yoga class near them tonight, and the leisure operators, councils and clubs whose sessions are invisible online. The data exists in booking systems but is not published in any comparable form.
**Source.** OpenActive (UK) — *Modelling Opportunity Data 2.0*, github.com/openactive/modelling-opportunity-data, `2.0/index.html` (line 614 RFC 2119 conventions; lines 740, 746, 904, 905, 907), plus `1.1/` and `EditorsDraft/`. **READ.**
**Deliverables required.** Published opportunity data describing sessions/events, with stable identifiers, usable by third-party discovery applications. **Inputs.** Booking-system session data; venue and organiser records; leader/instructor details.
**Requirements.**
- `R-05/R1` **[S]** RFC 2119 conventions apply (marked up as `rfc2119` classes in the document).
- `R-05/R2` **[S]** Applications **MUST** respect privacy and data protection laws and **must not publish** certain personal information about individuals (e.g. volunteers).
- `R-05/R3` **[S]** Information about a leader's **qualifications or experience MUST NOT be published as open data without getting their consent** — consent is a precondition, not a courtesy.
- `R-05/R4` **[S]** When a URI is supplied for a resource, it **MUST be stable and unchanging over time**.
- `R-05/R5` **[S]** Applications indexing opportunity data **SHOULD** use that URI as the unique identifier for the resource.
- `R-05/R6` **[S]** Data **MAY** be discoverable from a web page, and applications **MUST** be able to (retrieve it as specified).
**Component relationships.** Opportunity (a bookable session) → organiser → place → leader (four entities with different privacy statuses: the session is public, the leader is a person). URI → downstream index (identity across independent consumers; instability breaks every index at once). Booking system → open data feed → discovery app (a publication pipeline crossing commercial boundaries).
**Whole-product constraints.** **Consent-gated publication** is the distinguishing constraint: the most useful attribute for a participant (who is leading, and are they qualified?) is exactly the one requiring individual consent. Identifier stability must survive booking-system migration. Sessions are perishable — a stale feed sends people to a cancelled class.
**Acceptance.** AC1 *(source)*: no leader qualification data is published without recorded consent. AC2 *(source)*: resource URIs remain resolvable and unchanged across feed regenerations. AC3 *(source)*: no personal information excluded by R2 appears in the feed. AC4 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: withdrawal of consent removes the affected attributes from the published feed within a stated window.
**Assumptions, gaps, unresolved.** **[I]** Consent withdrawal timing is not stated — hence AC4 is proposed. Cancellation propagation latency is unresolved.

---

### R-04 · IPTC SportsML-G2 · ISIC R / FORD 6
**Beneficiary, problem, context.** Sports news agencies, broadcasters and data providers who must exchange results, statistics, schedules and standings across many sports, where football, baseball and motor racing have irreconcilably different notions of "an event" and "a score".
**Source.** IPTC — *SportsML-G2 3.x*, github.com/iptc/sportsml-3: `specification/` (core plus sport-specific XSDs: `sportsml-specific-{american-football, baseball, basketball, curling, golf, ice-hockey, motor-racing, rugby, soccer}.xsd`, alongside `NewsML-G2_2.22-spec-All-Power.xsd`), `documentation/` (generated XSD documentation, e.g. `sportsml_xsd.html`), `examples/`. **READ.**
**Deliverables required.** A core schema plus per-sport extension schemas; generated documentation; worked examples. **Inputs.** Event, competitor, statistic and standings data; NewsML-G2 packaging.
**Requirements.**
- `R-04/R1` **[S]** The model is **core + per-sport extension**: a shared core schema with separate sport-specific schemas layered on it.
- `R-04/R2` **[S]** SportsML is bound to **NewsML-G2 2.22**, i.e. sports data is carried inside the general news-exchange envelope rather than as a standalone format.
- `R-04/R3` **[S]** Documentation is **generated from the schemas** and published alongside them, so the schema is the single source of truth.
- `R-04/R4` **[S]** Examples are maintained per sport as part of the deliverable.
- `R-04/R5` **[I]** A consumer supporting only the core must be able to process a sport-specific document to the extent of the core — otherwise per-sport extension defeats the purpose.
**Component relationships.** Core schema → sport extension (specialisation; the extension may add but must not contradict). SportsML → NewsML-G2 (packaging: provenance, rights, embargo and versioning come from the news envelope, not from SportsML). Schema → generated docs (derivation). Examples → conformance evidence.
**Whole-product constraints.** Live data: scores change during transmission, so revision and correction semantics come from NewsML-G2. Rights and embargoes attach to sports data commercially. Adding a new sport must not require changing the core.
**Acceptance.** AC1 *(source)*: a sport-specific instance validates against core + extension. AC2 *(source)*: documentation regenerates from the schemas without manual edits. AC3 *(source)*: instances are valid NewsML-G2 items. AC4 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: a core-only consumer extracts competitors and result without understanding the sport extension.
**Assumptions, gaps, unresolved.** **[I]** I read schemas and generated documentation, not a prose specification with clause numbers. Whether a new sport can be added without a core release is unresolved.

---

### S-04 · iFixit repair documentation and repairability scoring · ISIC S / FORD 2 · **SNIPPET — unverified**
**Beneficiary, problem, context.** People trying to fix their own devices, community repair volunteers, and buyers deciding between products. A repair fails when a step is ambiguous or a required tool is discovered halfway through; a purchase decision fails when repairability is invisible at the point of sale.
**Source.** iFixit (and iFixit Europe, technical contributor to `S-01` ORDS) — *Repairability Scoring Rubric v2.2*, *Creating a Repair Guide*, *Guidelines for creating a Teardown*, *Ensuring Quality Repair Information*, ifixit.com. **SNIPPET — all requirements unverified.** Note cluster `REPAIR`: iFixit Europe, The Restart Project and the Open Repair Alliance co-produced the Open Repair Data Standard, so `S-01`, `S-04` and `S-05` share an origin.
**Deliverables required (unverified).** Repair guides (replacement procedures), teardowns, and a repairability score per device. **Inputs.** A device; disassembly observation; tool inventory; component criticality weights.
**Requirements.**
- `S-04/R1` **[S-unverified]** Guide details **must include difficulty, time required, and tools required**.
- `S-04/R2` **[S-unverified]** Photo markup uses a fixed colour order — **red for the first item, orange for second, yellow for third** — to keep markups unambiguous.
- `S-04/R3` **[S-unverified]** The **built-in markup tool must be used** rather than external image editing, so markup remains editable and correctable by others.
- `S-04/R4` **[S-unverified]** If more than four or five pieces of markup are needed, the work **should be split into multiple steps**.
- `S-04/R5` **[S-unverified]** Teardowns and replacement guides are **distinct genres**: teardowns show internal hardware highlights; replacement guides show the steps to remove a broken component so a replacement can be installed.
- `S-04/R6` **[S-unverified]** Scoring method: trace the path for replacing each critical component; record each disassembly and reassembly action; multiply its proxy time by the scaling factor for any required tool; sum the products; map the result into a predefined range corresponding to a score; multiply by the component's importance weight. **The sum of individual component scores is 80% of the overall score.**
- `S-04/R7` **[S-unverified]** Criteria are updated **at least once annually**; v2.2 is current as of Q4 2025. *(Revision condition.)*
**Component relationships.** Teardown → guide (observation feeds procedure). Guide steps → tool list (a step's tool requirement must appear in the guide header, or the repairer is stranded). Disassembly path + tool scaling + component weight → component score → overall score (an explicit calculation, 80% of the total). Rubric version → published scores (a score is only comparable within a rubric version).
**Whole-product constraints.** Editability by third parties is a design constraint (R3) — the artefact is communal, not authored once. Scores across rubric versions are not comparable, so every published score must carry its version. Disassembly is partially irreversible: a guide that gets the order wrong destroys the device.
**Acceptance.** AC1 *(source, unverified)*: every guide states difficulty, time and tools before step 1. AC2 *(source, unverified)*: no step carries more than ~5 markup elements. AC3 *(source, unverified)*: a published score cites its rubric version. AC4 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: two independent assessors scoring the same device under v2.2 agree within one point.
**Assumptions, gaps, unresolved.** **Severe:** the rubric wiki pages were not read; the 80% weighting and the colour order are second-hand. What constitutes the remaining 20% is **unknown to me**.

---

### S-01 · Open Repair Data Standard · ISIC S / FORD 5
**Beneficiary, problem, context.** Community repair groups (repair cafés, Restart parties) collecting data at busy events, and the campaigners and regulators who need evidence about which products fail, how, and whether they can be fixed. Data is recorded "usually by volunteers" under time pressure, so the standard must be collectable in a noisy room, not only correct in principle.
**Source.** Open Repair Alliance — *Open Repair Data Standard (ORDS)*, github.com/openrepair/datastandard, `standard.rst`: §Guiding principles, §Collected data. Published at standard.openrepair.org; v0.3 is the latest released version (adds estimated product ages and unique IDs for product categories). Data areas: product-related, repair-related, session-related. **READ.**
**Deliverables required.** Published repair records conforming to the standard, structured across the three data areas, using shared codelists. **Inputs.** Event records (Fixometer or equivalent); product categories; repair outcomes.
**Requirements.**
- `S-01/R1` **[S]** Data must be **Structured** — valid in line with the requirements of the standard.
- `S-01/R2` **[S]** Data must be **Comparable** — linkable across publishers through **codelists and shared references**.
- `S-01/R3` **[S]** Data must be **Open** — appropriately licensed and published.
- `S-01/R4` **[S]** Data must be **Accurate** — as accurate as possible.
- `S-01/R5` **[S]** Data must be **Timely** — kept up to date and updated regularly.
- `S-01/R6` **[S]** Scope is **repair of small electricals and consumer electronics**; extension to other repair areas is anticipated but not current.
- `S-01/R7` **[S]** The standard explicitly acknowledges the tension between ideal data and what can be collected in busy repair environments — **not all organisations are expected to collect all fields**.
**Component relationships.** Session → repair record → product (three nested scopes: the event, the attempt, the thing). Product category codelist + unique category IDs → cross-publisher comparison (R2's mechanism). Publisher → aggregated Alliance dataset (many small groups into one evidence base). Fixometer (and equivalent tools) → ORDS output (collection instrument feeding the standard).
**Whole-product constraints.** **Collectability under adverse conditions is a first-class constraint**, which is why obligation is graded rather than absolute. Comparability depends entirely on shared codelists, so codelist governance is the load-bearing part. Data concerns products, not people, which is what makes open publication viable.
**Acceptance.** AC1 *(source)*: published data validates against the standard's structure. AC2 *(source)*: product categories use the shared codelist IDs. AC3 *(source)*: an explicit open licence accompanies the publication. AC4 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: records from two different groups can be merged and counted by product category without manual reconciliation.
**Assumptions, gaps, unresolved.** **[I]** The guiding principles are stated as expectations of member organisations rather than as RFC-2119 obligations — conformance is social as much as technical. Version currency beyond v0.3 unverified.

---

### T-02 · FAO Food Insecurity Experience Scale · ISIC T / FORD 5 · **SNIPPET — unverified**
**Beneficiary, problem, context.** Households whose own food provisioning is failing, and the statistical offices and agencies who must measure that across very different countries and languages so that the numbers are comparable. This sits in ISIC T because the phenomenon measured is the household's own-use provisioning capability.
**Source.** FAO Voices of the Hungry — *Food Insecurity Experience Scale survey module and analytical protocol*, fao.org (FIES Technical Paper v1.1); microdata catalogue at microdata.fao.org. **SNIPPET — all requirements unverified.**
**Deliverables required (unverified).** An 8-item survey module with yes/no responses; a statistically validated prevalence estimate.
**Requirements.**
- `T-02/R1` **[S-unverified]** The module consists of **eight questions** with **yes/no** responses, capturing a range of food-insecurity severity.
- `T-02/R2` **[S-unverified]** The module may be administered with either a **one-month or a 12-month recall period**, chosen according to research or programme priorities.
- `T-02/R3` **[S-unverified]** Question stems are framed as *"During the last 12 months, was there a time when, because of lack of money or other resources: …"*.
- `T-02/R4` **[S-unverified]** Data **must be statistically validated** against the assumptions of the **Rasch model**; the analytical protocol is developed and maintained by FAO.
- `T-02/R5` **[S-unverified]** **Only if the Rasch requirements are satisfied may the items be used** to produce a meaningful measure of food insecurity — validation is a gate, not a diagnostic.
- `T-02/R6` **[S-unverified]** The expected response pattern is monotone: a respondent answering yes to an item is expected to answer yes to all less severe items, and no to an item implies no to all more severe items.
**Component relationships.** Item severity ordering → respondent severity (the Rasch scale is the joining structure). Survey administration → raw responses → Rasch validation → prevalence estimate (a pipeline where a failed gate invalidates everything downstream). Translation → item severity (a mistranslated item shifts its severity and breaks comparability). National module → global reference scale (equating step for cross-country comparison).
**Whole-product constraints.** Cross-cultural comparability is the whole point and the whole difficulty. The measure is of *experience*, so it depends on respondent honesty about a stigmatised condition. Recall period choice changes what is measured, so it must be reported. Validation can fail — an unusual property for a survey instrument, and one that must be designed for.
**Acceptance.** AC1 *(source, unverified)*: all eight items are administered with the stated stem and reference period. AC2 *(source, unverified)*: Rasch fit statistics satisfy the FAO protocol before any prevalence is published. AC3 *(source, unverified)*: the recall period is reported with the estimate. AC4 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: where validation fails, the failure is published rather than the estimate.
**Assumptions, gaps, unresolved.** **Severe:** the technical paper was not read; item wordings, fit thresholds and equating procedure are second-hand. **Unresolved:** what a country should do when items fail validation year after year.

---

### T-01 · ILO Domestic Workers Convention No. 189 · ISIC T / FORD 5 · **SNIPPET — unverified**
**Beneficiary, problem, context.** Domestic workers — approximately **75.6 million people worldwide, 4.5% of all workers**, mostly women and many migrants — who work in private households where labour inspection does not reach, and the households that employ them without recognising themselves as employers.
**Source.** International Labour Organization — *Convention No. 189, Domestic Workers Convention, 2011*, Articles 7, 9 and 10; text at un.org (ILO_C_189.pdf) and ilo.org. **SNIPPET — both hosts blocked; all requirements unverified.**
**Deliverables required (unverified).** National measures giving effect to the Convention; for each worker, communicated terms and conditions of employment, preferably in writing.
**Requirements.**
- `T-01/R1` **[S-unverified]** Article 7: domestic workers **must be informed of their terms and conditions of employment in an easily understandable manner, preferably through a written contract**, including employer identity, workplace, starting date, duration, and termination conditions.
- `T-01/R2` **[S-unverified]** Article 9: measures must ensure workers are **free to agree with the employer whether to reside in the household**.
- `T-01/R3` **[S-unverified]** Article 9: workers residing in the household are **not obliged to remain in the household or with household members during daily and weekly rest or annual leave**.
- `T-01/R4` **[S-unverified]** Article 10: measures towards **equal treatment with workers generally** as regards normal hours of work, overtime compensation, daily and weekly rest, and annual paid leave.
- `T-01/R5` **[S-unverified]** **Weekly rest shall be at least 24 consecutive hours.**
- `T-01/R6` **[S-unverified]** **Stand-by hours** — periods when the worker is not free to dispose of their time and must remain at the disposal of the household — are to be regulated.
**Component relationships.** Convention → ratifying state → national law → household employer → worker (a four-hop chain in which each hop can absorb the obligation without transmitting it — the central implementation problem). Written particulars → enforceability (the artefact that makes the rest inspectable). Stand-by definition → hours counted → pay owed (a definitional link with direct financial effect). Residence choice → rest entitlement (R2 conditions R3).
**Whole-product constraints.** The workplace is a private home: inspection, evidence and enforcement all confront privacy. The employer is usually not an organisation and has no HR function. Many workers are migrants whose immigration status is tied to the employer — a power asymmetry that makes formally available rights practically unusable. Effects of dismissal can be immediate homelessness for live-in workers.
**Acceptance.** AC1 *(source, unverified)*: each worker can produce a statement of terms covering employer, workplace, start date, duration and termination conditions, in a language they understand. AC2 *(source, unverified)*: weekly rest of at least 24 consecutive hours is provided and recorded. AC3 *(source, unverified)*: live-in workers are not required to remain in the household during rest periods. AC4 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: stand-by hours are separately recorded and compensated under a stated rule.
**Assumptions, gaps, unresolved.** **Severe:** the Convention text was not read; article numbering and the 24-hour figure are second-hand. **Unresolved:** the Convention binds states, not households directly — the brief's "beneficiary" framing therefore requires an intermediate national instrument that I have not identified for any specific jurisdiction.

---

### U-01 · IATI Standard · ISIC U / FORD 5
**Beneficiary, problem, context.** People in countries receiving aid, the governments planning around it, and the donors and NGOs spending it. Without a common publication format, no one can see who is funding what, where, and whether two donors are funding the same clinic twice.
**Source.** IATI Secretariat — *IATI Standard*, github.com/IATI/IATI-Standard-SSOT (the Single Source of Truth: `templates/en/{codelist,schema_element}.rst`, `README_standalone.rst`) with github.com/IATI/IATI-Extra-Documentation `en/activity-standard.rst` and `en/rulesets/standard-ruleset.rst`. **READ.** Cluster `IATI` (the two repositories are one governance origin).
**Deliverables required.** Published activity files conforming to the activity standard, plus organisation files; codelists; rulesets. **Inputs.** Activity, transaction, budget, result and participating-organisation data; standard codelists.
**Requirements.**
- `U-01/R1` **[S]** Organisations publish data in an **activity file**; the expectation stated is that **every organisation publishes** its activities.
- `U-01/R2` **[S]** Documentation is generated from a **Single Source of Truth**: schema elements and codelists render from templates, so the published documentation cannot diverge from the schema.
- `U-01/R3` **[S]** A **standard ruleset** exists separately from the schema, expressing constraints that XML validation cannot.
- `U-01/R4` **[S]** Codelists are versioned artefacts referenced by the schema, not enumerations inside it.
- `U-01/R5` **[I]** Conformance is therefore three-layered: schema validity, codelist membership, and ruleset satisfaction.
**Component relationships.** SSOT → generated documentation + schema (one source, two products). Schema → codelists (vocabulary authority, independently versioned). Ruleset → cross-field and cross-file constraints. Publisher file → aggregation (the IATI Registry/Datastore) → country-level analysis. Activity → participating organisations (the links that let two donors' records be recognised as the same project).
**Whole-product constraints.** Publishers are self-organising and unfunded for this task, so obligation must be light enough to be met; that admits low-quality data, which the ruleset layer exists to surface. Data is published continuously and consumed retrospectively. Traceability between a donor's activity and an implementer's activity is the hardest and most valuable property.
**Acceptance.** AC1 *(source)*: an activity file validates against the schema. AC2 *(source)*: coded values are members of the referenced codelist versions. AC3 *(source)*: the file satisfies the standard ruleset. AC4 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: a funder's activity identifier appears in the implementer's file so a chain of funding is reconstructable end to end.
**Assumptions, gaps, unresolved.** **[I]** The default clone branch was `version-2.03`-era content; **the current version in force is unconfirmed**. Whether ruleset failures block publication or merely flag it is unresolved.

---

### U-04 · Open SDG platform · ISIC U / FORD 5
**Beneficiary, problem, context.** National statistical offices publishing their country's Sustainable Development Goal indicators, and the citizens, journalists and UN reviewers who read them. Each country has different data availability, disaggregations and proxies, but the presentation must remain comparable and honest about its limits.
**Source.** Open SDG partnership — *Open SDG*, github.com/open-sdg/open-sdg: `docs/indicator-configuration.md` (lines 142, 667, 675, 735, 760), `docs/configuration.md`, `docs/data-configuration.md`. **READ.**
**Deliverables required.** A configured national SDG reporting platform: indicator configurations, data files, and site configuration. **Inputs.** National indicator data with disaggregations; targets; metadata; proxy designations.
**Requirements.**
- `U-04/R1` **[S]** A **progress score can only be determined for individual time series**; where the data has many possible time series (multiple series, units or disaggregations), the series to score must be identified.
- `U-04/R2` **[S]** `target` is the value required to reach the target of the specified time series; it **cannot be zero — a target of zero will be reset to 0.001**.
- `U-04/R3` **[S]** Where `proxy` is set to "both", a setting listing **which series codes are flagged as proxy data is required**.
- `U-04/R4` **[S]** Dates **must be in the format `YYYY-MM-DD`**.
- `U-04/R5` **[S]** A configurable notice can display very important information "which site viewers must keep in mind when using the data" above the graph.
**Component relationships.** Indicator config → data file → chart + progress score (a rendering pipeline where configuration decides what is even measurable). Series/unit/disaggregation → the one series that carries the headline score (a selection with political consequences). Proxy flag → displayed caveat (honesty mechanism). Notice → viewer interpretation (an explicit channel for caveats that data alone cannot carry).
**Whole-product constraints.** The platform must present incomparable national realities in a comparable frame while marking where a proxy substitutes for the real indicator. The zero-target rule (R2) is a revealing constraint: an "eliminate entirely" target cannot be represented, and is silently rewritten — an accuracy hazard. Publication is periodic and politically visible.
**Acceptance.** AC1 *(source)*: an indicator with multiple series designates exactly one series for progress scoring. AC2 *(source)*: proxy series are enumerated wherever `proxy: both` is set. AC3 *(source)*: all dates are `YYYY-MM-DD`. AC4 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: any indicator whose true target is zero is displayed with an explicit note that 0.001 is a rendering substitute.
**Assumptions, gaps, unresolved.** **Open feasibility question:** R2 means goals framed as elimination (zero deaths, zero new infections) cannot be represented faithfully by the progress mechanism. I regard this as a genuine unresolved defect rather than a configuration detail.

---

### N1-04 · IVOA VOTable · FORD 1 / ISIC M
**Beneficiary, problem, context.** Astronomers combining tables from many observatories and archives. A table of sources is useless for cross-matching unless its coordinate frame, time system and column semantics travel with it.
**Source.** IVOA — *VOTable*, github.com/ivoa-std/VOTable, `VOTable.tex` (line 105 key-word conventions; lines 611, 667, 733, 804, 808), `VOTable.attr.tex`, `VOTable.elem.tex`. **READ.**
**Deliverables required.** A VOTable document with declared version, identified elements, and referenced coordinate/time systems. **Inputs.** Tabular data; UCDs and units; IVOA vocabularies.
**Requirements.**
- `N1-04/R1` **[S]** MUST/SHALL/SHOULD/MAY/RECOMMENDED are used in the standard sense.
- `N1-04/R2` **[S]** The root element **MUST define `version`** equal to the document version; **all elements of that version come from the corresponding namespace/schema**.
- `N1-04/R3` **[S]** `ID` is an identifier which **must be unique throughout a VOTable document**.
- `N1-04/R4` **[S]** The reference-frame value **must be taken from the IVOA `refframe` vocabulary** — an external, versioned controlled vocabulary.
- `N1-04/R5` **[S]** Elements carrying time values **MUST reference the `TIMESYS` element using the VOTable `ref` attribute**.
- `N1-04/R6` **[S]** A `TIMESYS` element referenced via a `ref` attribute **MUST appear** (i.e. dangling references are forbidden).
**Component relationships.** VOTable → version declaration (interpretation authority for every element within). Column → `TIMESYS` via `ref` (time semantics attached by reference, not repeated per column). Coordinate frame → external IVOA vocabulary (vocabulary is separately governed and versioned). `ID` → intra-document references (uniqueness is what makes referencing safe).
**Whole-product constraints.** Time and coordinate systems are declared once and referenced, so a document is only interpretable as a whole — a fragment loses meaning. External vocabulary dependence means a document's validity can depend on a vocabulary version. Archives serve these documents to unknown clients over decades.
**Acceptance.** AC1 *(source)*: the root declares `version` and all elements belong to that version. AC2 *(source)*: no duplicate `ID` in a document. AC3 *(source)*: every time-valued element resolves a `ref` to a present `TIMESYS`. AC4 *(source)*: reference frames are members of the IVOA `refframe` vocabulary. AC5 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: a document remains interpretable when the vocabulary service is unreachable, because the frame string is self-identifying.
**Assumptions, gaps, unresolved.** **[I]** Behaviour on vocabulary-version mismatch is unresolved in what I read.

---

### N1-02 · OPTIMADE API · FORD 1 / ISIC J
**Beneficiary, problem, context.** Materials scientists searching many independent materials databases at once for structures matching a compositional or property filter. Each database has its own query language, so a search means learning five APIs and reconciling five vocabularies.
**Source.** Materials-Consortia — *OPTIMADE API specification*, github.com/Materials-Consortia/OPTIMADE, `optimade.rst` (line 27, line 119 RFC 2119, lines 143, 157, 164, 186), plus `schemas/`. **READ.** ~447 normative lines.
**Deliverables required.** A conforming HTTP API exposing entry types with defined properties, a filter language, and standard response envelopes. **Inputs.** A materials database; property definitions; entry identifiers.
**Requirements.**
- `N1-02/R1` **[S]** RFC 2119 key words apply.
- `N1-02/R2` **[S]** **Identifiers MUST start with a lowercase letter `[a-z]` or an underscore, followed by any number of lowercase alphanumerics `[a-z0-9]` and underscores.**
- `N1-02/R3` **[S]** **Entry types MUST be named according to the rules for identifiers.**
- `N1-02/R4` **[S]** **Entry property names MUST follow the identifier rules and MUST NOT have the same name as any of the entry types.**
- `N1-02/R5` **[S]** Every property **MUST equal a valid OPTIMADE data type** as listed under §Data types.
- `N1-02/R6` **[S]** **Taken together, the ID and entry type MUST uniquely identify the entry** — identity is two-part, scoped per database.
**Component relationships.** Entry type + ID → entry (composite identity). Property name → data type (typing authority). Filter language → properties (queries are only as portable as the shared property set). Provider prefixes → database-specific extensions (the mechanism that lets a provider add fields without colliding). Client → many providers (federated query is the product).
**Whole-product constraints.** Federation across independently operated databases with no central registry of content. Namespace discipline is what prevents collision, hence the strict identifier grammar. Providers differ in coverage, so a federated query returns partial results by nature and the client must present that honestly.
**Acceptance.** AC1 *(source)*: all entry types and property names satisfy the identifier grammar. AC2 *(source)*: no property shares a name with an entry type. AC3 *(source)*: `(type, id)` is unique within a provider. AC4 *(source)*: every property value conforms to its declared OPTIMADE data type. AC5 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: the same filter executed against three providers returns results distinguishable by provider without client-side heuristics.
**Assumptions, gaps, unresolved.** **[I]** Cross-provider deduplication of the *same physical structure* is not addressed by identity rules that are provider-scoped — a real limitation for the stated use.

---

### E2-05 · RISC-V Supervisor Binary Interface · FORD 2 / ISIC J
**Beneficiary, problem, context.** Operating-system kernels running on RISC-V hardware from many vendors. Without a stable firmware interface, every kernel needs per-board code for timers, IPIs, performance counters and power management.
**Source.** RISC-V International — *RISC-V SBI Specification*, github.com/riscv-non-isa/riscv-sbi-doc, `src/ext-pmu.adoc` (§Performance Monitoring Unit Extension, EID `0x504D55` "PMU"; lines 19, 352, 413, 420, 422), `src/ext-sse.adoc`, `src/ext-mpxy.adoc`. **READ.** Cluster `RISCV` with `E2-01`.
**Deliverables required.** A firmware (SBI implementation) exposing extensions identified by EID, with defined function semantics and error returns. **Inputs.** Hardware counters and features; a supervisor-mode OS; shared memory regions.
**Requirements.**
- `E2-05/R1` **[S]** Extensions are identified by an **Extension ID**; the PMU extension is EID `0x504D55` ("PMU" in ASCII).
- `E2-05/R2` **[S]** **All firmware counters must have the same number of bits and can be up to 64 bits.**
- `E2-05/R3` **[S]** Reserved bit ranges (e.g. bits `8:(XLEN-1)`) **are reserved for future use and must be zero** — forward-compatibility is enforced by writers, not readers.
- `E2-05/R4` **[S]** The **shared memory address must be set during boot** via the specified call; a flag may be used to indicate this.
- `E2-05/R5` **[S]** **The SBI implementation must initialise all the given valid** shared-memory fields.
- `E2-05/R6` **[S]** XLEN-dependence is explicit — field widths vary with the base architecture width.
**Component relationships.** Supervisor (OS) → SBI (firmware) → hardware (a privilege-boundary crossing; the SBI is the only sanctioned path). EID → function ID → arguments (dispatch structure). Shared memory region → both parties (a mutable region with divided ownership; R5 assigns initialisation responsibility to the firmware). Boot-time setup → runtime calls (ordering dependency: a runtime call before boot setup is undefined).
**Whole-product constraints.** Privilege boundary: the firmware is more privileged, so malformed input must not compromise it. Reserved-must-be-zero is a forward-compatibility contract that only works if writers comply today. Boot ordering creates a strict temporal dependency. XLEN parameterisation means one specification covers 32- and 64-bit targets with different field layouts.
**Acceptance.** AC1 *(source)*: all firmware counters report identical width ≤64 bits. AC2 *(source)*: calls with non-zero reserved bits are rejected. AC3 *(source)*: shared memory is fully initialised by the implementation before first use. AC4 *(source)*: the PMU extension is discoverable at EID `0x504D55`. AC5 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: a runtime PMU call issued before boot-time shared-memory setup returns a defined error rather than undefined behaviour.
**Assumptions, gaps, unresolved.** **[I]** Whether firmware must *reject* or may *ignore* non-zero reserved bits is the kind of ambiguity that produces divergent implementations; I did not find it settled in the section read.

---

### E2-03 · CHIPS Alliance riscv-dv · FORD 2 / ISIC M
**Beneficiary, problem, context.** Processor design teams verifying a RISC-V core. Hand-written directed tests miss corner cases; the team needs randomised instruction streams that are legal, reproducible, and comparable against a reference model.
