**Source.** CHIPS Alliance — *riscv-dv*, github.com/chipsalliance/riscv-dv: `docs/source/{getting_started, configuration, handshake}.rst`, target and ISA configuration, plus the generator and comparison flow. **READ.**
**Deliverables required.** A configurable random instruction generator producing test programs, plus a handshake mechanism for signalling between test and testbench, plus a comparison flow against an instruction-set simulator. **Inputs.** Target ISA and extension configuration; privileged-mode settings; a reference ISS; a DUT trace.
**Requirements.**
- `E2-03/R1` **[S]** Generation is driven by an explicit **target/ISA configuration** (documented in `configuration.rst`); the generator must not emit instructions outside the configured ISA subset.
- `E2-03/R2` **[S]** A defined **handshake** mechanism exists for the generated program to communicate with the testbench (`handshake.rst`).
- `E2-03/R3` **[S]** The flow compares DUT execution against a reference model — verification is by differential comparison, not by assertion alone.
- `E2-03/R4` **[I]** Generation must be **seeded and reproducible**: a failing random test is only useful if it can be re-run exactly.
- `E2-03/R5` **[I]** Generated programs must terminate and must not fault except where the configuration intends faults.
**Component relationships.** Configuration → generator → test program → {DUT, reference ISS} → trace comparison (a pipeline whose output is a *difference*, not a pass/fail from one side). Seed → program (reproducibility anchor). Handshake → testbench observability (lets the test tell the testbench where it is). Coverage model → configuration feedback (which corners remain unhit).
**Whole-product constraints.** Randomisation plus reproducibility is the defining tension: the value comes from unpredictability, the debuggability from determinism. The reference model is an authority that may itself be wrong — a mismatch does not say which side erred. Legality of generated code is configuration-dependent, so a configuration error looks like a DUT bug.
**Acceptance.** AC1 *(source)*: generated programs contain only instructions permitted by the configured ISA. AC2 *(source)*: DUT and reference traces are comparable and mismatches are localised. AC3 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: re-running a recorded seed reproduces the identical program bit-for-bit. AC4 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: a mismatch report identifies the first divergent instruction with architectural state from both sides.
**Assumptions, gaps, unresolved.** **[I]** R4 and R5 are interpretation: I did not find explicit normative statements of reproducibility or termination in the documents I read, though the flow presumes both.

---

### M3-03 · PHA4GE Wastewater Contextual Data Specification · FORD 3 / ISIC E
**Beneficiary, problem, context.** Public-health laboratories doing wastewater pathogen genomics, and the surveillance networks that must pool their results. A sequence without contextual data — where, when, what sewershed, what population, how the sample was processed — cannot be compared with another lab's sequence, and pooling is the entire point of wastewater surveillance.
**Source.** PHA4GE — *Wastewater Contextual Data Specification*, github.com/pha4ge/wastewater-contextual-data-specification: `README.md` (§About: an interoperable framework for wastewater microbial genomics), `Mappings/README.md` (`NCBI_PHA4GE-Wastewater_mapping.xlsx`, `ENA_PHA4GE-Wastewater_mapping.xlsx`, `PHA4GE-to-ODM-dictionary.xlsx`; the `PHA4GE_to_NCBISRA_fields` mapping with Source ID / Source field title), `Testing and Validation/`. **READ.**
**Deliverables required.** A field specification with ontology-backed terms; metadata template(s); field and reference guides; curation and new-term-request SOPs; mappings to external repositories. **Inputs.** Sampling and laboratory metadata; ontology terms (GenEpiO); target repository requirements.
**Requirements.**
- `M3-03/R1` **[S]** The specification provides **standardised, ontology-based fields and terms** — each field carries an ontology ID (e.g. `GENEPIO:0001123`).
- `M3-03/R2` **[S]** Terms are **implemented via metadata template(s)**, supported by field and reference guides.
- `M3-03/R3` **[S]** **Curation and new-term-request SOPs** are part of the specification — vocabulary growth is a governed process.
- `M3-03/R4` **[S]** Mappings are maintained to **NCBI BioSample (SARS-CoV-2_wwsurv)**, to **ENA packages (ERC000023 GSC MIxS wastewater sludge and others)**, and to the **PHES-ODM** structure.
- `M3-03/R5` **[S]** A testing and validation component exists as a distinct part of the deliverable.
- `M3-03/R6` **[S]** Each mapping row records the **Source ID (ontology ID)** and **Source field title**, so mappings are traceable to term identity rather than to a label.
**Component relationships.** Ontology term → specification field → template column → repository attribute (a four-step identity chain; the ontology ID is what survives renaming). Specification → NCBI/ENA/PHES-ODM mappings (submission is the point of use; a field that cannot be mapped cannot be submitted). SOP → new terms → template version (governed extension). Validation → template conformance.
**Whole-product constraints.** Two authorities must both be satisfied: the scientific community's semantics and the sequence repositories' submission schemas. Sewershed data can be re-identifying at small population scales — a privacy constraint on a nominally environmental dataset. Terms evolve during an outbreak, when change is least welcome.
**Acceptance.** AC1 *(source)*: every specification field resolves to an ontology ID. AC2 *(source)*: a completed template maps without loss to NCBI BioSample and to ENA. AC3 *(source)*: a new term enters only through the documented SOP. AC4 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: a template completed under version N is upgradeable to version N+1 with a published crosswalk.
**Assumptions, gaps, unresolved.** **[I]** The mapping workbooks are `.xlsx`, so mapping fidelity is not machine-verifiable in the repository. **Unresolved:** minimum population threshold below which sewershed identification becomes re-identifying.

---

### M3-04 · HL7 FHIR conformance rules · FORD 3 / ISIC Q
**Beneficiary, problem, context.** Patients whose records must move between a hospital, a clinic and an app, and the implementers who must be able to say precisely what their system does and does not support. "We support FHIR" is not a testable claim unless conformance itself is specified.
**Source.** HL7 International — *FHIR*, github.com/HL7/fhir, `source/conformance-rules.html` (lines 26–31, 41–44, 53, 71, 77–78, 84–88); read via raw.githubusercontent.com. **READ.**
**Deliverables required.** A conformance claim scoped to a specific exchange mechanism, optionally published as a CapabilityStatement. **Inputs.** Implementation capabilities; profiles; the conformance-layer resources.
**Requirements.**
- `M3-04/R1` **[S]** Applications claiming conformance make the claim **in respect of a specific** exchange mechanism, and claim conformance to **one (or more)** of the listed exchange paradigms — conformance is scoped, never global.
- `M3-04/R2` **[S]** FHIR provides a **conformance layer** implemented using named conformance resources.
- `M3-04/R3` **[S]** Systems wishing to assert formal conformance **MAY** publish a **CapabilityStatement** defining how they conform; assertions of conformance in the absence of one are of limited value.
- `M3-04/R4` **[S]** The specification uses **SHALL, SHOULD, MAY** per RFC 8174's clarification of RFC 2119, but — **unlike RFC 2119** — it allows that different (contextual) readings apply.
- `M3-04/R5` **[S]** **SHALL** = an absolute requirement for all implementations; **SHALL NOT** = an absolute prohibition; **SHOULD/SHOULD NOT** = best practice to be considered, which may be ignored for valid reasons in a particular implementation context.
- `M3-04/R6` **[S]** "FHIR-like" solutions (based on FHIR but not conformant) set trading-partner expectations that are then unmet — a stated hazard, not merely a preference.
**Component relationships.** CapabilityStatement → implementation behaviour (the machine-readable claim). Profile → resource constraints (narrowing for a use case). Conformance resources → automated testing tools. Trading partner A ↔ B (expectations formed from claims; R6 is about what happens when claims are loose).
**Whole-product constraints.** Conformance is *plural and scoped*: two conformant systems may not interoperate, which is the specification's own acknowledged condition. SHOULD is deliberately escapable with reasons, so testable conformance rests on SHALL plus the published CapabilityStatement. Clinical data carries safety consequences, so silent partial support is dangerous.
**Acceptance.** AC1 *(source)*: a conformance claim names the exchange mechanism(s) it applies to. AC2 *(source)*: a published CapabilityStatement accurately describes supported resources and interactions. AC3 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: an automated test derived from the CapabilityStatement passes against the live system. AC4 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: any SHOULD not implemented is listed with its reason.
**Assumptions, gaps, unresolved.** **[I]** R4's allowance for contextual readings of the key words is unusual and weakens mechanical conformance checking. I read the conformance-rules page only, not the full specification.

---

### A4-05 · FAO Open Foris Arena · FORD 4 / ISIC A
**Beneficiary, problem, context.** National forest inventory teams and agricultural survey units, often in ministries with limited IT capacity, who must design a survey, collect data in the field (frequently offline), clean it, and produce official statistics — repeatedly, over years, with changing staff.
**Source.** FAO — *Open Foris Arena*, github.com/openforis/arena, `README.md` (lines 17, 39, 76–78) and the survey/processing platform structure; companion `openforis/collect` (`A-05`). **READ.** Cluster `FAO`.
**Deliverables required.** A cloud-based platform for storing and processing data collected in field inventories or questionnaires, with survey setup, data entry, validation and analysis. **Inputs.** Survey design; field data; environment configuration; an email service.
**Requirements.**
- `A4-05/R1` **[S]** The platform must provide **a fast and flexible way to set up a survey and start entering data** — survey design is a user activity, not a development activity.
- `A4-05/R2` **[S]** It covers both **field inventories and questionnaires** in one system.
- `A4-05/R3` **[S]** Deployment requires an environment file (`arena.env`) that **must be a text file with the specified content**.
- `A4-05/R4` **[S]** An email service must be configured; for SendGrid **only `SENDGRID_API_KEY` is required**, for Office 365 `EMAIL_AUTH_USER` and `EMAIL_AUTH_PASSWORD` **are required**, for Amazon SES `EMAIL_AMAZON_SES_HOST` is required and `EMAIL_AMAZON_SES_PORT` is optional — **conditional requirement sets per provider**.
- `A4-05/R5` **[I]** Survey definitions must be versionable, because a multi-year inventory changes its instrument between cycles while needing comparable results.
**Component relationships.** Survey definition → data entry forms → collected records → processing chains → statistics (a full lifecycle in one artefact). Environment configuration → deployment (institutional self-hosting is expected). Email provider → user account lifecycle (conditional configuration per chosen provider). Arena ↔ Collect (`A-05`) — the same FAO lineage addressing desktop/offline and cloud modes.
**Whole-product constraints.** Institutional continuity across staff turnover: the survey definition is the institutional memory. Offline field conditions. Self-hosting by ministries with limited operations capacity, which is why configuration requirements are conditional and minimal. Statistics are official outputs, so processing must be reproducible and auditable.
**Acceptance.** AC1 *(source)*: a survey can be defined and data entry started without code changes. AC2 *(source)*: deployment succeeds with a correctly formed `arena.env` and one supported email provider's required variables. AC3 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: a processing chain re-run on the same data yields byte-identical statistics. AC4 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: a survey revised between cycles retains a documented mapping to the prior cycle's variables.
**Assumptions, gaps, unresolved.** **[I]** R5 and AC4 are my additions; I did not find survey versioning stated as a requirement. **Cluster caveat:** Arena and Collect share an origin and partly overlapping purpose; treat them as one family, not two independent confirmations.

---

### A4-04 · FoodOn ontology · FORD 4 / ISIC I
**Beneficiary, problem, context.** Food-safety investigators, dietary researchers and supply-chain analysts who must connect "chicken breast, raw" in a recipe database, "broiler carcass" in an inspection record, and a species name in a genomics record. Without a shared ontology the same food is three unrelated strings.
**Source.** FoodOn consortium — *FoodOn*, github.com/FoodOntology/foodon: `foodon.owl`, `foodon-base.owl`, `foodon-full.owl`, `foodon_siren.owl`, `foodon-synonyms.tsv`, `src/ontology/` with the OBO Dashboard/ODK workflow (`docs/odk-workflows/RepoManagement.md`), `src/ontology/scripts/README_ingredients.md` (ingredient recogniser: §Pipeline, §Type classifications and OWL roots), `README_ncbitaxon_manager.md`. **READ.**
**Deliverables required.** Released OWL artefacts at several scopes (base, full, siren), a synonym table, and a build pipeline; term requests handled through the ODK workflow. **Inputs.** Source vocabularies (NCBITaxon and others); ingredient text; community term requests.
**Requirements.**
- `A4-04/R1` **[S]** Multiple release products are maintained at different scopes — `foodon-base.owl`, `foodon.owl`, `foodon-full.owl` — so consumers import only what they need.
- `A4-04/R2` **[S]** External taxonomies are managed by an explicit **import manager** (`ncbitaxon_manager`), not copied ad hoc — imports are reproducible slices.
- `A4-04/R3` **[S]** **Synonyms are maintained as a first-class artefact** (`foodon-synonyms.tsv`), because real-world food text does not use ontology labels.
- `A4-04/R4` **[S]** An ingredient recogniser pipeline maps free text to **type classifications and OWL roots** — the ontology is built to be applied to messy text, not only to be browsed.
- `A4-04/R5` **[S]** The repository follows the **ODK (Ontology Development Kit) workflow** for repository management and releases — build and release are standardised, not bespoke.
**Component relationships.** Source ontologies (NCBITaxon etc.) → import manager → FoodOn imports (provenance and version pinning). Core terms + imports → release products at three scopes. Synonyms + recogniser → free-text ingredient strings (the application surface). ODK workflow → releases (reproducibility). Term request → curation → next release (governed growth).
**Whole-product constraints.** Food is culturally variable: the same term denotes different things across cuisines and regulatory regimes, so the ontology must carry that variance rather than resolve it. Imports create version dependencies on ontologies FoodOn does not control. Applied use (text recognition) imposes recall demands that pure ontology design would not.
**Acceptance.** AC1 *(source)*: each release product builds reproducibly through the ODK workflow. AC2 *(source)*: imported taxonomy terms trace to a pinned source version. AC3 *(source)*: the ingredient recogniser assigns OWL roots to input text. AC4 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: a term deprecated in a release resolves to a replacement rather than disappearing.
**Assumptions, gaps, unresolved.** **[I]** Recogniser accuracy targets are not stated. **Unresolved:** how conflicting culturally-specific definitions of one food are represented.

---

### S5-01 · Psych-DS · FORD 5 / ISIC M
**Beneficiary, problem, context.** Psychology researchers and the people who later reuse their data — meta-analysts, replicators, reviewers. A typical psychology dataset is a folder of CSVs with idiosyncratic column names and no machine-readable description of what the columns mean, so reuse means emailing the original author.
**Source.** Psych-DS community — *Psych-DS specification*, github.com/psych-ds/psych-DS: `psychDS-docs/index.md` (§Motivation, §Structure), `psychDS-docs/rules/csv_data/Datafile.md` (lines 5, 11), `psychDS-docs/rules/files/tabular_data/data/Datafile.md`, `psychDS-docs/meta/context.md`. **READ.**
**Deliverables required.** A dataset directory conforming to the Psych-DS layout, with CSV datafiles and machine-readable metadata. **Inputs.** Raw tabular data; variable descriptions; a JSON-LD metadata context.
**Requirements.**
- `S5-01/R1` **[S]** A **Datafile** is a CSV file **under the `/data` directory** in which the official Psych-DS compliant data is stored.
- `S5-01/R2` **[S]** Datafiles **must follow Psych-DS file naming conventions**, which include the use of specified name components.
- `S5-01/R3` **[S]** Rule **`columnsMatchMetadata`** (default `True`): **each datafile must only use column headers that appear in the `variableMeasured` property** of the metadata — the metadata is authoritative over the data.
- `S5-01/R4` **[S]** Rules are named, individually addressable objects with defaults, forming a checkable rule set rather than prose guidance.
- `S5-01/R5` **[S]** Metadata uses a JSON-LD context (`meta/context.md`), so descriptions are linked-data terms, not free labels.
**Component relationships.** `/data/*.csv` → metadata `variableMeasured` (bidirectional obligation: R3 makes an undocumented column an error). Filename components → dataset structure (naming encodes participant/session/task facets). Rule set → validator → conformance report. JSON-LD context → external vocabularies (shared meaning for variables across studies).
**Whole-product constraints.** Adding a column is a documentation act, not just a data act — this inverts normal research practice and is the specification's principal behavioural demand. Conformance is machine-checkable, so it can be a submission gate. Datasets are deposited once and read for decades.
**Acceptance.** AC1 *(source)*: every datafile is a CSV under `/data` with a conforming filename. AC2 *(source)*: no column header appears that is absent from `variableMeasured`. AC3 *(source)*: the validator reports rule-by-rule outcomes. AC4 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: a dataset that passes validation can be loaded and every column labelled without contacting the author.
**Assumptions, gaps, unresolved.** **[I]** `columnsMatchMetadata` defaults to `True` but is presented as configurable — whether a dataset may declare it `False` and still claim conformance is unresolved.

---

### S5-05 · OSF preregistration · FORD 5 / ISIC M · **SNIPPET — unverified**
**Beneficiary, problem, context.** Researchers who want their confirmatory claims to be credible, and readers who cannot otherwise distinguish a predicted result from one found after looking. Preregistration exists to make the difference visible.
**Source.** Center for Open Science — *OSF Registries / preregistration templates*, osf.io/registries, cos.io/initiatives/prereg. **SNIPPET — all requirements unverified.**
**Deliverables required (unverified).** A time-stamped, read-only registration of a study plan, posted to a public repository before data collection or analysis, with a persistent identifier.
**Requirements.**
- `S5-05/R1` **[S-unverified]** The registration is a **time-stamped, read-only version of the study plan** posted to a public repository **before beginning data collection or analysis**.
- `S5-05/R2` **[S-unverified]** OSF archives responses, attached files and associated metadata into **a single time-stamped snapshot**; the originally submitted content and its submission date **are fixed and cannot be quietly altered afterwards**.
- `S5-05/R3` **[S-unverified]** OSF assigns the registration a **persistent identifier (DOI)** for citation in a manuscript's methods section or a grant report.
- `S5-05/R4` **[S-unverified]** **Eleven templates** are currently available, with more in development; the general OSF Preregistration template is the default for a standard quantitative study.
- `S5-05/R5` **[S-unverified]** The template requires documenting **hypotheses, methods and planned analyses**, including a statistical analysis plan.
**Component relationships.** Study plan → registration snapshot → DOI → manuscript citation (the chain that lets a reader check what was planned). Template choice → required fields (different designs need different disclosures). Registration → later paper (deviation reporting is where the value is realised or lost). Embargo → eventual public release (a timing mechanism for competitive work).
**Whole-product constraints.** **Irreversibility is the product**: the snapshot's value comes from being unalterable. Timing is the binding constraint — a registration after data inspection is worthless, and that ordering cannot be verified from the artefact alone. Deviations are legitimate but must be declared, so the system must support amendment without erasure.
**Acceptance.** AC1 *(source, unverified)*: the registration is time-stamped, read-only and dated before the first data collection. AC2 *(source, unverified)*: a DOI resolves to the frozen snapshot. AC3 *(source, unverified)*: hypotheses and the analysis plan are present. AC4 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: any deviation from the registered plan is recorded as an amendment that preserves and points to the original.
**Assumptions, gaps, unresolved.** **Severe:** templates were not read. **Unresolved:** nothing in the artefact proves data were not already collected — the central limitation of preregistration as a mechanism.

---

### H6-04 · Erlangen CRM / CIDOC CRM in OWL · FORD 6 / ISIC R
**Beneficiary, problem, context.** Museums, archives and heritage researchers who need to state that *this object was made by that person at that place in that period, according to this scholarly opinion* — and to reconcile that statement with another institution's contradictory one. CIDOC CRM is the ISO-standard conceptual model (ISO 21127); Erlangen CRM is its OWL-DL implementation.
**Source.** FAU Erlangen (Erlangen CRM) implementing CIDOC CRM — github.com/erlangen-crm/ecrm, `README.md`: `ecrm_current.owl` (current version, namespace `http://erlangen-crm.org/current/`, **includes `rdfs:label` for each class and property**); `ecrm_<yymmdd>.owl` (older versions, namespace `http://erlangen-crm.org/<yymmdd>/`). CIDOC CRM itself is recognised as an ISO standard (ISO 21127, renewed 2023) and published at cidoc-crm.org — **that base standard is SNIPPET**. **READ (the OWL implementation).**
**Deliverables required.** A versioned OWL-DL ontology implementing CIDOC CRM, with labelled classes and properties, at dated namespaces. **Inputs.** The CIDOC CRM specification; OWL-DL expressivity constraints.
**Requirements.**
- `H6-04/R1` **[S]** Each release is published at a **date-stamped namespace** (`/yymmdd/`), with `/current/` as the moving pointer — **published data pins a date, not `current`**.
- `H6-04/R2` **[S]** The current version **includes `rdfs:label` for every class and property**; older versions do not necessarily.
- `H6-04/R3` **[S]** The artefact is an **OWL** implementation, so it is bound by OWL-DL expressivity — some CRM statements are representable only approximately.
- `H6-04/R4` **[I]** Conformance is two-layered: to CIDOC CRM (the conceptual model) and to this OWL encoding of it.
- `H6-04/R5` **[S-unverified, from CIDOC CRM]** CIDOC CRM is an ISO standard (ISO 21127:2023) — cited from search results, not read.
**Component relationships.** CIDOC CRM specification → Erlangen OWL encoding (implementation, with loss). Dated namespace → published triples (identity across time; changing the namespace changes every URI). Labels → human-facing tools. Event-centric model → objects, actors, places, times (the CRM's core move: everything is asserted through events, which is what allows contradictory attributions to coexist).
**Whole-product constraints.** Scholarly disagreement is normal and must be representable — the model records *who asserted what*, not just facts. Data outlives institutions and software. Namespace versioning means an integration spanning two institutions may span two CRM versions.
**Acceptance.** AC1 *(source)*: every class and property in `ecrm_current.owl` has an `rdfs:label`. AC2 *(source)*: the ontology loads as valid OWL-DL. AC3 *(source)*: a dated release remains resolvable after `current` advances. AC4 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: two contradictory attributions of the same object coexist without inconsistency, each traceable to its asserter.
**Assumptions, gaps, unresolved.** **[I]** Which CIDOC CRM version `ecrm_current` implements was not established from the README. The base standard is behind a host I could not reach — **a real gap for any conformance claim**.

---

### H6-01 · TEI P5 Guidelines · FORD 6 / ISIC R
**Beneficiary, problem, context.** Scholars encoding manuscripts, correspondence, dictionaries, inscriptions and spoken corpora so that the text carries its editorial apparatus — abbreviations expanded, damage noted, hands distinguished, uncertainty recorded — and remains usable when the encoding software is gone.
**Source.** TEI Consortium — *TEI P5 Guidelines*, github.com/TEIC/TEI, `P5/Source/Guidelines/en/` (chapter sources, e.g. `CO-CoreElements.xml` with sections "Elements Available in All TEI Documents", "Paragraphs", "Treatment of Punctuation", "Functions of Punctuation", "Hyphenation", "Highlighting and Quotation", "What Is Highlighting?", "Emphasis, Foreign Words, and Unusual Language"; also `AI-AnalyticMechanisms.xml`, `BIB-Bibliography.xml`, `CC-LanguageCorpora.xml`, `CE-CertaintyResponsibility.xml`, `CH-LanguagesCharacterSets.xml`, `CMC-ComputerMediatedCommunication.xml`, `DI-PrintDictionaries.xml`, `DR-PerformanceTexts.xml`, `DEPRECATIONS.xml`), plus `P5/Exemplars/`, `P5/Test/`, `P5/ReleaseNotes/`. **READ.**
**Deliverables required.** The Guidelines (prose + element specifications in a single ODD source), generated schemas, exemplars (customisations), and a deprecation register. **Inputs.** Chapter sources; element/class definitions; community proposals.
**Requirements.**
- `H6-01/R1` **[S]** The Guidelines are maintained as **XML source from which both prose and schemas are generated** — one source, two products (the ODD approach).
- `H6-01/R2` **[S]** Certain elements are **available in all TEI documents** (the core module), while others belong to modules selected per project.
- `H6-01/R3` **[S]** **Certainty and responsibility** are a dedicated chapter (`CE-`): an encoder may record how sure they are and who is responsible for a reading — uncertainty is structural.
- `H6-01/R4` **[S]** **Exemplars** (`P5/Exemplars/`) are published customisations, so subsetting is an anticipated, supported practice rather than a deviation.
- `H6-01/R5` **[S]** A **`DEPRECATIONS.xml`** register exists, so element removal is a declared, scheduled process.
- `H6-01/R6` **[S]** A test suite (`P5/Test/`) accompanies the Guidelines.
**Component relationships.** ODD source → Guidelines prose + RELAX NG/DTD schemas + exemplars (single-source generation). Module selection → the schema a project validates against (customisation is the normal case; "plain TEI" is rare). Certainty/responsibility elements → any assertion in the text (attachable uncertainty). Deprecation register → project migration planning.
**Whole-product constraints.** Every project uses a different subset, so "TEI-conformant" always requires naming a customisation. The encoding must survive centuries and must record editorial judgement, not just characters. Deprecation must never silently invalidate existing corpora — hence a register rather than removal.
**Acceptance.** AC1 *(source)*: prose and schemas regenerate from the ODD source without manual edits. AC2 *(source)*: a document validates against its project's generated customisation schema. AC3 *(source)*: deprecated elements are listed with their status before removal. AC4 *(proposed — requires acceptance)*: an encoded uncertainty (`CE-` mechanisms) round-trips through a publication pipeline without being flattened to plain text.
**Assumptions, gaps, unresolved.** **[I]** I read chapter structure and section headings rather than transcribing normative sentences; **no clause-level quotations are cited**. Which P5 release is current was not established from the working tree.

---

That completes the 54 construction briefs. The remaining two deliverables follow.

## 4. Proposal collection (invented — **not** required by any source)

Everything in this section is **[H] hypothetical**: my inventions, kept separate from the source-required deliverables above. Each entry names the case it serves, the construction, and why it is worth building. Requirements inherited from the source case are preserved by reference (e.g. "inherits `O-02/R1` immutability").

### 4.1 Per-case proposals (two materially different constructions each)

I give two per case, chosen to differ in *kind* — a different product form, a different maker, a different beneficiary, or a different physical/social medium — not merely a different name or parameter.

**A-01 fiboa** — (i) **Provenance-grade boundary attestation service**: a service that re-derives `determination:method` from evidence (imagery timestamps, GNSS tracks, survey records) and issues a signed statement, so a subsidy office can accept `auto-imagery` boundaries with a stated error budget instead of rejecting them. Inherits R5, R6. (ii) **Merge referee**: a command-line tool that takes N field collections and refuses the merge unless licence and identity rules (R1–R3, R7) can be satisfied, emitting the minimal set of collection renames required — turning the spec's hardest rule into a mechanical step.

**A-02 Modus** — (i) **Lab turnaround clock**: an operational instrument, not a data format — a dashboard keyed to submit/result pairs (R1, R5) that shows a lab its own median time from sample receipt to result distribution, per method, so the competitive constraint the README asserts becomes measurable. (ii) **Method-change impact simulator**: given a proposed new test method (R2), replays a year of historical results to show which agronomic recommendations would change, so the change process has evidence rather than opinion.

**B-05 GISTM** — (i) **Downstream consequence re-check calendar**: a service that watches settlement and infrastructure growth downstream of a classified TSF and raises a review flag when the population basis of Requirement 4.1's classification has materially changed — treating the classification as state that expires. (ii) **Community-readable disclosure card**: a one-page, plain-language rendering of the 15.1 disclosure designed for the people actually living downstream, generated from the operator's disclosure so it cannot diverge from it.

**B-01 OMF** — (i) **Model-of-record ledger**: an append-only registry that hashes each OMF project file at hand-off between operator, consultant and auditor, so disputes about "which model was used for the reserve statement" become checkable. Inherits R1, R5 (version declaration). (ii) **Sub-block resolution explainer**: a viewer that renders where a block model's sub-blocking actually changes the estimate, so a non-specialist reviewer can see where resolution matters rather than trusting a single number.

**C-05 ONIE** — (i) **Port conformance bounty harness**: packaging the ONIE Compliance Environment (R2) plus the VM target (R3) as a hosted service an ODM can run on a pull request, so hardware support becomes a testable contribution rather than a vendor negotiation. (ii) **Install-time provenance receipt**: extending the signing path (R4) to emit a receipt recording which image, which key and which port installed which OS on which serial number — the missing audit trail for fleets.

**C-03 MTConnect** — (i) **Gap-honest client library**: a client that, on reconnect, uses `from`/`at` semantics (R2, R3) to detect buffer overrun and marks the resulting series as *discontinuous* rather than interpolating — a small library that removes a whole class of silent analytics errors. (ii) **Filter-aware anomaly detector**: a detector that models `minimumDelta` suppression (R1) explicitly, so "no data" is scored as "no change beyond delta" and not as sensor failure.

**D-04 EEBus** — (i) **Household power arbitration rehearsal rig**: a bench that simulates an EV charger, heat pump and PV inverter negotiating under a fuse limit, letting an installer rehearse the failure modes (peer disappears mid-negotiation) before commissioning a real house. Inherits R2, and my AC3. (ii) **Use-case conformance passport**: a printable card for an appliance stating which EEBus use cases it implements at which SHIP/SPINE versions (R1) — because a householder currently cannot tell whether two "EEBus" devices will actually cooperate.

**D-01 OCPI** — (i) **Roaming dispute reconciler**: a service both CPO and eMSP can run that re-derives a CDR from session and tariff history and shows exactly which party's record diverges — inherits R1 (`REMOVED` rather than delete), R6, and my AC4. (ii) **Privacy audit for feeds**: a checker that flags locations likely to be private (R2) by cross-referencing land use and access patterns, since the rule is absolute but compliance is currently self-assessed.

**E-03 ODM2** — (i) **Specimen–sensor reconciliation notebook**: a reusable analysis that pairs each lab specimen result with the sensor observations at the same site and time window, reporting systematic divergence — the comparison ODM2 makes possible but nobody routinely does. (ii) **Equipment recalibration recall**: when a calibration record changes (R5), automatically list every result that depended on that instrument, so retraction is scoped rather than blanket.

**E-04 Smart Data Models WasteWater** — (i) **Discharge-compliance profile**: the missing profile my AC4 requires — a named, versioned set of attributes that a plant must publish for regulatory comparison, layered over the two-property base (R1). (ii) **Cross-city plant benchmark**: a comparison built only on the profile, which by construction reports how many cities *cannot* be compared and why — making the base model's permissiveness visible instead of hidden.

**F-02 BuildingSync** — (i) **Audit-level truth-in-labelling badge**: a generated badge on every audit file stating its declared level and the elements actually present, so an owner paying for a Level 2 audit can see whether they received one. Inherits R2, R3, my AC4. (ii) **Level 2 translation gap register**: a published, machine-readable list of which Level 2 elements do *not* translate to an energy model — turning the acknowledged "partial" into an addressable backlog.

**F-01 BCF-API** — (i) **Dangling-viewpoint detector**: a service that re-resolves every viewpoint's element GUIDs against the current model revision and reports which issues have lost their referents — the unresolved question in my brief, made operational. (ii) **Site-photo issue capture**: a phone-first capture path that creates a BCF topic from a site photograph with location, so the person who sees the defect files it, rather than describing it to someone with the model open.

**G-05 UNTP DPP** — (i) **Claim comparability referee**: given two DPPs from different issuers on the same topic (R3) and metric (R4), reports whether the metrics are actually comparable (same criterion, R5; same boundary) and refuses to rank them if not. (ii) **Evidence-first passport builder for small producers**: a tool that starts from the conformity assessments a small producer already has and constructs the minimum valid DPP around them, inverting the usual order and lowering the entry cost.

**G-02 UBL** — (i) **Three-authority validator**: a single validation run reporting schema, Schematron and code-list outcomes separately with their versions (R1, R2, R4), so a failure names which authority rejected the document. (ii) **Customisation diff**: given two CVA customisations, show exactly which fields and code lists differ — what a supplier needs when onboarding to a second jurisdiction.

**H-01 GTFS** — (i) **Conditional-requirement explainer**: a validator that, for each Conditionally Required file, prints the condition, the evaluated inputs and the verdict (R3) — turning a class of confusing errors into readable reasoning. (ii) **Expiry-aware feed badge**: a small artefact a consumer can check to see a feed's declared service period and remaining validity (my AC4), so stale feeds fail loudly.

**H-05 DCSA eBL** — (i) **Endorsement chain reconstructor**: builds and displays the chain of title from the API alone, reporting where it cannot (my AC5) — the gap between data and legal possession made visible. (ii) **Array-order conformance probe**: a test that round-trips documents through a carrier's API and diffs array ordering (R4), because this requirement is easy to violate silently and consequential when violated.

**I-04 LIVES** — (i) **Match-quality report for municipalities**: publishes, per feed, how many businesses matched a consumer platform and which failed, so a city can fix its own records rather than wondering why scores do not appear. Inherits my AC4. (ii) **Violation-optional graceful renderer**: a reference UI showing what a feed *without* `violations.csv` (R4) can and cannot tell a diner, so cities can see the value of publishing the optional file.

**I-05 FSMA 204** — (i) **Friday-4:30pm drill kit**: a rehearsal package that fires a simulated FDA request at a weekend hour and measures whether the 24-hour sortable-spreadsheet obligation (R5) is met without engineering help. (ii) **Transformation lot-link checker**: audits a facility's records for transformation events whose output lot codes do not link to input lots (R4) — the specific place traceability breaks.

**J-04 OpenAPI** — (i) **Prose-vs-schema conflict reporter**: a tool that detects where the JSON Schema and the prose disagree and asserts the prose (R4) — because R4 means schema-only validation is insufficient and nobody currently checks. (ii) **Optionality drift comparator**: runs two generators over one description and diffs their required/optional decisions (my AC4), surfacing tooling disagreement before it reaches consumers.

**J-02 OpenTelemetry** — (i) **Status-scoped conformance report**: a report stating which Development-status requirements (R6) an SDK implements, so a conformance claim carries its expiry conditions. (ii) **Fallback-Meter chaos probe**: deliberately supplies invalid Meter inputs in production-like conditions to verify R5's degrade-never-fail behaviour end to end.

**K-02 FDC3** — (i) **Context-type resolution explainer**: shows a user *why* an intent resolved to these apps and not others, in terms of declared input/return context types (R3, R4) — currently opaque and a frequent support burden. (ii) **Self-echo idempotence test**: a conformance test for R5's requirement that an app receives `userChannelChanged` even when it caused the change, catching a common implementation shortcut.

**K-04 FIX Orchestra** — (i) **Rules-of-engagement diff service**: diffs two Orchestra files for a venue across dates and emits a member-facing change notice, replacing prose release notes with derived ones (R1, R6). (ii) **Scenario-conditional conformance suite generator**: generates a test suite from a venue's Orchestra file including scenario-conditional rules (R3), so a member can certify before connecting rather than during.

**L-01 RESO** — (i) **Two-version certification label**: states both Web API and Data Dictionary versions in one badge (R5, R6) — because "RESO certified" alone is not a claim. (ii) **Replication convergence checker**: runs a full pull and an incremental sync and proves they converge (my AC4), which is the property consumers actually depend on and nobody tests.

**L-04 PDTF** — (i) **Per-field provenance panel** for buyers: shows, field by field, who asserted it and who verified it (my AC3), which is the product's core promise made visible. (ii) **Re-use ledger across transactions**: since the record outlives one sale, a ledger showing which claims were re-used from a previous transaction and when they were last verified — with staleness surfaced.

**M-01 ODK XForms** — (i) **Resumption state prover**: a harness that interrupts a partially-completed form at every question and verifies relevance/requiredness re-derivation on resume (my AC4) — the failure mode enumerators actually hit. (ii) **Error-message translation coverage report**: reports which `jr:requiredMsg`/`jr:constraintMsg` (R4) lack translations for a deployment's languages, since untranslated errors defeat the point.

**M-02 RO-Crate** — (i) **Offline-openability test**: opens a crate with the network disabled and reports what remains interpretable (my AC4) — a direct test of the longevity claim. (ii) **Crate-in-crate composer**: a tool that assembles a collection crate from member crates preserving each member's licence and authorship, exercising the recursive composition property.

**N-04 ESCO** — (i) **Release diff for downstream holders**: a machine-readable diff of added, deprecated and merged concepts per release (my AC3), because badges, CVs and vacancies embed concept URIs that must be migrated. (ii) **Cross-language match audit**: samples matched vacancy/CV pairs across language borders and reports where the match rests on a single skill URI, exposing brittle matches.

**N-05 HR Open** — (i) **Erasure-safe hire record**: a design that lets a candidate's screening result be deleted on request without breaking the hire record's integrity (my AC4) — a legal requirement the schema set does not obviously address. (ii) **JSON/XML drift monitor**: continuously round-trips sample instances between the parallel representations (R3) and reports divergence.

**O-02 OCDS** — (i) **Retraction pattern**: a documented, conformant way to signal "this release was published in error" without editing it (the unresolved question), so publishers stop breaking R1 in practice. (ii) **Compiled-record equivalence checker**: verifies that merging a publisher's releases reproduces their own compiled record (my AC5), catching merge-rule misimplementation.

**O-04 USWDS** — (i) **Decision-rationale corpus**: publishing the recorded reasoning from R1/R2 approvals as a searchable body, so other design systems can learn *why*, not just *what*. (ii) **Deprecation window announcer**: a mechanism that announces removals with a stated support window (my AC4) to the agencies consuming the library, who currently learn from breakage.

**P-05 LRMI** — (i) **Competency-framework bridge kit**: because LRMI's usefulness depends on an external framework, a kit that connects LRMI-described resources to a chosen curriculum framework and reports alignment coverage. (ii) **Term-currency register**: marks which repository documents are current versus superseded (the "OLD STUFF" problem), so implementers stop building on retired guidance.

**P-03 Ed-Fi** — (i) **Dual-path identity reconciler**: proves that bulk-loaded and API-loaded records for one student resolve to one identity (my AC3) — the seam where district data goes wrong. (ii) **Descriptor divergence map**: shows where a district's local descriptor values have drifted from state or national ones, quantifying the cost of local extensibility.

**Q-05 OpenHIE** — (i) **Prerequisite gate runner**: executes each workflow's stated assumptions (R1) as checks before the workflow runs, so failures occur at the prerequisite rather than midway (my AC4). (ii) **Registry-substitution test suite**: proves component replaceability (R4) by running all workflows against two different conformant client registries.

**Q-02 OMOP** — (i) **ETL-convention conformance linter**: converts the prose in `etlConventions` into checkable assertions where possible, and lists where it cannot — addressing the model's main quality gap. (ii) **Site deviation declaration**: a standard artefact in which a site declares intentional departures from conventions, so multi-site result differences can be attributed (my AC4).

**R-05 OpenActive** — (i) **Consent lifecycle manager for activity leaders**: records consent for qualification publication (R3), and withdraws attributes from the feed within a stated window (my AC4) — the missing half of the consent requirement. (ii) **Perishability monitor**: flags feeds whose sessions have passed or been cancelled without update, since a stale feed sends people to a locked door.

**R-04 SportsML** — (i) **Core-only degradation test**: proves a core-only consumer can still extract competitors and result from any sport-specific document (my AC4), which is what makes the core/extension split worth having. (ii) **New-sport onboarding kit**: a template extension plus conformance checks so adding a sport does not require a core release (the unresolved question).

**S-04 iFixit** — (i) **Inter-assessor agreement study**: two assessors, one rubric version, one device, measured agreement (my AC4) — because a score that varies by assessor cannot support purchasing decisions. (ii) **Rubric-version-stamped score badge**: every published score carries its rubric version (R7), preventing cross-version comparison that the annual update makes invalid.

**S-01 ORDS** — (i) **Two-group merge test**: merges records from two independent repair groups and counts by product category without manual reconciliation (my AC4) — a direct test of R2's comparability claim. (ii) **Noisy-room collection form**: a data-entry instrument designed around R7's explicit acknowledgement of busy environments — minimum viable fields first, enrichment later, never blocking the repair.

**T-02 FIES** — (i) **Validation-failure publication protocol**: a standard way to publish that Rasch validation failed (my AC4) instead of publishing an estimate anyway, plus what to report in its place. (ii) **Translation severity check**: re-estimates item severities per language version to detect items whose translation shifted their position on the scale, breaking comparability.

**T-01 ILO C189** — (i) **Household-scale terms-of-employment generator**: produces a plain-language written statement covering R1's required particulars, in the worker's language and the employer's, for a household that has never employed anyone. (ii) **Stand-by hours recorder**: a simple, low-friction record of stand-by periods (R6) that both parties can see, since the definitional ambiguity of stand-by is where pay disputes originate.

**U-01 IATI** — (i) **Funding-chain tracer**: reconstructs donor→implementer→sub-implementer chains from activity identifiers (my AC4) and reports where the chain breaks. (ii) **Ruleset triage report**: since publishers are unfunded, a report that ranks a publisher's ruleset failures by how much they impede downstream use, so limited effort goes where it matters.

**U-04 Open SDG** — (i) **Zero-target honest renderer**: displays elimination targets with an explicit note that 0.001 is a substitute (my AC4), fixing a genuine misrepresentation. (ii) **Proxy prominence audit**: reports how visible proxy flags (R3) are in the rendered site, since a caveat buried below a chart does not function as a caveat.

**N1-04 VOTable** — (i) **Self-contained interpretability check**: verifies a document remains interpretable with the vocabulary service unreachable (my AC5). (ii) **Dangling-ref linter**: enforces R6 (referenced `TIMESYS` must exist) across an archive, catching a failure that silently produces wrong times.

**N1-02 OPTIMADE** — (i) **Provenance-preserving federated result set**: presents results such that each entry's provider is inseparable from it (my AC5), preventing silent conflation. (ii) **Cross-provider duplicate reporter**: identifies structures that appear in several providers under different `(type,id)` pairs — the limitation provider-scoped identity creates.

**E2-05 RISC-V SBI** — (i) **Reserved-bits fuzzer**: probes firmware with non-zero reserved bits to determine reject-versus-ignore behaviour (the ambiguity), publishing a compatibility matrix across implementations. (ii) **Boot-order violation harness**: issues runtime calls before boot-time setup to verify a defined error rather than undefined behaviour (my AC5).

**E2-03 riscv-dv** — (i) **Seed-replay proof**: verifies a recorded seed reproduces an identical program bit-for-bit (my AC3), which the whole debugging workflow assumes. (ii) **Mismatch attribution assistant**: presents the first divergent instruction with architectural state from both DUT and reference (my AC4), addressing "which side is wrong".

**M3-03 PHA4GE** — (i) **Template version crosswalk**: publishes machine-readable upgrades between specification versions (my AC4), so mid-outbreak vocabulary changes do not strand submitted data. (ii) **Re-identification risk screen**: estimates, from sewershed population, when a sample's location becomes identifying — the unresolved threshold, made a check.

**M3-04 HL7 FHIR** — (i) **CapabilityStatement-derived test suite**: generates conformance tests directly from a system's published statement (my AC3), turning a claim into evidence. (ii) **SHOULD-exception register**: a published list of unimplemented SHOULDs with reasons (my AC4), so trading partners can see the shape of partial support that R6 warns about.

**A4-05 Open Foris Arena** — (i) **Cycle-to-cycle variable mapping**: documents how a revised survey's variables map to the previous cycle (my AC4), preserving the time series that is the whole value of a national inventory. (ii) **Reproducibility receipt**: re-runs a processing chain and certifies byte-identical statistics (my AC3), which official outputs require.

**A4-04 FoodOn** — (i) **Deprecation redirect service**: resolves retired terms to replacements (my AC4), protecting data annotated years earlier. (ii) **Cultural variance annotator**: represents competing culturally-specific definitions of one food side by side rather than forcing a single winner — the unresolved modelling question.

**S5-01 Psych-DS** — (i) **Submission-gate validator**: a journal- or repository-side check that a deposited dataset passes `columnsMatchMetadata` and naming rules before acceptance (R2, R3). (ii) **Undocumented-column nudger**: an authoring-time assistant that prompts for a `variableMeasured` entry the moment a new column appears, aligning the behavioural demand with the moment of work.

**S5-05 OSF preregistration** — (i) **Amendment-preserving deviation log**: records deviations as amendments pointing to the original (my AC4), so honest deviation is easier than silent revision. (ii) **Ordering evidence attachment**: attaches independently time-stamped evidence that data collection had not begun — addressing the central unresolved limitation, that the artefact cannot prove its own timing.

**H6-04 Erlangen CRM** — (i) **Contradiction-tolerant attribution viewer**: displays competing attributions of one object with their asserters (my AC4), which is what the event-centric model exists to permit but tools rarely show. (ii) **Namespace-pinning linter**: flags published data that references `/current/` instead of a dated namespace (R1), preventing silent meaning drift.

**H6-01 TEI P5** — (i) **Customisation conformance card**: states which modules and constraints a project's ODD selects, so "TEI-conformant" becomes a specific claim (R2, R4). (ii) **Uncertainty survival test**: verifies that `CE-` certainty and responsibility encodings survive a publication pipeline rather than being flattened (my AC4).

### 4.2 Composed systems (recursive composition; each explains what passes between parts and what must stay consistent)

**CS-1 · Field-to-payment assurance chain** — `A-01` fiboa boundaries + `A-02` Modus soil results + `A4-03` ADAPT operations + `G-05` UNTP DPP. *What passes:* field identity `(collection,id)` from fiboa; soil analyte values with method identity from Modus; application records from ADAPT; all of it becomes evidence (`G-05/R6`) behind a claim in a product passport. *What must stay consistent:* the field identifier across all four, and the time basis — fiboa's observation-time semantics (`A-01/R6`) must not be conflated with operation time. *Emergent outcome:* a claim like "grown on land not deforested after date X with nutrient application within limits" that carries verifiable evidence rather than assertion. *Inherited requirements:* fiboa R1–R7, Modus R2 (method governance), UNTP R1 (granularity) and R7 (VCDM 2.0).

**CS-2 · Repair evidence loop** — `S-01` ORDS + `S-04` iFixit rubric + `G-05` DPP + `E-04`-style profile thinking. *What passes:* repair attempt outcomes by product category (ORDS codelists) upward into repairability evidence; scores downward into passports at model granularity (`G-05/R1`). *What must stay consistent:* product category identity across ORDS codelists and passport product identity; rubric version stamping (`S-04/R7`). *Emergent outcome:* a repairability claim on a product passport grounded in what actually happened at repair cafés, not only in a lab teardown. *Note:* ORDS and iFixit share an origin cluster — this composition therefore does not constitute independent corroboration.

**CS-3 · Outbreak-to-plate traceback** — `I-05` FSMA 204 + `G-01` EPCIS + `M3-03` PHA4GE + `A4-04` FoodOn. *What passes:* traceability lot codes and CTE/KDE records (FSMA) expressed as EPCIS events; wastewater genomic signals (PHA4GE) as the detection trigger; FoodOn terms reconciling product descriptions across parties. *What must stay consistent:* lot identity through transformation (`I-05/R4`) and food-term identity (FoodOn ontology IDs) across supply-chain participants. *Emergent outcome:* the 24-hour spreadsheet obligation (`I-05/R5`) becomes a query rather than a fire drill. *Feasibility caveat:* FSMA requirements are unverified; this composition should be treated as a design hypothesis.

**CS-4 · Building lifecycle handover** — `F-01` BCF + `F-02` BuildingSync + `F-04` Brick + `F-05` RealEstateCore. *What passes:* design-stage issues and their viewpoints (BCF) resolve into as-built element identities; audit findings (BuildingSync, at a declared level) attach to those elements; Brick/REC carry them into operation. *What must stay consistent:* element identity across model revisions — the unresolved question in `F-01` — and audit level declarations. *Emergent outcome:* an operator can ask "which unresolved design issue explains this HVAC fault?" *Inherited:* `F-01/R1` field consistency; `F-02/R2` per-use-case conformance.

**CS-5 · Mobility trip across operators** — `H-01` GTFS + `H-02` GBFS + `H-03` TOMP + `H-06` MDS. *What passes:* scheduled service (GTFS), vehicle availability (GBFS), booking and payment (TOMP), and regulatory reporting (MDS). *What must stay consistent:* stop/place identity between GTFS and GBFS station definitions; a trip identifier that survives the operator boundary. *Emergent outcome:* a single journey combining bus and shared bike that a city can also regulate. *Cluster note:* GTFS and GBFS share MobilityData stewardship.

**CS-6 · Clinical study definition to evidence** — `M3-01` CDISC USDM + `M3-04` FHIR + `Q-02` OMOP + `S5-05` preregistration. *What passes:* a machine-readable study definition (USDM) drives data capture (FHIR) which lands in a common model (OMOP) for analysis; the analysis plan is frozen beforehand (preregistration). *What must stay consistent:* endpoint definitions across all four, and the frozen analysis plan against the executed one. *Emergent outcome:* deviation between planned and executed analysis becomes detectable rather than deniable. *Inherited:* `Q-02/R2` ETL conventions; `M3-04/R1` scoped conformance.

**CS-7 · Public money to public outcome** — `O-02` OCDS + `U-01` IATI + `S-03` 360Giving + `U-04` Open SDG. *What passes:* contracting processes (`ocid`), aid activities (IATI identifiers), grants (360Giving), aggregated into indicator movement (Open SDG). *What must stay consistent:* organisation identity across four schemes — the hardest part — and immutability of published records (`O-02/R1`). *Emergent outcome:* tracing a stated SDG indicator change back to the contracts and grants that plausibly produced it.

**CS-8 · Heritage object across institutions** — `H6-04` CIDOC/Erlangen CRM + `R-03` Linked Art + `R-01` IIIF + `H6-01` TEI + `H6-02` RiC-O. *What passes:* object and event identity (CRM), presentation of images (IIIF), transcription of associated texts (TEI), and archival context of the records (RiC-O). *What must stay consistent:* the dated CRM namespace (`H6-04/R1`) across institutions, and the identity of the object across all representations. *Emergent outcome:* a contested attribution can be shown with its evidence — image, transcript, archival record — and its asserter.

**CS-9 · Energy at the meter boundary** — `D-01` OCPI + `D-03` OCPP + `D-04` EEBus + `D-02` SunSpec. *What passes:* the charger-to-operator link (OCPP), the operator-to-provider roaming link (OCPI), the in-home negotiation (EEBus), and inverter/battery state (SunSpec). *What must stay consistent:* EVSE identity across OCPP and OCPI — the seam noted in `D-01` — and the physical limit that all parties are negotiating against. *Emergent outcome:* a car charging on a visiting network without exceeding the house fuse, billed correctly.

**CS-10 · Survey instrument to public statistic** — `M-01` ODK XForms + `S5-04` XLSForm + `A4-05` Arena + `S5-03` DDI-CDI + `S5-02` SDMX + `U-05` NADA. *What passes:* an authored form (XLSForm→XForms), field collection (Arena/ODK), variable-level description (DDI-CDI), aggregate dissemination (SDMX), and cataloguing (NADA). *What must stay consistent:* variable identity from question to published aggregate — the thing that usually breaks. *Emergent outcome:* a published statistic whose every number traces to a question as asked, in the language it was asked in. *Cluster note:* ODK and XLSForm share an origin.

**CS-11 · Water safety across the chain** — `E-01` EPANET + `E-03` ODM2 + `M3-03` PHA4GE + `E-02` SWMM. *What passes:* network hydraulic model (EPANET) locating where a contaminant would travel; observations and specimens (ODM2) confirming; wastewater genomics (PHA4GE) as early signal; stormwater model (SWMM) for the surface pathway. *What must stay consistent:* site identity and spatial reference (`E-03/R2`) across models and observations.

**CS-12 · Skills-to-work chain** — `N-04` ESCO + `N-05` HR Open + `P-01` Open Badges + `P-05` LRMI. *What passes:* skill concept URIs (ESCO) into vacancy and candidate records (HR Open), into badges asserting attainment (Open Badges), into learning resources aligned to those skills (LRMI). *What must stay consistent:* the skill concept URI, across releases (`N-04/R4`) — every downstream artefact embeds it. *Emergent outcome:* a learner can see which specific learning resource closes the gap between their badges and a real vacancy. *Caveat:* two of four sources here are SNIPPET-only.

**CS-13 · Household support pathway** — `Q-03` HSDS + `T-01` C189 particulars + `T-02` FIES + `Q-05` OpenHIE alerting. *What passes:* a household's measured food insecurity (FIES) and the services available to it (HSDS), with alerting workflows (OpenHIE) reaching the household, and — where a domestic worker is employed in it — the terms-of-employment record (C189). *What must stay consistent:* household identity without creating a surveillance record; consent for contact. *Emergent outcome:* the ISIC T shortage partly addressed by composition — measurement, entitlement and referral joined, where no single source covers the household as employer and as provisioning unit.

**CS-14 · Assurance of a claim, generically** — `G-05` UNTP + `M3-04` FHIR CapabilityStatement + `J-05` C2PA + `O-01` OSCAL. *What passes:* a claim, its criteria, its evidence, and a statement of what the asserting system actually supports; C2PA binds media evidence to its origin; OSCAL expresses the control assessment behind the assertion. *What must stay consistent:* the identity of the asserter and the scope of the claim (`M3-04/R1`). *Emergent outcome:* a general pattern — *claim + criterion + evidence + declared capability* — reusable across sustainability, health and security domains.

**CS-15 · Irreversibility register** — `O-02` OCDS immutability + `S5-05` frozen preregistration + `H-05` eBL endorsement + `B-05` tailings classification. *What passes:* nothing operational — this is a **cross-domain pattern library**: four independent domains that solved "this record must not be quietly changed" differently (new release, read-only snapshot, ordered endorsement chain, disclosed classification). *What must stay consistent:* the definition of what counts as an alteration. *Emergent outcome:* a designer facing a new irreversibility problem can choose among four field-tested mechanisms with their trade-offs stated.

**CS-16 · Conditional-obligation pattern library** — `H-01` GTFS Conditionally Required + `M-01` ODK `required` expressions + `K-04` FIX Orchestra scenario conditions + `A4-05` Arena per-provider config + `F-02` BuildingSync per-use-case elements. *What passes:* five distinct encodings of "required, but only sometimes". *What must stay consistent:* the ability to explain a verdict to a human. *Emergent outcome:* a reusable design catalogue — and the observation that every one of these five specifications independently discovered that flat requiredness is insufficient.

### 4.3 Generators and configurators (each with the generator **and** a concrete product it must produce)

**GEN-1 · Conditional-requirement validator generator.** *Generator:* takes a specification's requiredness rules expressed in a small common notation (presence conditions, cross-field predicates, scenario scoping) and emits a validator that reports, per record, the condition text, the evaluated inputs and the verdict. *Concrete product it must produce:* a GTFS validator that, for `stops.txt`, prints the Conditionally Required condition from `H-01/R5`, the fields it evaluated, and PASS/FAIL — and the same generator, fed FIX Orchestra scenario rules (`K-04/R3`), produces a venue conformance suite. *Why:* CS-16 shows five specifications solved this separately; the reasoning-report property is what each one lacks.

**GEN-2 · Two-authority conformance labeller.** *Generator:* given a pair of independently versioned standards that must be cited together, emits a badge artefact and a verification endpoint. *Concrete product:* a RESO badge stating Web API version *and* Data Dictionary version (`L-01/R5`, `R6`), verifiable against a live server; the same generator produces the BuildingSync audit-level badge (`F-02`, my AC4) and the iFixit rubric-version score stamp (`S-04/R7`).

**GEN-3 · Provenance-panel builder.** *Generator:* given a schema plus a claims/evidence model, produces a per-field provenance UI showing asserter, verifier, evidence link and staleness. *Concrete product:* the PDTF buyer panel (`L-04`, my AC3) rendering title, EPC and search fields with their verification status; the same generator fed UNTP DPP claims (`G-05/R6`) produces a product-passport evidence panel.

**GEN-4 · Single-source documentation-and-schema pipeline.** *Generator:* an ODD-style pipeline taking one model source and emitting prose documentation, machine schemas, and test fixtures that cannot diverge. *Concrete product:* regenerating the IATI documentation and schema from its SSOT (`U-01/R2`) with a CI gate that fails on divergence; the same pipeline applied to Smart Data Models produces `spec.md` plus its four translations from `model.yaml` (`E-04/R3`).

**GEN-5 · Instrument-to-statistic tracer generator.** *Generator:* given a form definition and a dissemination structure, emits a variable-lineage map plus a checker that fails when a published aggregate cannot be traced to a question. *Concrete product:* for an FAO Arena forest inventory (`A4-05`), a lineage document from each XLSForm question (`S5-04`) through DDI-CDI variable descriptions (`S5-03`) to the SDMX dataflow (`S5-02`) — and a report naming any published figure without a traceable question.

**GEN-6 · Degradation-behaviour test generator.** *Generator:* takes a specification's stated degrade-not-fail and prerequisite rules and emits chaos tests. *Concrete product:* an OpenTelemetry SDK test asserting a working Meter is returned for invalid input (`J-02/R5`); the same generator fed OpenHIE workflow prerequisites (`Q-05/R1`) emits prerequisite-gate tests, and fed EEBus peer-loss conditions emits the safe-fallback test in `D-04`'s AC3.

---

## 5. Stress collection (54 hypothetical variations, one per selected case)

Each is **[H] hypothetical**. Each states the changed condition and its consequences. **No inherited requirement is deleted**; where a requirement becomes unsatisfiable, that is stated as a conflict to be resolved, not removed.

1. **A-01 fiboa — adversarial provenance.** *Change:* boundaries are submitted by parties with a financial interest in the outcome. *Consequence:* R5's `determination:method` becomes a claim rather than a fact; `unknown` becomes the strategically preferred value. R1–R4, R6, R7 all stand; a new evidence-binding obligation must be added on top, and AC3 (method in enumeration) no longer implies trustworthiness.

2. **A-02 Modus — lab closes mid-season.** *Change:* the issuing laboratory ceases trading with results outstanding. *Consequence:* R1's submit/result pairing has no counterparty; R5 (result links to submit) survives only if the FMIS retained the submit document. R2's method governance is unaffected. Adds a requirement for result portability to a successor lab without re-sampling.

3. **B-05 GISTM — downstream town doubles.** *Change:* population downstream grows tenfold after classification. *Consequence:* R2's consequence classification is stale though never formally invalidated; R5's disclosure now publishes a wrong classification. R3, R4 stand. Forces an explicit re-classification trigger, which the standard as I understand it does not clearly provide.

4. **B-01 OMF — v2 goes to release mid-project.** *Change:* the beta format stabilises with incompatible changes while a project is in flight. *Consequence:* R5's stability caveat becomes an active migration obligation; R1's single-file project must now declare version unambiguously (my AC3 becomes mandatory). R2–R4 stand.

5. **C-05 ONIE — the ODM abandons the platform.** *Change:* the vendor stops maintaining a machine port still in fleet use. *Consequence:* R1's additive port model now needs a custodianship path; R2's compliance environment becomes the only evidence the port still works. R3–R5 stand. Adds an orphan-port maintenance requirement.

6. **C-03 MTConnect — agent restarts under load.** *Change:* the agent restarts, resetting its sequence-number timeline. *Consequence:* R2 and R3 (`at`/`from`) reference a timeline that no longer exists; clients cannot distinguish restart from overrun. R1, R5, R6, R7 stand. Elevates my AC5 (gap detection) from proposed to necessary.

7. **D-04 EEBus — two energy managers claim authority.** *Change:* a householder installs a second home energy manager. *Consequence:* R2's role model permits both to act as clients of the same server features; nothing arbitrates. R1, R3, R4 stand. Requires an authority-precedence rule that the peer-to-peer model does not supply.

8. **D-01 OCPI — tariff changes mid-session.** *Change:* a CPO publishes a new tariff while a session is running. *Consequence:* R1–R6 stand, but the tariff-to-CDR relationship becomes ambiguous; my AC4 (one CDR ↔ one session ↔ one tariff version) can no longer be satisfied without a tariff-version pin at session start.

9. **E-03 ODM2 — instrument found miscalibrated retroactively.** *Change:* a calibration error is discovered affecting two years of sensor data. *Consequence:* R5's equipment provenance is what makes scoped retraction possible; R6's annotations must now carry retraction semantics without altering values. R1–R4 stand. Adds a requirement that downstream analyses can be notified.

10. **E-04 Smart Data Models — a regulator mandates comparison.** *Change:* a regulator requires cross-city discharge comparison. *Consequence:* R1's two-property minimum becomes actively harmful; the profile in my AC4 becomes mandatory. R2–R5 stand. The generator template (R3) must now support profile-specific required sets.

11. **F-02 BuildingSync — the auditor and the owner disagree on level.** *Change:* the owner contracted Level 2; the delivered file supports only Level 1 elements. *Consequence:* R2's per-use-case checking gives the evidence; R3's partial-Level-2 acknowledgement becomes a contractual problem. R1, R4, R5 stand. My AC4 (declared level) becomes a payment gate.

12. **F-01 BCF — the model is re-exported and GUIDs change.** *Change:* an authoring tool regenerates element identifiers. *Consequence:* every viewpoint dangles; R1–R3 remain satisfied while the artefact becomes useless — the sharpest illustration that schema conformance is not fitness. My AC3 must be supplemented by a GUID-stability requirement on the upstream tool, which BCF cannot impose.

13. **G-05 UNTP — an issuer revokes a credential after purchase.** *Change:* a conformity claim is revoked post-sale. *Consequence:* R7's VCDM revocation works, but R2's aggregation means one revoked claim sits among valid ones; R4's comparison must now handle mixed-validity passports. R1, R3, R5, R6 stand. Adds a requirement to surface partial revocation to a non-expert buyer.

14. **G-02 UBL — a jurisdiction mandates a field the base schema lacks.** *Change:* a new legal requirement for an unmodelled data element. *Consequence:* R3's CVA customisation cannot add elements, only constrain values; the jurisdiction forks or waits. R1, R2, R4, R5 stand. Exposes extension as the model's boundary.

15. **H-01 GTFS — an agency merges with another mid-feed.** *Change:* two agencies combine, changing agency and route identifiers. *Consequence:* R4's required `agency.txt` is satisfiable, but every downstream consumer's stored identifiers break silently. R1–R3, R5, R6 stand. Requires a deprecation/continuity mechanism the format does not define.

16. **H-05 DCSA — the holder of an eBL becomes unreachable.** *Change:* the party holding title cannot be contacted. *Consequence:* R1–R4 stand; the endorsement chain is intact but frozen. Cargo is physically present and legally unclaimable. Adds a requirement for a defined escalation that does not break chain integrity.

17. **I-04 LIVES — two cities merge inspection regimes.** *Change:* scoring scales differ between the merged jurisdictions. *Consequence:* R1–R5 stand (the format is agnostic), and precisely because it is agnostic, scores become incomparable within one feed. Makes the unresolved comparability question operational.

18. **I-05 FSMA 204 — a supplier refuses to share KDEs.** *Change:* an upstream trading partner will not provide required elements. *Consequence:* R1–R7 stand as obligations on the receiving firm, which cannot satisfy R5 for lots it received blind. Forces either refusal to trade or non-compliance — a conflict the rule places on the weaker party.

19. **J-04 OpenAPI — a generator vendor treats the JSON Schema as authoritative.** *Change:* tooling ignores R4. *Consequence:* R4 is violated silently and downstream clients diverge; R2, R5, R6 remain satisfiable. Elevates my AC4 (two-generator comparison) to a release gate.

20. **J-02 OpenTelemetry — a Development-status requirement changes after certification.** *Change:* the spec revises a requirement a certified SDK implemented. *Consequence:* R6's status marking is what makes the certification interpretable; without my AC4 (status-scoped report) the certification is unfalsifiable. R1–R5 stand.

21. **K-02 FDC3 — apps span two bridged desktops with different app directories.** *Change:* agent bridging joins two desktops whose directories disagree about an app's context types. *Consequence:* R3 and R4 (resolution by context type) yield different answers on each side. R1, R2, R5, R6 stand. Requires a directory-reconciliation rule.

22. **K-04 FIX Orchestra — a venue changes a rule with 24 hours' notice.** *Change:* a scenario-conditional rule changes overnight. *Consequence:* R6's migration path and R1's machine-readability make regeneration possible; my AC4 (generated conformance suite) becomes the only way members can re-certify in time. R2–R5 stand.

23. **L-01 RESO — a data licence is revoked for one consumer.** *Change:* an MLS withdraws a portal's licence. *Consequence:* R3's server-driven paging gives the provider control to stop serving, but replicated data already held is out of scope. R1, R2, R4–R6 stand. Adds a deletion-propagation requirement the transport spec does not address.

24. **L-04 PDTF — a verified claim turns out to be wrong.** *Change:* an authoritative source's data was incorrect. *Consequence:* R3's verification chain identifies who verified, which converts a data problem into a liability question. R1, R2, R4, R5 stand. The unresolved liability gap becomes the blocking issue.

25. **M-01 ODK — a form is edited between deployment and collection.** *Change:* the form definition changes while devices hold partially completed records. *Consequence:* R3's dynamic requiredness may now reference removed fields; R1, R2, R4, R5 stand. My AC4 (resumption) must extend to version mismatch, not only interruption.

26. **M-02 RO-Crate — an external identifier service shuts down.** *Change:* ORCID/DOI resolution becomes unavailable. *Consequence:* R2 and R3 stand structurally; interpretability degrades. My AC4 (offline openability) becomes the survival property. Adds a requirement to inline minimal human-readable labels alongside identifiers.

27. **N-04 ESCO — a concept is merged with another.** *Change:* two skills are merged in a release. *Consequence:* R4 (identity stability, my interpretation) is broken by design; every badge and vacancy embedding the retired URI needs migration. R1–R3 stand. My AC3 (release diff) becomes mandatory.

28. **N-05 HR Open — a candidate exercises erasure mid-process.** *Change:* a right-to-erasure request arrives after assessment but before offer. *Consequence:* R4's Candidate schema and the assessment/interview chain must lose one node without breaking the rest. R1–R3, R5 stand. My AC4 becomes a legal requirement rather than a proposal.

29. **O-02 OCDS — a release is published containing personal data in error.** *Change:* a release must be withdrawn. *Consequence:* R1's immutability directly conflicts with a legal erasure duty. R2–R7 stand. This is a genuine unresolved conflict; my proposed retraction pattern (§4.1) is the minimum viable answer and still leaves the original published copy in consumers' hands.

30. **O-04 USWDS — a component must change urgently for accessibility.** *Change:* a serious accessibility defect requires immediate change. *Consequence:* R1–R3's approval ladder with recorded reasoning adds latency exactly when speed matters. R4, R5 stand. Requires an expedited path that still records rationale, rather than one that bypasses it.

31. **P-05 LRMI — the host vocabulary deprecates a term.** *Change:* schema.org changes a term LRMI relies on. *Consequence:* R3 (my interpretation of schema.org alignment) means published pages now carry a retired term; R1, R2, R4 stand. My AC3 (additive change, resolvable retirements) becomes necessary at the host level, which LRMI does not control.

32. **P-03 Ed-Fi — a district migrates vendors mid-year.** *Change:* the student information system changes during a school year. *Consequence:* R5's shared student identity must survive; R1–R4 stand. My AC3 (bulk/API identity convergence) becomes the migration acceptance test.

33. **Q-05 OpenHIE — the client registry is offline during an emergency.** *Change:* the identity component fails while care continues. *Consequence:* R1's prerequisites are unmet, so by my AC4 the workflow should fail visibly — but clinically, care cannot stop. R2–R4 stand. Forces a defined degraded mode with later reconciliation, which the specification does not describe.

34. **Q-02 OMOP — two sites interpret `gender_concept_id` differently.** *Change:* one site populates it with gender identity despite R3's explicit restriction. *Consequence:* R1 (requiredness) and R5 (domain constraint) are both satisfied; results are wrong. R2, R4, R6, R7 stand. Shows that machine-checkable conformance cannot enforce semantic scope — my proposed site-deviation declaration is the only available mitigation.

35. **R-05 OpenActive — a leader withdraws consent after publication.** *Change:* consent for qualification publication is revoked. *Consequence:* R3 stands but is now retrospective; the feed and every downstream index hold the data. R1, R2, R4–R6 stand. My AC4's withdrawal window must extend to indexers, which the publisher does not control.

36. **R-04 SportsML — a new sport with no extension appears at a major event.** *Change:* coverage is needed before an extension exists. *Consequence:* R1's core must carry it alone; my AC4 (core-only degradation) becomes the fallback path rather than a nicety. R2–R5 stand.

37. **S-04 iFixit — a manufacturer changes a design mid-production run.** *Change:* two devices share a model number but differ internally. *Consequence:* R6's component-path scoring yields two different scores for one model; R7's annual revision cycle is too slow. R1–R5 stand. Requires score scoping to a production variant, not a model name.

38. **S-01 ORDS — a group collects only three fields.** *Change:* a volunteer group records minimal data. *Consequence:* R7 explicitly anticipates this, so R1 (structured) is satisfiable while R2 (comparable) degrades. R3–R6 stand. Exposes that graded obligation trades comparability for participation — a deliberate and defensible trade that must be reported in any aggregate.

39. **T-02 FIES — items fail Rasch validation three years running.** *Change:* persistent validation failure in one country. *Consequence:* R5 forbids producing a measure; R1–R4, R6 stand. The country has no food-insecurity statistic at all. My AC4 (publish the failure) is the only honest output; the unresolved question of what to do instead remains open.

40. **T-01 ILO C189 — the worker's immigration status is tied to the employer.** *Change:* leaving the employment means losing the right to remain. *Consequence:* R1–R6 remain formally satisfiable while being practically unusable; R3's freedom to leave during rest periods is nominal. Nothing in the Convention's own terms resolves this; it requires a separate immigration instrument.

41. **U-01 IATI — a publisher stops publishing.** *Change:* an organisation ceases IATI publication mid-programme. *Consequence:* R1's expectation is unenforceable; existing files remain but stop updating, and traceability chains (my AC4) break at that node. R2–R5 stand. Requires downstream consumers to distinguish "no activity" from "no publication".
