## 4. Construction briefs (54)

### ISIC A — Agriculture, forestry and fishing

**A-03 · Primary domain: fisheries stock assessment (FORD 1)**
Ben: NOAA stock-assessment scientists and the regional fishery management councils they advise. Prob: each model structure historically demanded a separate software system, so analysts spend scarce assessment-cycle time learning tools instead of testing structural hypotheses. Ctx: a regulatory calendar with fixed council deadlines; results feed catch limits, an irreversible economic effect.
Src: NOAA Fisheries, "FIMS — The repository for development of FIMS", https://github.com/NOAA-FIMS/FIMS, repository README, READ 2026-09-09.
Deliv `[S]`: an R package fitting integrated statistical population-dynamics models to fishery data; an "Introducing FIMS" vignette as the entry point. In `[S]`: catch, index and composition fishery data; C++ core with Rcpp bindings.
R1 `[S]` The system must fit *integrated statistical population-dynamics models to fishery data*. R2 `[S]` It must support multiple model types including surplus-production and catch-at-age. R3 `[S]` User interaction is via R; computation in C++ through Rcpp. R4 `[S]` Licence GPL-3.0. R5 `[S]` Status is "Lifecycle: experimental" — breaking change is expected. R6 `[S]` Outputs must carry the disclaimer that the repository "is a scientific product and is not official communication of the National Oceanic and Atmospheric Administration".
Rel: R-facing API ↔ C++ model core (passes parameter vectors and likelihood values; the *parameter naming convention* must stay consistent across the boundary); vignette ↔ package (passes worked examples; must be regenerated when R2's model set grows).
Con: US-government work, no copyright under 17 U.S.C. §105 `[S]`; experimental status forbids treating fitted output as an official assessment `[I]`.
AC: AC-S1 a surplus-production and a catch-at-age model both run to convergence in the same session. AC-S2 the vignette executes without error on a clean install. AC-P1 *(requires acceptance)* a model-comparison harness reproduces a previously published assessment within a stated tolerance.
Asm/Miss/Unres: the README does not state numerical convergence criteria, supported data formats, or a versioning policy; "experimental" has no defined exit condition.

**A-05 · Primary domain: agricultural production data exchange (FORD 4)**
Ben: farm-management software vendors and equipment makers moving production records between businesses. Prob: transfer previously required adopting a code toolkit, coupling data exchange to software dependencies. Ctx: business-to-business transfer across rival vendors; a committee must arbitrate.
Src: AgGateway ADAPT, "ADAPT Standard — Standard data schema for agriculture interoperability", https://github.com/ADAPT/Standard, repository README and governance section, READ 2026-09-09.
Deliv `[S]`: a root schema; standard data type definitions; unit-of-measure abbreviations — all as JSON files. In `[S]`: the 2015 ADAPT Framework toolkit as predecessor; adaptstandard.org.
R1 `[S]` The standard is "a data schema, set of standard data type definitions and unit of measure abbreviations designed for the business-to-business transfer of agricultural production data." R2 `[S]` It must be data-only — no software dependency. R3 `[S]` Change proposals traverse the states New → Discussion/Implementing → Implemented → AC review → Approved → Candidate → Member review → Ready for release → Released. R4 `[S]` The Architecture Committee reviews. R5 `[S]` Licence MIT.
Rel: root schema ↔ data-type definitions ↔ unit abbreviations (passes type and unit identifiers; *identifier stability across releases* is the invariant); issue tracker ↔ release (passes state transitions and a "Score" status per component).
Con: no component may be released before its counterparts reach the corresponding status `[S]`.
AC: AC-S1 every released component carries a state of "Released" and a matching Score. AC-S2 a consumer parses the JSON files with no ADAPT code dependency. AC-P1 *(requires acceptance)* unit abbreviations round-trip through a third-party unit library without loss.
Asm/Miss/Unres: no version number or release date on the page; "Score" is undefined in the read text; member-review quorum unknown.

### ISIC B — Mining and quarrying

**B-01 · Primary domain: mining data interchange (FORD 2)**
Ben: mine geologists and planners exchanging block models, surfaces and drillhole data between rival packages. Prob: interchange today is via proprietary or lossy formats. Ctx: a vendor-neutral committee under an industry guidelines body.
Src: Global Mining Guidelines Group, "omf — Open Mining Format Project Committee", https://github.com/gmggroup/omf, repository root and LICENSE, READ-thin 2026-09-09.
Deliv `[S]`: **none present at the cited URL.** The repository contains a LICENSE file only (1 commit).
In `[S]`: MIT License, copyright 2023 Global Mining Guidelines Group; repository description "Open Mining Format Project Committee".
R1 `[S]` Any artefact published here is MIT-licensed to the Global Mining Guidelines Group's terms. R2 `[I]` A committee, not a single vendor, holds the format.
Rel: none observable.
Con: `[I]` a format governed by a committee must publish normative text somewhere reachable, or implementers cannot conform.
AC: AC-S1 the licence file is present and grants unrestricted use. AC-P1 *(requires acceptance)* the committee publishes element and geometry definitions at a stable, citable URL.
Asm/Miss/Unres: **recorded shortfall — element types, geometry types, version numbers, file rules and governance are all absent from the sampled source.** Whether the specification lives in a member-only or differently-named repository is unresolved. Every substantive statement about OMF's content in this package is withheld rather than inferred.

**B-06 · Primary domain: upstream well data tooling (FORD 2)**
Ben: drilling and completions data engineers who must inspect what a WITSML server actually returns. Prob: schema-conformance problems surface only at integration time, across SOAP and ETP transports and multiple standard versions. Ctx: multi-vendor well sites; concurrent real-time streams.
Src: PDS Technology, "PDS WITSMLstudio Desktop", https://github.com/pds-technology/witsml-studio, README, READ 2026-09-09.
Deliv `[S]`: a Windows desktop application (C#/WPF) with four plug-ins: DataReplay, EtpBrowser, ObjectInspector, WitsmlBrowser. In `[S]`: a `witsml` git submodule; a WITSML server reachable by SOAP or ETP.
R1 `[S]` It must "connect to any WITSML server via SOAP or ETP". R2 `[S]` ObjectInspector must display "WITSML data objects with corresponding Energistics schema information". R3 `[S]` DataReplay must simulate streaming data in and out of a server. R4 `[S]` Build requires a **recursive** clone to retrieve the `witsml` submodule. R5 `[S]` Licence: PDS Open Source WITSML™ Product License Agreement.
Rel: plug-in host ↔ plug-ins (passes a connection handle and a data-object stream; *the schema version negotiated on connect* must be consistent across all four plug-ins); submodule ↔ ObjectInspector (passes schema definitions).
Con: Windows-only `[S]`; a non-recursive clone silently loses schema data `[I]`.
AC: AC-S1 a recursive clone builds; a non-recursive clone fails visibly. AC-S2 ObjectInspector renders schema annotations for a live object. AC-P1 *(requires acceptance)* DataReplay round-trips a recorded log with byte-identical object payloads.
Asm/Miss/Unres: which WITSML/PRODML/RESQML/ETP versions are supported is **not stated** in the read text.

### ISIC C — Manufacturing

**C-05 · Primary domain: industrial interoperability publishing (FORD 2)**
Ben: automation vendors implementing OPC UA companion specifications. Prob: normative machine-readable artefacts must be discoverable, versioned and identical for every implementer, or "conformant" devices disagree. Ctx: a standards body with member-only drafts and a public release channel.
Src: OPC Foundation, "UA-Nodeset", https://github.com/OPCFoundation/UA-Nodeset, README, READ 2026-09-09.
Deliv `[S]`: per specification — `*.NodeSet2.xml` ("The formal definition of the Nodes defined by the specification"), `*.Types.xsd`, `*.NodeIds.csv`; plus non-normative C# classes and binary representations.
In `[S]`: `UANodeSet.xsd`, `OPCBinarySchema.xsd`; branches `latest`, `v1.05`, `v1.04`, `v1.03`; a member-only repository holding drafts and release candidates.
R1 `[S]` This repository contains only *latest released* normative files. R2 `[S]` On release, a companion specification "will be added to the latest branch and tagged". R3 `[S]` NodeSets validate against `UANodeSet.xsd`. R4 `[S]` Defects are "reported as a mantis issue against the appropriate specification".
Rel: member-only draft repo → public repo (passes a release-approved artefact set; the *tag* is the shared identity); NodeSet ↔ Types.xsd ↔ NodeIds.csv (passes NodeIds; the invariant is that a NodeId means the same node in all three).
Con: drafts must never appear on public release branches `[S]`; NodeIds are effectively immutable once released `[I]`.
AC: AC-S1 every released specification has a tag and appears on `latest`. AC-S2 each NodeSet validates against `UANodeSet.xsd`. AC-P1 *(requires acceptance)* no NodeId is reassigned to a different node between tags.
Asm/Miss/Unres: licence not stated in the read text; branch-retirement policy unknown.

**C-06 · Primary domain: dimensional metrology and quality reporting (FORD 2)**
Ben: quality engineers producing First Article Inspection reports and the customers auditing them. Prob: measurement intent, plans, results and statistics live in disconnected tools, so traceability from design characteristic to measured value breaks. Ctx: aerospace-style supplier qualification with contractual reporting duties.
Src: DMSC QIF artefacts via J. Michaloski, "QIF", https://github.com/johnmichaloski/QIF, README, READ 2026-09-09.
Deliv `[S]`: FAIR (First Article Inspection Report) generation code; XML information models. In `[S]`: six application schemas — QIFRules, QIFResults, QIFPlans, QIFProduct, QIFStatistics, QIFMeasurementResources — bundled into a QIF Document, over a shared library (Auxiliary, Characteristics, Expressions, Features, Geometry, PMI, Primitives, Statistics, Topology, Traceability, Units, Visualization); CodeSynthesis and Xerces C++.
R1 `[S]` QIF "is an ANSI standard sponsored by the Dimensional Metrology Standards Consortium (DMSC)". R2 `[S]` The six application schemas share one reusable core library. R3 `[S]` Data generation proceeds broadly clockwise from MBD → Plans → Resources → Rules → Results → Statistics. R4 `[S]` A QIF Document bundles the applicable schemas.
Rel: MBD ↔ Plans (passes characteristics to be measured); Plans ↔ Results (passes the measurement plan identity — the invariant is that every result cites a planned characteristic); Results ↔ Statistics (passes measured values with units); Traceability library ↔ all (passes provenance).
Con: units must resolve through the shared Units component for cross-schema comparability `[I]`.
AC: AC-S1 a generated FAIR validates against the QIF schemas. AC-S2 every reported result resolves to a characteristic defined in the product model. AC-P1 *(requires acceptance)* a report re-generated from the same inputs is byte-stable except for timestamps.
Asm/Miss/Unres: QIF version number not stated; whether this repository is normative or illustrative is unresolved (it is an individual's repository implementing a DMSC standard).

### ISIC D — Electricity, gas, steam and air conditioning supply

**D-02 · Primary domain: substation protection and control configuration (FORD 2)**
Ben: transmission and distribution utilities configuring Protection Automation and Control Systems. Prob: PACS configuration is vendor-locked; profile management and SCL file handling differ per supplier, so utilities cannot re-tender or audit their own substations. Ctx: divided authority between utility engineering and equipment vendors; changes have irreversible grid-safety consequences.
Src: LF Energy CoMPAS, organization page and repository set, https://github.com/com-pas, READ 2026-09-09.
Deliv `[S]`: open-source components for IEC 61850 model implementation (profile management) and PACS configuration — including an SCL editor, SCL data services and validation, CIM-to-SCL mapping, and SCL auto-alignment.
In `[S]`: IEC 61850-6 Edition 2 or greater SCL files; a demo environment at demo.compas.energy; Apache-2.0.
R1 `[S]` Mission: "develop open source software components related to IEC 61850 model implementation (profile management) and configuration of a power industry Protection Automation and Control System (PACS)." R2 `[S]` The editor targets "SCL IEC 61850-6 Edition 2 or greater". R3 `[S]` Components must include validation of SCL data. R4 `[S]` Licence Apache-2.0. R5 `[S]` The demo uses a *shared database* — "uploaded files can be seen by everyone".
Rel: CIM→SCL mapper ↔ SCL editor (passes network model elements; the invariant is consistent equipment identity between CIM and SCL); editor ↔ validation service (passes the SCL document; must agree on the edition); auto-alignment ↔ editor (passes reconciled file variants).
Con: R5 makes the demo unsuitable for utility data `[I]`; edition compatibility is a hard gate `[S]`.
AC: AC-S1 an Edition-2 SCL file loads, validates and round-trips through the editor. AC-S2 the project meets OpenSSF Best Practices as displayed. AC-P1 *(requires acceptance)* CIM→SCL mapping is idempotent for an unchanged source model.
Asm/Miss/Unres: which SCL profiles (SSD/SCD/CID) each component handles is not enumerated on the org page.

**D-05 · Primary domain: distributed energy export control (FORD 2)**
Ben: Australian households and installers on dynamic connection agreements with a DNSP. Prob: a distributor's export limit changes through the day; without automated curtailment the connection is non-compliant or the customer over-exports. Ctx: real-time control with safety consequences; a certifying authority (the DNSP) sits outside the operator.
Src: open-dynamic-export (independent maintainer), https://github.com/longzheng/open-dynamic-export, README, READ 2026-09-09.
Deliv `[S]`: a Node.js/TypeScript controller implementing dynamic export control/solar curtailment for inverters.
In `[S]`: CSIP-AUS / SEP2 / IEEE 2030.5 endpoints from DNSPs; SunSpec Modbus (TCP and RTU) inverters and meters; tariff and feed-in signals.
R1 `[S]` Must "satisfy dynamic connection requirements (CSIP-AUS/SEP2/IEEE 2030.5) of various Australian energy distributors (DNSPs)". R2 `[S]` Must support fixed/zero export limits — worked example "1.5kW export limit". R3 `[S]` Must support two-way (time-based) tariff export limitation and negative feed-in limitation. R4 `[S]` Must perform "DER status/capability/settings reporting". R5 `[S]` Must implement "DER control scheduling and default DER control fallback". R6 `[S]` Must apply software-based limit ramping via `setGradW` or `rampTms`. R7 `[S]` Licence MIT. R8 `[S]` Certified by SA Power Networks on 1/10/2024.
Rel: 2030.5 client ↔ scheduler (passes DERControl events with start/duration; the invariant is a single authoritative active control); scheduler ↔ Modbus writer (passes a watt limit and ramp rate); meter reader ↔ scheduler (passes site export measurement, closing the loop).
Con: on loss of the utility server the **default DER control fallback** governs — the system must fail to a safe limit, not to unlimited export `[S]`; certification binds behaviour to one DNSP's acceptance `[I]`.
AC: AC-S1 a scheduled DERControl is applied within its window and released at its end. AC-S2 loss of server contact triggers the default control. AC-S3 ramping honours `setGradW`/`rampTms`. AC-P1 *(requires acceptance)* measured site export never exceeds the active limit by more than a stated tolerance over a 5-minute window.
Asm/Miss/Unres: tolerance and measurement interval are unspecified; certification scope beyond SA Power Networks unknown.

### ISIC E — Water supply; sewerage, waste management

**E-02 · Primary domain: urban drainage and sewer modelling (FORD 2)**
Ben: municipal stormwater and combined-sewer utilities planning capacity and overflow control. Prob: runoff quantity and quality must be predicted for both single storms and long continuous records before irreversible capital works are committed. Ctx: regulatory consent conditions; public-domain expectations.
Src: US EPA Office of Research and Development, CESER, Water Infrastructure Division (Cincinnati), "Stormwater-Management-Model", https://github.com/USEPA/Stormwater-Management-Model, README, READ 2026-09-09.
Deliv `[S]`: a dynamic hydrology–hydraulic water-quality simulation engine in C, released in the public domain, with a `Build.md`.
In `[S]`: rainfall series, catchment and conveyance network descriptions; stormwater, wastewater and combined-sewer system definitions.
R1 `[S]` Must perform "single event or long-term (continuous) simulation of runoff quantity **and quality** from primarily urban areas". R2 `[S]` Must cover stormwater, wastewater and combined sewer collection systems. R3 `[S]` Source is C and "released in the Public Domain". R4 `[S]` EPA "has relinquished control of the information and no longer has responsibility to protect the integrity, confidentiality, or availability of the information."
Rel: hydrology module → hydraulics module (passes runoff hydrographs at inlets; the invariant is mass balance); hydraulics → quality module (passes flows and volumes; pollutant mass must be conserved).
Con: public-domain release means no warranty and no upstream integrity guarantee — downstream users must pin and verify their own copy `[I]`.
AC: AC-S1 both a single-event and a continuous run complete on the same network. AC-S2 the build documented in `Build.md` succeeds. AC-P1 *(requires acceptance)* continuity error for flow and for each pollutant stays under a stated percentage.
Asm/Miss/Unres: version number not stated on the page; regression-test expectations not described.

**E-01 · Primary domain: drinking-water distribution (FORD 2)**
Ben: water utilities designing, operating and security-testing pressurised distribution networks. Prob: pressure, flow, water age and contaminant transport cannot be observed everywhere, so they must be simulated. Ctx: public health consequences; contamination scenarios are high-stakes and time-bounded.
Src: US EPA with the OpenWaterAnalytics community, "EPANET2.2", https://github.com/USEPA/EPANET2.2, README, READ 2026-09-09.
Deliv `[S]`: an extended-period hydraulic and water-quality solver for pressurised pipe networks. In `[S]`: network of pipes, junctions, pumps, valves, storage tanks and reservoirs.
R1 `[S]` Must "perform extended-period simulation of the hydraulic and water quality behavior within drinking water distribution systems". R2 `[S]` Must track flow per pipe, pressure per node, tank water height, a chemical concentration, water age, and source tracing over the simulation period. R3 `[S]` Version 2.2.0 is the latest official EPA release; the previous version 2.00.12 dates from 2008. R4 `[S]` Licence MIT. R5 `[S]` Provided "on an 'as is' basis"; users assume responsibility for use.
Rel: hydraulic solver → quality solver (passes the flow field per timestep; the invariant is that quality transport uses the same hydraulic timestep and network topology); tank model ↔ hydraulic solver (passes storage levels across periods, carrying state between timesteps).
Con: R5 forbids relying on the model as sole evidence for a public-health decision `[I]`; a 14-year gap between R3's versions means legacy inputs must keep loading `[I]`.
AC: AC-S1 an extended-period run reports flow, pressure, tank level, concentration, age and source trace. AC-S2 a 2.00.12-era input file runs under 2.2.0. AC-P1 *(requires acceptance)* hydraulic results for a benchmark network match published values within a stated tolerance.
Asm/Miss/Unres: no stated numeric tolerance; interaction with SWMM (E-02) not defined by either source.

### ISIC F — Construction

**F-04 · Primary domain: BIM-to-BEM information delivery (FORD 2)**
Ben: energy modellers who receive IFC models from designers and must run building energy simulation. Prob: models arrive missing the thermal properties simulation needs, and the gap is discovered only after handover. Ctx: divided authority — the designer authors, the analyst consumes; rework is costly and late.
Src: LabEEE (UFSC), "IDS_BimBem — Information Delivery Specification (IDS) to validation IFC models in BIM-BEM process", https://github.com/labeee/IDS_BimBem, README, READ 2026-09-09.
Deliv `[S]`: an IDS file (`IDS_BIM-BEM.ids`) constructed in XML, based on buildingSMART's Information Delivery Specification. In `[S]`: IFC models; the buildingSMART IDS schema.
R1 `[S]` The specification must validate IFC models for the BIM-BEM process. R2 `[S]` It must address "the thermal characteristics of building materials in energy analysis models". R3 `[S]` It must be XML and conform to buildingSMART IDS. R4 `[S]` Licence MIT.
Rel: IDS file ↔ IFC model (passes a pass/fail per specification clause; the invariant is that both sides agree on the IFC schema version); IDS file ↔ any IDS-conformant checker (passes the ruleset, decoupling the requirement from the tool).
Con: the checker is not shipped — conformance depends on third-party IDS support `[S]/[I]`.
AC: AC-S1 the `.ids` file is accepted by an IDS-conformant validator. AC-S2 a model lacking material thermal properties fails. AC-P1 *(requires acceptance)* a passing model imports into an energy engine without manual property entry.
Asm/Miss/Unres: **which IFC entities and property sets are checked is not stated in the README** — it lives inside the `.ids` file, which I did not read. No IFC version is named.

**F-03 · Primary domain: construction data-dictionary quality assurance (FORD 2)**
Ben: organisations publishing classification dictionaries into bSDD, and the BIM software and clients relying on them. Prob: uncontrolled dictionaries produce ambiguous, circular or IFC-colliding definitions that silently corrupt downstream model checking. Ctx: mixed authority — automated gates, community reports, a paid expert review; a "verified badge" is the visible outcome.
Src: buildingSMART International, "bSDD verification procedure", https://github.com/buildingSMART/bSDD/blob/master/Documentation/bSDD%20verification%20procedure.md, sections *Organisation review / Import validation / User (reactive) review / Detailed verification*, and requirement groups DCT, GEN, CLS, PRP, CPR, REL, READ 2026-09-09.
Deliv `[S]`: four verification types — Organisation review (on registration, bSI team, free), Import validation (on upload, automated, free), User/reactive review (on request, community + bSI team, free), Detailed verification (on owner request, bSI team or delegates, **paid**) — plus a verified badge.
In `[S]`: an uploaded dictionary; ISO 704:2022 as the definitional authority; IFC as the mapping target.
R1 `[S]` DCT-01 dictionary should be "Active". R2 `[S]` DCT-02 dictionary name must not be misleading. R3 `[S]` GEN-01 mandatory fields: `OrganizationCode`, `DictionaryCode`, `DictionaryName`, `DictionaryVersion`, `LanguageIsoCode`. R4 `[S]` GEN-02 an English version of all relevant content. R5 `[S]` GEN-03 translations "precise and faithful to the original". R6 `[S]` GEN-05 consistent naming conventions. R7 `[S]` GEN-08 contact e-mail must be a professional, domain-specific address. R8 `[S]` GEN-09/10/11 definitions must avoid circularity, be accurate, and not be negative — per ISO 704:2022. R9 `[S]` GEN-12 custom URIs must resolve to existing pages. R10 `[S]` CLS-01 classes mapped to IFC correctly, avoiding abstract classes and proxies. R11 `[S]` CLS-02 parent–child relations form a tree with no circular dependencies. R12 `[S]` CLS-05 the prefix `Ifc` (and lookalikes) is forbidden. R13 `[S]` PRP-01 numeric properties must specify `Dimension`, and "Unit must match the Dimension when present". R14 `[S]` PRP-02 do not recreate existing IFC properties. R15 `[S]` PRP-03 the prefix `PSET_` is reserved. R16 `[S]` PRP-04 data type ∈ {Boolean, Character, Integer, Real, String, Time}. R17 `[S]` PRP-05 one aspect per property. R18 `[S]` PRP-06/07 allowed values only for a defined, countable option set, each clear and distinct. R19 `[S]` CPR-01 referenced properties come from `Active` dictionaries. R20 `[S]` REL-01/02/03 relations avoid circularity and must be logically consistent and purposeful.
Rel: import validator → dictionary store (passes structural conformance; gate on every upload); reviewer ↔ owner (passes findings and a status change; the badge is issued only after status becomes Active); dictionary ↔ IFC (passes class and property mappings — the invariant is that bSDD never shadows IFC names).
Con: **no numeric thresholds exist** — verification is qualitative `[S]`; detailed verification price is "determined individually" by content volume, iterations and complexity `[S]`.
AC: AC-S1 upload is rejected if any GEN-01 field is absent. AC-S2 a dictionary with a class named `Ifc*` or a property named `PSET_*` fails. AC-S3 a numeric property without `Dimension`, or with a unit inconsistent with its dimension, fails. AC-S4 a cycle in class parentage or in relations fails. AC-S5 the verified badge is granted only after status → Active. AC-P1 *(requires acceptance)* a machine-checkable subset (GEN-01, PRP-01, PRP-03, PRP-04, CLS-02, CLS-05, REL-01) runs in under a stated time per 10,000 concepts.
Asm/Miss/Unres: which requirements are automated versus human-judged is not fully mapped; appeal route after a failed detailed verification is unstated.

### ISIC G — Wholesale and retail trade; repair of motor vehicles

**G-01 · Primary domain: supply-chain event capture (FORD 2)**
Ben: trading partners recording and sharing what happened to physical objects across company boundaries. Prob: each party knows only its own leg; recalls, provenance claims and border controls need a shared event vocabulary. Ctx: competing firms, asymmetric visibility, legal consequence.
Src: GS1 AISBL, "EPCglobal-epcis-2_0.xsd", https://github.com/gs1/EPCIS/blob/master/XSD/EPCglobal-epcis-2_0.xsd, XML schema header and type definitions; specification title "EPC INFORMATION SERVICE (EPCIS) Version 2.0"; copyright "(C) 2006-2022 GS1 AISBL, All Rights Reserved", READ 2026-09-09.
Deliv `[S]`: a normative XSD in namespace `urn:epcglobal:epcis:xsd:2`, with Standard Business Document Header and EPCglobal namespaces.
In `[S]`: EPCs and quantity data; business step and disposition vocabularies (CBV).
R1 `[S]` Every event carries `eventTime`, `eventTimeZoneOffset` and `action`. R2 `[S]` ObjectEvent captures an event pertaining to one or more objects identified by EPCs. R3 `[S]` AggregationEvent applies to objects with a physical association. R4 `[S]` TransformationEvent captures inputs consumed and outputs produced. R5 `[S]` TransactionEvent describes association/disassociation of objects to business transactions and requires `bizTransactionList`. R6 `[S]` AssociationEvent covers associations "more permanent or persistent than a physical aggregation" and requires `parentID`. R7 `[S]` Dimensions: *what* = EPC lists and quantities; *when* = `eventTime`, `recordTime`; *where* = `readPoint`, `bizLocation`; *why* = `bizStep`, `disposition`.
Rel: schema ↔ CBV (passes controlled vocabulary values for `bizStep`/`disposition`; the invariant is that vocabulary URIs resolve identically for all partners); schema ↔ SBDH (passes routing/envelope metadata).
Con: `eventTimeZoneOffset` alongside `eventTime` means local time is preserved, not just an instant — consumers must not normalise it away `[I]`; the 2006–2022 copyright span signals long backward-compatibility obligations `[I]`.
AC: AC-S1 an event missing `eventTime`, `eventTimeZoneOffset` or `action` fails validation. AC-S2 a TransactionEvent without `bizTransactionList`, or an AssociationEvent without `parentID`, fails. AC-P1 *(requires acceptance)* the same real-world event expressed by two partners produces the same canonical event hash.
Asm/Miss/Unres: **the returned summary claimed "six core event types" but enumerated five — unresolved.** Cardinality of optional elements not captured.

**G-02 · Primary domain: supply-chain visibility tooling (FORD 2)**
Ben: developers who must stand up EPCIS capture and query without building from the XSD. Prob: standards conformance, format plurality (XML/JSON/JSON-LD) and version migration are each substantial engineering efforts. Ctx: regulatory deadlines (DPP, EUDR, CSRD) create time pressure.
Src: OpenEPCIS (benelog), organization page, https://github.com/openepcis, READ 2026-09-09.
Deliv `[S]`: `epcis-repository-ce` (scalable EPCIS 2.0 repository), `openepcis-document-converter`, `openepcis-event-hash-generator`, `epcis-testdata-generator`.
In `[S]`: GS1 EPCIS 2.0 and CBV 2.0; existing EPCIS 1.2 documents.
R1 `[S]` "100% GS1 EPCIS 2.0 and CBV 2.0 conformant, with backward compatibility for the older EPCIS 1.2". R2 `[S]` Capture and query of events in JSON, JSON-LD and XML. R3 `[S]` Streaming conversion for large event documents. R4 `[S]` Canonical event hash generation. R5 `[S]` Licence Apache 2.0. R6 `[S]` Declared use cases: traceability and visibility; DPP, EUDR and CSRD readiness; provenance and anti-counterfeiting; recall management; cold-chain monitoring.
Rel: converter ↔ repository (passes normalised events; the invariant is semantic equivalence across XML/JSON-LD); hash generator ↔ repository (passes a canonical hash used as a de-duplication and integrity key); test-data generator ↔ everything (passes synthetic events for conformance runs).
Con: R1's conformance claim is only meaningful against G-01's normative schema — the two must be version-locked `[I]`; streaming (R3) forbids whole-document buffering `[I]`.
AC: AC-S1 an EPCIS 1.2 document converts and captures without loss. AC-S2 XML and JSON-LD renderings of one event yield the same hash. AC-P1 *(requires acceptance)* a document larger than available memory converts successfully.
Asm/Miss/Unres: conformance is self-declared; no third-party certification is cited. **Cluster note: G-01 and G-02 share cluster CL-EPCIS — origins are not fully independent.**

### ISIC H — Transportation and storage

**H-04 · Primary domain: container shipping event visibility (FORD 2)**
Ben: shippers and forwarders needing consistent milestone events across carriers. Prob: each carrier exposes different event semantics, so track-and-trace is rebuilt per carrier. Ctx: many parties, commercial confidentiality between them.
Src: DCSA, "DCSA-TNT — DCSA Track & Trace API" reference implementation, https://github.com/dcsaorg/DCSA-TNT and `/blob/master/README.md`, READ-thin 2026-09-09.
Deliv `[S]`: a reference implementation composed of `tnt-application`, `tnt-domain`, `tnt-persistence`, `tnt-service`, `tnt-transfer-obj`, built on DCSA-Core and DCSA-Event-Core.
In `[S]`: Maven/Spring Boot build; PostgreSQL; Docker Compose; port 9090; health endpoint `/v1/actuator/health`.
R1 `[S]` The implementation builds with `mvn package` and runs on port 9090. R2 `[S]` Persistence is PostgreSQL. R3 `[S]` **"This reference implementation does not do any authentication/authorization and should not be used in production as is."** R4 `[S]` Without access control it "would expose data for all parties to all other parties" regardless of entitlement.
Rel: `tnt-domain` ↔ `tnt-persistence` (passes event entities); `tnt-service` ↔ `tnt-application` (passes API responses); DCSA-Event-Core ↔ TNT (passes shared event definitions — the invariant is a common event vocabulary across DCSA standards).
Con: R3/R4 make party-scoped authorisation a mandatory external addition before any real deployment `[S]`.
AC: AC-S1 the health endpoint returns healthy after `mvn package` and startup. AC-S2 a deployment without an authorisation layer is rejected in review. AC-P1 *(requires acceptance)* a party querying events sees only shipments it is entitled to.
Asm/Miss/Unres: **API version, event type list, required fields and subscription/notification behaviour are absent from the read text** — a recorded gap for this sampled case.

**H-01 · Primary domain: municipal mobility regulation (FORD 5)**
Ben: city transport agencies regulating shared vehicles in the public right of way, and the operators who must comply. Prob: policies communicated as PDFs cannot be machine-checked, so compliance is disputed after the fact. Ctx: divided authority (agency writes, operator executes); changing evidence; time-boxed events and emergencies.
Src: Open Mobility Foundation, "Mobility Data Specification — Policy", https://github.com/openmobilityfoundation/mobility-data-specification/tree/main/policy, sections on policies, rules, geographies, rule types, units, and publication timing, READ 2026-09-09.
Deliv `[S]`: a Policy API publishing machine-readable municipal policies — "vehicle deployment caps, speed limits, data sharing requirements, events, and emergencies".
In `[S]`: GeoJSON `Feature` objects as geographies; agency-issued policy documents.
R1 `[S]` A policy requires name, unique ID, mode identifier, description, start date, end date and an array of rules. R2 `[S]` A rule requires name, unique ID, rule type, applicable geographies and vehicle states. R3 `[S]` Rule types are exactly: `count`, `time`, `distance`, `speed`, `user`; `user` is informational and non-enforceable. R4 `[S]` Units are `seconds|minutes|hours|days`, `km|miles`, `mph|kph`, `devices`. R5 `[S]` "Geographical data shall be represented as GeoJSON `Feature` objects." R6 `[S]` "In order to give providers sufficient time to poll, `start_date` must be at least 20 minutes after `published_date`." R7 `[S]` Updates are made by publishing a new policy that lists superseded IDs in `prev_policies`; a policy is revoked by publishing empty rules with a predecessor reference. R8 `[S]` "In most cases, the Policy endpoints should be made public." R9 `[S]` Agencies must handle version-specific requests; versioned URLs are encouraged.
Rel: policy ↔ geography (passes a geography ID; the invariant is that the referenced geography exists and is stable for the policy's life); policy ↔ `prev_policies` (passes supersession lineage — this is the audit trail); agency publication → provider poll (passes the ruleset; R6 is the timing contract between them).
Con: policies are append-only in effect — history must remain resolvable through `prev_policies` `[I]`; public endpoints mean rules are also visible to the public and press `[S]/[I]`.
AC: AC-S1 a policy whose `start_date` is under 20 minutes after `published_date` is rejected. AC-S2 every rule's geography resolves to a GeoJSON Feature. AC-S3 a superseded policy remains retrievable and names its successor. AC-S4 a `user` rule is never used to compute a violation. AC-P1 *(requires acceptance)* an operator polling once every 15 minutes never misses a policy activation.
Asm/Miss/Unres: enforcement/penalty semantics are outside the spec; conflict resolution between overlapping geographies is not described in the read text.

### ISIC I — Accommodation and food service

**I-01 · Primary domain: travel and hospitality messaging (FORD 5)**
Ben: hotels, airlines, distributors and their integrators exchanging availability, rate and booking messages. Prob: bilateral message formats multiply integration cost across a fragmented industry. Ctx: long-lived integrations; annual release trains.
Src: OpenTravel Alliance, "OpenTravel-Specifications", https://github.com/OpenTravel/OpenTravel-Specifications, repository root and `OTA2.0` directory listing, READ-thin 2026-09-09.
Deliv `[S]`: published specification folders `CodeLists`, `OTA1.0`, `OTA2.0`; within OTA2.0, dated object suites — `OTA2.0_2016A_ObjectSuite`, `2017A`, `2017B`, `2018A`, `2020A`.
In `[S]`: code lists shared across message versions.
R1 `[S]` Specifications are versioned as dated "Object Suites" (year + letter). R2 `[S]` Two generations coexist (OTA 1.0 and OTA 2.0). R3 `[S]` Code lists are maintained separately from object suites.
Rel: code lists ↔ object suites (passes enumerated values; the invariant is that a code's meaning is stable across suites); suite N ↔ suite N+1 (passes model continuity for implementers upgrading).
Con: parallel generations imply implementers must state which suite they support `[I]`.
AC: AC-S1 each object suite is retrievable as a self-contained release. AC-P1 *(requires acceptance)* a consumer built on `2018A` continues to parse `2020A` messages for unchanged objects.
Asm/Miss/Unres: **README, governance, licence, model file formats and message-type inventory were not retrievable** (page load error on the directory view). Everything about conformance is unresolved for this case.

**I-04 · Primary domain: public nutrient data services (FORD 3)**
Ben: developers of menu, diet and food-labelling applications; ultimately consumers reading nutrition information. Prob: the legacy nutrient API forced one-food-at-a-time retrieval and offered no paged browsing, so applications over-fetched or cached stale data. Ctx: a government data service with a migration deadline.
Src: USDA FoodData Central, issue "New Food Data Central API Released", https://github.com/USDA/USDA-APIs/issues/92, READ 2026-09-09.
Deliv `[S]`: a new FDC API with endpoints for multiple foods in **full or abridged** formats, "list" endpoints for paged browsing, and the ability to request one or more nutrients; an OpenAPI v3 specification published in HTML, JSON and YAML.
In `[S]`: an API key (a `DEMO_KEY` appears in the specification URLs); the legacy NDB API.
R1 `[S]` Must support requesting multiple foods in one call. R2 `[S]` Must offer both full and abridged response formats. R3 `[S]` Must provide paged browsing of food lists. R4 `[S]` Must allow requesting one or more specific nutrients. R5 `[S]` Must publish an OpenAPI v3 specification in HTML, JSON and YAML. R6 `[S]` Access is key-based. R7 `[S]` "The discontinuation date for the NDB API has been extended to May 31, 2020."
Rel: OpenAPI document ↔ client generators (passes the interface contract); legacy NDB ↔ FDC (passes a migration window — R7 is the shared deadline); API key issuance ↔ rate policy (passes identity for quota).
Con: a dated cut-over means every dependent product inherits a hard migration date `[S]`.
AC: AC-S1 one request returns several foods in abridged form. AC-S2 a list endpoint pages deterministically. AC-S3 the three OpenAPI renderings describe the same interface. AC-P1 *(requires acceptance)* an abridged response is a strict subset of the full response for the same food.
Asm/Miss/Unres: rate limits, versioning policy and licence are not stated in the issue.

### ISIC J — Information and communication

**J-06 · Primary domain: public-sector standards selection (FORD 5)**
Ben: users of government services, via the departments that must choose standards; and suppliers who need to know what will be required. Prob: standards get adopted by habit or vendor pressure rather than by evidence of a user problem. Ctx: divided authority across departments; a board with final say; open participation.
Src: UK Central Digital and Data Office, "open-standards — Collaboration space for discussing and exploring technical and data standards", https://github.com/co-cddo/open-standards, README describing the four stages, READ 2026-09-09.
Deliv `[S]`: a four-stage public process — Suggestion, Response, Proposal, Solution — operated as issues in the open, plus Open Standards Board decisions.
In `[S]`: public suggestions; expert panels; challenge owners; the external guide "How open standards are selected"; Board Terms of Reference.
R1 `[S]` Stage 1 collects areas "where users of government services face problems that might be solved by open standards". R2 `[S]` Stage 2 converts selected suggestions into challenges with appointed leaders, then invites recommended standards or approaches. R3 `[S]` Stage 3: the challenge owner and community assess responses to "identify one that they will submit to the Open Standards Board for approval". R4 `[S]` Stage 4: the Board decides whether the approach "should be adopted across government". R5 `[S]` The Board comprises appointed experts from government, non-profits, academia and industry and must follow its Terms of Reference.
Rel: suggestion → challenge (passes a stated user problem — the invariant is that the problem statement survives unchanged into the challenge); challenge → proposal (passes candidate standards with evidence); proposal → Board (passes a single recommendation; authority transfers here).
Con: the process is public throughout, so commercially sensitive input cannot enter it `[I]`; only one proposal per challenge reaches the Board `[S]`.
AC: AC-S1 every adopted standard traces to a recorded user problem. AC-S2 each stage transition is visible in the public record. AC-P1 *(requires acceptance)* median elapsed time per stage is published.
Asm/Miss/Unres: **no timing requirements are stated**; evidence requirements live in an external guide I could not fetch.

**J-04 · Primary domain: agent/tool protocol authorization (FORD 2)**
Ben: developers exposing tools and data to AI clients over a protocol, and the users whose third-party credentials are at stake. Prob: the protocol shipped without authentication, yet most useful integrations need it, and clients must not be handed users' third-party credentials. Ctx: mixed local/remote deployment; multi-tenant remote servers; headless flows; irreversible credential exposure if done wrongly.
Src: Model Context Protocol, discussion "Authentication", https://github.com/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/discussions/64, READ 2026-09-09.
Deliv `[S]`: an Authorization section in the specification, with OAuth 2.0 as the initial standard, "broadly pluggable" for alternatives.
In `[S]`: OAuth 2.0; RFC 8693 token exchange; W3C DIDs (proposed); stdio and SSE transports.
R1 `[S]` Two distinct layers must be separated: client→server, and server→third-party API on the user's behalf. R2 `[S]` The design must respect that "users may trust servers with their credentials but not clients". R3 `[S]` A sketched flow: an `/install` endpoint initiates OAuth 2.0; on completion the server generates a cryptographically secure session ID; the client connects to `/sse` carrying that session ID; the server refreshes tokens transparently. R4 `[S]` For local servers, authentication is "essentially a solved problem (just bake credentials into the server implementation)". R5 `[S]` Remote servers must support multi-tenancy without exposing third-party credentials to untrusted clients. R6 `[S]` Headless flows must work without browser interaction. R7 `[S]` Long-lived refresh tokens (~6 months) require secure storage and rotation. R8 `[S]` The chosen mechanism must remain pluggable.
Rel: client ↔ server (passes a session identifier, never a third-party token — this is the security invariant); server ↔ third-party API (passes access tokens the client never sees); token store ↔ refresh loop (passes refresh tokens; carries long-lived state).
Con: a session ID in a query parameter is exposed to logs and referrers `[I]`; stdio transport cannot reach an external authorization server `[S]`.
AC: AC-S1 a client can complete a call without ever holding a third-party credential. AC-S2 two tenants on one remote server cannot read each other's tokens. AC-S3 a headless client authenticates without a browser. AC-P1 *(requires acceptance)* refresh-token rotation is verified by a test that invalidates the prior token.
Asm/Miss/Unres: DID-based and RFC 8693 options were discussed but not decided; token-lifetime figures are participant estimates, not normative.

### ISIC K — Financial and insurance activities

**K-06 · Primary domain: government standards cataloguing for payments (FORD 5)**
Ben: UK government technologists choosing a message standard for financial communications. Prob: without a curated catalogue, each department re-evaluates the same standards and reaches different answers. Ctx: a proprietary standard being catalogued by a public body; periodic review.
Src: UK GDS Data Standards Authority, catalogue entry "Universal financial industry message scheme (ISO20022)", https://github.com/alphagov/data-standards-authority/blob/main/content/standards/iso20022/index.html.md, READ 2026-09-09.
Deliv `[S]`: a catalogue entry carrying name, contraction, purpose, specification URL, domain, maintainer, licence and review date.
In `[S]`: ISO as maintainer; specification at iso20022.org message definitions; a payments-filtered view of that portal.
R1 `[S]` Purpose: ISO 20022 is a multi-part International Standard for financial communications providing "a common platform for the development of messages" via a modelling methodology, a central dictionary of business items, and XML/ASN.1 design rules. R2 `[S]` Domain: payments and domain-specific financial services. R3 `[S]` Maintainer: ISO. R4 `[S]` Licence: **Proprietary**. R5 `[S]` Review date recorded as 2021-02-02.
Rel: catalogue entry ↔ external specification (passes a citable URL; the invariant is that the URL keeps resolving to the same standard); entry ↔ review cycle (passes a date that triggers re-assessment).
Con: R4 means adoption carries licensing obligations the catalogue itself does not resolve `[I]`; a 2021 review date on a 2026 catalogue is stale `[I]`.
AC: AC-S1 the entry states maintainer, licence and review date. AC-P1 *(requires acceptance)* entries past their review date are visibly flagged.
Asm/Miss/Unres: **no explicit UK-government recommendation status and no related-standards list appear in the read text** — endorsement level is unresolved.

**K-05 · Primary domain: financial message tooling (FORD 2)**
Ben: engineers building payment integrations who need to parse, produce and validate ISO 20022 messages. Prob: the standard is large and evolving; a single library cannot track all of it. Ctx: an archived, read-only project — a maintenance-cessation case.
Src: Moov, "iso20022 — Enabling ISO 20022 standards in the Go programming language", https://github.com/moov-io/iso20022, README, READ 2026-09-09.
Deliv `[S]`: a Go library reading and writing ISO 20022 messages, plus an HTTP API with `POST /convert`, `GET /health`, `POST /print`, `POST /validator`.
In `[S]`: XML and JSON message forms; the payments business-domain catalogue.
R1 `[S]` Must create, parse and validate ISO 20022 messages used in financial networks. R2 `[S]` Must support XML and JSON. R3 `[S]` Scope is the payments business-domain catalogue. R4 `[S]` Licence Apache 2.0. R5 `[S]` Requires Go modules; Go 1.14 or greater suggested. R6 `[S]` **Archived by the owner on Jan 12, 2023 and now read-only**; superseded by targeted repositories such as `rtp20022`. R7 `[S]` Docker support and the CLI are "under construction".
Rel: parser ↔ validator (passes a parsed message tree; the invariant is that validation applies to the same schema version the parser used); HTTP handlers ↔ library (passes message payloads).
Con: R6 makes this a dependency with no security-fix path — a supply-chain constraint on anything embedding it `[I]`.
AC: AC-S1 a valid message survives XML→JSON→XML conversion. AC-S2 an invalid message is rejected by `/validator`. AC-P1 *(requires acceptance)* consumers pin the archived commit and record an exit plan to `rtp20022`.
Asm/Miss/Unres: which specific message sets are implemented is not enumerated; no statement about the proprietary licensing of the underlying ISO schemas.

### ISIC L — Real estate activities

**L-03 · Primary domain: building energy audit data exchange (FORD 2)**
Ben: building owners, auditors and programme administrators handling commercial energy audits at scale. Prob: audits arrive as bespoke spreadsheets and PDFs, so measures cannot be tracked, compared, or fed into energy models without re-keying. Ctx: repeated audits over a building's life; multiple software tools; programme reporting duties.
Src: BuildingSync (US DOE national-laboratory effort), "BuildingSync® Schema", https://github.com/BuildingSync/schema, README, READ 2026-09-09.
Deliv `[S]`: a building-data exchange schema standardising "(a) reporting audits in an electronic format, (b) tracking proposed, implemented, and discarded energy conservation measures, and (c) storing building characteristics (at multiple levels) for audits, benchmarking, and building energy analysis"; a Use Case TestSuite; a Use Case Validator.
In `[S]`: ASHRAE Level 1 and partial Level 2 audit content; BEDES terms; CSV use-case templates converted into Schematron.
R1 `[S]` Must support reporting audits electronically. R2 `[S]` Must track measures in three states — proposed, implemented, discarded. R3 `[S]` Must store building characteristics at multiple levels. R4 `[S]` Use cases are defined via CSV templates converted into Schematron documents. R5 `[S]` A validator must let users verify an instance against a specific use case. R6 `[S]` Schema elements connect to BEDES. R7 `[S]` "Example files must pass validation for any schema updates."
Rel: schema ↔ use-case Schematron (passes constraints narrower than the schema — the invariant is that a use case never contradicts the base schema); BuildingSync instance → OpenStudio/EnergyPlus translator (passes building geometry and systems for a Level 1/partial Level 2 audit); schema ↔ BEDES (passes term definitions, keeping vocabulary consistent across programmes).
Con: one schema must serve audit reporting, measure tracking and modelling simultaneously — a use-case mechanism is the only way to keep it both broad and checkable `[I]`; R7 makes examples part of the release gate `[S]`.
AC: AC-S1 every schema update leaves all example files valid. AC-S2 an instance can be validated against a named use case and fails when a use-case-required element is absent. AC-P1 *(requires acceptance)* a Level 1 audit instance translates to an OpenStudio model without manual completion.
Asm/Miss/Unres: licence terms not stated in the read text; current version number not given (development on `develop-v2`); ASHRAE level definitions are external.

**L-01 · Primary domain: real-estate data interchange (FORD 5)**
Ben: MLSs, brokerages and adjacent service providers (tour, assessment, showing, photography) exchanging listing data. Prob: a decentralised landscape with local extensions makes payloads unpredictable; consumers cannot tell standard fields from local ones. Ctx: ratified standard with a version floor; many independent publishers.
Src: RESO, "RESO Common Format" proposal, https://github.com/RESOStandards/transport/blob/main/proposals/reso-common-format.md, sections on context, single- vs multi-valued payloads and compliance, **RATIFIED November 2023**, requires Data Dictionary 1.7+, READ 2026-09-09.
Deliv `[S]`: a normative JSON representation corresponding to the RESO Data Dictionary, using RESO's registered URN namespace.
In `[S]`: RESO Data Dictionary 1.7 or later; versioned RESO reference metadata; OData conventions.
R1 `[S]` Every payload requires the `@reso.context` field. R2 `[S]` Context format: `urn:reso:metadata:{version}:resource:{resource-name}`, e.g. `urn:reso:metadata:1.7:resource:property`. R3 `[S]` Single-valued payloads represent one record at the top level. R4 `[S]` Multi-valued payloads represent a collection of zero or more records wrapped in a `value` array. R5 `[S]` Clients "should be prepared to check for the `value` property to determine whether to interpret the payload as single- or multi-valued". R6 `[S]` Consumers must extract standard elements "using versioned RESO reference metadata", because payloads may carry local elements of unknown schema. R7 `[S]` Both human-friendly and OData machine-friendly enumeration values are supported. R8 `[S]` Requires Data Dictionary 1.7+.
Rel: payload ↔ reference metadata (passes the version in `@reso.context` — the invariant is that the cited version's metadata governs interpretation); publisher ↔ consumer (passes standard fields plus opaque local fields; the contract is that local fields are ignorable, not fatal).
Con: a zero-length collection is still a valid multi-valued payload, so absence of `value` — not emptiness — signals single-valued `[S]/[I]`; enumeration duality means two spellings must map to one meaning `[I]`.
AC: AC-S1 a payload lacking `@reso.context` is non-conformant. AC-S2 a consumer correctly distinguishes single- from multi-valued by presence of `value`. AC-S3 unknown local fields do not cause rejection. AC-P1 *(requires acceptance)* human-friendly and OData enumeration renderings of one record compare equal after normalisation.
Asm/Miss/Unres: behaviour when `@reso.context` cites an unsupported version is unspecified in the read text.

### ISIC M — Professional, scientific and technical activities

**M-02 · Primary domain: research metadata schema documentation (FORD 1)**
Ben: repository operators and DOI registrants who must describe research outputs consistently; downstream, anyone discovering or citing that work. Prob: schema versions accumulate and implementers need to know which properties exist in which version. Ctx: long-lived identifiers; multiple live versions in production simultaneously.
Src: DataCite, "schema-docs", https://github.com/datacite/schema-docs, README and repository structure, READ 2026-09-09.
Deliv `[S]`: documentation for the active metadata schema versions, built and published via ReadTheDocs from a `docs` folder.
In `[S]`: schema versions **4.7 (released 3 March 2026), 4.6 (released 5 December 2024), 4.5 (released 22 January 2024)**; all versions at schema.datacite.org.
R1 `[S]` Documentation must cover the three active versions with their release dates. R2 `[S]` Publication is via ReadTheDocs, configured by `.readthedocs.yaml`. R3 `[S]` "All schema versions are available at https://schema.datacite.org/".
Rel: `docs` source ↔ ReadTheDocs build (passes versioned documentation sets; the invariant is one documentation branch per schema version); documentation ↔ schema files at schema.datacite.org (passes property definitions — these must not diverge).
Con: three concurrently supported versions means every property statement must be version-qualified `[I]`.
AC: AC-S1 each of 4.5, 4.6 and 4.7 has retrievable documentation carrying its release date. AC-P1 *(requires acceptance)* each documented property is reconciled against the corresponding XSD for its version.
Asm/Miss/Unres: **the mandatory/recommended/optional classification of properties is not on the repository page** — it lives inside the docs, which I did not read.

**X-02 · Primary domain: geospatial array-data conventions (FORD 1)**
Ben: earth-observation data providers and analysts using Zarr for large gridded datasets; the wider community that already speaks CF. Prob: two conventions can encode the same coordinate and projection information differently, so tools disagree about what a file means. Ctx: competing established conventions, an active standards working group, an unresolved vote.
Src: Zarr developers, GeoZarr specification issue "CF Convention Alignment", https://github.com/zarr-developers/geozarr-spec/issues/120, READ 2026-09-09.
Deliv `[S]`: "a proposal for registering CF metadata via GeoZarr's convention mechanism"; a straw-man proposal for community review.
In `[S]`: the CF (Climate and Forecast) conventions; GeoZarr's `zarr_conventions` registration mechanism with UUIDs; GeoZarr's `spatial:` and `proj:` conventions.
R1 `[S]` The work must align GeoZarr with CF by registering CF metadata through GeoZarr's convention mechanism. R2 `[S]` It must decide whether CF-on-Zarr is registered as a GeoZarr convention with its own UUID or stays independent. R3 `[S]` It must answer "How do CF coordinate variables relate to the `spatial:` convention?" R4 `[S]` It must answer "How do CF grid_mapping attributes relate to the `proj:` convention?" R5 `[S]` It must define "the minimal CF subset that GeoZarr should acknowledge or require". R6 `[S]` It must specify "how we handle cases where CF and GeoZarr encode the same information differently". R7 `[S]` Acceptance includes consultation with CF leadership and documented recommendations for the SWG vote.
Rel: CF metadata ↔ GeoZarr conventions registry (passes a convention identity/UUID; the invariant is that a dataset declares which conventions govern it); `grid_mapping` ↔ `proj:` (both carry CRS — one must be authoritative or a reconciliation rule must exist).
Con: conflicting encodings of the same fact are the central hazard — a precedence rule is unavoidable `[I]`; the decision is not the working group's alone (CF leadership must be consulted) `[S]`.
AC: AC-S1 a written recommendation exists for each of the five questions. AC-S2 CF leadership has been consulted before the SWG vote. AC-P1 *(requires acceptance)* a test dataset carrying both CF and GeoZarr encodings is read identically by two independent implementations.
Asm/Miss/Unres: **all five questions were open at the time of reading** — no decisions recorded.

### ISIC N — Administrative and support service activities

**N-01 · Primary domain: building metadata for facility operations (FORD 2)**
Ben: facility operators and analytics vendors deploying software across many buildings. Prob: every building names its points differently, so applications must be re-engineered per site. Ctx: long-lived assets, incremental extension, tooling that expects stability.
Src: Brick Consortium, "Brick — Uniform metadata schema for buildings", https://github.com/BrickSchema/Brick, README, READ 2026-09-09.
Deliv `[S]`: an RDF class hierarchy of building subsystems, entities and equipment; "a minimal, principled set of relationships for connecting these entities together into a directed graph representing a building"; a method of encapsulation for composing complex components from lower-level ones; a built `Brick.ttl`.
In `[S]`: RDF; `generate_brick.py` / `make`; semantic versioning.
R1 `[S]` Brick must provide an RDF class hierarchy for building subsystems and their equipment. R2 `[S]` It must provide a *minimal* relationship set forming a directed graph. R3 `[S]` It must support encapsulation/composition of components. R4 `[S]` Versioning is major.minor.patch; a minor release is targeted "roughly every 6 months". R5 `[S]` Minor releases carry "largely backwards-compatible extensions and new features" and are developed in separate branches; patch releases are smaller backwards-compatible changes merged directly to master. R6 `[S]` "Every evening, a nightly build is produced containing the latest commits." R7 `[S]` "The Brick.ttl file needs to be created using either make or python generate_brick.py" before running tests. R8 `[S]` Licence BSD-3-Clause.
Rel: source ontology modules → `generate_brick.py` → `Brick.ttl` (passes the compiled ontology — the invariant is that tests and consumers use a generated, not hand-edited, artefact); nightly build ↔ downstream tools (passes an unstable but current ontology).
Con: R2's minimality is a design constraint that must survive every extension `[S]/[I]`; R5 makes backwards compatibility the default promise, so removals need a major version `[I]`.
AC: AC-S1 tests fail if `Brick.ttl` has not been generated. AC-S2 a minor release does not break a consumer written against the prior minor. AC-P1 *(requires acceptance)* the nightly build is distinguishable from a release by version metadata inside the file.
Asm/Miss/Unres: whether SHACL shapes are normative is not stated in the read text.

**N-02 · Primary domain: public procurement transparency (FORD 5)**
Ben: citizens, auditors, suppliers and buying agencies who need comparable contracting data across jurisdictions. Prob: procurement data is published in incompatible national formats, defeating comparison and oversight. Ctx: many publishers, different legal regimes, a standard maintained in the open.
Src: Open Contracting Partnership, "standard — Documentation of the Open Contracting Data Standard (OCDS)", https://github.com/open-contracting/standard, repository README, READ-thin 2026-09-09.
Deliv `[S]`: documentation of OCDS, with `docs`, `schemas`, `scripts` and `tests` directories; the normative documentation is published at standard.open-contracting.org.
In `[S]`: the OCDS Standard Development Handbook; development on branch `1.2-dev`.
R1 `[S]` The standard's documentation must be readable at standard.open-contracting.org. R2 `[S]` The repository must carry schemas alongside documentation, with tests. R3 `[S]` Development follows the OCDS Standard Development Handbook. R4 `[S]` Active development targets version 1.2 (`1.2-dev`).
Rel: schemas ↔ documentation (passes field definitions; the invariant is that documented fields exist in the schema and vice versa); tests ↔ schemas (passes validation coverage); handbook ↔ contributors (passes process rules).
Con: `[I]` publishers in different jurisdictions must map local processes onto one release/record model, so extensibility is mandatory (see N-04).
AC: AC-S1 documentation builds from the repository and matches the published site. AC-S2 schema tests pass on the development branch. AC-P1 *(requires acceptance)* every documented field is exercised by at least one test fixture.
Asm/Miss/Unres: **contracting-process stages, the release/record model, translation policy, governance and licence were not in the read text** — a recorded gap for this sampled case.

### ISIC O — Public administration and defence

**O-04 · Primary domain: citizen identity for government services (FORD 2)**
Ben: members of the public needing one account across participating agencies; the agencies relying on that assurance. Prob: per-agency accounts multiply credentials and identity-proofing cost, and weak proofing risks benefit fraud. Ctx: government scale, accessibility obligations, high-value irreversible transactions.
Src: GSA / 18F, "identity-idp — Login.gov Core App: Identity Provider (IdP)", https://github.com/18F/identity-idp, README and repository structure, READ-thin 2026-09-09.
Deliv `[S]`: the core code base and documentation for the identity-management system powering secure.login.gov; documentation at `docs/SECURITY.md`, `docs/backend.md`, `docs/frontend.md`.
In `[S]`: Rails application; SAML and OIDC (repository topics); dependency-audit tooling (`.bundler-audit.yml`, `.snyk`); Docker, Node (`.nvmrc`), Ruby (`.ruby-version`), Brewfile.
R1 `[S]` "Login.gov is the public's one account for government. Use one account and password for secure, private access to participating government agencies." R2 `[S]` The system must support relying-party integration over SAML and OIDC. R3 `[S]` A published security policy and vulnerability-management tooling must be maintained. R4 `[S]` Local development configuration is documented separately.
Rel: IdP ↔ relying party (passes assertions/tokens; the invariant is a stable subject identifier per agency pairing); IdP ↔ proofing pipeline (passes evidence and an assurance outcome — see below).
Con: one account across agencies means a compromise is systemic, not local `[I]`; government service means accessibility and language obligations apply `[I]`.
AC: AC-S1 a relying party can integrate over either SAML or OIDC. AC-S2 security documentation and dependency auditing are present and current. AC-P1 *(requires acceptance)* assurance level is asserted explicitly on every authentication response.
Asm/Miss/Unres: **IAL1/IAL2 behaviour is not described in the read text** — the IAL distinction I found came from a third-party Azure AD B2C sample, not from this source, so it is *not* asserted here. Licence terms and public-domain status were not confirmed beyond the presence of `LICENSE.md`.

**O-06 · Primary domain: cross-border digital identity wallets (FORD 2)**
Ben: EU residents presenting credentials, and relying parties across member states accepting them. Prob: without one presentation protocol, wallets and verifiers cannot interoperate across borders or credential formats. Ctx: regulated framework, formal assessment, staged versions with dated milestones.
Src: EU Digital Identity Wallet, issue "OIDF OpenID for Verifiable Presentations v1.0", https://github.com/eu-digital-identity-wallet/eudi-doc-standards-and-technical-specifications/issues/2, READ 2026-09-09.
Deliv `[S]`: adoption of OpenID for Verifiable Presentations v1.0 within the ARF's list of standards for protocols and interfaces supporting the EUDI Framework.
In `[S]`: OAuth 2.0 (RFC 6749) as the base protocol; credential formats W3C Verifiable Credentials, ISO mdoc, IETF SD-JWT VC.
R1 `[S]` The specification "defines a mechanism on top of OAuth 2.0 (RFC6749) for requesting and delivering Presentations of Credentials". R2 `[S]` It must support W3C VC, ISO mdoc and IETF SD-JWT VC credential formats. R3 `[S]` Rationale: "OAuth 2.0 is used as a base protocol as it provides the required rails to build a simple, secure, and developer-friendly Credential presentation layer on top of it." R4 `[S]` Scope covers cross-border interoperability and wallet-provider / relying-party functional requirements. R5 `[S]` Status: v1.0 Final approved **July 2025**; EC assessment completed with **no identified gaps**; v1.1 adopted by OIDF, estimated completion **end of 2026**; conformance testing specifications submitted **May 2026**.
Rel: wallet ↔ relying party (passes a presentation request and a verifiable presentation; the invariant is that the requested credential format is one both support); ARF ↔ specification (passes an adoption decision with a version pin); conformance suite ↔ implementations (passes test results — the evidence of interoperability).
Con: three credential formats must be handled by one protocol, so format negotiation is mandatory `[S]/[I]`; a moving version target (v1.1) requires implementers to plan migration `[S]`.
AC: AC-S1 a presentation exchange completes for each of the three credential formats. AC-S2 the implementation cites the approved v1.0 Final. AC-P1 *(requires acceptance)* the implementation passes the conformance suite submitted in May 2026.
Asm/Miss/Unres: whether v1.1 will be mandatory, and the transition window, are unresolved.

### ISIC P — Education

**P-03 · Primary domain: K-12 education data interoperability (FORD 5)**
Ben: school districts and state agencies moving student, staff and assessment data between systems; vendors building to one target. Prob: point-to-point integrations per vendor pair are unaffordable at district scale. Ctx: many independent implementers claiming compatibility; the claim needs a definition.
Src: Ed-Fi Alliance, "Ed-Fi-API-Standards — Documentation and guidance on building Ed-Fi compatible API applications", https://github.com/Ed-Fi-Alliance-OSS/Ed-Fi-API-Standards, README, READ 2026-09-09.
Deliv `[S]`: normative API standards — Resources API **5.0**, Descriptors API **5.0**, Discovery API **1.0** (with a draft 2.0) — plus the Ed-Fi API Guidelines **4.0** and a "Tips for Success in Building an Ed-Fi Compatible API" document.
In `[S]`: the Ed-Fi Data Standard and its Unifying Data Model.
R1 `[S]` "An Ed-Fi API must … Implement the following Open API specifications": Resources API 5.0, Descriptors API 5.0, Discovery API 1.0. R2 `[S]` Implementations must "adhere to all of the normative guidance in the Ed-Fi API Guidelines" (v4.0). R3 `[S]` The interface is REST-based, "where the messages conform to the Ed-Fi Data Standard". R4 `[S]` The Data Standard supplies a Unifying Data Model for K-12 enterprise information.
Rel: Discovery API → Resources/Descriptors APIs (passes endpoint and version discovery — this is how a client learns what an implementation supports); Data Standard ↔ Resources API (passes the message shapes; the invariant is that resource payloads match the standard's model); Descriptors API ↔ Resources API (passes controlled vocabularies referenced by resources).
Con: three specifications at different version numbers must be satisfied together — partial implementation is not compatibility `[S]/[I]`.
AC: AC-S1 all three OpenAPI specifications are implemented at the stated versions. AC-S2 the Discovery endpoint advertises the implemented versions. AC-S3 payloads validate against the Data Standard model. AC-P1 *(requires acceptance)* a client built against another vendor's Ed-Fi API works unmodified after re-pointing its base URL.
Asm/Miss/Unres: authentication, paging and versioning specifics live in the Guidelines document, which I did not read; MUST/SHOULD wording is therefore not quotable here.

**P-04 · Primary domain: cross-sector education data definitions (FORD 5)**
Ben: state and institutional data managers linking PreK-12, postsecondary and workforce data; researchers depending on comparable definitions. Prob: the same concept carries different names, definitions and option sets across sectors, so linked analysis is unsound. Ctx: voluntary federal-sponsored community; multiple technical form factors.
Src: CEDS (Common Education Data Standards), organization page and repository set, https://github.com/cedstandards, READ 2026-09-09.
Deliv `[S]`: CEDS-Elements (standard element names, definitions and option sets); CEDS-IDS (Integrated Data Store); CEDS-Data-Warehouse; CEDS-Data-Warehouse-Parquet; CEDS-Ontology (draft OWL).
In `[S]`: the Domain Entity Schema (DES) as conceptual foundation; ceds.ed.gov; CEDS-Collaborative-Exchange.
R1 `[S]` Purpose: "an education data management initiative whose purpose is to streamline the understanding of data within and across P-20W institutions and sectors." R2 `[S]` It must publish "standard elements names, definitions, and option sets". R3 `[S]` The IDS applies "3rd normal form database normalization" for operational integration. R4 `[S]` The Data Warehouse uses "star schema data warehouse normalization techniques for improved query performance". R5 `[S]` A Parquet form serves "data engineering and data science needs in the cloud". R6 `[S]` Governance is an Open Source Community under Apache 2.0.
Rel: DES → Elements → IDS → Data Warehouse → Parquet (a chain that passes element identity through four physical forms; the invariant is that one element means the same thing in all of them); Ontology ↔ Elements (passes formal semantics, still draft).
Con: normalisation for integration (R3) and denormalisation for query (R4) pull in opposite directions, so both forms must be generated from one definition rather than maintained separately `[I]`.
AC: AC-S1 an element's definition is identical across Elements, IDS and Warehouse artefacts. AC-S2 the Parquet form loads with the same column semantics as the warehouse. AC-P1 *(requires acceptance)* a change to an element propagates to all four forms in one release.
Asm/Miss/Unres: no explicit requirements are stated on the organization page; the ontology's status ("draft") leaves formal semantics unresolved.

### ISIC Q — Human health and social work

**Q-07 · Primary domain: observational health research (FORD 3)**
Ben: researchers running the same analysis across many health systems' data without moving patient records. Prob: source data models differ per institution, so a study must be re-implemented everywhere, and results cannot be compared. Ctx: distributed data custodians, privacy limits, evidence that changes as data refreshes.
Src: OHDSI (Observational Health Data Sciences and Informatics), organization page, https://github.com/OHDSI, READ-thin 2026-09-09.
Deliv `[S]`: "Definition and DDLs for the OMOP Common Data Model (CDM)"; Atlas — "an open source software tool for researchers to conduct scientific analyses on standardized observational data"; CohortMethod (R package for cohort studies on OMOP-structured data); PatientLevelPrediction (patient-level predictive modelling); Vocabulary-v5.0.
In `[S]`: institutional observational data; standardised vocabularies.
R1 `[S]` Source data must be transformed into the OMOP CDM before analysis. R2 `[S]` Standardised vocabularies (Vocabulary-v5.0) must supply concept identity. R3 `[S]` Analysis tools operate on "standardized observational data", not on raw source models. R4 `[S]` Licensing across repositories is open source (Apache-2.0, MIT observed).
Rel: source EHR/claims → ETL → OMOP CDM (passes mapped concepts; the invariant is that a concept ID means the same clinical idea at every site); CDM ↔ Atlas/CohortMethod/PatientLevelPrediction (passes cohort definitions and result sets — a cohort definition must execute unchanged at every site); Vocabulary ↔ CDM (passes concept hierarchies).
Con: analyses travel, data does not — so every requirement must be expressible as portable code `[I]`; vocabulary version differences between sites silently change cohort membership `[I]`.
AC: AC-S1 a cohort definition authored in Atlas executes at a second site without edits. AC-S2 the CDM DDL applies cleanly to a new site's database. AC-P1 *(requires acceptance)* each site records the vocabulary version used with every result.
Asm/Miss/Unres: **mission statement, governance and per-repository licensing were not stated on the org page** beyond repository-level licence badges.

**Q-04 · Primary domain: national clinical data exchange (FORD 3)**
Ben: US patients and their clinicians moving records between systems; certified health-IT vendors. Prob: base FHIR is too permissive to guarantee that a given data element will be present or comparable. Ctx: certification consequences; a balloted standard with formal issue governance.
Src: HL7 International, "US-Core — FHIR R4 version of US Core IG", https://github.com/HL7/US-Core/, README and build description, READ 2026-09-09.
Deliv `[S]`: an Implementation Guide on FHIR R4 defining profiles, Must-Support elements, search parameters, and value-set/code-system bindings; published as human-readable HTML plus a machine-processable NPM package.
In `[S]`: SUSHI compiling the IG from `sushi-config.yaml`; the HL7 IG Publisher; Jekyll; Python and Bash helpers; the HL7 Jira tracker.
R1 `[S]` The IG is built on FHIR R4. R2 `[S]` It defines profiles constraining FHIR resources for the US context. R3 `[S]` It defines Must-Support elements that systems must support for certification. R4 `[S]` It defines required search parameters for FHIR RESTful APIs. R5 `[S]` It defines terminology bindings to US standards. R6 `[S]` Publication uses SUSHI → IG Publisher → Jekyll, producing HTML and an NPM package. R7 `[S]` Versioning follows FHIR Releases and Versioning with semantic numbering (e.g. 9.0.0 major, 9.1.0 STU update, 9.0.1 technical correction). R8 `[S]` "All issues should be logged using the official Jira tracker system so they can be reviewed and voted upon by the HL7 International Cross Groups Projects Workgroup."
Rel: `sushi-config.yaml` + FSH sources → SUSHI → IG Publisher (passes the IG resource then the rendered guide; the invariant is that HTML and NPM package are generated from one source); IG ↔ implementers (passes profiles and Must-Support obligations); Jira ↔ ballot (passes issues into a voting process — authority sits with the workgroup, not the editors).
Con: certification linkage makes Must-Support a compliance obligation, not advice `[S]/[I]`; a technical correction must not change conformance `[I]`.
AC: AC-S1 the guide builds to both HTML and an NPM package from source. AC-S2 a server missing a Must-Support element fails validation against the profile. AC-S3 every accepted change traces to a Jira issue with a workgroup vote. AC-P1 *(requires acceptance)* the declared version number matches the semantic class of the change (major/STU/correction).
Asm/Miss/Unres: the specific profile list and current version were not enumerated in the read text.

**X-03 · Primary domain: scholarly text encoding (FORD 6)**
Ben: libraries, museums, publishers and academics encoding literary and linguistic texts for long-term scholarly use. Ctx: a large international community; guidelines and schemas generated from one source; very high change volume.

R3 `[S]` The standard is "international and interdisciplinary … used by libraries, museums, publishers, and academics to represent all kinds of literary and linguistic texts".
Rel: P5 ODD sources → Guidelines HTML + schemas (passes element and attribute definitions; the invariant is that prose and schema never disagree because both derive from the same source); community issues ↔ dev branch (passes proposed changes).
Con: `[I]` a scheme aiming to be "maximally expressive and minimally obsolescent" must add without removing, so deprecation rather than deletion is the norm.
