### ISIC S — Other service activities

**S-03 · Primary domain: community repair impact accounting (FORD 2)**
Ben: community repair groups and their funders, who must show the environmental value of repairs. Prob: a repaired kettle's avoided emissions are unknown to a volunteer at an event; without a lookup, impact claims are guesswork. Ctx: volunteer data capture, heterogeneous products, uneven source data.
Src: The Restart Project, "environmental-impact-data", https://github.com/TheRestartProject/environmental-impact-data, README, READ 2026-09-09.
Deliv `[S]`: a dataset of product weight and pre-use CO2e by product category — "CO2e data on 491 products and product weight data on 824 products" out of "1406 products"; per-category average weight and pre-use CO2e values; a data-availability and data-quality evaluation per category.
In `[S]`: product manufacturers; peer-reviewed journals; public bodies including ADEME (French Environment and Energy Management Agency); Open Repair Data Standard categories plus a few unpowered categories.
R1 `[S]` Categories must follow ORDS, extended with some unpowered product categories. R2 `[S]` For each category, an average product weight and a pre-use CO2e value must be calculated. R3 `[S]` Data availability and data quality must be evaluated for every category. R4 `[S]` Licence CC BY-SA 4.0. R5 `[S]` It must be usable by the Fixometer to estimate repair impact on restarters.net. R6 `[S]` Caveat: "much of the source data did not contain relevant CO2e or product weight data".
Rel: ORDS category (S-01/S-02) → this dataset (passes the category key — the invariant is that category identifiers match exactly, or lookups fail silently); dataset → Fixometer (passes per-item weight and CO2e for aggregation into group and network totals).
Con: quality varies by category and must travel with the number `[S]`; share-alike licensing propagates to derived impact datasets `[S]`.
AC: AC-S1 every ORDS category used at events resolves to a weight and, where available, a CO2e value. AC-S2 each category carries its data-quality assessment. AC-P1 *(requires acceptance)* impact figures are never published without the category-level quality flag attached.
Asm/Miss/Unres: units and system boundary for "pre-use CO2e" are not stated in the read text; no update cadence.

**S-02 · Primary domain: open repair evidence (FORD 5)**
Ben: repair campaigners, regulators and researchers needing evidence about what breaks and what is fixable. Prob: repair knowledge sits in thousands of individual events and is lost. Ctx: a federation of independent groups pooling data under a shared standard; periodic publication.
Src: Open Repair Alliance, "data — Source of the Open Repair Alliance downloadable datasets", https://github.com/openrepair/data, README, READ 2026-09-09.
Deliv `[S]`: compiled and published repair datasets from the Alliance, organised in `aggregated`, `partners` and `quests` folders.
In `[S]`: records contributed by member repair networks; the Open Repair Data Standard.
R1 `[S]` Current structure is **ORDS v0.3**. R2 `[S]` The collection contains **305,649 powered items** as of October 2025. R3 `[S]` "Data last updated: October 2025 with data to end of July 2025." R4 `[S]` Licence CC BY-SA 4.0.
Rel: partner datasets → aggregated dataset (passes records conformed to ORDS v0.3; the invariant is the standard version — mixing versions corrupts aggregation); dataset → impact lookup (S-03) (passes product category).
Con: a publication lag of roughly three months between data cut-off and release means analyses are never current `[S]/[I]`; share-alike propagates to derivatives `[S]`.
AC: AC-S1 every published file declares its ORDS version. AC-S2 the aggregate count and the data cut-off date are stated with each release. AC-P1 *(requires acceptance)* records failing ORDS v0.3 validation are excluded and counted in a rejects report.
Asm/Miss/Unres: **field-level contents were not read** (they live in ORDS, S-01, which was not sampled); no stated update cadence.

### ISIC T — Households as employers; own-use production

> **Both T cases are SNIPPET-only. Every requirement below is unverified against the original document, because dol.gov and uscis.gov are blocked by egress policy.**

**T-05 · Primary domain: household employment compliance (FORD 5)**
Ben: private households that hire a nanny, cleaner or home-care worker, and the workers themselves. Prob: a household is a legal employer with verification duties it has no HR department to discharge. Ctx: an individual acting as employer; penalties for error; irreversible immigration consequences for the worker.
Src: USCIS, "I-9 Central — Completing Form I-9: Domestic Workers", https://www.uscis.gov/i-9-central/completing-form-i-9/domestic-workers, **SNIPPET, unverified**.
Deliv `[S-unverified]`: guidance on when and how a household employer completes Form I-9 for a domestic worker. In: Form I-9; the worker's documentation.
R1 `[S-unverified]` A household that employs a domestic worker may have Form I-9 obligations. R2 `[I]` The determination depends on employment status (employee vs independent contractor), which the household must make.
Rel: household employer ↔ worker (passes identity/work-authorisation documents — the invariant is that the worker chooses which acceptable documents to present); employer ↔ retained record (carries state for the retention period).
Con: `[I]` the same household simultaneously holds obligations under immigration, wage-hour and tax regimes, each with different thresholds.
AC: AC-S1-unverified a completed I-9 exists for each covered worker within the required timeframe. AC-P1 *(requires acceptance)* the household can produce the record on request without exposing unrelated personal data.
Asm/Miss/Unres: **timeframes, retention periods, exemptions and the contractor boundary are all unread.** Everything here is provisional.

**T-01 · Primary domain: domestic-work employment terms (FORD 5)**
Ben: home care workers and the households employing them. Prob: domestic work is typically arranged verbally, so hours, duties, pay, time off and termination are contested later. Ctx: an isolated one-to-one employment relationship with no HR intermediary; power asymmetry.
Src: US Department of Labor, Women's Bureau, "Employment Sample Agreement for Home Care Workers" (released November 2023 per search snippet), https://www.dol.gov/sites/dolgov/files/WB/Files/Employment-Sample-Agreement-for-Home-Care-Workers.pdf, **SNIPPET, unverified**.
Deliv `[S-unverified]`: a sample employment agreement for home care workers; companion samples exist for cleaners and nannies.
In: the parties' own terms; DOL wage-hour rules as background.
R1 `[S-unverified]` The agreement is a voluntary "helpful starting point", not a mandate. R2 `[S-unverified]` Its purpose is to "create a shared understanding of the terms of employment", reducing future misunderstanding or conflict. R3 `[I]` To do that, it must cover duties, schedule, pay rate, overtime, time off and termination.
Rel: sample template → negotiated agreement (passes clause structure; the invariant is that both parties hold the same executed text); agreement ↔ pay records (passes agreed rate and hours).
Con: voluntary status means the document has persuasive, not binding, force unless executed `[I]`.
AC: AC-S1-unverified both parties hold a signed copy stating pay, hours and duties. AC-P1 *(requires acceptance)* the agreement is available in the worker's preferred language.
Asm/Miss/Unres: **actual clause list unread.** Interaction with the Domestic Service Final Rule (T-02) is unexamined.

### ISIC U — Extraterritorial organizations and bodies

**U-05 · Primary domain: humanitarian case-data standardisation (FORD 5)**
Ben: anti-trafficking agencies and humanitarian responders sharing case data; ultimately trafficking survivors. Prob: individual-level case data is sensitive, so it is either not shared or shared in incompatible ad-hoc forms; and the standard's licensing is unclear to would-be adopters. Ctx: extreme sensitivity, cross-agency authority, irreversible harm from disclosure.
Src: IOM (UN Migration), "About the Human Trafficking Case Data Standard (HTCDS) with HXL hashtags", issue #7, https://github.com/UNMigration/HTCDS/issues/7, READ 2026-09-09.
Deliv `[S]` (as proposed in the issue by Emerson Rocha of HXL-CPLP): spreadsheets with HXL hashtags representing individuals and attributes; documentation explaining each hashtag; examples using **fictional** data for tool testing; visualisation dashboards built with existing HXL tools.
In `[S]`: HTCDS; the HXL standard and its tooling.
R1 `[S]` An HXL-compatible expression of HTCDS should be produced. R2 `[S]` Each hashtag must be documented. R3 `[S]` Worked examples must use fictional data. R4 `[S]` Existing HXL tools must be able to consume the result. R5 `[S]` Licensing is a blocker: "HTCDS does not have an typical well know software/database-like license", creating uncertainty for humanitarian organisations. R6 `[S]` Restrictive terms could prevent UN agencies, the Red Cross and Amnesty from adopting the structure.
Rel: HTCDS field definitions → HXL hashtags (passes semantic mapping; the invariant is one hashtag per HTCDS concept); fictional examples → HXL tools (passes test data that carries no disclosure risk); licence ↔ adoption (authority constraint — without a clear licence the mapping cannot be used).
Con: real case data must never enter the public examples `[S]`; sensitivity means the standard's *structure* can be public while instances cannot `[I]`.
AC: AC-S1 every HTCDS concept maps to a documented hashtag. AC-S2 all published examples are fictional. AC-S3 an HXL tool renders a dashboard from the example file. AC-P1 *(requires acceptance)* a licence is published that permits use by UN agencies and NGOs.
Asm/Miss/Unres: **HTCDS's own requirements and maintainer statement were not readable from this issue**; the licensing question was open.

**U-04 · Primary domain: humanitarian sector identity (FORD 2)**
Ben: aid workers and humanitarian organisations needing one verified identity across coordination platforms. Prob: each humanitarian platform maintained its own accounts, fragmenting access during emergencies. Ctx: an inter-agency service now being retired — a decommissioning case with migration consequences.
Src: UN OCHA, "hid-api — Humanitarian ID authentication service", https://github.com/UN-OCHA/hid-api, README, READ 2026-09-09 (repository archived).
Deliv `[S]`: an OAuth 2.0 and OpenID Connect server, with a REST API documented at api.humanitarian.id/docs/.
In `[S]`: MongoDB backend; Node.js + hapi server; `EmailService`; `AuthController` handling token creation and OIDC workflows.
R1 `[S]` Must operate as an OAuth 2.0 and OpenID Connect server. R2 `[S]` User flow is short: log in, approve website access, redirect to the destination application. R3 `[S]` A REST API must be available for programmatic access. R4 `[S]` **"After careful review, the Humanitarian ID project will be officially decommissioned on 31 December 2025."** R5 `[S]` Users must "transition to alternative authentication methods as soon as possible".
Rel: HID ↔ relying humanitarian platforms (passes OIDC tokens; the invariant is a stable subject identifier — which is exactly what breaks at decommissioning); email service ↔ account verification (passes verification messages).
Con: R4/R5 make identity continuity the dominant requirement — every dependent platform inherits a hard cut-off `[S]`; the repository is archived, so no fixes will arrive `[I]`.
AC: AC-S1 the service issues OIDC tokens accepted by a relying platform. AC-S2 the decommissioning notice is visible to all integrators. AC-P1 *(requires acceptance)* each relying platform records a named successor identity provider and an account-mapping plan before the cut-off.
Asm/Miss/Unres: licence terms and account-verification specifics not stated; no successor is named in the source.

### FORD 1 — Natural sciences

**E-03 · Primary domain: water-quality data schema (ISIC E)**
Ben: community and government water monitoring programmes in Canada publishing comparable results. Prob: the US federal exchange format is XML-first and US-shaped; Canadian initiatives needed a JSON form that still maps to it. Ctx: cross-border reuse of a government standard by a non-government publisher.
Src: DataStream (The Gordon Foundation), "wqx — Water Quality Exchange (WQX) JSON Schema", https://github.com/datastreamapp/wqx, README, READ 2026-09-09.
Deliv `[S]`: a JSON table schema validating eight record categories — `project`, `location`, `activitymetric`, `biological`, `biological-habitat`, `habitat`, `instantaneous`, `physical-chemistry`; published as npm package `wqx`.
In `[S]`: the WQX standard, "developed by the US Environmental Protection Agency (EPA) and the US Geological Society (USGS)", an implementation of the ESAR (Environmental Sampling, Analysis and Results) data standard; EPA's domain-values download.
R1 `[S]` The schema must validate the eight named categories. R2 `[S]` `definitions.values.json` is rebuilt from EPA's domain values, and "This should only be run if the version of WQX is updated". R3 `[S]` Origin: the 2018 review produced DataStream's WQX Open Data Schema (DS-WQX), adapted for Canadian monitoring initiatives. R4 `[S]` Licence MIT.
Rel: EPA domain values → `definitions.values.json` → schema (passes controlled vocabularies; the invariant is that the code list version matches the WQX version); schema ↔ DataStream platform (passes validated records for the open-access sharing platform).
Con: R2 couples this schema's release cycle to an upstream US government publication `[S]/[I]`; a JSON adaptation must not silently diverge from the XML original if data is to flow back `[I]`.
AC: AC-S1 a record in each of the eight categories validates. AC-S2 regenerating domain values changes nothing unless the WQX version changed. AC-P1 *(requires acceptance)* a validated JSON record converts to WQX XML accepted by EPA (see E-04).
Asm/Miss/Unres: WQX version currently targeted is not stated; the DS-WQX/WQX divergence set is undocumented here.

**B-03 · Primary domain: geologic map databases (ISIC B)**
Ben: state and federal geologists producing digital geologic maps, and everyone consuming them. Prob: without a fixed schema, each map is a bespoke database and cannot be validated, merged or reused. Ctx: a draft national standard succeeding an earlier one (NCGMP09 v1.1, 2010); GIS-platform dependency.
Src: USGS, "GeMS_ToolsDocumentation", https://github.com/DOI-USGS/gems-tools-arcmap/wiki/GeMS_ToolsDocumentation (with the sibling `gems-tools-pro` wiki), READ 2026-09-09.
Deliv `[S]`: an ArcGIS toolbox for creating, manipulating and validating GeMS-style databases, including a **Validate Database** tool that "audits a geodatabase for conformance with the GeMS schema and reports compliance" at three levels.
In `[S]`: the GeMS schema; formal documentation at ngmdb.usgs.gov/Info/standards/GeMS.
R1 `[S]` Level 2 compliance requires non-spatial tables `DataSources`, `DescriptionOfMapUnits`, `GeoMaterialDict`, and a `GeologicMap` feature dataset containing `ContactsAndFaults` and `MapUnitPolys`. R2 `[S]` "Required fields within required elements are present and correctly defined." R3 `[S]` `ContactsAndFaults` requires `Type`, `IsConcealed`, `ExistenceConfidence`, `IdentityConfidence`, `LocationConfidenceMeters`, `DataSourceID`, `Label`, `Notes`, `ContactsAndFaults_ID`, `Symbol`. R4 `[S]` `MapUnitPolys` must carry `MapUnit` attributes referencing `DescriptionOfMapUnits`. R5 `[S]` "GeMS-style databases use `_ID` values as primary keys; these values are repeated as ID values in other tables where they serve as foreign keys." R6 `[S]` `_ID` values may be plain text or GUIDs. R7 `[S]` Validation must check topology and confirm that "all map units in MapUnitPolys have entries in DescriptionOfMapUnits table" without duplicates.
Rel: `MapUnitPolys` ↔ `DescriptionOfMapUnits` (passes MapUnit keys — the invariant is referential integrity with no duplicates); every feature ↔ `DataSources` (passes `DataSourceID`, carrying provenance for each mapped line and polygon); geometry ↔ topology rules (passes spatial consistency).
Con: uncertainty is first-class — `ExistenceConfidence`, `IdentityConfidence` and `LocationConfidenceMeters` must accompany every contact/fault, so a map cannot assert precision it lacks `[S]`; tooling is ArcGIS-bound `[S]`.
AC: AC-S1 Validate Database reports the compliance level and names each missing required element or field. AC-S2 an orphaned MapUnit fails validation. AC-S3 `LocationConfidenceMeters` is populated for every contact/fault feature. AC-P1 *(requires acceptance)* the same database validates identically under the ArcMap and Pro toolboxes.
Asm/Miss/Unres: the definitions of Levels 1 and 3 were not captured; GeMS is described as a **draft** standard.

### FORD 2 — Engineering and technology

**H-06 · Primary domain: standards conformance testing (ISIC H)**
Ben: carriers, platforms and software vendors adopting DCSA standards, and DCSA itself while drafting them. Prob: "we implemented the standard" is unverifiable without executable scenarios, and defects in the standard surface only when two parties interact. Ctx: multiple interacting commercial parties; concurrent roles; standards still under development.
Src: DCSA, "Conformance-Gateway — DCSA conformance framework and reference implementations", https://github.com/dcsaorg/Conformance-Gateway, README, READ 2026-09-09.
Deliv `[S]`: a conformance framework with scenarios and party roles; four testing modes — fully automated, manual scenario, Web UI via Selenium, and AWS environment testing; results inspectable through a Web UI at localhost:4200.
In `[S]`: DCSA standards under test — Booking, Electronic Bill of Lading, Arrival Notices, Port Calls, Commercial Schedules, Verified Gross Mass, Track and Trace, Operational Vessel Schedules.
R1 `[S]` "The DCSA Conformance Framework is used by the adopters of each DCSA standard to measure the conformance of their implementations." R2 `[S]` During standard development DCSA uses it to verify standard correctness by implementing scenarios with interacting parties. R3 `[S]` In fully automated mode "the orchestrator will run all the scenarios defined in the standard". R4 `[S]` Manual mode lets test code perform actions for specific party roles. R5 `[S]` Licence Apache 2.0. R6 `[S]` Local development requires Java 25+, Maven 3.9+, Node.js 20+, or Docker Desktop.
Rel: orchestrator ↔ party adapters (passes scenario steps and expected messages; the invariant is that both sides agree on the standard version under test); scenario set ↔ standard (passes the definition of conformance — this is the reflexive link that lets testing find standard defects); results UI ↔ orchestrator (passes per-step outcomes).
Con: a scenario is only as authoritative as the standard version it encodes, so scenarios must be versioned with the standard `[I]`; testing spans parties in different companies, so the harness must simulate the absent counterparty `[S]/[I]`.
AC: AC-S1 all scenarios for a chosen standard run unattended and produce a per-step report. AC-S2 a party can be driven manually while the rest are automated. AC-P1 *(requires acceptance)* a scenario failure distinguishes an implementation defect from an ambiguity in the standard.
Asm/Miss/Unres: pass/fail thresholds and certification consequences are not described.

**E-04 · Primary domain: regulatory water-data submission (ISIC E)**
Ben: Tribal and State water-quality data managers who must submit to EPA's WQX programme. Prob: managing monitoring data and separately preparing a regulatory submission duplicates effort and introduces transcription error. Ctx: a regulated submission channel; heterogeneous local coding; on-premises IT.
Src: Open Environment / Windsor, "open-waters — Web-based water quality data management software, for submitting WQX data to EPA using Exchange Network technologies", https://github.com/open-environment/open-waters, README, READ 2026-09-09.
Deliv `[S]`: web-based software tracking monitoring locations, projects, samples and results, with automated EPA submission over Exchange Network technologies; bulk import; import translation; import templates.
In `[S]`: spreadsheets; Excel templates provided by Open Waters or user-defined; EPA-acceptable value lists.
R1 `[S]` Users are "Tribal and State water quality data managers … who have the need to submit this data to EPA's WQX program". R2 `[S]` "Submission to EPA is completely automated and handled behind-the-scenes, thus eliminating the burden of separate data submission to EPA." R3 `[S]` "Samples, results, monitoring locations, and metrics can be bulk imported from your favorite spreadsheet program." R4 `[S]` Users can define their own import templates or use the supplied Excel templates. R5 `[S]` Import Translation must map organisational codes to EPA-acceptable values before submission. R6 `[S]` Validation example: Sample Collection Method and Equipment are required when the activity type includes "Sample". R7 `[S]` Licence GNU GPL v3. R8 `[S]` Requires Windows Server 2008+, IIS 7.5+, .NET Framework 4.6.1, SQL Server 2012+.
Rel: spreadsheet import → translation layer → WQX submission (passes records, then EPA-coded records; the invariant is that translation is applied before, never after, submission); local code list ↔ EPA domain values (passes the mapping table — the same domain values E-03 consumes).
Con: R8 pins the deployment to a Windows/SQL Server stack `[S]`; automated submission means a mapping error propagates straight into the federal record `[I]`.
AC: AC-S1 a bulk import of samples, results, locations and metrics succeeds from a user-defined template. AC-S2 an activity typed "Sample" without collection method/equipment is rejected. AC-S3 a submission reaches EPA without a separate manual step. AC-P1 *(requires acceptance)* every translated value is logged with its original local code for audit.
Asm/Miss/Unres: error handling when EPA rejects a submission is not described.

### FORD 3 — Medical and health sciences

**I-05 · Primary domain: nutrient data serving (ISIC I)**
Ben: application developers and dietitians who need queryable access to branded-food nutrient data. Prob: the official datasets ship as CSV bulk files, unusable directly for search-driven applications. Ctx: large product catalogues; text search over descriptions and brands.
Src: littlebunch, "fdc-api — REST API and utilities for USDA Food Data Central CSV datasets", https://github.com/littlebunch/fdc-api, README, READ 2026-09-09.
Deliv `[S]`: a REST server to "query and retrieve USDA FoodData Central datasets", with browse, search, per-food nutrient retrieval and lists of foods by nutrient content; a demo at go.littlebunch.com using the Branded Food Products dataset.
In `[S]`: FDC CSV datasets; FDC IDs; GTIN/UPC codes; nutrient numbers (e.g. **208** for Energy KCAL); food groups; ingredient text.
R1 `[S]` Must serve keyword, phrase, wildcard and regex searches. R2 `[S]` Must support paging (`page=1&max=50`) and sorting by fields such as food description or company name. R3 `[S]` Must support POST-based searches filtered by food group and search field. R4 `[S]` Datastore is Couchbase, chosen "for N1QL and the built-in full text search engine"; a CouchDB implementation also exists. R5 `[S]` The architecture allows other stores through "implementations of the ds/DataSource interface". R6 `[S]` Licence GPL-3.0. R7 `[S]` "The build requires go version 12"; Couchbase 6 or greater recommended; Docker deployment supported.
Rel: FDC CSV loader → datastore → REST handlers (passes normalised food and nutrient records; the invariant is that nutrient numbers keep their FDC meaning); `ds/DataSource` interface ↔ concrete stores (passes a storage contract that permits substitution).
Con: GPL-3.0 constrains embedding in closed products `[S]`; the data itself is upstream (I-04) and changes on USDA's schedule `[I]`.
AC: AC-S1 a wildcard and a regex search each return expected foods. AC-S2 paging is stable across repeated calls. AC-S3 a second datastore can be added by implementing `ds/DataSource` only. AC-P1 *(requires acceptance)* nutrient values match the source CSV for a sampled set of FDC IDs.
Asm/Miss/Unres: refresh process when USDA republishes datasets is not described.

**Q-05 · Primary domain: consumer access to payer claims data (ISIC Q)**
Ben: health-plan members and their authorised caregivers wanting their claims and coverage history; payers who must expose it. Prob: consumers cannot easily obtain their own claims data, and each payer's format differs. Ctx: joint industry–standards development; consumer-facing APIs; sensitive data with authorisation.
Src: HL7 International with the CARIN Alliance, "carin-bb — CARIN Blue Button IG", https://github.com/HL7/carin-bb/, repository README, READ-thin 2026-09-09.
Deliv `[S]`: an implementation guide describing "the CARIN Blue Button® Framework and Common Payer Consumer Data Set", providing "a set of resources that payers can display to consumers via a FHIR API".
In `[S]`: FHIR; payer claims and coverage data; joint HL7/CARIN Alliance governance.
R1 `[S]` The guide's purpose is "enabling consumers and their authorized caregivers to access more of their digital health information with less friction". R2 `[S]` Payers expose the data via a FHIR API. R3 `[S]` The data scope is the Common Payer Consumer Data Set. R4 `[S]` The work is jointly governed by the CARIN Alliance and HL7.
Rel: payer claims system → FHIR profiles → consumer app (passes an explanation-of-benefit style record; the invariant is that the consumer-facing meaning matches the adjudicated claim); authorisation layer ↔ API (passes consumer consent — access is delegated, and caregivers are explicitly in scope).
Con: "authorized caregivers" means delegation must be first-class, not an afterthought `[S]/[I]`; joint governance means neither body can change the set alone `[I]`.
AC: AC-S1 a consumer retrieves their claims through the FHIR API. AC-S2 an authorised caregiver can retrieve them with the consumer's delegation. AC-P1 *(requires acceptance)* every exposed element is traceable to the Common Payer Consumer Data Set definition.
Asm/Miss/Unres: **ExplanationOfBenefit profile details, version, ballot status and the mandate to implement were not in the read text** — recorded gap.

### FORD 4 — Agricultural and veterinary sciences

**A-06 · Primary domain: agricultural laboratory testing (ISIC A)**
Ben: soil and plant-tissue labs, agronomists, and growers who must report data for sustainability and compliance schemes. Prob: exchanging soil test data is "important, but difficult"; re-keying causes errors and slows turnaround. Ctx: many labs, many farm-management systems, growing reporting obligations.
Src: AgGateway Modus project, "Modus — Agricultural Lab Testing Data Standard", https://github.com/AgGateway/Modus, README, READ 2026-09-09.
Deliv `[S]`: the Modus standard in two forms — **v1 (XML)** and **v2 (JSON)** — plus a change process to "propose additions or changes to the standard (e.g., request a new test method)" via ModusStandard.org.
In `[S]`: lab work orders, sample collection, drying and analysis records, results.
R1 `[S]` The standard must cover work-order generation, sample collection, drying analysis and result distribution. R2 `[S]` Two format versions exist: v1 XML and v2 JSON. R3 `[S]` AgGateway holds ownership "to make them available to industry according to a specified license". R4 `[S]` A published process must allow proposing new test methods. R5 `[S]` Farm-management systems must be able to "produce work orders and consume test results".
Rel: farm-management system → work order → lab (passes sample identity and requested methods; the invariant is that the sample ID on the order is the sample ID on the result); lab → results → farm-management system (passes analyte values, method identity and units); change process ↔ method registry (passes new test-method definitions — an extension point that must not fork).
Con: soil test data "drives much agricultural decision-making", so a method mismatch silently changes agronomic recommendations `[S]/[I]`; two format generations must coexist during migration `[I]`.
AC: AC-S1 a work order produced by one system is accepted by an independent lab system. AC-S2 a result document identifies the test method for every analyte. AC-P1 *(requires acceptance)* v1 XML and v2 JSON representations of the same result carry identical analyte values and units.
Asm/Miss/Unres: **mandatory fields of a result document are not stated**; licence terms not detailed in the read text.

**A-01 · Primary domain: plant breeding data exchange (ISIC A)**
Ben: breeding programmes, gene banks and analysis tools moving trial and genotype data between databases. Prob: each breeding database has its own API, so tools must be rewritten per institution. Ctx: international public and private breeding programmes; long-lived germplasm identities.
Src: BrAPI community, "BrAPI v2.1", https://github.com/plantbreeding/BrAPI (branch `brapi-V2.1`, README), READ 2026-09-09.
Deliv `[S]`: a standardised RESTful web-service API specification in four modules, with reference documentation on URL structure, response formats, date/time encoding, location encoding, error handling and search services.
In `[S]`: existing breeding databases and tools.
R1 `[S]` "The Breeding API (BrAPI) is a Standardized RESTful Web Service API Specification for communicating Plant Breeding Data." R2 `[S]` **Core** module: Programs, Trials, Studies, Locations, People, Lists. R3 `[S]` **Phenotyping** module: Observation Units, Observations, Observation Variables, Traits, Scales, Methods, Images. R4 `[S]` **Genotyping** module: Samples, Markers, Variant Sets, Variants, Call Sets, Calls, References, Reads, Vendor Orders. R5 `[S]` **Germplasm** module: Germplasm, Germplasm Attributes, Seed Lots, Crosses, Pedigree, Progeny. R6 `[S]` The specification must define URL structure, response format, date/time encoding, location encoding, error handling and search services. R7 `[S]` V2.1 reached released status on **1 July 2022**; V2.0 was released March 2020.
Rel: Core ↔ Phenotyping (passes Study and Observation Unit identity — the invariant is that an observation always resolves to a study and a unit); Germplasm ↔ Phenotyping/Genotyping (passes germplasm identity, linking what was grown to what was measured and sequenced); search services ↔ all modules (passes deferred query handles for large result sets).
Con: trait/scale/method must travel together or an observation value is uninterpretable `[I]`; module independence must not break cross-module identity `[I]`.
AC: AC-S1 an observation retrieved from one implementation resolves its study, unit, trait, scale and method. AC-S2 date/time and location encodings match the specification exactly. AC-P1 *(requires acceptance)* a client written against one BrAPI v2.1 server runs unmodified against another.
Asm/Miss/Unres: **versioning rules, conformance expectations and licence are not stated in the read text.** Note: implementations (TERRA-REF, CIP-RIU, Breeding Insight, brapir) are cluster CL-BRAPI members, not independent needs.

### FORD 5 — Social sciences

**N-04 · Primary domain: procurement standard extensibility (ISIC N)**
Ben: national and supranational procurement authorities whose legal regimes require fields the core standard lacks. Prob: a single global schema either bloats or excludes; extensions must add without forking. Ctx: multiple jurisdictions publishing simultaneously against one core.
Src: OCDS extensions community, organization page, https://github.com/open-contracting-extensions, READ 2026-09-09.
Deliv `[S]`: profiles — "OCDS for the European Union", "OCDS for Public Private Partnerships (PPPs)", "OCDS for eForms", "OCDS for the Agreement on Government Procurement" — and individual extensions, e.g. one that "Adds a links object to packages, to support pagination" and one that "Allows the point of delivery or site of works for a given line item to be indicated".
In `[S]`: the OCDS core (N-02); Makefile-based builds across repositories.
R1 `[S]` Extensions add defined capabilities to the core (pagination links; delivery point/site of works per line item). R2 `[S]` Profiles bundle extensions for a jurisdiction or contracting form. R3 `[S]` Builds are Makefile-driven per repository.
Rel: core schema ← extension patch (passes additive schema changes; the invariant is that a core-only consumer can still read an extended document); profile ↔ set of extensions (passes a curated bundle with a jurisdictional purpose); extension ↔ documentation build (passes merged field definitions).
Con: additivity is the whole contract — an extension that changes core semantics breaks every existing consumer `[I]`; four profiles must not contradict each other where they overlap `[I]`.
AC: AC-S1 a document using an extension validates against the core for all core fields. AC-S2 each profile lists exactly which extensions it includes. AC-P1 *(requires acceptance)* two profiles applied to one publisher produce no conflicting field definitions.
Asm/Miss/Unres: **`extension.json` structure, schema-patch mechanics, governance and approval requirements were not visible on the org page** — recorded gap.

**S-04 · Primary domain: consumer repair rights (ISIC S)**
> **SNIPPET-only; commission.europa.eu is blocked. All requirements unverified against the original.**
Ben: EU consumers wanting goods repaired rather than replaced; independent repairers; manufacturers carrying new duties. Prob: repair is often slower, dearer or impossible because parts, information and tools are withheld. Ctx: an EU-wide legal instrument with dated application; manufacturer obligations across long product lifetimes.
Src: European Commission, "Right to repair — questions and answers", 18 July 2024, https://commission.europa.eu/document/download/2d443b31-dc2a-4e54-a9ec-5fbb61c895e9_en?filename=2024_07_18_QA_Right2repair_FINAL.pdf, **SNIPPET, unverified**. Related snippets: Regulation (EU) 2023/1670 ecodesign requirements for smartphones, tablets and cordless phones, August 2023; French repairability score 0–10 at point of sale for smartphones, laptops, televisions, front- and top-load washing machines, vacuum cleaners and pressure washers; EU repairability label graded A–E for smartphones and tablets from 20 June 2025.
Deliv `[S-unverified]`: an obligation to repair, with supporting availability of spare parts and repair information.
R1 `[S-unverified]` Manufacturers must make spare parts available promptly, and parts must remain on the market for five or ten years depending on the part. R2 `[S-unverified]` France requires a 0–10 repairability score displayed at point of sale for the listed categories, based on manufacturer self-assessment. R3 `[S-unverified]` Assessment parameters include ease of disassembly, availability and price of spare parts, accessibility of repair documentation, and software restrictions. R4 `[S-unverified]` An A–E repairability label applies to smartphones and tablets from 20 June 2025.
Rel: manufacturer self-assessment → published score/label → consumer decision (passes a comparable number; the invariant is a shared scoring method, or scores are incomparable); parts obligation ↔ repairer (passes availability and price, which are themselves scored).
Con: self-assessment plus a legal consequence requires audit `[I]`; part-availability windows outlive typical product support cycles `[I]`.
AC: AC-S1-unverified a listed product displays its score/label at point of sale. AC-P1 *(requires acceptance)* the score is reproducible by an independent assessor from published evidence.
Asm/Miss/Unres: **the actual legal text of Directive (EU) 2024/1799 was not read.** Category lists and dates come from secondary snippets and may be imprecise.

### FORD 6 — Humanities and the arts

**R-02 · Primary domain: digital preservation storage layout (ISIC R)**
Ben: repositories, libraries and archives responsible for content that must survive software generations. Prob: content locked inside a repository application's private storage cannot be recovered if that application dies. Ctx: decades-long retention; filesystem and object-storage targets; recovery by strangers.
Src: OCFL editors, "spec — The Oxford Common File Layout (OCFL) specifications and website", https://github.com/OCFL/spec, README, READ 2026-09-09.
Deliv `[S]`: the OCFL specification and implementation notes for **v1.1 (current)** and v1.0, with earlier drafts 0.1, 0.2, 0.3, 0.9; published at ocfl.io.
In `[S]`: separate repositories for specification extensions, test fixtures and use cases; a wiki carrying communication channels and meeting notes.
R1 `[S]` The initiative addresses "the need for a preservation-centric, common approach to filesystem (or cloud / object storage) layout for digital repositories". R2 `[S]` Its goal is "to establish a specified recommendation for such a layout". R3 `[S]` Each version ships specification plus implementation notes. R4 `[S]` Extensions, test fixtures and use cases are maintained separately. R5 `[S]` The initiative encourages inclusion of community software tooling, linked from implementation documentation.
Rel: specification ↔ test fixtures (passes conformance examples; the invariant is that a fixture's expected layout is exactly what the spec mandates); specification ↔ extensions (passes optional behaviours that must not alter core layout); implementation notes ↔ implementers (passes non-normative guidance, kept separate so it can evolve faster).
Con: preservation means the layout must be interpretable **without** the original software — so the layout has to be self-describing `[I]`; two live versions (1.0, 1.1) must both remain readable `[I]`.
AC: AC-S1 an implementation passes the published test fixtures for v1.1. AC-P1 *(requires acceptance)* a person with only the specification and the storage can reconstruct object versions with no repository software.
Asm/Miss/Unres: **object/inventory/versioning rules, conformance language, editors and licence were not in the read text** — they live in the specification documents at ocfl.io, which I did not fetch.

**R-05 · Primary domain: museum image services (ISIC R)**
Ben: researchers, educators and developers reusing museum images; the museum managing rights. Prob: images and their descriptions must be usable by third-party viewers, while rights-restricted works must not be exposed. Ctx: mixed rights status across a collection; public API with unknown consumers.
Src: Harvard Art Museums, "api-docs — IIIF section", https://github.com/harvardartmuseums/api-docs/blob/master/sections/iiif.md, READ 2026-09-09.
Deliv `[S]`: IIIF services — an Image API implementation ("The service we provide adheres to version 2.1 of the API") and a Presentation API implementation ("The museums sevices conforms to the Presentation API version 2.1"); a top-level collection at `https://iiif.harvardartmuseums.org/collections/top`; manifests under `https://iiif.harvardartmuseums.org/manifests` addressed as `/ENDPOINT/ITEM_ID` (e.g. `/object/299843`, `/gallery/1600`); hosted Mirador 2 and Mirador 3 viewers accepting `manifest`, `collection` and `showmainmenu` parameters.
In `[S]`: the museum's digitised images and object records; IIIF 2.1.
R1 `[S]` Image API conforms to version 2.1. R2 `[S]` Presentation API conforms to version 2.1. R3 `[S]` A top-level collection endpoint must exist. R4 `[S]` Manifests are addressable by endpoint type and item ID. R5 `[S]` Hosted viewers accept `manifest`, `collection` and `showmainmenu`. R6 `[S]` **"Image URLs for objects that have rights restrictions are excluded for most API users"**, particularly affecting 20th–21st-century artworks.
Rel: object record → manifest → viewer (passes descriptive metadata and image references; the invariant is that a manifest only cites image URLs the requester may use); rights status ↔ manifest generation (an authority gate applied per user class).
Con: R6 means the same manifest identifier can yield different content by user class — consumers must handle absent images gracefully `[S]/[I]`; version 2.1 (not 3.x) limits which viewers work `[I]`.
AC: AC-S1 a manifest URL opens in an independent IIIF 2.1 viewer. AC-S2 a rights-restricted object's manifest omits image URLs for a general API user. AC-P1 *(requires acceptance)* the omission is signalled explicitly rather than as a silent gap.
Asm/Miss/Unres: **image size/region/rotation parameters, rate limits and terms of use are not stated** in the read text.

## 5. PROPOSAL COLLECTION *(separately labeled — invented alternatives and combinations, not source-required deliverables)*

Two materially different constructions per selected case. Each pair deliberately varies **product form** (object / procedure / instrument / creative work / generator / service) or **who creates versus who uses the output**.

**A-03** · P-A-03-1 *Assessment Provenance Card* — a one-page printed artefact issued with every catch limit, showing model structure, data vintage, and which structural choices moved the answer; value: council members can see contested assumptions without reading code. · P-A-03-2 *Structural sensitivity tournament* — an operating procedure where each assessment must run two rival structures and publish the divergence; value: turns "experimental" status into disclosed uncertainty rather than hidden risk.
**A-05** · P-A-05-1 *Unit-abbreviation misuse detector* — a linter that flags agricultural payloads where a unit is syntactically valid but agronomically implausible; value: catches the errors schema validation cannot. · P-A-05-2 *Committee-state visualiser* — a public board rendering each component's position in the nine-state workflow; value: implementers can time their work to a component's readiness.
**B-01** · P-B-01-1 *Orphan-standard rescue kit* — a procedure and template repository for reconstructing a citable specification when a committee's public repo holds only a licence; value: makes the failure mode I found repairable rather than terminal. · P-B-01-2 *Mine-model interchange conformance corpus* — a set of small reference block models and surfaces any format can be tested against; value: lets buyers test claims without the specification.
**B-06** · P-B-06-1 *Schema-drift bench* — an appliance that records a live well-data session and replays it against a second server version, diffing object-by-object; value: turns version incompatibility into evidence before a rig deploys it. · P-B-06-2 *Recursive-clone guard* — a build-time check that fails loudly when a schema submodule is absent; value: removes a silent, high-consequence setup error.
**C-05** · P-C-05-1 *NodeId immutability ledger* — an append-only public record of every NodeId assignment across releases; value: makes accidental reassignment detectable across vendors. · P-C-05-2 *Companion-spec starter generator* — a configurator producing a skeleton NodeSet2.xml, Types.xsd and NodeIds.csv from a domain model; concrete product: a complete artefact triple for a conveyor-scale device.
**C-06** · P-C-06-1 *Characteristic-to-result traceability poster* — a printed shop-floor chart mapping each drawing characteristic to its measurement plan and result file; value: inspectors see coverage gaps physically. · P-C-06-2 *FAIR diff service* — compares two First Article Inspection reports for the same part across suppliers; value: exposes measurement-method differences behind identical pass results.
**D-02** · P-D-02-1 *Substation configuration rehearsal room* — a sandbox that replays a proposed SCL change against recorded protection events before commissioning; value: catches misconfiguration without energising equipment. · P-D-02-2 *SCL edition-migration cookbook* — a written procedure with worked examples for moving Edition-1 files to Edition-2+; value: unblocks utilities stuck on legacy files.
**D-05** · P-D-05-1 *Fallback drill* — a scheduled operating procedure that deliberately severs the utility connection to verify the default DER control engages; value: proves the safety path is real, not assumed. · P-D-05-2 *Household export explainer* — a plain-language monthly statement showing when and why export was curtailed; value: converts an opaque control into a trust-building account.
**E-02** · P-E-02-1 *Continuity-error budget dashboard* — surfaces mass-balance error per run as a first-class quality metric; value: stops silently accepting non-conserving models. · P-E-02-2 *Storm-story generator* — turns a continuous simulation into a narrated sequence of overflow events for public consultation; concrete product: a 12-page briefing for one catchment.
**E-01** · P-E-01-1 *Water-age map for consumers* — a per-address view of simulated water age and source trace; value: makes an invisible quality determinant legible to residents. · P-E-01-2 *Legacy-input compatibility suite* — a corpus of 2.00.12-era networks run against every new release; value: protects a 14-year installed base.
**F-04** · P-F-04-1 *Handover gate procedure* — designers must attach an IDS pass report before a model is accepted for energy analysis; value: moves rework upstream of the analyst. · P-F-04-2 *IDS authoring workbench for energy modellers* — lets the consumer, not the author, express their information needs as a shareable `.ids`; value: shifts who creates the requirement.
**F-03** · P-F-03-1 *Dictionary health report card* — a periodic per-dictionary scorecard against the GEN/CLS/PRP/REL requirement IDs; value: makes qualitative verification comparable over time. · P-F-03-2 *ISO 704 definition coach* — an interactive writing aid that flags circular, negative or inaccurate definitions as they are typed; value: prevents the defect instead of reviewing it.
**G-01** · P-G-01-1 *Event-hash reconciliation service* — two partners exchange only hashes to confirm they recorded the same event without revealing volumes; value: verification under commercial confidentiality. · P-G- 01-2 *Timezone-preservation audit* — a checker proving `eventTimeZoneOffset` survives every hop in a supply chain; value: local-time evidence is what regulators and courts actually ask for.
**G-02** · P-G-02-1 *Regulation-readiness rehearsal pack* — synthetic EPCIS event sets shaped to DPP, EUDR and CSRD reporting shapes, for dry-running compliance before a deadline; value: tests the pipeline when no real data exists yet. · P-G-02-2 *Streaming-conversion pressure rig* — a harness that feeds documents larger than memory and reports the failure boundary; value: turns a claimed capability into a measured limit.
**H-04** · P-H-04-1 *Entitlement overlay* — a party-scoped authorisation layer packaged specifically to close the reference implementation's stated gap; value: the README names the hazard and nothing ships to fill it. · P-H-04-2 *"Do not deploy" seal* — a build-time banner and health-endpoint field that announces the missing auth layer to anyone probing the service; value: makes the warning travel with the running system, not just the README.
**H-01** · P-H-01-1 *Policy rehearsal simulator* — replays a draft policy against last season's trip data to show its likely effect before publication; value: agencies see consequences while the decision is reversible. · P-H-01-2 *Supersession lineage viewer* — renders the `prev_policies` chain as a readable history for hearings and appeals; value: turns machine lineage into public accountability.
**I-01** · P-I-01-1 *Object-suite diff atlas* — a published comparison between consecutive dated suites; value: integrators can scope an upgrade before committing. · P-I-01-2 *Specification-recovery procedure* — a documented method for reconstructing usable docs when a repository's README is unreachable; value: directly addresses the gap encountered in this case.
**I-04** · P-I-04-1 *Migration countdown kit* — a checklist plus test fixtures for moving off a retiring public API by a dated cut-off; value: generalises the NDB→FDC pattern to any government API sunset. · P-I-04-2 *Abridged/full equivalence prover* — verifies an abridged response is a strict subset of the full one; value: lets clients cache the cheap form safely.
**J-06** · P-J-06-1 *Problem-statement fidelity tracker* — follows the original user problem through all four stages and flags where wording drifted; value: prevents supplier-shaped rewriting of a citizen need. · P-J-06-2 *Stage-latency publication* — a standing report of elapsed time per stage; value: the source states no timing requirement, and the absence is itself a governance risk.
**J-04** · P-J-04-1 *Credential-flow red-team scenario deck* — written adversarial scenarios (malicious client, compromised session ID, headless agent) to test the trust boundary; value: exercises the exact asymmetry the discussion identified. · P-J-04-2 *Local-vs-remote deployment decision aid* — a short interactive guide that outputs a concrete auth design; concrete product: a one-page design for a multi-tenant remote server with rotation policy.
**K-06** · P-K-06-1 *Stale-entry sentinel* — flags catalogue entries past their review date and drafts the re-review agenda; value: a 2021 review date on a 2026 catalogue is a live defect. · P-K-06-2 *Licence-consequence annexe* — for each proprietary catalogued standard, a plain statement of what adoption obliges a department to buy; value: the catalogue records "Proprietary" and stops there.
**K-05** · P-K-05-1 *Archived-dependency exit plan template* — a repeatable procedure for pinning, auditing and replacing an archived library in a payments path; value: turns an unmaintained dependency into a managed one. · P-K-05-2 *Round-trip fidelity corpus* — messages that survive XML→JSON→XML unchanged, published as a shared test set; value: lets successor libraries prove parity.
**L-03** · P-L-03-1 *Measure lifecycle ledger* — tracks each energy conservation measure through proposed → implemented → discarded with reasons; value: the schema stores the states; nothing explains the discards. · P-L-03-2 *Use-case authoring clinic* — a guided path from a programme's CSV template to a working Schematron validator; concrete product: a validator for a municipal Level 1 audit programme.
**L-01** · P-L-01-1 *Local-field observatory* — samples payloads across publishers and reports which non-standard fields recur, as evidence for the next Data Dictionary; value: turns extension sprawl into standardisation input. · P-L-01-2 *Context-assertion linter* — rejects payloads whose `@reso.context` version disagrees with their actual field set; value: catches mislabelled payloads before they poison a consumer.
**M-02** · P-M-02-1 *Version-qualified property browser* — shows for any property which of 4.5/4.6/4.7 contain it and how obligation changed; value: three live versions make unqualified statements unsafe. · P-M-02-2 *Docs-to-schema reconciler* — a check that every documented property exists in the corresponding XSD and vice versa; value: prevents prose and schema drifting apart.
**X-02** · P-X-02-1 *Conflict-precedence table* — a single normative table stating which convention wins for each contested attribute; value: the five open questions all reduce to precedence. · P-X-02-2 *Dual-encoded reference dataset* — one dataset carrying both CF and GeoZarr encodings, published as the interoperability test; value: converts an argument into an experiment.
**N-01** · P-N-01-1 *Ontology-generation provenance stamp* — embeds build source and date inside `Brick.ttl` so nightly builds are distinguishable from releases; value: the nightly build is otherwise indistinguishable downstream. · P-N-01-2 *Minimality auditor* — reviews proposed relationships against the "minimal, principled set" commitment; value: guards a design constraint that erodes silently over releases.
**N-02** · P-N-02-1 *Fixture coverage map* — shows which documented fields have no test fixture; value: makes documentation-to-test gaps visible. · P-N-02-2 *Jurisdiction mapping workbook* — a structured method for mapping a national procurement process onto the release/record model; concrete product: a completed mapping for one country's tendering law.
**O-04** · P-O-04-1 *Assurance-level disclosure* — every authentication response states its assurance level explicitly to the relying party; value: agencies currently infer it. · P-O-04-2 *Single-account blast-radius exercise* — a tabletop procedure for what one compromised account can reach across agencies; value: one account for government concentrates risk that no single agency owns.
**O-06** · P-O-06-1 *Three-format presentation conformance matrix* — a published grid of which wallets and verifiers handle W3C VC, ISO mdoc and SD-JWT VC; value: cross-border interoperability fails at format negotiation. · P-O-06-2 *v1.0→v1.1 migration clock* — a tracker pairing each implementation with its planned migration date; value: a moving target with no published transition window.
**P-03** · P-P-03-1 *Compatibility claim verifier* — runs the three required OpenAPI specifications against a vendor's endpoint and issues a pass/fail statement a district can read; value: "Ed-Fi compatible" is currently a vendor assertion. · P-P-03-2 *Discovery-first client template* — a client that configures itself entirely from the Discovery API; value: makes the version contract operational rather than documentary.
**P-04** · P-P-04-1 *Single-definition propagation pipeline* — generates Elements, IDS, Warehouse and Parquet artefacts from one source so a change lands everywhere in one release; value: four physical forms otherwise drift. · P-P-04-2 *Sector-boundary crosswalk narrative* — a written account of where PreK-12, postsecondary and workforce definitions genuinely disagree; value: names the places where linking is unsound rather than papering over them.
**Q-07** · P-Q-07-1 *Vocabulary-version stamp* — binds the vocabulary version to every published result; value: identical cohort definitions silently differ across vocabulary versions. · P-Q-07-2 *Portable-study courier* — packages a study as code plus expected diagnostics so a remote site can run it unattended and return only summaries; value: keeps data still while evidence moves.
**Q-04** · P-Q-04-1 *Must-Support conformance harness* — probes a server for every Must-Support element and reports the gaps a certification body would find; value: turns a certification obligation into a self-check. · P-Q-04-2 *Version-class checker* — verifies that a release numbered as a technical correction changed no conformance requirement; value: the semantic classes are stated but unenforced.
**R-06** · P-R-06-1 *Manifest longevity check* — verifies an exported manifest still opens in independent viewers after each release; value: manifests are public contracts outliving the editor. · P-R-06-2 *Curator-facing manifest clinic* — a short operating procedure for non-developers to fix common manifest defects; value: the problem is authorship access, not tooling absence.
**X-03** · P-X-03-1 *Prose-schema parity report* — lists schema elements lacking Guidelines prose and vice versa; value: both derive from one source, so divergence is a build defect. · P-X-03-2 *Deprecation register* — a public record of elements discouraged but retained, with successor guidance; value: "minimally obsolescent" needs an explicit memory.
**S-03** · P-S-03-1 *Quality-flagged impact statement* — a printed impact figure that always carries its category-level data-quality grade; value: prevents a weak estimate travelling as a strong claim. · P-S-03-2 *Category gap campaign* — a procedure that ranks categories by missing CO2e/weight data and recruits manufacturers to fill them; value: converts a documented data gap into a work programme.
**S-02** · P-S-02-1 *Rejects report* — publishes records excluded for failing ORDS v0.3, with reason codes; value: makes the aggregate's coverage honest. · P-S-02-2 *Publication-lag banner* — states the data cut-off next to every derived statistic; value: a three-month lag is invisible in downstream charts.
**T-05** · P-T-05-1 *Household-employer obligation map* — one sheet showing which duties (verification, wage-hour, tax) attach at which thresholds; value: a household has no HR function to assemble this. · P-T-05-2 *Minimal-disclosure record wallet* — a way to hold employment-verification records so they can be produced without exposing unrelated personal data; value: the household is both employer and private home.
**T-01** · P-T-01-1 *Two-language agreement pack* — the sample agreement rendered side-by-side in the worker's preferred language; value: shared understanding is the stated purpose and language is the first barrier. · P-T-01-2 *Agreement-to-payslip link* — a simple method binding agreed rate and hours to each pay record; value: makes the voluntary document consequential.
**U-05** · P-U-05-1 *Fictional-data generator for sensitive standards* — produces realistic but wholly synthetic case records for tool testing; concrete product: a 500-row HXL-tagged example file for HTCDS. · P-U-05-2 *Licence-blocker escalation procedure* — a documented route for resolving an unlicensed standard that agencies cannot adopt; value: the issue names licensing as the adoption blocker.
**U-04** · P-U-04-1 *Identity-provider succession plan template* — names the successor, the account-mapping method and the cut-off rehearsal; value: a dated decommissioning with no named successor. · P-U-04-2 *Orphaned-integration census* — enumerates every platform still relying on the retiring service; value: the cost of the shutdown is otherwise unknown.
**E-03** · P-E-03-1 *Cross-border round-trip prover* — shows a JSON record converts to WQX XML that EPA accepts and back without loss; value: the JSON adaptation's fidelity is currently assumed. · P-E-03-2 *Upstream-version tripwire* — alerts when EPA's domain values change so the schema's code lists can be regenerated deliberately; value: the README makes regeneration conditional on an upstream event nobody watches.
**B-03** · P-B-03-1 *Confidence-aware map renderer* — draws contacts and faults with line treatments derived from `LocationConfidenceMeters` and the confidence fields; value: uncertainty is recorded but conventionally rendered away. · P-B-03-2 *Compliance-level certificate* — a machine-generated statement of the achieved GeMS level attached to every published map database; value: consumers currently cannot tell.
**H-06** · P-H-06-1 *Ambiguity ledger* — records every scenario failure judged to be a standard defect rather than an implementation defect; value: closes the loop between conformance testing and standard drafting. · P-H-06-2 *Counterparty simulator catalogue* — packaged stand-ins for each party role so a single company can test alone; value: conformance today needs a willing partner.
**E-04** · P-E-04-1 *Translation audit trail* — logs every code translation with its original local value; value: automated submission sends translated data straight into the federal record. · P-E-04-2 *Rejection playbook* — a written procedure for what a data manager does when EPA rejects a submission; value: the automation has no documented failure path.
**I-05** · P-I-05-1 *Source-fidelity spot-check* — samples served nutrient values against the source CSV and reports drift; value: a serving layer can silently diverge from the dataset. · P-I-05-2 *Datastore-substitution kit* — a reference implementation of the `ds/DataSource` interface for a second backend; value: proves the stated portability.
**Q-05** · P-Q-05-1 *Caregiver delegation ceremony* — a documented, revocable procedure for a member to grant and withdraw caregiver access; value: authorised caregivers are in scope but delegation is unmodelled here. · P-Q-05-2 *Claim-to-consumer translation layer* — renders an adjudicated claim into language a member can act on; value: exposure is not comprehension.
**A-06** · P-A-06-1 *Method-identity guard* — refuses to compare analyte values across differing test methods; value: soil results drive agronomic decisions and method mismatch is invisible. · P-A-06-2 *v1↔v2 equivalence corpus* — paired XML and JSON documents proving identical values and units; value: two live generations need a fidelity contract.
**A-01** · P-A-01-1 *Trait-scale-method bundle validator* — rejects observations whose trait, scale and method do not travel together; value: an observation value alone is uninterpretable. · P-A-01-2 *Cross-server client conformance run* — one client executed against several BrAPI servers with a published compatibility grid; value: makes portability measurable.
**N-04** · P-N-04-1 *Additivity guard* — mechanically proves an extension leaves every core field readable by a core-only consumer; value: additivity is the whole contract and is currently conventional. · P-N-04-2 *Profile overlap report* — flags fields defined differently by two profiles a publisher must satisfy simultaneously; value: four profiles, one publisher, no conflict check.
**S-04** · P-S-04-1 *Score reproducibility dossier* — the evidence pack a manufacturer publishes so an independent assessor can recompute the repairability score; value: self-assessment plus legal consequence needs audit. · P-S-04-2 *Parts-availability clock* — a per-model countdown of remaining obligatory availability; value: buyers and repairers can see how long a product stays repairable.
**R-02** · P-R-02-1 *Cold-recovery drill* — a rehearsed procedure reconstructing object versions from storage alone, with no repository software; value: this is the actual preservation claim and is rarely tested. · P-R-02-2 *Fixture-first implementation guide* — a path that starts from the published test fixtures rather than the prose; value: shortens the route to conformance.
**R-05** · P-R-05-1 *Explicit-omission signal* — marks rights-withheld images as deliberately absent rather than missing; value: silent gaps look like errors. · P-R-05-2 *Rights-aware teaching pack builder* — assembles a classroom set only from images a given user class may actually retrieve; concrete product: a 40-image gallery manifest valid for general API users.
