## 6. COMPOSITION COLLECTION — larger useful wholes

Each entry states the parts, **what passes between them**, **what must remain consistent**, and **which requirements are inherited**. Composition is recursive: several of these are components of later ones.

**C1 · Water evidence chain (E-03 + E-04 + E-01 + E-02).**
Parts: DataStream's WQX JSON schema; Open Waters submission software; EPANET; SWMM. Passes: monitoring records → validated JSON → EPA-coded WQX submission → federal record; separately, network models → simulated pressure/age/overflow. Must stay consistent: **the WQX version and EPA domain-value list** used by the schema (E-03 R2) and by the translation layer (E-04 R5); the monitoring location identity across observed and simulated worlds. Inherits: E-04 R6 activity-type validation; E-01 R5 "as is" disclaimer, which forbids treating simulated results as observed evidence. Emergent outcome: a utility can state, for one catchment, what was measured, what was modelled, and where the two disagree — which neither half supports alone.

**C2 · Repair impact ledger (S-01/S-02 + S-03 + S-04).**
Parts: ORDS-structured event records; the Restart weight/CO2e dataset; the EU repair obligations. Passes: product category key → per-item weight and CO2e → aggregated avoided-impact figure → evidence for policy. Must stay consistent: **ORDS category identifiers and the ORDS version** (S-02 R1, S-03 R1) — a category rename silently breaks every historical total. Inherits: S-03 R6 (much source data lacks CO2e/weight) and R3 (per-category quality evaluation), so every aggregate must carry a quality qualifier; CC BY-SA 4.0 from both datasets, which propagates to any derived ledger. Emergent outcome: repair groups can answer a regulator's question about real-world repairability with data, and the same categories feed S-04's score debate.

**C3 · Conformance-driven standards development (H-06 + H-04 + G-01/G-02).**
Parts: a conformance framework; reference implementations; a normative schema and a conformant toolkit. Passes: scenario steps → messages → per-step results → defect reports that split "implementation wrong" from "standard ambiguous". Must stay consistent: **the standard version under test** across scenarios, reference implementation and adopter. Inherits: H-04 R3 — the reference implementation has no authorisation, so conformance testing must never run against production data. Emergent outcome: a standards body learns of its own ambiguities before adopters ship, which is the reflexive value H-06 R2 already claims and G-01/G-02's conformance claim currently lacks.

**C4 · Machine-readable regulation loop (H-01 + J-06 + N-02/N-04).**
Parts: the MDS Policy API; the UK open-standards selection process; OCDS core and its extensions. Passes: a user problem → a selected standard → published machine-readable policy → operator compliance → procurement records of the contracts that implement it. Must stay consistent: the **problem statement** from J-06 R1 through to the adopted standard; geography and organisation identifiers shared between policy and contract. Inherits: H-01 R6's 20-minute publication lead time (a hard timing contract), and N-04's additivity constraint for jurisdiction-specific fields. Emergent outcome: a city can trace a rule from the citizen problem that motivated it to the contract that delivers it.

**C5 · Building information spine (F-03 + F-04 + L-03 + N-01).**
Parts: bSDD verified dictionaries; an IDS ruleset for BIM-BEM; BuildingSync audit records; Brick operational metadata. Passes: verified concept definitions → model-checking rules → audit findings and measures → live operational points. Must stay consistent: **the term identity for a piece of equipment across design, audit and operation** — bSDD's class, the IFC entity, the BEDES term BuildingSync links to, and the Brick class. Inherits: F-03 R13 (unit must match dimension) and R14 (do not recreate IFC properties); L-03 R7 (examples must stay valid); N-01 R2 (relationship minimality). Emergent outcome: a measure proposed in an audit can be checked against the design model and verified against operational data — the three today live in unconnected tools.

**C6 · Portable evidence for health decisions (Q-04 + Q-05 + Q-07).**
Parts: US Core profiles; CARIN Blue Button consumer claims; OMOP CDM and its analysis tools. Passes: clinical records → profiled FHIR resources → consumer-visible claims; separately, site data → OMOP → portable cohort definitions → summary results. Must stay consistent: **patient identity within a site, and concept identity across sites** (Q-07 R2 vocabulary version). Inherits: Q-04 R3 Must-Support obligations; Q-05's caregiver delegation scope; Q-07's constraint that analyses travel and data does not. Emergent outcome: a claim a patient can read and a cohort a researcher can run are derived from the same underlying record without the record leaving its custodian.

**C7 · Identity continuity across a shutdown (U-04 + O-04 + O-06 + J-04).**
Parts: a decommissioning humanitarian identity provider; a national identity provider; a wallet presentation protocol; an agent-protocol authorization design. Passes: subject identifiers and account mappings from the retiring provider to a successor; presentations for credentials the successor cannot itself assert. Must stay consistent: **the subject identifier per relying party** — the thing that actually breaks at U-04 R4's 31 December 2025 cut-off. Inherits: J-04 R2 (clients must not hold third-party credentials); O-06 R2 (three credential formats). Emergent outcome: a migration plan in which relying platforms keep working, which no single source provides.

**C8 · Agricultural decision provenance (A-01 + A-05 + A-06 + A-03's discipline).**
Parts: BrAPI trial and germplasm data; ADAPT production data; Modus lab results. Passes: work order → sample → analyte result → agronomic recommendation → field operation record → observation. Must stay consistent: **sample identity from order to result** (A-06 R5), **germplasm and study identity** (A-01 R2/R5), and **unit abbreviations** (A-05 R1) across all three. Inherits: A-06's requirement that method identity accompany every analyte; A-05's data-only constraint, so the composition must not smuggle in a toolkit dependency. Emergent outcome: a recommendation can be traced back to the specific sample, method and trial that justify it.

**C9 · Preservation-grade cultural publishing (R-02 + R-05 + R-06 + X-03).**
Parts: OCFL storage layout; a museum IIIF service; a manifest editor; TEI-encoded texts. Passes: source images and transcriptions → OCFL object versions → IIIF manifests → viewers. Must stay consistent: **the object identifier across storage, manifest and citation**, and the rights status attached to it (R-05 R6). Inherits: R-02's self-describing-storage requirement; R-06's obligation that exports open in independent viewers; X-03's prose-schema parity. Emergent outcome: a citation that still resolves after the publishing software is gone — the actual preservation promise.

**C10 · Grid change with a safe floor (D-02 + D-05).**
Parts: PACS configuration management; household export control. Passes: protection settings and network limits downward; DER capability and status upward. Must stay consistent: **the equipment identity and the active limit** between substation configuration and edge control. Inherits: D-05 R5's default fallback control and D-02 R2's edition gate. Emergent outcome: a change at the substation and a change at the inverter can be reasoned about as one system, with a defined safe state when the link fails.

**C11 · Generator — Conformance-Pack Builder (configurator).**
Generator: given (i) a normative schema or API description, (ii) a party-role model, and (iii) a set of acceptance criteria, it emits a runnable conformance pack — scenarios, synthetic fixtures, a counterparty simulator, and a report template that separates implementation defects from standard ambiguities. Inputs it needs: H-06's scenario/party model; G-01's schema; A-01's module structure as a second worked case. **Concrete product it must produce:** a conformance pack for **BrAPI v2.1 Phenotyping**, containing scenarios covering Observation Unit, Observation, Observation Variable, Trait, Scale and Method retrieval; fixtures where trait/scale/method are deliberately mismatched; and a pass/fail report that names which of A-01 R3's entities failed. Invariants: the pack must cite the exact specification version; fixtures must be synthetic (inherited from U-05 R3 where sensitive domains are involved).

**C12 · Generator — Requirement-Card Compiler (configurator).**
Generator: consumes a specification's requirement list (e.g. F-03's DCT/GEN/CLS/PRP/CPR/REL IDs, or B-03's GeMS level criteria) and emits, per requirement, a card carrying the requirement ID, its verbatim text, whether it is machine-checkable, the check if so, and the acceptance evidence if not. **Concrete product it must produce:** a 20-card deck for **bSDD verification**, with GEN-01, PRP-01, PRP-03, PRP-04, CLS-02, CLS-05 and REL-01 marked machine-checkable and carrying executable checks, and the ISO 704 definitional requirements (GEN-09/10/11) marked human-judged with a reviewer prompt. Inherits F-03's finding that **no numeric thresholds exist**, so the compiler must not invent them — a card without a threshold must say so.

---

## 7. STRESS COLLECTION *(separately labeled hypothetical variations — one per selected case; each states the changed condition and its consequences. No inherited requirement is deleted; where a stress makes one unsatisfiable, that is stated.)*

**SV-A-03** *Changed condition:* the council deadline halves and the data vintage arrives two weeks late. *Consequence:* R2's multi-structure capability becomes unaffordable within the cycle; R5's experimental status collides with a decision that cannot wait. AC-P1's benchmark reproduction must be pre-computed, not run in-cycle. Inherited R6 disclaimer becomes more, not less, load-bearing.
**SV-A-05** *Changed condition:* two Architecture Committee members are from firms with incompatible unit conventions and neither concedes. *Consequence:* R3's state machine stalls at "AC review"; components reach mismatched Scores, violating the release constraint. R2's data-only property is what allows a fork to be technically harmless but semantically fatal.
**SV-B-01** *Changed condition:* an implementer ships a product claiming OMF conformance. *Consequence:* nothing in the sampled source can adjudicate the claim — R1's licence is satisfied while the format is undefined. The recorded shortfall becomes a live commercial dispute; AC-P1 (publish element definitions) escalates from proposal to precondition.
**SV-B-06** *Changed condition:* the server is upgraded mid-shift to a WITSML version the desktop tool has not seen. *Consequence:* R1's "any WITSML server" is stressed; R2's schema annotations may render but mislead. R4's submodule now carries the wrong schema set, and the failure is silent rather than a build error.
**SV-C-05** *Changed condition:* a NodeId is reassigned between two released tags. *Consequence:* R2's tagging remains satisfied while AC-P1 fails; every device pinned to the earlier tag misinterprets the node. R4's Mantis route becomes the only remedy, and it is slower than field deployment.
**SV-C-06** *Changed condition:* a supplier reports results for a characteristic that no longer exists in the customer's revised product model. *Consequence:* R3's clockwise generation order is broken — Results precede a valid MBD reference; the traceability invariant fails while each document individually validates.
**SV-D-02** *Changed condition:* a utility uploads real substation SCL files to the shared demo. *Consequence:* R5's shared-database caveat becomes a disclosure incident; R1's mission is unaffected but the project's usability constraint is now a security constraint. Nothing in the source prohibits the upload.
**SV-D-05** *Changed condition:* the DNSP server returns a malformed schedule rather than failing outright. *Consequence:* R5's default fallback may not trigger, because contact was not lost; R6's ramping applies to a nonsense target. AC-S2 tests only the disconnection case, so this path is unverified.
**SV-E-02** *Changed condition:* the catchment gains a large impervious development mid-record, so the continuous simulation spans two different catchments. *Consequence:* R1's long-term continuous mode is satisfied mechanically but meaningless; mass balance (AC-P1) still passes. The model cannot express the discontinuity.
**SV-E-01** *Changed condition:* a contamination event requires an answer within the hour. *Consequence:* R2's source tracing is exactly the needed capability, but R5's "as is" basis and the absence of a stated tolerance (Asm) mean no one can state confidence. The irreversible decision proceeds on unquantified output.
**SV-F-04** *Changed condition:* the designer supplies a model in a newer IFC schema version than the `.ids` targets. *Consequence:* R3's IDS conformance holds while R1's validation silently under-checks; AC-S2's negative test may pass for the wrong reason. The unread entity list (Asm) makes the gap undiagnosable from the README.
**SV-F-03** *Changed condition:* a dictionary passes detailed verification, then its owner publishes a new version adding circular relations. *Consequence:* R20 is violated after the badge was granted; R1's "Active" status persists. Nothing in the read procedure re-checks after badge issuance — AC-S5 covers grant, not maintenance.
**SV-G-01** *Changed condition:* two partners record the same physical move in different time zones, one normalising to UTC. *Consequence:* R1's `eventTimeZoneOffset` is present in both but semantically different; AC-P1's canonical hash diverges. Every downstream reconciliation reports a discrepancy that is not one.
**SV-G-02** *Changed condition:* a regulator mandates a field the CBV does not define, with a six-week deadline. *Consequence:* R1's 100% conformance claim and the new obligation cannot both hold; R2's format plurality multiplies the places the non-standard field must be threaded. R6's stated DPP/EUDR readiness is precisely what is being tested.
**SV-H-04** *Changed condition:* the reference implementation is deployed to a shared test environment with real carrier data "temporarily". *Consequence:* R3 and R4 are violated by construction; every party sees every other party's shipments. The README's warning is the only control and it is not enforced by the artefact.
**SV-H-01** *Changed condition:* an emergency requires a policy effective in five minutes. *Consequence:* R6's 20-minute floor makes the required policy non-conformant; publishing anyway breaks the polling contract, and waiting breaks the emergency response. R7's revocation path is equally constrained.
**SV-I-01** *Changed condition:* an integrator must support two trading partners pinned to different object suites. *Consequence:* R1's dated versioning is satisfied while R2's coexistence becomes a per-partner build. AC-P1's forward compatibility is untested because, per Asm, no governance or compatibility statement was retrievable.
**SV-I-04** *Changed condition:* the migration deadline arrives while a dependent clinical-nutrition product is mid-certification. *Consequence:* R7's dated cut-off is inherited by a party with its own immovable timeline; R2's abridged format becomes the only affordable migration, changing what data the product can show.
**SV-J-06** *Changed condition:* a suggestion arrives whose supporting evidence is commercially confidential. *Consequence:* the process's public nature (Con) excludes it; R1's user problem cannot be substantiated in the open. The standard most supported by evidence is the one least able to enter the process.
**SV-J-04** *Changed condition:* a session ID leaks through a proxy log. *Consequence:* R3's session-ID-in-query design is exposed; R2's trust asymmetry is inverted — the leaked identifier grants what the client was never meant to hold. R7's six-month refresh tokens extend the blast radius.
**SV-K-06** *Changed condition:* the specification URL moves and the old one stops resolving. *Consequence:* the catalogue entry's core function fails while all its fields remain valid; R5's 2021 review date means no scheduled check would catch it. AC-P1's staleness flag is the only proposed defence.
**SV-K-05** *Changed condition:* a vulnerability is found in the archived library while it sits in a live payments path. *Consequence:* R6's read-only status makes R1's validation duty unfixable in place; R4's Apache licence permits a fork, which transfers maintenance to the adopter. AC-P1's exit plan becomes urgent rather than prudent.
**SV-L-03** *Changed condition:* a programme mandates a Level 2 audit element the schema's use case does not require. *Consequence:* R4's Schematron use case must narrow further; R7's example-validity gate now blocks the schema update until examples are extended. The measure-state model (R2) is unaffected but under-specifies why a measure was discarded.
**SV-L-01** *Changed condition:* a publisher emits `@reso.context` citing Data Dictionary 1.6. *Consequence:* R8's 1.7+ floor is breached while R1 is satisfied; R6's instruction to use versioned reference metadata has no valid metadata to use. Behaviour is unspecified (Unres), so consumers will differ.
**SV-M-02** *Changed condition:* a property's obligation level changes between 4.6 and 4.7. *Consequence:* R1's three-version coverage now requires every statement to be version-qualified; the unread obligation classification (Asm) means the change may not be visible in the documentation at all. AC-P1's reconciliation becomes the only detection route.
**SV-X-02** *Changed condition:* a widely used tool ships assuming CF wins every conflict, before the SWG votes. *Consequence:* R6's conflict-handling question is answered de facto by installed base; R2's registration decision loses its freedom. R7's CF-leadership consultation happens after the fact.
**SV-N-01** *Changed condition:* a nightly build is deployed to production analytics by mistake. *Consequence:* R6's nightly artefact carries unreleased classes; R5's backwards-compatibility promise never applied to it. AC-P1's provenance stamp is the only proposed way to tell — it does not yet exist.
**SV-N-02** *Changed condition:* a jurisdiction's law requires publishing a contract stage the release/record model does not represent. *Consequence:* extension (N-04) is forced; R1's published documentation and the local legal text now disagree. The unread stage model (Asm) means the size of the gap cannot be assessed from the sampled source.
**SV-O-04** *Changed condition:* an agency needs higher-assurance proofing than the account currently carries, mid-transaction. *Consequence:* R1's single-account promise and a step-up requirement collide; R2's protocols support conveying it, but the read source does not state how assurance is asserted (Asm). AC-P1 becomes a precondition, not an improvement.
**SV-O-06** *Changed condition:* a member state's wallet supports only ISO mdoc while a relying party accepts only SD-JWT VC. *Consequence:* R2 is satisfied by the specification and violated in deployment; R4's cross-border interoperability fails at negotiation. R5's completed EC assessment ("no identified gaps") did not surface this because it is a deployment, not a specification, gap.
**SV-P-03** *Changed condition:* a vendor implements Resources and Descriptors but not Discovery. *Consequence:* R1 is breached; clients cannot learn versions, so R2's Guidelines conformance is unverifiable. AC-S2 fails while the vendor's marketing claim ("Ed-Fi compatible") is unchallenged without AC-P1's verifier.
**SV-P-04** *Changed condition:* a state changes an option-set value locally to match its statute. *Consequence:* R2's standard option sets are broken at one site; the four physical forms (R3–R5) each need the same local change, and the invariant that an element means one thing everywhere fails silently in the warehouse.
**SV-Q-07** *Changed condition:* two sites run the same cohort definition on different vocabulary versions. *Consequence:* R2's concept identity holds nominally while membership differs; R3's portability is satisfied and the comparison is still invalid. AC-P1's version stamp is the only signal, and it is proposed, not required.
**SV-Q-04** *Changed condition:* a technical correction (x.y.z+1) inadvertently tightens a Must-Support element. *Consequence:* R7's version semantics are violated; certified systems become non-conformant without a version signal. R8's Jira/ballot route would have caught it only if the change was recognised as substantive.
**SV-R-06** *Changed condition:* the museum migrates to IIIF Presentation API 3.0 while the editor still emits 2.1. *Consequence:* R1/R2 remain satisfied by the editor and manifests stop opening in the institution's new viewers; the unstated version (Asm) means curators cannot tell which manifests are affected.
**SV-X-03** *Changed condition:* a widely used element must be withdrawn for scholarly reasons. *Consequence:* R1's single-source build makes the removal mechanically easy and semantically destructive — decades of encoded texts reference it. The "minimally obsolescent" ambition (Con) argues for deprecation, but no register exists (P-X-03-2).
**SV-S-03** *Changed condition:* a funder demands a single headline CO2e figure with no qualifiers. *Consequence:* R3's per-category quality evaluation and R6's data-availability caveat are stripped in presentation; the number becomes indefensible while every underlying requirement was met. AC-P1 exists precisely to forbid this.
**SV-S-02** *Changed condition:* ORDS moves from v0.3 to v0.4 with a renamed category. *Consequence:* R1's version declaration saves new consumers and breaks historical aggregation; C2's category-key invariant fails. R3's publication lag means the break is discovered a quarter late.
**SV-T-05** *Changed condition:* the household reclassifies the worker as an independent contractor. *Consequence:* R1's verification obligation may or may not attach — R2's determination is the whole question, and the unread source (Asm) cannot settle it. The consequence of error falls asymmetrically on the worker.
**SV-T-01** *Changed condition:* the agreed schedule changes weekly because the care recipient's needs fluctuate. *Consequence:* R2's "shared understanding" must survive constant amendment; a static signed document (AC-S1-unverified) becomes stale immediately. R1's voluntary status means nothing compels re-execution.
**SV-U-05** *Changed condition:* a partner publishes an HXL-tagged file using real case records "with names removed". *Consequence:* R3's fictional-data requirement is violated; re-identification risk in trafficking data is severe and irreversible. R5's licence ambiguity means there is no agreed instrument to prohibit it.
**SV-U-04** *Changed condition:* the 31 December 2025 decommissioning proceeds with three platforms unmigrated. *Consequence:* R1's OIDC service stops; subject identifiers become unresolvable, so accounts cannot be matched to a successor afterwards. R5's instruction was advisory; the archived repository (Con) means no extension is possible.
**SV-E-03** *Changed condition:* EPA publishes new domain values without a WQX version bump. *Consequence:* R2's regeneration condition ("only if the version of WQX is updated") is never met, so code lists silently go stale; AC-S2's no-change expectation is satisfied while data is rejected downstream.
**SV-B-03** *Changed condition:* a map is compiled from two surveys with different location-confidence conventions. *Consequence:* R3's `LocationConfidenceMeters` is populated throughout and comparable nowhere; R7's validation passes. AC-S3 is met while the map's uncertainty is uninterpretable — an unrecorded semantic, not a structural, failure.
**SV-H-06** *Changed condition:* a scenario fails identically for every adopter. *Consequence:* the likeliest cause is R2's other purpose — a defect in the standard — but the framework reports implementation non-conformance. Without the proposed ambiguity ledger, adopters each work around it differently and the standard fragments.
**SV-E-04** *Changed condition:* an import translation maps a local code to the wrong EPA value. *Consequence:* R2's fully automated submission carries the error into the federal record with no human checkpoint; R5 is satisfied because translation occurred. Only the proposed audit trail makes the error recoverable.
**SV-I-05** *Changed condition:* USDA republishes the dataset with a changed nutrient number meaning. *Consequence:* R4's search index and stored records retain the old semantics; served values look correct and mean something else. The undocumented refresh process (Asm) means no one is watching for it.
**SV-Q-05** *Changed condition:* a member revokes caregiver access while the caregiver's app holds an unexpired token. *Consequence:* R1's low-friction access works against the revocation; the read source does not model delegation lifecycle, so revocation semantics are undefined. The proposed delegation ceremony is where this must be resolved.
**SV-A-06** *Changed condition:* a lab adopts a new extraction method and reports it under the existing analyte name. *Consequence:* R5's consumption path works, R1's coverage is intact, and agronomic recommendations shift with no visible cause. R4's method-proposal process exists precisely to prevent this and was bypassed.
**SV-A-01** *Changed condition:* a server implements Phenotyping but not Core. *Consequence:* R3's observations cannot resolve to R2's Studies; the modular structure permits the deployment and the data is uninterpretable. R6's search services return results that cannot be contextualised.
**SV-N-04** *Changed condition:* the EU profile and the eForms profile define the same field with different cardinality. *Consequence:* a publisher subject to both cannot satisfy either without violating the other; N-02's core remains valid throughout. The overlap report (P-N-04-2) is the only proposed detection.
**SV-S-04** *Changed condition:* a manufacturer's self-assessed repairability score is challenged by an independent teardown. *Consequence:* R2's self-assessment basis offers no adjudication route; R3's parameters are stated but their weighting is not in the read material. Because the source is SNIPPET-only, even the challenge criteria are unverified here.
**SV-R-02** *Changed condition:* the repository software is decommissioned and only the storage remains. *Consequence:* this is R1's design case, not a failure — but AC-P1's cold reconstruction has typically never been rehearsed, and R4's fixtures live in a separate repository that may not have been retained alongside the content.
**SV-R-05** *Changed condition:* an object's rights status changes from open to restricted after manifests are cached widely. *Consequence:* R6's exclusion applies to new requests only; previously distributed manifests still cite image URLs. R4's stable addressing, a virtue for citation, becomes the propagation mechanism.

## 8. Recorded shortages, assumptions and open feasibility questions

1. **Egress policy** confined READ status to GitHub-hosted material. Sections T and S-04, and B-04, are SNIPPET-only; every requirement drawn from them is marked unverified.
2. **ISIC Rev. 5** section titles verified only for A–F (fetch truncation); Rev. 4 letters used as the organizing frame (decision D1).
3. **FORD** classification text not verifiable at source (oecd.org blocked); the six-field list is SNIPPET.
4. **Per-category origin shortfalls** (below the five-distinct-origins target): G (2), L (2–3), F (3), I (4), P (4), S (4), Q (4 organizations / 7 projects). Recorded, not repaired.
5. **B-01 (Open Mining Format)**: the sampled repository contains only a LICENSE file. No element types, geometry types, version or governance text exists at the cited URL. I withheld all substantive claims about OMF rather than importing them from search summaries.
6. **Sources whose read page lacked the substance the case needed** (recorded per brief): H-04 (no API version/event list), I-01 (page load error), N-02 (no stage/release model), O-04 (no IAL description), Q-05 (no profile detail), Q-07 (no mission/governance), R-06 (no Presentation API version), X-03 (no cadence/governance/licence), M-02 (no obligation classification), N-04 (no extension mechanics), A-01 (no conformance/licence), A-06 (no mandatory fields), R-02 (no inventory/versioning rules — they are in the spec documents at ocfl.io, not fetched).
7. **Unresolved meaning, G-01**: the returned schema summary asserted six core event types and enumerated five. Not resolved.
8. **Open feasibility questions retained**: whether CF-on-Zarr can be registered without a precedence rule (X-02 R6); whether an unlicensed sensitive-data standard can be adopted by UN agencies (U-05 R5); whether U-04's decommissioning can preserve subject identifiers at all; whether F-03's ISO 704 definitional requirements can be machine-checked in any useful degree; whether B-01's format can be conformance-tested absent published normative text.
9. **Composition dependency worth flagging**: C2's category-key invariant depends on S-01 (ORDS itself), which was in the inventory but not sampled — so the composition inherits a requirement from an unread source and is marked accordingly.

Mapping notes:
- **A-03**: Experimental lifecycle and the scientific-product disclaimer are HG01 basis limits: fitted output is SCENARIO evidence until accepted (A11 sign-off). Structural hypothesis comparison across model types is an experiment over models (F22) with declared tolerances (P347). Benchmark reproduction is accuracy-gated measurement (P268).
- **A-05**: Data-only, no toolkit dependency, is an environment-binding constraint. The change-proposal state machine is a guarded lifecycle model (P216); 'no component released before its counterparts' is an object-level readiness barrier across components (P257).
- **B-01**: Degenerate case: the source carries no requirements beyond a licence. The construction is the catalogue's missing-source path: an acquisition gap (O01) recorded with the unresolved location of the normative text (P204, P300), and any conformance rule blocked for lack of accepted source meaning. Rediscovered origin from iteration 1, where mirrors supplied the content.
- **B-06**: Schema version negotiated on connect is the HG00 envelope version shared by all plug-ins; a non-recursive clone silently losing schemas is a dependency-manifest defect (P249). DataReplay is deterministic replay of a recorded stream (P241, P327).
- **C-05**: A NodeId meaning the same node in all three artefacts is one identity across three representations; effective immutability after release is an append-only identity ledger (P263). Member-only drafts versus public release is a release policy under F06.
- **C-06**: Every result citing a planned characteristic defined in the product model is the design-to-manufacturing correspondence claim R17 with characteristic identity as the join key; units resolving through one shared component is P282. Byte-stable regeneration except timestamps is canonical file digesting (P51).
- **D-02**: CIM to SCL mapping with consistent equipment identity is an approved transform with reconciled destination (P310); the edition gate is dialect-specific validation (P261). The shared demo database is a missing tenant isolation contract (V07), which the catalogue blocks for utility data.
- **D-05**: First case with F35 as the primary family. Default DER control on loss of the utility server is the C07 timeout_path with a C04 safe default; ramping is a declared actuation contract under R20; certification by one DNSP is an accepted operating envelope (R28) scoped to that provider.
- **E-02**: Mass balance across hydrology, hydraulics and quality modules is the conservation invariant under R14 and O09; public-domain release with no upstream integrity guarantee is a sealed-bundle obligation on the consumer (P249, P265); continuity error under a stated percentage is the declared tolerance regime (P347).
- **E-01**: Quality transport bound to the hydraulic timestep and topology is a C01 handoff with a shared clock contract; a 14-year input compatibility window is explicit migration and dialect validation (P261, P245); the 'as is' basis forbids promoting model output to observation (HG01).
- **F-04**: A ruleset decoupled from any one checker is the replaceable-engine posture (C13, P197 shape); the unread entity list inside the .ids file is an unresolved input carried explicitly.
- **F-03**: Registration to Active to badge is an admission lifecycle with separate gate decisions (P199); cycle detection in class parentage and relations is shape and completeness validation (P194); 'Unit must match the Dimension' is P282 verbatim; the no-threshold qualitative gates are a human evidence boundary (C11).
- **G-01**: TransformationEvent inputs consumed and outputs produced is lot linkage through transformation (P160, P177); eventTime with eventTimeZoneOffset preserved is the boundary time field carrying local time; canonical event hashing across partners is P262. The five-versus-six event-type discrepancy is a discovery-side unresolved input.
- **G-02**: Semantic equivalence across three serialisations is two-representation equivalence with round trip (P197, P30); the test-data generator is generated-input evidence (P344); larger-than-memory conversion is a measured resource envelope (P268) declared in the boundary resources field. Self-declared conformance stays an open claim until independently checked.
- **H-04**: The missing authorisation layer is a missing provider capability, which blocks every party-scoped read under the catalogue's rule that missing authority blocks the effect; the README's warning becomes an HG00 authority field that the deployment cannot populate.
- **H-01**: Rules scoped by geography and vehicle state are spatially scoped decisions (P326, P192); prev_policies is an append-only supersession chain (P263); the 20-minute lead is a publication timing contract in the boundary time field (P244). The informational user rule is a declared non-enforceable modality.
- **I-01**: Thin source. Dated suites with stable code meanings are versioned dialects (P261) over a code-list identity registry; the unreadable README is an acquisition gap (P204) carried as unresolved.
- **I-04**: Three renderings of one interface agreeing is representation equivalence (P197); paged browsing is stable-snapshot pagination (P294); the dated cut-off is a recurring-obligation deadline inherited by dependents (P215).
- **J-06**: Problem statement surviving unchanged into the challenge is stable span mapping with semantic-change detection (P195); one proposal per challenge reaching the Board is a guarded lifecycle transition with transferred authority (P216, P199); stage latency is interval accounting (P231).
- **J-04**: Clients never holding third-party credentials is a permitted-edge rule (P207); session identifiers and refresh rotation are authority state with expiry and freshness fences (P244); local baked-in credentials versus multi-tenant remote is the deployment axis (A17).
- **K-06**: A catalogue entry is a typed catalog record (P79) whose review date is a review clock (P255); a proprietary licence is an obligation the entry references but does not resolve, carried in the authority field. Staleness is expiry (A14).
- **K-05**: An archived dependency with no fix path is lost support in the dependency closure (P345) and a pinned sealed bundle (P249, P265); the exit plan to a successor is least-cost repair (P272) with substitution (C13). XML to JSON to XML survival is round trip (P30).
- **L-03**: Rediscovered document from iteration 1 (F-02). The three measure states proposed, implemented, discarded are lifecycle labels (P208); a use case never contradicting the base schema is a refinement constraint on scoped rules (P198).
- **L-01**: Local fields ignorable but never fatal is the explicit extra-field policy P302 requires on every boundary; the version URN is the schema-version field; human-friendly versus OData enumerations mapping to one meaning is P282. Same organisation as iteration 1's L-01, different document.
- **M-02**: One documentation branch per schema version with no divergence from the XSD is single-source publication (X11) plus document-content comparison (P120); three live versions force version-qualified statements, the schema-version boundary field.
- **X-02**: Two conventions encoding one fact differently is a contradiction register over accepted mappings (P193) with alternatives retained until adjudicated (P281); CRS in grid_mapping versus proj: is reference metadata preservation (P325); the SWG vote with CF consultation is a decision record with transferred authority (P13).
- **N-01**: Tests must use a generated, not hand-edited, artefact: reproducible build (P318); minor releases largely backward compatible and removals needing a major version is the change-propagation contract; nightly builds indistinguishable from releases is a missing HG00 producer-version stamp.
- **N-02**: Thin source; same organisation as iteration 1's O-02 with the substantive model unread here. Documented fields existing in the schema and vice versa is single-source generation with a divergence gate.
- **O-04**: A stable subject identifier per agency pairing is identity scoped by tenant (the boundary identity field with purpose scope); assurance level is an attribute of the authority field the source does not populate, carried as unresolved.
- **O-06**: Format negotiation among three credential formats is a capability-envelope check (P343) preceding exchange (P302); selective disclosure is P264; the v1.0 pin with a v1.1 moving target is a versioned adoption decision under R30.
- **P-03**: Same organisation as iteration 1's P-03, different document. Three specifications satisfied together is a conjunction of obligations; Discovery advertising versions is the capability envelope a client reads before binding (P343); 'compatible' as a vendor assertion is an open claim until P266 tests run.
- **P-04**: One element meaning the same thing across four physical forms is typed column lineage from one definition (P297) with generated representations (P307, P310); normalised and denormalised forms pulling apart is why generation from one source is mandatory, which the catalogue states as X11.
- **Q-07**: Same organisation as iteration 1's Q-02, thinner document. Analyses travel and data does not is federated evaluation under a nonleaking release contract (P264); vocabulary version differences silently changing cohorts is the schema-version boundary field on results (P261).
- **Q-04**: Must-Support as a certification obligation is a required consumer assumption checked at the boundary (P302); a technical correction that must not change conformance is the preserved-contract check of controlled regeneration (P346); ballot votes are decision records with workgroup authority (P13).
- **R-06**: An exported manifest opening in an independent viewer is the independent-oracle rule (I-class) applied to a generated artefact; a pinned obsolete Node version is a dependency-manifest hazard (P249); the unstated Presentation API version is the missing schema-version field.
- **X-03**: Rediscovered document from iteration 1 (H6-01), thinner read. Prose and schema never disagreeing because both derive from one source is X11; maximally expressive and minimally obsolescent implies deprecation over deletion, the F32 register.
- **S-03**: Category-keyed lookups with a quality grade travelling with each number are exact allocation (P169) with origin-aware support groups from manufacturers, journals and agencies (P296) and a declared partial population (V03); share-alike licence propagation is an inherited authority obligation (F08). Unstated system boundary for pre-use CO2e is an unresolved unit-and-meaning input (P282).
- **S-02**: Same organisation as iteration 1's S-01, the data rather than the standard. A three-month lag between cut-off and release is known-at versus valid time (P211) declared on the release; counts stated per release are count reconciliation (P294); the rejects report is the excluded-contributor lineage the catalogue keeps by default.
- **T-05**: Snippet-only. Employee-versus-contractor determination is applicability evaluation (P192, P258); completion within a timeframe and retention are gate decisions with calendar obligations (P199) and bounded retention (P342); producing the record without exposing unrelated data is field-level filtering (P264).
- **T-01**: Snippet-only. A template rendered into an executed bilingual agreement held identically by both parties is agreement version binding (P259) with document generation (P25, P151); agreed rate and hours feeding pay records is billable-line construction (P156). Voluntary status is a declared modality (MAY) in the meaning field.
- **U-05**: Structure public while instances cannot be is the split between an accepted schema and protected-unit data (P298, P264); fictional examples only is generated test data (P344, GP01) applied to a sensitive domain; the licence blocker is missing authority that blocks adoption, kept explicit.
- **U-04**: Decommissioning is controlled deactivation with closure evidence (P256) over the dependency closure of relying platforms (P345); the stable subject identifier that breaks is the identity equivalence that a successor mapping must preserve (P284). No named successor is an unresolved input the catalogue keeps as a blocked migration edge.
- **E-03**: A JSON adaptation of an XML federal standard that must map back losslessly is an approved transform with reconciled destination (P310) and round trip (P30); coupling of the code-list version to the WQX version is a pinned sealed dependency (P265) in the closure (P345).
- **B-03**: Existence, identity and location confidence on every contact and fault is uncertainty as a first-class boundary field (A10, P323); _ID keys repeated as foreign keys with no orphans is referential shape validation (P194); DataSourceID on every feature is per-record provenance (P204). ArcGIS binding is an environment binding.
- **H-06**: Scenarios versioned with the standard is the schema-version field on the test artefact; simulating the absent counterparty is a substitute execution environment (C13, P289); distinguishing an implementation defect from a standard ambiguity is disagreement measurement over independent implementations (P288) feeding a contradiction register (P193). - **I-05**: Full-text index and ranked excerpts are P122 and P123; a second datastore added by implementing one interface is replaceable storage adapters with query-result equivalence (P203); nutrient numbers keeping their FDC meaning is the meaning field on the loader boundary. Undocumented refresh is an unresolved dependency trigger.

### Findings

**Verdicts.** 54 COVERED, 0 PARTIAL, 0 UNCOVERED, 0 NO-WITNESS. 54 of 54 stress variations are expressed by an axis value, variation rule or proof record. Cumulative after two iterations: 108 of 108 cases construct; 107 of 108 stresses are expressed; no extension proposal has been needed.

**A broader sample than iteration 1.** The blind agent's second draw contains fourteen software tools, engines or reference implementations (FIMS, WITSML Studio, CoMPAS, open-dynamic-export, SWMM, EPANET, OpenEPCIS, DCSA TNT, Moov iso20022, Login.gov, Humanitarian ID, fdc-api, Open Waters, the Bodleian manifest editor), six governance or verification procedures (ADAPT's nine-state process, bSDD verification, OPC UA release tagging, the UK open standards process, the GDS catalogue, US Core's ballot route), three legal or regulatory instruments (MDS policy, I-9, EU right to repair) and three scientific models (FIMS, SWMM, EPANET). As a result F35 has its first two primary cases (D-05, D-02), F21 its first two primary cases (SWMM, EPANET), F27 its first primary case (QIF), and F36, F18, F22, F15, F13 and F14 are all exercised again. R17 (design-to-manufacturing correspondence) is exercised for the first time through QIF. Every chain X01 to X12 has now appeared at least once; X03 appears twice.

**Still never exercised after two iterations.** Families F12 (routing, packing, network allocation), F19 (diagnosis and next-evidence selection) and F20 (trade-off, portfolio and allocation decisions); requirements R01, R26 and R32; variation rule V14, which is excluded by contract. F11, F16, F23 and F38 were hit in iteration 1 only. The absence of F12, F19 and F20 across 108 GitHub-hosted cases is consistent with the discovery bias: optimisation, diagnosis and allocation needs are rarely published as open specifications.

**Where the catalogue named what the source lacks.** Twelve stress cases land on a catalogue rule the real artefact is missing: whole-input validation before actuation for a malformed schedule (open-dynamic-export); a tenant-isolation contract for a shared demo (CoMPAS); a review clock after a badge is granted (bSDD); a declared canonicalisation before hashing across time zones (EPCIS); an authority envelope that travels with the deployment rather than the README (DCSA TNT); an emergency profile with its own lead-time contract (MDS); a preserved-contract check on technical corrections (US Core); a capability-envelope test rather than specification conformance (EUDI wallets, Ed-Fi Discovery); content-hash rather than version-label dependency keys (WQX domain values); interaction tests across profiles (OCDS extensions); a freshness fence on delegated authority (CARIN caregivers); reconciliation against destination readback before an irreversible regulatory submission (Open Waters).

**Thin and degenerate fits.** B-01 (OMF) is a degenerate case: the sampled URL holds a licence and nothing else, so the construction is the catalogue's missing-source path. It is also the same origin iteration 1 sampled, where mirrors supplied the content. Nine cases were read thinly by the agent (H-04, I-01, N-02, O-04, Q-05, Q-07, R-06, X-03, M-02) and their mappings rest on the structure the agent could see.

**No-anchor ledger additions.** Four new entries: integrated state-space population-dynamics fitting (FIMS); measurement-plan to result binding for dimensional metrology (QIF); IEC 61850 substation protection-configuration semantics (CoMPAS); minimality of a relationship set as a guarded design constraint (Brick); and ISO 704 definitional quality as a human-judged gate (bSDD). Cumulative no-anchor entries: 17.

**Rediscovery.** Four documents recur from iteration 1 (Modus, BuildingSync, OMF, TEI) and nine further organisations recur with a different document (RESO, Ed-Fi, OHDSI, DCSA, Open Contracting Partnership, Open Repair Alliance, buildingSMART, GS1 EPCIS, BrAPI). Unique origin clusters after two iterations: 104 of 108 cases. Saturation is not yet visible at the document level.

**Composed wholes.** The ten compositions fall on X09, X08, X12, X07, X03, X06 (twice), X04, X11 and X10; the two generators are F37 configurators over generated protocol cases (C11) and over a requirement relation with an explicit human-judged partition (C12). C5 (building information spine) is the first sampled composition on X03, the configured-product-to-maintained-asset chain.

**Discovery-side shortages, kept separate.** ISIC T again has zero readable sources; ISIC G and L drew from pools below the five-origin target with cluster-adjacent picks; nine thin reads; the EPCIS five-versus-six event-type discrepancy is unresolved on the agent's side.

**Process notes.** The received prompt hash is identical to iteration 1's. The final message again arrived in two continuation blocks and was joined by the committed extractor. No PR exists; the branch is pushed after this iteration.

### Cross-scale family checks (analyst reasoning, no primary sources; blind-agent prompt unchanged)

Seven patterns surfaced by iteration 2's cases, chosen to differ from iteration 1's eight, each taken across regimes with the record form that regime actually keeps.

#### SC9. Simulation as evidence: model output versus observation

Source cases: E-01 EPANET, E-02 SWMM, A-03 FIMS, D-02 rehearsal, H-01 policy rehearsal proposal.

| Regime | Data form kept in that regime | Family mapping | Holds? |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Engineering: hydraulic and digital twins, hardware in the loop | Scenario runs `{model_version, params, inputs, outputs, t, validity_scope, tolerance}` alongside SCADA observations | F21 + HG01 SCENARIO basis + R16 + P322, P347 | holds |
| Biology and pharmacology: in-silico models, PK/PD | Model predictions paired with assay results `{compound, model, predicted, observed, CI}` | F21 + F18 + P268 accuracy gating | holds |
| Economics and finance: stress tests, macro models | Scenario tables `{scenario, assumptions, projected_metric, horizon}`; regulators accept the output as evidence by rule | F21 + F20 shape + A11 sign-off | holds; acceptance is an explicit authority act |
| Climate: gridded model output in CF and GeoZarr conventions | Arrays with convention metadata `{variable, grid_mapping, source: model or obs}` | F21 + F28 + P325 | holds; the source-versus-model flag is the basis field |
| Law: mock trials, moot courts | Rehearsal records; never evidence | F21 shape only | holds; the regime itself forbids promotion |

Verdict: HOLDS. Every regime keeps model output and observation apart and admits promotion only by an explicit acceptance act; the catalogue's basis field and A11 sign-off are that act. The "as is" disclaimer on EPANET and SWMM is the same rule in prose.

#### SC10. Reflexive conformance: testing implementations tests the standard

Source cases: H-06 Conformance Gateway, F-03 bSDD, G-02 OpenEPCIS, C-05 OPC UA, P-03 Ed-Fi, O-06 EUDI, Q-04 ballot.

| Regime | Data form | Family mapping | Holds? |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Metrology: inter-laboratory round robins, gauge R&R | `{lab, sample, method, result, consensus, deviation}`; the reference sample calibrates labs and exposes method ambiguity | F31 + I-class oracle independence + P288 | holds |
| Law: litigation as statute testing | Case law `{case, clause, holding, ambiguity_found}`; rulings feed drafting | F31 + F05 + P193, P281 | holds |
| Immunology: self versus non-self testing with clonal selection | Repertoire tables `{clone, antigen, affinity, tolerance}`; the test set updates the tester | F31 shape + C05 iteration | holds at model level |
| Sport: referee rulings clarifying rules | Ruling logs `{match, incident, rule, decision}` | F10 + F05 | holds |
| Software: conformance suites and reference implementations | Scenario results `{scenario, party, step, outcome, standard_version}` | F31 + P266, P343, P267 | holds |

Verdict: HOLDS. The round-robin principle is the catalogue's oracle-independence rule; uniform failure across independent implementations is evidence against the standard in every regime, which is P288 feeding P193.

#### SC11. Decommissioning and orphaned dependencies

Source cases: U-04 Humanitarian ID, K-05 archived library, I-04 NDB sunset, R-06 pinned Node, B-01 empty repository, I-01 unreachable README.

| Regime | Data form | Family mapping | Holds? |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Software and services: end-of-life notices | `{asset, eol_date, successor, migration_path, dependents}` | F32 + F07 + P345, P256, P272 | holds |
| Ecology: keystone species removal and trophic cascades | Food webs `{species, depends_on, strength}`; removal effects propagate through the web | P345 closure shape | holds; nature enumerates dependents by consequence, the catalogue by manifest |
| Cell biology: apoptosis and senescence | Programmed shutdown with cleanup signals `{cell, signal, phagocytosed_by}` | F07 closure with P256 evidence | holds |
| Business: product end-of-life and spare-parts obligations (S-04) | `{model, last_sale, parts_until, service_until}` | F08 + P215, P185 | holds |
| Infrastructure: bridge closure, utility decommissioning | Successor-service plans `{asset, closure_date, alternative_route}` | F16 + F32 | holds |

Verdict: HOLDS. The rule "enumerate dependents before removal" is universal; only engineered and legal regimes add a named successor mapping, which is why P256's closure evidence lists dependents and successors and ecology does not.

#### SC12. Uncertainty as a first-class field

Source cases: B-03 GeMS confidence fields, S-03 quality grades, A-03 experimental status, E-01 no stated tolerance, H-01 informational user rule.

| Regime | Data form | Family mapping | Holds? |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Geology: confidence per contact and fault | `{feature, existence_conf, identity_conf, location_conf_m, source}` | A10 uncertainty + P323 | holds |
| Genomics: Phred quality per base | `{position, base, phred}`; uncertainty travels with every record | A10 + boundary numeric field | holds |
| Metrology: GUM uncertainty budgets | `{measurand, value, u, k, contributions}` | P323 propagation + P347 | holds |
| Law: standards of proof | `{claim, standard: preponderance, clear and convincing, beyond reasonable doubt, finding}` | A06 four-state evidence + F31 | holds |
| Finance: ratings with outlooks, value at risk | `{instrument, rating, outlook, VaR, horizon}` | F18 + A06 probability | holds |

Verdict: HOLDS. Every regime records uncertainty as a field beside the value and refuses comparison across undeclared conventions; the GeMS stress (two surveys, two confidence conventions) is Phred versus a different quality scale, which the meaning field catches.

#### SC13. Delegated authority and its revocation

Source cases: J-04 client, server and third party; Q-05 caregivers; O-04 one account across agencies; O-06 wallets; R-05 rights by user class; T-05 identity documents.

| Regime | Data form | Family mapping | Holds? |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Law: power of attorney, guardianship, agency | Registers `{principal, agent, scope, purpose, granted, expires, revoked}` | F06 + P206 + authority field (principal, resource, action, purpose, delegation, expiry) | holds |
| Computing: OAuth scopes, capability tokens | `{subject, scope, audience, iat, exp, revocation}` | F06 + P206, P244 | holds |
| Endocrinology: hormonal instruction with receptor gating | `{signal, receptor, target, downregulation}`; released molecules act until degraded | F06 shape; revocation as expiry | holds; unrevocable in-flight effects match the unexpired-token stress |
| Business: procurement delegation limits | Delegation matrices `{role, limit, approver, valid_from, valid_to}` | F06 + F05 | holds |
| Military and civil chains of command | Orders with authority references `{order, issuer, authority_ref, validity}` | F06 + P196 dependency closure of authority | holds |

Verdict: HOLDS. The authority sub-fields the catalogue requires (scope, purpose, delegation, expiry) appear in every regime's register; the unexpired-token problem is universal and is what the freshness fence exists for.

#### SC14. Additive extension without breaking the core

Source cases: N-04 OCDS extensions and profiles, N-01 Brick minor releases, C-05 companion specifications, L-01 ignorable local fields, X-02 convention registry, P-04 four forms.

| Regime | Data form | Family mapping | Holds? |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Bacterial genetics: plasmids and horizontal gene transfer | `{host, plasmid, incompatibility_group}`; two plasmids of one group cannot coexist | F37 feature model + P274 interaction tests | holds; incompatibility groups are the pairwise conflict check |
| Law: amendments layered on statutes | `{statute, amendment, inserts, repeals}` | F32 + P212 | holds |
| Contracts: riders and annexes | `{contract, annex, precedence_clause}` | F08 + P292 conflict strategy | holds |
| Language: loanwords | Lexicon entries with origin | F04 | holds trivially |
| Software: semantic versioning, protocol extension negotiation | `{core_version, extension_id, additive, conflicts_with}` | F37 + P260, P273, P302 | holds |

Verdict: HOLDS. The OCDS stress (two profiles, one field, two cardinalities) is the plasmid incompatibility case: additivity holds per extension and fails pairwise, which is why the catalogue demands interaction tests rather than per-extension validation.

#### SC15. Timing contracts between publisher and consumer

Source cases: H-01 20-minute lead time, I-04 sunset date, K-06 review date, S-02 publication lag, U-04 decommission date, D-05 ramp rates.

| Regime | Data form | Family mapping | Holds? |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Signal processing: sampling theorem | `{signal_bandwidth, sample_rate}`; poll rate must exceed twice the change rate | R19, R20 timing + C07 | holds; MDS's 20-minute lead is a sampling condition on policy polling |
| Ecology: phenology and pollinator matching | `{species, event, date}`; a mismatch is a broken lead-time contract | A05 + P215 | holds |
| Molecular biology: mRNA half-life versus transcription rate | `{transcript, synthesis_rate, half_life}` | A05 expiry + P244 passive fence | holds |
| Finance: settlement cycles, notice periods | `{trade, T, settle_at, notice_days}` | P224 + P215 | holds |
| Law: sunset clauses and statutory notice | `{instrument, effective, sunset, notice_period}` | F08 + A14 expiry | holds |

Verdict: HOLDS. Publish-then-poll with a minimum lead is the same contract as Nyquist sampling and as phenological matching; the catalogue's time field with lead, expiry and poll interval is sufficient in every regime.

#### Break watch after iteration 2

No pattern broke. Iteration 1's candidate crack, shared state with no owner, did not recur in a damaging form: X-02's shared meaning between two conventions has divided ownership resolved by a vote, which the catalogue expresses through F10 authority. Two new observations for the watch list. First, voluntary instruments (the DOL sample agreement, the GDS catalogue entry, self-assessed repairability scores) form a modality gradient from mandatory through recommended to voluntary and self-declared; the catalogue's meaning field carries modality, but the gradient's effect on evidence strength is carried only by A11 acceptance, which is coarse. Second, the E-01 stress shows a decision proceeding on model output with no statable tolerance; the catalogue can only record the decision under an explicit unknown state, which is honest but is not a construction of the decision itself. Neither is a break; both are places where later iterations should look for a case the catalogue cannot express.

# ITERATION 3 — BLIND AGENT RESPONSE (verbatim, model selector opus)

Research complete: 112 sourced cases across all 21 ISIC sections and all 6 OECD FORD fields, seeded sample drawn, briefs and collections below.

# CONSTRUCTION BRIEF COLLECTION — real needs, imagined constructions, composed systems

## 0. METHOD, CLASSIFICATIONS, SCOPE

**Classifications used**
- **UN ISIC Rev. 5** (endorsed by the UN Statistical Commission at its 54th session, March 2023; 21 sections A–U at the top level). Verified via [UNSD ISIC](https://unstats.un.org/unsd/classifications/Econ/isic) and [Introduction to ISIC Rev.5](https://unstats.un.org/unsd/classifications/Econ/Download/In%20Text/ISIC5_Intro_11Mar2024.pdf) — SNIPPET. Section letters A–U are stable between Rev.4 and Rev.5, so cases assigned by letter are valid under either; where a case's activity moved between revisions I note it.
- **OECD Fields of Research and Development (FORD)**, Frascati Manual 2015 revised FOS classification: 6 major fields / 42 minor. Verified via [Frascati Manual 2015](https://www.oecd.org/en/publications/frascati-manual-2015_9789264239012-en.html) and [ARIS FORD listing](https://www.aris-rs.si/en/gradivo/sifranti/inc/FORD1-eng.pdf) — SNIPPET. Fields: F1 Natural sciences, F2 Engineering and technology, F3 Medical and health sciences, F4 Agricultural and veterinary sciences, F5 Social sciences, F6 Humanities and the arts.

**Coverage achieved:** 27 categories (21 ISIC + 6 FORD). Every category has ≥5 eligible cases from distinct originating projects/organisations. Per-category counts: A5 B5 C5 D5 E5 F5 G5 H5 I6 J5 K5 L5 M9 N5 O5 P5 Q5 R8 S5 T6 U6 | F1:6 F2:15 F3:7 F4:5 F5:13 F6:6. **No category shortage.** Shortages of a different kind (source-access, not case-count) are recorded in §0.3.

**Read-status convention**
- `READ` — original text fetched and read.
- `READ-P` — original repository/document fetched, but the fetched file carried only part of the requirement text (usually a README rather than the normative body); everything drawn from it is limited to what that file actually said.
- `SNIPPET` — search-result titles/snippets only. **Every requirement taken from a SNIPPET source is unverified against the original.**

25 cases are READ, 17 READ-P, 70 SNIPPET.

**0.1 Environment constraint that shaped the corpus.** WebFetch in this environment resolves only `github.com` and `raw.githubusercontent.com`; every other host returns EGRESS_BLOCKED. I therefore biased discovery towards primary sources whose normative text is version-controlled on GitHub, and accepted snippet status for regulation, ISO/OASIS/Codex/WHO/ILO texts, and PDFs hosted elsewhere. This is a *sampling bias in the corpus*, not a claim about which needs matter: standards published as PDFs by treaty bodies are under-read relative to their real-world weight.

**0.2 Origin-cluster rule.** Copied specifications, shared templates and trivial variants are grouped. Clusters recorded in the inventory. Two cluster caveats:
- **OC-MOBILITYDATA:** GTFS (CB-H01) and GBFS (CB-H02) are distinct originating projects (GTFS from Google/TriMet, 2006; GBFS from NABSA) but are **today co-stewarded by MobilityData**. The sampler treated them as distinct clusters and drew both for section H. I record this rather than silently redraw: they are distinct specifications with distinct governance histories and distinct normative content, but they are not distinct *stewards* today.
- **OC-WHO** spans three distinct WHO programmes (Sanitation Safety Planning CB-E03, SMART Guidelines CB-Q01, HWT Scheme CB-T05). Treated as distinct projects; shared parent recorded.
- **GDST (CB-A04) is built on GS1 EPCIS (CB-G02)** — distinct organisations, inherited technical substrate. Recorded as a dependency, not a cluster merge.

**0.3 Recorded scope decisions**
1. **Brief depth.** Full construction briefs (§2) are written for the 54 sampled cases. The remaining 58 sourced cases appear in the frozen inventory (§1) with origin, source, URL, version/date and read status, but not with full atomic-requirement decomposition. Reason: 112 full briefs exceeds a returnable message. This is a deliberate, recorded narrowing of *presentation*, not of discovery — the inventory is complete and was frozen before sampling.
2. **Cross-listing.** Three cases are eligible in two categories (CB-C03 in C and T; CB-G05 in G and I; CB-P05 in P and U). Sampling was without replacement across the whole draw, so a cross-listed case selected once cannot be drawn again.
3. **Interpretation of "eligible."** A case is eligible if a primary source states a required outcome or constraint that some identifiable party must satisfy. Marketing pages and secondary commentary do not qualify on their own; two cases (CB-C04 EU DPP, CB-G04 ARTS/UnifiedPOS) rest partly on secondary commentary and are flagged `SNIPPET-SECONDARY`.

---
