## 2. CONSTRUCTION BRIEFS — the 54 sampled cases

Legend for provenance: **[S]** source fact · **[I]** my interpretation · **[H]** hypothetical addition. Acceptance criteria marked **(source)** follow the source; **(proposed — requires acceptance)** are mine.

---

### A — Agriculture, forestry and fishing

**CB-A03 · ICAR animal data exchange + cattle milk recording · ISIC A · FORD 4**
*Beneficiary/situation:* A dairy herd manager and the national milk-recording organisation that certifies her yields; robotic milking systems from three vendors write to three formats, and payment, breeding value and certification all hang on the same records. *Source:* ICAR, "Section 2 — Guidelines for Dairy Cattle Milk Recording" and "Section 15 — Guidelines for Data Exchange", https://www.icar.org/Guidelines/02-Overview-Cattle-Milk-Recording.pdf and https://www.icar.org/Guidelines/15-Data-Exchange.pdf, plus ADE standards page — **SNIPPET** (all requirements below unverified against the original).
*Required deliverables (source):* a data-exchange specification usable between recording devices and recording databases; approval/checking procedures for milk-recording devices.
*Available inputs:* AMS/parlour event streams, ICAR-approved animal ID devices, lab sample results.
*Atomic requirements:* `A03-R1` [S] the animal must be marked with an ICAR-approved identification device or system. `A03-R2` [S] the sample, the milk weight and the cow ID must be linked at the milking. `A03-R3` [S] milk-recording devices must read animal identification with ≥98% success rate. `A03-R4` [S] AMS data may be exchanged either in the manufacturer's format on request or via the ICAR ADE milking-data standard. `A03-R5` [S] the ADE specification is open-source and free to use and covers dairy cows, milking sheep, beef cattle, sheep, farmed buffalo and deer.
*Component relationships:* ID device → milking event → sample vial → lab result. What passes: `animalID`, `milkingTimestamp`, `milkWeight`, `sampleID`. Must remain consistent: the animal identity across all four, and the milking occasion as the join key (`A03-R2` is precisely the anti-decoupling requirement).
*Whole-product constraints:* species coverage beyond cattle; vendor neutrality; the 98% read rate is a device-level gate that constrains system-level claims.
*Acceptance criteria:* `A03-AC1` **(source)** device ID read success ≥98% under the approval/checking procedure. `A03-AC2` **(source)** every recorded milk weight resolves to exactly one cow ID and one sample. `A03-AC3` **(proposed — requires acceptance)** a round-trip test moves one herd-day of AMS data through ADE and back with zero loss of animal-occasion linkage.
*Assumptions/unknowns:* the exact ADE message schema, its versioning policy, and whether the 98% figure is per-device or per-installation were not verified. Unresolved: whether "success rate" is measured on first read or after retries — this materially changes device design.

**CB-A02 · AgStack Asset Registry (GeoID) · ISIC A · FORD 4**
*Beneficiary/situation:* A smallholder co-op agronomist and the buyer's due-diligence team need to say "this is the same field" across a carbon registry, a traceability system and a satellite analytics vendor, without any of them sharing farmer identity. *Source:* AgStack/Linux Foundation, asset-registry, https://github.com/agstack/asset-registry — **READ-P** (README only; endpoint list and matching rules not in the fetched file). Supporting: Linux Foundation press announcement of a global field-boundary dataset — SNIPPET.
*Required deliverables (source):* an API that registers a geospatial asset and returns a unique identifier.
*Available inputs:* field polygons as WKT, GPS traces, mobile capture (e.g. TerraTrac).
*Atomic requirements:* `A02-R1` [S] register an asset — typically a polygon, but any geospatial geometry — and return a 256-bit / 16-character alphanumeric unique ID (GeoID). `A02-R2` [S] the API registers the asset using its WKT representation and returns only the GeoID. `A02-R3` [S] GeoIDs are deterministic identifiers for any geospatial feature (fields, points, forests, countries). `A02-R4` [I] because only the GeoID is returned, the registry is usable as a privacy-preserving join key: ownership data need not travel with it.
*Component relationships:* boundary capture → registry → downstream analytics. What passes: geometry in, GeoID out. Must stay consistent: the geometry-to-GeoID function, or two parties computing the same field get different IDs.
*Whole-product constraints:* determinism under small geometry perturbation is the hard problem — two tracings of the same field differ by metres.
*Acceptance criteria:* `A02-AC1` **(source)** registering a WKT polygon returns a 16-character alphanumeric GeoID. `A02-AC2` **(proposed — requires acceptance)** two independent field walks of the same parcel, differing by ≤5 m RMS, resolve to a matched GeoID under the registry's matching call.
*Assumptions/unknowns:* the S2-cell approach and the tolerance/matching semantics were not in the fetched README — genuinely open. Feasibility question retained: is deterministic identity under geometric noise achieved by tolerance matching, by canonicalisation, or not at all?

### B — Mining and quarrying

**CB-B01 · Open Mining Format v2 · ISIC B · FORD 2**
*Beneficiary/situation:* A resource geologist hands an orebody model to an independent engineer for a feasibility review; today the model crosses vendor boundaries as a lossy export and the review re-does work already done. *Source:* Global Mining Guidelines Group, OMF, https://github.com/gmggroup/omf and https://gmggroup.org/omf/ — **READ** (repo README) + SNIPPET (GMG pages).
*Required deliverables (source):* an open industry-specific file format for 3D mining data, published open-source with source code and documentation on GitHub, plus a reference implementation.
*Available inputs:* drillhole data, wireframes, surfaces, block models.
*Atomic requirements:* `B01-R1` [S] OMF v1 (2017) supports points, lines, surfaces, meshes and volumes. `B01-R2` [S] v2 extends support to block models — computer-generated representations of orebodies. `B01-R3` [S] all source code and documentation are published to GitHub. `B01-R4` [S] the first v2 implementation is in Rust, released open-source. `B01-R5` [I] the format's purpose is interchange, so element-attribute association must survive a round trip.
*Component relationships:* writer → OMF container → reader. What passes: geometry elements plus attribute arrays bound to vertices/cells. Must stay consistent: the coordinate reference and the element-to-attribute binding, or grades detach from blocks.
*Whole-product constraints:* open licensing; a beta-status format constrains what may be claimed to regulators; block models are large, so container efficiency is a real constraint.
*Acceptance criteria:* `B01-AC1` **(source)** a file containing points, lines, surfaces, meshes, volumes and (v2) block models reads back in a conforming implementation. `B01-AC2` **(proposed — requires acceptance)** round trip through two independent implementations preserves block grades to the stated numeric precision.
*Assumptions/unknowns:* v2 remains beta; the normative schema text was not read. Unresolved: whether OMF carries provenance of estimation runs or only their outputs — this decides if it can support audit.

**CB-B05 · EITI Standard 2023, beneficial ownership · ISIC B · FORD 5**
*Beneficiary/situation:* A citizen journalist and a national multi-stakeholder group (MSG) trying to establish who actually profits from a licence, in a country where politically exposed persons hold stakes through nominees. *Source:* EITI, 2023 EITI Standard, Requirement 2.5 (with 2.2, 2.3, 2.6), https://eiti.org/sites/default/files/2023-06/2023%20EITI%20Standard.pdf — **SNIPPET**.
*Required deliverables (source):* public disclosure of beneficial ownership of companies operating in the country's extractive industries.
*Available inputs:* licence registers, company registries, MSG risk assessments.
*Atomic requirements:* `B05-R1` [S] the objective is to enable the public to know who ultimately owns and controls companies operating in the extractive industries, particularly those the MSG identifies as high risk. `B05-R2` [S] full disclosure of beneficial ownership by politically exposed persons is requested regardless of level of ownership. `B05-R3` [S] countries are encouraged to adopt a disclosure threshold of 10% or lower. `B05-R4` [S] where state-owned enterprises hold beneficial ownership, the name of the state, level of ownership, and detail of how ownership or control is exerted must be specified. `B05-R5` [I] the MSG is the authority that designates "high risk", so the scope of disclosure is set locally, not globally.
*Component relationships:* licence register ↔ company register ↔ PEP list. What passes: company identity, natural-person identity, ownership percentage, control mechanism. Must stay consistent: the company identifier across registers, and the definition of "control" used by each.
*Whole-product constraints:* divided authority (MSG vs. state registrar); irreversibility — publishing a natural person's ownership cannot be undone and carries safety consequences.
*Acceptance criteria:* `B05-AC1` **(source)** for each in-scope company the disclosure names the beneficial owner(s), the level of ownership and how control is exerted. `B05-AC2` **(source)** PEP holdings appear with no threshold applied. `B05-AC3` **(proposed — requires acceptance)** every high-risk company designated by the MSG has a non-null disclosure or a published reason for its absence.
*Assumptions/unknowns:* the exact wording of 2.5 and its sub-clauses is unverified. Unresolved: whether "encouraged to adopt 10%" is a requirement or a recommendation — that distinction decides whether a compliance product can test it.

### C — Manufacturing

**CB-C03 · Global Village Construction Set · ISIC C (and T) · FORD 2**
*Beneficiary/situation:* A rural workshop that wants to build and maintain its own tractor, brick press and sawmill rather than import them, and needs the designs to be modular enough that one person can fix one module. *Source:* Open Source Ecology, GVCS, https://wiki.opensourceecology.org/wiki/Global_Village_Construction_Set and https://www.opensourceecology.org/gvcs/ — **SNIPPET**.
*Required deliverables (source):* fabrication-ready plans for 50 industrial machines, documented for multiple audiences (graphical lists, hierarchical machine/module breakdowns on Dozuki, wiki module lists).
*Available inputs:* the existing module library, prior machine builds, the stated cost target.
*Atomic requirements:* `C03-R1` [S] the set comprises 50 machines that together allow building "a small, sustainable civilization with modern comforts", including 3-D printer, backhoe, bakery oven, bulldozer, cement mixer, tractor, metalworking set, earth brick press, sawmill, power module. `C03-R2` [S] machines are developed by module-based design, so modules can be developed in parallel. `C03-R3` [S] documentation is maintained at different places for different target groups. `C03-R4` [S] cost target: on average about eight times cheaper than comparable market machinery. `C03-R5` [S] GVCS 1.0 is targeted for 2028.
*Component relationships:* module library → machine → product ecology of machines. What passes: shared modules (power unit, hydraulics, frame) and their interfaces. Must stay consistent: module interface dimensions and power couplings — the whole parallel-development claim rests on them.
*Whole-product constraints:* the 8× cost ratio is a design constraint on every module, not a marketing figure; documentation must serve builders with different skill levels concurrently.
*Acceptance criteria:* `C03-AC1` **(source)** a machine is complete when it has an economically-feasible business model, not merely a working prototype. `C03-AC2` **(proposed — requires acceptance)** a module swapped between two GVCS machines fits and functions without rework.
*Assumptions/unknowns:* the interface standard between modules was not located. Unresolved feasibility: whether the 8× cost ratio survives once safety-critical components (hydraulics, ROPS) are included.

**CB-C05 · OpenTitan silicon root of trust · ISIC C · FORD 2**
*Beneficiary/situation:* A device maker who must prove to a regulator and to customers that firmware on shipped hardware is the firmware that was signed, and who cannot audit a proprietary root of trust. *Source:* lowRISC CIC, OpenTitan, https://github.com/lowRISC/opentitan and Security Model Specification, https://opentitan.org/book/doc/security/index.html — **SNIPPET**.
*Required deliverables (source):* an open-source silicon root of trust with a Security Model Specification defining logical security properties of the discrete IC.
*Available inputs:* the monorepo (hardware, software, utilities), IP documentation.
*Atomic requirements:* `C05-R1` [S] the Security Model Specification covers device and software attestation, provisioning, secure boot, chip lifecycle, firmware update, chip identity and chip ownership transfer. `C05-R2` [S] the repository is a monorepo with all components in one repository. `C05-R3` [S] the OpenTitan trademark is licensed only to implementations conforming to OpenTitan standards. `C05-R4` [S] endorsement by Silicon Creators is contingent on adherence to physical implementation guidelines and standard requirements. `C05-R5` [I] chip ownership transfer is the state-machine requirement that distinguishes this from a boot ROM: authority over the device changes hands during its life.
*Component relationships:* Silicon Creator → chip identity → owner → firmware signer. What passes: identity certificates, ownership records, attestation evidence. Must stay consistent: the chip identity across lifecycle stages and across ownership transfer.
*Whole-product constraints:* irreversible effects (fusing lifecycle state), divided authority (creator vs. owner), and a trademark gate that binds conformance to naming.
*Acceptance criteria:* `C05-AC1` **(source)** conformance to OpenTitan standards is the condition of trademark use and Silicon Creator endorsement. `C05-AC2` **(proposed — requires acceptance)** after ownership transfer, the previous owner's signing key can no longer produce firmware the device accepts, while chip identity remains verifiable.
*Assumptions/unknowns:* the normative security-model text was not read. Unresolved: what evidence a *third party* (not the owner) can obtain about lifecycle state.

### D — Electricity, gas, steam and air conditioning supply

**CB-D05 · Green Button / ESPI · ISIC D**
*Beneficiary/situation:* A householder who wants an efficiency analyst to see her interval consumption without handing over her utility login, and a utility that must not release data to anyone the regulator has not authorised. *Source:* Green Button Alliance, OpenESPI Resource Server, https://github.com/GreenButtonAlliance/OpenESPI-Resource-Server; ESPI = NAESB REQ.21 — **SNIPPET**.
*Required deliverables (source):* a standardised machine-to-machine interface permitting a Data Custodian to share energy usage or retail customer information with authorised third parties; reference Data Custodian, Third Party, Common and OAuth2 authorization-server implementations.
*Available inputs:* utility interval meter data, OAuth2 infrastructure, regulator's authorisation rules.
*Atomic requirements:* `D05-R1` [S] ESPI is a standardised machine-to-machine interface permitting a Data Custodian to share Energy Usage Information or Retail Customer Information held by that custodian with Authorized Third Parties. `D05-R2` [S] a Third Party permitted to receive that information must have met the requirements of the applicable regulatory authority and governing documents. `D05-R3` [S] Green Button implements NAESB REQ.21 ESPI. `D05-R4` [S] the reference stack separates Data Custodian, Third Party, Common and OAuth2 Authorization Server modules. `D05-R5` [I] authorisation is therefore two-layered: the customer's consent *and* the regulator's prior qualification of the third party.
*Component relationships:* customer → authorization server → data custodian → third party. What passes: an access grant scoped to a customer, a period and a data granularity. Must stay consistent: the customer/account identity between the custodian's records and the grant.
*Whole-product constraints:* divided authority; revocation must propagate; data is personal and retrospective.
*Acceptance criteria:* `D05-AC1` **(source)** an unqualified third party cannot obtain usage information even with customer consent. `D05-AC2` **(proposed — requires acceptance)** revoking a grant stops delivery within one billing-data refresh cycle and leaves no further usable token.
*Assumptions/unknowns:* NAESB REQ.21 normative text not read; certification requirements not verified.

**CB-D01 · OCPI 2.3.0 · ISIC D · FORD 2**
*Beneficiary/situation:* A driver whose home-country subscription must open a charge point abroad and produce one correct bill; the charge point operator and the e-mobility provider have no contract other than the roaming protocol. *Source:* EV Roaming Foundation, OCPI, https://github.com/ocpi/ocpi — **READ**.
*Required deliverables (source):* a core specification plus separately released optional modules covering EV roaming.
*Available inputs:* charge point telemetry, tariff catalogues, token registries.
*Atomic requirements:* `D01-R1` [S] the most recent specification is OCPI 2.3.0. `D01-R2` [S] the specification is organised as a core document with separately released optional modules. `D01-R3` [S] core roaming modules include Tokens, Sessions, CDRs, Tariffs, Locations, Commands and Charging Profiles. `D01-R4` [S] additional optional modules include Payments and Bookings. `D01-R5` [I] the CDR is the settlement artefact; Sessions and Tariffs must be reconcilable to it or roaming billing disputes have no resolution path.
*Component relationships:* Locations (what exists) → Tokens (who may use it) → Commands (start/stop) → Sessions (what happened) → Tariffs (what it costs) → CDRs (what is owed). What passes: token identity, session identity, tariff reference. Must stay consistent: the tariff in force at session start must be the tariff applied in the CDR.
*Whole-product constraints:* modules version separately, so a partner may implement a subset — capability discovery is mandatory in practice; money is at stake, so CDRs are effectively immutable once issued.
*Acceptance criteria:* `D01-AC1` **(source)** a conforming implementation exposes the modules it supports per the 2.3.0 core plus declared optional modules. `D01-AC2` **(proposed — requires acceptance)** for a completed roaming session, the CDR total recomputes exactly from the session's energy/time records and the tariff referenced at session start.
*Assumptions/unknowns:* no publication dates in the fetched README; "Edition 2" planned. Unresolved: how tariff changes mid-session are meant to be handled.

### E — Water supply; sewerage, waste management, remediation

**CB-E05 · Basel Convention e-waste technical guidelines · ISIC E**
*Beneficiary/situation:* A customs officer and a refurbisher facing the same container: is this a shipment of reusable equipment or a transboundary movement of hazardous waste? The answer decides whether prior informed consent was required. *Source:* Basel Convention Secretariat, technical guidelines on transboundary movements of e-waste and used EEE, in particular the distinction between waste and non-waste, https://www.basel.int/implementation/ewaste/technicalguidelines/developmentoftgs/tabid/2377/default.aspx — **SNIPPET**.
*Required deliverables (source):* guidelines that distinguish waste from non-waste for used electrical and electronic equipment in transboundary movement.
*Available inputs:* functionality test results, packaging and documentation evidence, Annex II/Y49 and A1181 listings.
*Atomic requirements:* `E05-R1` [S] the guidelines were adopted on an interim basis and are non-legally binding. `E05-R2` [S] equipment includes components removable from equipment that can be tested for functionality and either directly reused or reused after repair or refurbishment. `E05-R3` [S] waste EEE is waste including all components, sub-assemblies and consumables that are part of the equipment at the time it becomes waste. `E05-R4` [S] non-hazardous e-wastes are listed in Annex II as Y49; hazardous e-wastes under A1181. `E05-R5` [S] both hazardous and non-hazardous e-waste transboundary movements are subject to the Prior Informed Consent procedure.
*Component relationships:* functionality test → classification → PIC notification → movement. What passes: the test record, the classification, the notification reference. Must stay consistent: the physical consignment identity between test record and notification.
*Whole-product constraints:* non-binding status against binding PIC consequences — the guidance is advisory but its outcome is not; irreversible effects (a wrongly classified export cannot be recalled).
*Acceptance criteria:* `E05-AC1` **(source)** each consignment is classified as waste or non-waste with the functionality evidence the guidelines contemplate. `E05-AC2` **(source)** movements classified as e-waste (Y49 or A1181) carry PIC. `E05-AC3` **(proposed — requires acceptance)** classification decisions are reproducible: two officers applying the guideline to the same evidence reach the same class.
*Assumptions/unknowns:* the specific functionality-test protocol was not read. Unresolved: how partially functional lots are treated.

**CB-E03 · WHO Sanitation Safety Planning (2nd ed.) · ISIC E · FORD 3**
*Beneficiary/situation:* A municipal sanitation manager whose service chain runs from pit latrines through informal emptiers to a treatment works and irrigation reuse — with farm workers and emptiers exposed at points nobody monitors. *Source:* WHO, *Sanitation Safety Planning: step-by-step risk management for safely managed sanitation systems*, 2nd edition, 2022, https://www.who.int/publications/i/item/9789240062887 — **SNIPPET**.
*Required deliverables (source):* a sanitation safety plan covering the whole service chain, including a monitoring plan (Module 5).
*Available inputs:* the 2018 WHO Guidelines on sanitation and health, the 2006 guidelines on safe use of wastewater/excreta/greywater, HACCP methods, Stockholm framework.
*Atomic requirements:* `E03-R1` [S] SSP is a step-by-step risk-based approach for local-level risk assessment and management across the sanitation service chain — containment, conveyance, treatment, and end use or disposal. `E03-R2` [S] the approach follows the Stockholm framework and uses HACCP methods and procedures. `E03-R3` [S] Module 5 develops a monitoring plan that regularly checks the system operates as intended and defines what to do if it is not. `E03-R4` [S] operational and verification monitoring provide assurance to operators, the public and authorities of adequate system performance. `E03-R5` [S] operational monitoring data should help managers make corrections that prevent hazard breakthrough. `E03-R6` [S] the 2022 edition is simplified, aligned to the 2018 guidelines, and strengthens climate risk assessment.
*Component relationships:* hazard analysis → control points → operational monitoring → corrective action → verification. What passes: hazard events, control limits, monitoring results, correction records. Must stay consistent: the exposure group definitions across every step (workers, farmers, consumers, community).
*Whole-product constraints:* the whole chain, including informal actors, must be in scope; corrections must be possible *before* breakthrough, which constrains monitoring latency.
*Acceptance criteria:* `E03-AC1` **(source)** a monitoring plan exists that checks the system operates as intended and states what to do when it does not. `E03-AC2` **(source)** operational monitoring is timely enough to permit corrections that prevent hazard breakthrough. `E03-AC3` **(proposed — requires acceptance)** every exposure group identified in hazard analysis has at least one control with a defined operational limit.
*Assumptions/unknowns:* module numbering and the climate-risk additions unverified. Unresolved: how informal emptiers — who are neither employees nor licensees — are brought under a monitoring plan's authority.

### F — Construction

**CB-F02 · Brick Schema · ISIC F · FORD 2**
*Beneficiary/situation:* An energy analyst asked to deploy the same fault-detection application across 40 buildings whose BMS point names are each a different local dialect. *Source:* Brick Consortium, https://github.com/BrickSchema/Brick — **READ**.
*Required deliverables (source):* a uniform metadata schema for buildings: an RDF class hierarchy, a minimal set of relationships, and a method of encapsulation.
*Available inputs:* BMS point lists, equipment schedules, existing Brick class sources (`bricksrc/equipment.py`, `bricksrc/point.py`).
*Atomic requirements:* `F02-R1` [S] Brick is an open-source, BSD-licensed uniform schema for representing metadata in buildings. `F02-R2` [S] it comprises an RDF class hierarchy of building subsystems, entities and equipment. `F02-R3` [S] it defines a minimal, principled set of relationships connecting entities into a directed graph representing a building. `F02-R4` [S] it provides a method of encapsulation for composing complex components from lower-level ones. `F02-R5` [S] Brick models producers and consumers of data as "points" — a sensor is the data source, not the physical sensing device. `F02-R6` [S] versioning is semantic; minor releases roughly every 6 months carry largely backwards-compatible extensions.
*Component relationships:* point → equipment → location, joined by the minimal relationship set. What passes: the graph, plus the mapping from Brick points to live BMS addresses. Must stay consistent: the point-identity mapping, or an application reads the wrong sensor.
*Whole-product constraints:* portability of applications is the purpose, so the schema must be sufficient for "canonical applications" without local extension; backwards compatibility across minor versions.
*Acceptance criteria:* `F02-AC1` **(source)** the model expresses the points, equipment and locations required by the target application using Brick classes and relationships. `F02-AC2` **(proposed — requires acceptance)** an unmodified application runs against two independently authored Brick models of different buildings and returns correct results in both.
*Assumptions/unknowns:* conformance rules were not stated in the fetched README. Unresolved: what "minimal, principled" excludes — the boundary with application-specific modelling.

**CB-F04 · OSHA 29 CFR 1926 Subpart M · ISIC F**
*Beneficiary/situation:* A site foreman sequencing a leading-edge pour with three trades working concurrently, who must decide before work starts which system protects each worker. *Source:* US OSHA, 29 CFR 1926 Subpart M, §1926.501 Duty to have fall protection, §1926.502 systems criteria, §1926.503 training, https://www.osha.gov/laws-regs/regulations/standardnumber/1926/1926.501 — **SNIPPET**.
*Required deliverables (source):* provision of a compliant fall protection system per activity, plus training.
*Available inputs:* the site plan, surface strength assessment, system options.
*Atomic requirements:* `F04-R1` [S] each employee on a walking/working surface with an unprotected side or edge 6 feet (1.8 m) or more above a lower level shall be protected by guardrail systems, safety net systems or personal fall arrest systems. `F04-R2` [S] the 6-foot trigger applies to unprotected sides and edges, leading edges, hoist areas, holes, formwork, rebar assemblies, roofing work and wall openings. `F04-R3` [S] the employer shall determine whether walking/working surfaces have the strength and structural integrity to support employees safely. `F04-R4` [S] employees shall be allowed to work on those surfaces only when the surfaces have the requisite strength and structural integrity. `F04-R5` [S] Subpart M separates duty (501), system criteria (502) and training (503).
*Component relationships:* surface determination → system selection → system criteria → training. What passes: the determination record, the selected system, the training record per worker. Must stay consistent: the mapping from *activity and location* to *system*, because the duty is activity-specific.
*Whole-product constraints:* the strength determination is a precondition, not a parallel task — `F04-R4` gates access; concurrent trades mean one surface may carry several duties at once.
*Acceptance criteria:* `F04-AC1` **(source)** no employee is exposed at ≥6 ft without one of the three named systems. `F04-AC2` **(source)** no employee works on a surface before the employer's strength determination. `F04-AC3` **(proposed — requires acceptance)** for every worker-hour logged at height, a system and a training record resolve.
*Assumptions/unknowns:* §1926.502 criteria not read. Unresolved: how the duty is allocated when several employers' workers occupy one surface.

### G — Wholesale and retail trade; repair of motor vehicles

**CB-G03 · EU Reg. 2018/858 Art. 61 — repair and maintenance information access · ISIC G**
*Beneficiary/situation:* An independent garage that can diagnose a fault but cannot complete the repair because the security-relevant procedure sits behind a manufacturer portal it is not approved to use. *Source:* Regulation (EU) 2018/858 Art. 61(2) and Annex X (as amended), referencing ISO 18541 Parts 1–5, https://eur-lex.europa.eu/legal-content/EN/TXT/HTML/?uri=CELEX:52022XC0729(02) — **SNIPPET**.
*Required deliverables (source):* manufacturer websites providing standardised access to OBD and repair/maintenance information for independent operators; an approval/authorisation route for security features.
*Available inputs:* manufacturer technical documentation, diagnostic tool references, software downloads.
*Atomic requirements:* `G03-R1` [S] manufacturers must make vehicle OBD information and RMI available on their own websites (Art. 61(2)). `G03-R2` [S] access must be unrestricted and standardised, covering OBD information, diagnostic and other equipment tools with complete references, available software downloads and RMI. `G03-R3` [S] access is via websites using a standardised format. `G03-R4` [S] the technical requirements and procedures reference Parts 1 to 5 of ISO 18541. `G03-R5` [S] independent operators covered include repairers, spare-part distributors, diagnostic-tool manufacturers and technical-information publishers. `G03-R6` [S] access to certain vehicle security features is provided only to independent operators complying with requirements including approval of the operator and authorisation of their employees by accredited entities.
*Component relationships:* accreditation body → operator approval → employee authorisation → security-feature access. What passes: accreditation status, operator identity, employee identity, scoped access token. Must stay consistent: employee authorisation must be revocable independently of operator approval.
*Whole-product constraints:* two competing goods held simultaneously — competitive access and vehicle security; divided authority between manufacturer, accreditation body and regulator.
*Acceptance criteria:* `G03-AC1` **(source)** a non-approved independent operator can obtain all non-security RMI in the standardised format. `G03-AC2` **(source)** security-relevant functions are reachable only after operator approval and employee authorisation by an accredited entity. `G03-AC3` **(proposed — requires acceptance)** withdrawal of an employee's authorisation blocks security-feature access at next use without disturbing the operator's other staff.
*Assumptions/unknowns:* ISO 18541 text not accessible; the current consolidated Annex X wording unverified.

**CB-G01 · Peppol BIS Billing 3.0 · ISIC G**
*Beneficiary/situation:* A supplier invoicing a public buyer across a border, whose invoice is rejected for reasons it cannot see, and an accounts-payable team that needs machine-routable invoices. *Source:* OpenPEPPOL, peppol-bis-invoice-3, https://github.com/OpenPEPPOL/peppol-bis-invoice-3 and https://docs.peppol.eu/poacc/billing/3.0/bis/ — **SNIPPET**.
*Required deliverables (source):* a Core Invoice Usage Specification of EN 16931 with Schematron validation artefacts.
*Available inputs:* EN 16931 semantic model, UBL 2.1 syntax, CEN TC434 rules.
*Atomic requirements:* `G01-R1` [S] the repository contains the resources making up Peppol BIS Billing 3.0, and instance documents compliant with it are compliant with EN 16931. `G01-R2` [S] Peppol BIS is a CIUS based on CEN/EN 16931:2017. `G01-R3` [S] the specification combines the EN 16931 semantic model with UBL 2.1 XML syntax. `G01-R4` [S] validation uses Schematron files where PEPPOL-prefixed rules are OpenPEPPOL's and CEN-prefixed rules are CEN TC434's — roughly 60 Peppol rules atop roughly 120 EN 16931 base rules. `G01-R5` [S] PEPPOL-EN16931-R010 requires the buyer electronic address (EndpointID with a valid EAS scheme identifier) so the invoice can be routed. `G01-R6` [S] PEPPOL-EN16931-R003 makes the buyer reference mandatory even though it is optional in base EN 16931.
*Component relationships:* semantic model → syntax binding → rule set → network routing. What passes: the invoice document plus its endpoint identifiers. Must stay consistent: the EAS scheme and identifier, which are simultaneously business data and routing data.
*Whole-product constraints:* a CIUS may restrict but not contradict the base standard; validation must be reproducible by both parties.
*Acceptance criteria:* `G01-AC1` **(source)** the instance passes both CEN and PEPPOL Schematron rule sets. `G01-AC2` **(source)** EndpointID with valid EAS present; buyer reference present. `G01-AC3` **(proposed — requires acceptance)** rejection messages cite the failing rule identifier so the sender can self-correct without support contact.
*Assumptions/unknowns:* rule counts are approximate in the snippet; current rule release unverified.

### H — Transportation and storage

**CB-H02 · GBFS v3.1-RC3 · ISIC H**
*Beneficiary/situation:* A trip planner and a city analyst consuming a shared-vehicle feed; a rider is directed to a bike that was taken four minutes ago, and a policy team cannot tell whether a gap is an outage or a real absence of vehicles. *Source:* MobilityData, GBFS, https://github.com/MobilityData/gbfs/blob/master/gbfs.md — **READ**.
*Required deliverables (source):* a set of JSON feed files with defined required/conditionally required status.
*Available inputs:* operator fleet and station telemetry, geofencing rules.
*Atomic requirements:* `H02-R1` [S] `gbfs.json` is REQUIRED (as of v2.0); `system_information.json` is REQUIRED; `manifest.json` is conditionally REQUIRED for multi-geography publishers. `H02-R2` [S] `station_information.json` and `station_status.json` are conditionally required for docked systems; `vehicle_status.json` for dockless. `H02-R3` [S] all files MUST be valid JSON. `H02-R4` [S] all endpoints MUST use HTTPS. `H02-R5` [S] REQUIRED files MUST NOT 404. `H02-R6` [S] all data MUST be UTF-8 encoded. `H02-R7` [S] near-realtime endpoints `station_status.json` and `vehicle_status.json` SHOULD be as close to realtime as possible, but in no case more than 5 minutes out of date. `H02-R8` [S] every response requires `ttl` — seconds before the data will be updated again (0 if it should always be refreshed). `H02-R9` [S] `geofencing_zones.json` is OPTIONAL. `H02-R10` [S] `vehicle_availability.json` (added v3.1-RC2) is OPTIONAL and describes future availability for systems allowing advance reservation.
*Component relationships:* discovery (`gbfs.json`) → system description → status feeds → optional zones/availability. What passes: system and vehicle/station identifiers, `last_updated`, `ttl`. Must stay consistent: station and vehicle IDs between the information and status feeds.
*Whole-product constraints:* freshness bound of 5 minutes is a hard ceiling on staleness, and `ttl` is the consumer's polling contract; a required file that 404s breaks the whole feed rather than degrading it.
*Acceptance criteria:* `H02-AC1` **(source)** required files return valid UTF-8 JSON over HTTPS and never 404. `H02-AC2` **(source)** near-realtime data is never more than 5 minutes stale. `H02-AC3` **(source)** each response carries `ttl`. `H02-AC4` **(proposed — requires acceptance)** every ID in a status feed resolves in the corresponding information feed.
*Assumptions/unknowns:* v3.1-RC3 is a release candidate — field-level detail may change before final.

**CB-H01 · GTFS Reference · ISIC H**
*Beneficiary/situation:* A small transit agency publishing a feed that a dozen apps must read identically, and a rider in a demand-responsive zone with no fixed stop. *Source:* GTFS Reference, revised 27 April 2026, https://github.com/google/transit/blob/master/gtfs/spec/en/reference.md — **READ**.
*Required deliverables (source):* a dataset of defined files with per-field presence conditions.
*Available inputs:* schedule data, zone geometries, translations.
*Atomic requirements:* `H01-R1` [S] Required files: `agency.txt`, `routes.txt`, `trips.txt`, `stop_times.txt`. `H01-R2` [S] `stops.txt` is required unless demand-responsive zones exist in `locations.geojson`. `H01-R3` [S] "Required" means the field or file must be included in the dataset and contain a valid value for each record. `H01-R4` [S] "Conditionally Required" means the field or file must be included under conditions outlined in the field or file description. `H01-R5` [S] `feed_info.txt` is Conditionally Required — required if `translations.txt` is provided, recommended otherwise.
*Component relationships:* agency → routes → trips → stop_times → stops/zones. What passes: agency, route, trip, stop identifiers and stop sequences. Must stay consistent: referential integrity across the ID chain; a trip without resolvable stop_times is unusable.
*Whole-product constraints:* the conditional-requirement machinery means feed validity is *contextual*, so validators must evaluate conditions, not just presence.
*Acceptance criteria:* `H01-AC1` **(source)** all Required files are present and every record carries valid values in Required fields. `H01-AC2` **(source)** where `translations.txt` is present, `feed_info.txt` is present. `H01-AC3` **(proposed — requires acceptance)** a feed with demand-responsive zones and no `stops.txt` validates, and a fixed-route feed without `stops.txt` fails.
*Assumptions/unknowns:* the demand-responsive (`locations.geojson`) semantics were only partly visible in the fetched section.

### I — Accommodation and food service

**CB-I01 · Codex CXC 1-1969 General Principles of Food Hygiene + HACCP annex · ISIC I**
*Beneficiary/situation:* A 20-seat kitchen and its inspector: the operator must produce a food safety plan proportionate to a small operation, and the inspector must judge it against an internationally recognised basis. *Source:* Codex Alimentarius Commission, CXC 1-1969, General Principles of Food Hygiene (rev. 2020), including the HACCP annex, https://iawg-fsq.org/docs/9287838d-3e49-46bf-9ffa-3b096b7c167d — **SNIPPET**.
*Required deliverables (source):* a HACCP-based food safety plan resting on prerequisite Good Hygiene Practices.
*Available inputs:* process flow, hazard data, monitoring capability, records.
*Atomic requirements:* `I01-R1` [S] HACCP is described in 7 principles set out in an annex to CXC 1-1969. `I01-R2` [S] all seven principles must be applied in the HACCP system. `I01-R3` [S] Codex HACCP is not a standard or list of requirements but defines principles for creating a food safety plan with implementation recommendations. `I01-R4` [S] GHPs and prerequisite programmes should provide the foundation for an effective HACCP system. `I01-R5` [S] implementation guidance runs from assembling the HACCP team through establishing documentation and record keeping.
*Component relationships:* GHP prerequisites → hazard analysis → CCPs → limits → monitoring → corrective action → verification → records. What passes: hazards, control limits, monitoring results. Must stay consistent: the process flow diagram used for hazard analysis and the one actually operated.
*Whole-product constraints:* principle-based, not prescriptive — so any product built on it must supply the missing specificity without claiming Codex authority for it; scale-proportionality is explicit.
*Acceptance criteria:* `I01-AC1` **(source)** all seven principles are applied. `I01-AC2` **(source)** GHP prerequisites are in place before HACCP is credited. `I01-AC3` **(proposed — requires acceptance)** each CCP has a measurable limit, a monitoring frequency, a named responsible person and a defined correction.
*Assumptions/unknowns:* the rev. 2020 text was not read directly; the exact wording of the seven principles is unverified.

**CB-G05 · Open Food Facts data + write API · ISIC I (and G)**
*Beneficiary/situation:* A shopper with a nut allergy scanning a barcode in a shop aisle, and a volunteer contributing the photo that makes the next scan work. *Source:* Open Food Facts, API documentation, https://openfoodfacts.github.io/openfoodfacts-server/api/ — **SNIPPET**.
*Required deliverables (source):* an API to add or edit products; derived data returned as a function of what is contributed.
*Available inputs:* barcode, product photos, nutrition panel, ingredient list, category and labels.
*Atomic requirements:* `G05-R1` [S] `code` (barcode), `user_id` and `password` are required when adding or editing a product. `G05-R2` [S] submitting nutritional values and category yields the Nutri-Score. `G05-R3` [S] submitting ingredients yields NOVA group, additives, allergens, normalised ingredients, vegan/vegetarian flags. `G05-R4` [S] submitting category and labels yields the Green-Score. `G05-R5` [S] photo submission is the lowest-friction contribution; the backend (Product Opener) and Robotoff generate derived data from photos. `G05-R6` [I] the derived-score availability is therefore a *function of contribution completeness* — the product's usefulness is conditional on inputs it does not control.
*Component relationships:* contributor → raw fields/photos → derivation (Product Opener, Robotoff) → scores. What passes: barcode as the join key. Must stay consistent: barcode-to-product identity across contributions from different countries and packaging revisions.
*Whole-product constraints:* volunteer supply, so completeness is uneven; allergen data has safety consequences while being crowd-sourced.
*Acceptance criteria:* `G05-AC1` **(source)** a product with nutrition values plus category returns a Nutri-Score; with ingredients, returns allergens and NOVA. `G05-AC2` **(proposed — requires acceptance)** the interface distinguishes "no allergen present" from "allergen data absent" wherever a score or flag is shown.
*Assumptions/unknowns:* authentication details and rate limits unverified.

### J — Information and communication

**CB-J05 · OpenAPI Specification 3.1.1 · ISIC J · FORD 1**
*Beneficiary/situation:* A team publishing an API that must be simultaneously human-readable documentation, a client-generator input and a contract test source. *Source:* OpenAPI Initiative, https://github.com/OAI/OpenAPI-Specification/blob/main/versions/3.1.1.md — **READ**.
*Required deliverables (source):* an OpenAPI Document (entry document) and, with its referenced documents, an OpenAPI Description.
*Available inputs:* the API surface, JSON Schema 2020-12.
*Atomic requirements:* `J05-R1` [S] an OpenAPI Document is a single JSON or YAML document conforming to the specification. `J05-R2` [S] the `openapi` field is REQUIRED and MUST be the version number of the OpenAPI Specification the document uses. `J05-R3` [S] an OAD MUST contain at least one `paths` field, `components` field, or `webhooks` field. `J05-R4` [S] each template expression in the path MUST correspond to a path parameter included in the Path Item itself and/or in each of the Path Item's Operations. `J05-R5` [S] path parameter values MUST NOT contain unescaped generic-syntax characters — forward slashes, question marks, or hashes. `J05-R6` [S] data types are those of JSON Schema Validation 2020-12: null, boolean, object, array, number, string, integer.
*Component relationships:* entry document → referenced documents → description. What passes: `$ref` targets. Must stay consistent: the `openapi` version across an OAD's documents, and path templates against declared parameters.
*Whole-product constraints:* one artefact serving three audiences (readers, generators, testers) constrains how much may be left implicit.
*Acceptance criteria:* `J05-AC1` **(source)** `openapi` present and valid; at least one of paths/components/webhooks present. `J05-AC2` **(source)** every path template expression has a matching declared parameter. `J05-AC3` **(proposed — requires acceptance)** the document validates and a generated client exercises every declared operation against a conformant server.
*Assumptions/unknowns:* the 3.1.1 publication date was not in the fetched text.

**CB-J03 · WCAG 2.2 · ISIC J**
*Beneficiary/situation:* A screen-reader user filing a benefits claim on a government form, and the delivery team that must prove conformance to a procurement clause. *Source:* W3C/WAI, WCAG repository, https://github.com/w3c/wcag; published at W3C TR and https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/ — **READ-P** (contributor guide read; the normative conformance clause was not).
*Required deliverables (source):* the guidelines document, individual success criterion files, term definitions, Understanding pages, techniques organised by technology, working examples and errata.
*Available inputs:* the WCAG 2.0/2.1/2.2 corpus.
*Atomic requirements:* `J03-R1` [S] the repository holds source for WCAG 2, Understanding WCAG 2, and techniques. `J03-R2` [S] techniques are organised by technology. `J03-R3` [S] formal publication occurs at W3C TR pages and WAI websites. `J03-R4` [I] Understanding pages and techniques are explicitly *non-normative* companions to success criteria — a product that treats a technique as a requirement will over-constrain. `J03-R5` [H] a conformance claim needs the scope of pages, the level (A/AA/AAA), the technologies relied upon and the date.
*Component relationships:* success criterion ← Understanding ← Techniques ← Working examples. What passes: the criterion identifier. Must stay consistent: criterion numbering across versions (2.0/2.1/2.2 supersets).
*Whole-product constraints:* normative/non-normative boundary; version supersetting means a 2.2 claim implies 2.1 and 2.0 criteria.
*Acceptance criteria:* `J03-AC1` **(source)** content is evaluated against success criteria, with techniques as evidence, not as the standard. `J03-AC2` **(proposed — requires acceptance)** the conformance claim names version, level, scope and date, and each failed criterion has a remediation owner.
*Assumptions/unknowns:* the WCAG 2.2 conformance requirements clause was not read. Recorded shortage: this brief rests on the contributor guide, not the standard.

### K — Financial and insurance activities

**CB-K02 · Mojaloop FSPIOP API · ISIC K**
*Beneficiary/situation:* A market trader receiving a payment from a customer at a different provider, in a scheme where neither provider will trust the other's ledger. *Source:* Mojaloop, mojaloop-specification, https://github.com/mojaloop/mojaloop-specification — **READ**.
*Required deliverables (source):* interoperability APIs (FSP Interoperability, Administration, Settlement, Third-party Payment Initiation).
*Available inputs:* scheme rules, participant directories, JWS/JWE key material.
*Atomic requirements:* `K02-R1` [S] the specification enables interoperable financial transactions between a Payer at one FSP and a Payee at another FSP. `K02-R2` [S] all participants should configure the API parameters as indicated by the API-level scheme rules. `K02-R3` [S] participants must implement JSON Web Encryption (JWE) and JSON Web Signature (JWS) for message protection. `K02-R4` [S] as soon as a financial transaction has been agreed to by both parties, it is deemed irrevocable. `K02-R5` [S] intermediaries may not modify the API message payload. `K02-R6` [I] `K02-R4` and `K02-R5` together mean error handling must be *compensating* (reversal transactions), never corrective (editing the original).
*Component relationships:* Payer FSP → switch → Payee FSP, with settlement downstream. What passes: quote, transfer, fulfilment, signed payloads. Must stay consistent: the transaction identity and the signed payload bytes end to end.
*Whole-product constraints:* irreversibility after agreement; scheme rules as an external authority that parameterises the API; concurrency across participants with independent ledgers.
*Acceptance criteria:* `K02-AC1` **(source)** payloads are JWS-signed and JWE-protected and arrive unmodified through intermediaries. `K02-AC2` **(source)** no interface permits revocation of an agreed transaction. `K02-AC3` **(proposed — requires acceptance)** a mis-sent payment is resolved only by a new, separately authorised compensating transfer with a traceable link to the original.
*Assumptions/unknowns:* version numbering across the API family is partial; financial-inclusion purpose is contextual rather than stated in the fetched text.

**CB-K04 · FIXP 1.1 session layer · ISIC K**
*Beneficiary/situation:* A trading venue and a member firm that need a session which survives a transport drop without duplicating or losing orders, at latencies where a heavyweight session layer is unaffordable. *Source:* FIX Trading Community, fixp-specification v1-1, https://github.com/FIXTradingCommunity/fixp-specification/blob/master/v1-1/doc/01Introduction.md — **READ-P** (scope section read; normative flow rules not in the fetched file).
*Required deliverables (source):* a session-layer specification, produced by the High Performance Working Group, independent of the application layer.
*Available inputs:* stream, datagram and message-oriented transports.
*Atomic requirements:* `K04-R1` [S] FIXP is a lightweight session layer with no restrictions on the application layer. `K04-R2` [S] it supports stream, datagram and message-oriented protocols. `K04-R3` [S] it provides negotiable delivery guarantees that may be asymmetrical. `K04-R4` [S] an established point-to-point session consists of a pair of flows, one in each direction between peers. `K04-R5` [I] asymmetry is the design point: market-data direction and order direction have different loss tolerances and may be negotiated separately.
*Component relationships:* negotiation → flow pair → recovery. What passes: flow type per direction, sequence state. Must stay consistent: both peers' view of the negotiated guarantee per direction.
*Whole-product constraints:* performance budget; reliability must be achieved "regardless of the reliability of the underlying transport".
*Acceptance criteria:* `K04-AC1` **(source)** a session establishes a flow in each direction with the negotiated guarantee. `K04-AC2` **(proposed — requires acceptance)** after transport loss and re-establishment, no application message is delivered twice on a flow negotiated as idempotent or recoverable.
*Assumptions/unknowns:* the flow-type definitions were not in the fetched introduction — retained as an unresolved gap. Feasibility question: whether asymmetrical guarantees complicate recovery beyond what an implementer can test.

### L — Real estate activities

**CB-L05 · ISO 19152 Land Administration Domain Model · ISIC L**
*Beneficiary/situation:* A national cadastre integrating customary, informal and formal tenure into one register without forcing customary holders into a title-deed shape that does not fit them. *Source:* described via GLTN STDM documentation (https://www.stdm.gltn.net/docs/1_5/introduction.htm) and a peer-reviewed account of LADM (https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/pii/S0264837715000174) — **SNIPPET**. **Recorded shortage: the ISO 19152 text itself is not reachable in this environment; nothing below is verified against the standard.**
*Required deliverables (source, as described):* a domain model for land administration capable of specialisation.
*Available inputs:* existing cadastral records, participatory mapping outputs.
*Atomic requirements:* `L05-R1` [S-as-described] LADM is a land administration domain model of which STDM is presented as a specialisation (or arguably a generalisation). `L05-R2` [S-as-described] the model's structural core in the STDM specialisation is Party – Social Tenure Relationship – Spatial Unit. `L05-R3` [S-as-described] to achieve integration with formal land administration systems, all data must be collected using that same structure. `L05-R4` [I] the model's value is that it does not require the relationship to be ownership — it requires only that a relationship be typed.
*Component relationships:* party ↔ relationship ↔ spatial unit. What passes: party identity, relationship type, spatial unit geometry. Must stay consistent: the relationship typology, which is the only thing preventing informal records from being read as ownership claims.
*Whole-product constraints:* competing legitimacies (statutory vs. customary); records may be evidentiary in disputes, so provenance matters.
*Acceptance criteria:* `L05-AC1` **(source-as-described)** every record resolves as party–relationship–spatial unit. `L05-AC2` **(proposed — requires acceptance)** the register can express two overlapping, non-identical claims on one spatial unit without either being silently dropped.
*Assumptions/unknowns:* class names, versions (19152:2012 vs later revision) and conformance classes are unverified.

**CB-L03 · HUD FY2024 HMIS Data Standards · ISIC L · FORD 5**
*Beneficiary/situation:* A continuum-of-care analyst who must produce an unduplicated count of people experiencing homelessness across shelters funded by different federal partners plus unfunded missions. *Source:* US HUD (with HHS and VA), FY2024 HMIS Data Standards Manual and Data Dictionary, https://files.hudexchange.info/resources/documents/HMIS-Data-Standards-Manual-2024.pdf — **SNIPPET**.
*Required deliverables (source):* Universal Data Elements collected by all participating projects.
*Available inputs:* intake records, project enrolment dates, federal partner program requirements.
*Atomic requirements:* `L03-R1` [S] the standards provide baseline data collection requirements developed by HUD, HHS and VA. `L03-R2` [S] UDEs are those which all participating continuum projects are required to complete. `L03-R3` [S] projects funded by any federal partner must collect the UDEs, as must projects with no federal funding that have agreed to enter data as part of the CoC's HMIS implementation. `L03-R4` [S] UDEs enable unique, unduplicated client records, establish participation in a project within a date range, and identify clients meeting time criteria for chronic homelessness. `L03-R5` [S] the FY2024 update took effect 1 October 2023 and changed demographic elements including race/ethnicity, gender identity and living situation options.
*Component relationships:* client record → enrolment (project + date range) → service episodes → counts. What passes: the client identity used for deduplication. Must stay consistent: identity across projects, which is what makes "unduplicated" meaningful, and the demographic response sets across a version change.
*Whole-product constraints:* changing evidence (element definitions revise annually with a hard effective date); competing needs (deduplication vs. client privacy and safety).
*Acceptance criteria:* `L03-AC1` **(source)** all participating projects produce complete UDEs. `L03-AC2` **(source)** enrolments establish participation within a date range and support chronic-homelessness time criteria. `L03-AC3` **(proposed — requires acceptance)** counts spanning the 1 Oct 2023 boundary state which demographic version each record used.
*Assumptions/unknowns:* the UDE list itself was not read.

### M — Professional, scientific and technical activities

**CB-M06 · Darwin Core · ISIC M · FORD 1**
*Beneficiary/situation:* A regional conservation officer combining museum specimens, citizen observations and eDNA samples to decide whether a species is actually absent or merely unrecorded. *Source:* TDWG, Darwin Core, https://github.com/tdwg/dwc — **READ**.
*Required deliverables (source):* a glossary of terms with identifiers, labels and definitions; Quick Reference Guide; list of terms document.
*Available inputs:* specimen catalogues, observation records, sampling event data.
*Atomic requirements:* `M06-R1` [S] Darwin Core is maintained by the Darwin Core Maintenance Interest Group. `M06-R2` [S] it includes a glossary of terms intended to facilitate sharing information about biological diversity by providing identifiers, labels and definitions. `M06-R3` [S] it is primarily based on taxa, their occurrence in nature as documented by observations, specimens, samples and related information. `M06-R4` [S] term metadata is published in the Quick Reference Guide and the list-of-terms document. `M06-R5` [I] because the same term set covers specimens, observations and samples, a consumer cannot infer evidence type from the record shape — evidence type must be carried explicitly.
*Component relationships:* dataset → occurrence records → taxon/event/location terms. What passes: term identifiers and their values. Must stay consistent: term identifier semantics across versions, since historical datasets stay in circulation.
*Whole-product constraints:* absence of a record is not evidence of absence — a constraint on every product built on it; versions from 2011 onward remain citable on Zenodo.
*Acceptance criteria:* `M06-AC1` **(source)** every field maps to a Darwin Core term identifier with its published definition. `M06-AC2` **(proposed — requires acceptance)** each record states its basis of record (specimen / observation / sample) and consumers can filter by it before inferring absence.
*Assumptions/unknowns:* current version/date not stated in the fetched README.

**CB-M04 · SPIRIT 2013 protocol checklist · ISIC M · FORD 3**
*Beneficiary/situation:* A trial methodologist writing a protocol that must satisfy an ethics committee, a funder, a registry and a future systematic reviewer, each reading for something different. *Source:* SPIRIT Group / EQUATOR, SPIRIT 2013 Statement and 33-item checklist, https://www.spirit-statement.org/wp-content/uploads/2013/01/SPIRIT-Checklist-download-8Jan13.pdf; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/23295957/ — **SNIPPET**.
*Required deliverables (source):* a protocol whose content covers the 33 checklist items, plus (via the Explanation and Elaboration paper) rationale and model examples.
*Available inputs:* trial design, statistical analysis plan, consent materials.
*Atomic requirements:* `M04-R1` [S] SPIRIT is a guideline for the minimum content of a clinical trial protocol. `M04-R2` [S] the 33-item checklist applies to protocols for all clinical trials. `M04-R3` [S] the checklist focuses on content rather than format. `M04-R4` [S] it recommends a full description of what is planned; it does not prescribe how to design or conduct a trial. `M04-R5` [S] an Explanation and Elaboration paper details rationale, evidence, guidance and model examples for each item. `M04-R6` [S] items cover areas including biological specimens and informed consent materials.
*Component relationships:* protocol ↔ registry entry ↔ consent materials ↔ analysis plan. What passes: item-level content. Must stay consistent: outcomes and analysis methods as stated in protocol, registry and eventual report — the whole point of the instrument.
*Whole-product constraints:* content-not-format means a product may reorganise but not omit; the checklist is a floor, and amendments must be traceable.
*Acceptance criteria:* `M04-AC1` **(source)** every one of the 33 items is addressed. `M04-AC2` **(source)** the protocol describes what is planned rather than prescribing design. `M04-AC3` **(proposed — requires acceptance)** each item cross-references its location in the protocol, and every protocol amendment records which items changed and why.
*Assumptions/unknowns:* individual item wording unverified.

**CB-N02 · JobTech Taxonomy · ISIC N · FORD 5**

**CB-N01 · HR Open Standards 4.x · ISIC N**
*Beneficiary/situation:* A staffing firm moving a candidate between an applicant tracking system, an assessment vendor and a learning platform, where today each hand-off is a bespoke integration. *Source:* HR Open Standards Consortium, standards page, https://www.hropenstandards.org/standards — **SNIPPET**.

### O — Public administration and defence

**CB-O05 · Akoma Ntoso v1.0 (OASIS LegalDocML) · ISIC O · FORD 6**
*Beneficiary/situation:* A parliamentary drafting office and a court's judgment system that must exchange documents whose *structure* (article, amendment, citation) carries legal meaning, and a citizen who needs the version in force on a given date. *Source:* OASIS, Akoma Ntoso Version 1.0 Part 1: XML Vocabulary, https://docs.oasis-open.org/legaldocml/akn-core/v1.0/akn-core-v1.0-part1-vocabulary.html — **SNIPPET**.
*Required deliverables (source):* a legal XML vocabulary and a common data/metadata model for interchange.
*Available inputs:* existing legislative and judicial document corpora, drafting systems.
*Atomic requirements:* `O05-R1` [S] Akoma Ntoso is a technical standard for representing executive, legislative and judiciary documents in a structured manner using a domain-specific legal XML vocabulary. `O05-R2` [S] the specifications provide a common standard for parliamentary, legislative and judicial documents, for interchange between institutions anywhere in the world. `O05-R3` [S] they establish a common data and metadata model allowing experience, expertise and tools to be shared and extended by participating peers, courts, parliaments, assemblies, congresses and administrative branches. `O05-R4` [S] the standard makes the structure and meaning of legal documents machine-readable. `O05-R5` [S] version 1.0 was approved in 2018; a revision was scheduled for approval by end July 2026.
*Component relationships:* draft → adopted act → amendment → consolidated version → citation. What passes: document identifiers, structural references, temporal metadata. Must stay consistent: the identifier scheme across versions, since a citation must resolve to the right point-in-time text.
*Whole-product constraints:* divided authority (each institution owns its documents); revision conditions (amendments change texts retrospectively in effect); state matters — "in force on date X" is the common query.
*Acceptance criteria:* `O05-AC1` **(source)** documents are represented in the AKN vocabulary with the common metadata model. `O05-AC2` **(proposed — requires acceptance)** a citation to a provision resolves to the consolidated text in force at a stated date, and the amendment chain that produced it is traversable.
*Assumptions/unknowns:* the element vocabulary and the FRBR-style identifier rules were not read.

**CB-O03 · EUDI Wallet Architecture and Reference Framework · ISIC O**
*Beneficiary/situation:* A person proving their age to a retailer without revealing their name, and a relying party that must be registered and accountable for what it asks. *Source:* European Commission, eudi-doc-architecture-and-reference-framework, https://github.com/eu-digital-identity-wallet/eudi-doc-architecture-and-reference-framework; ARF v2.2.0 — **SNIPPET**.
*Required deliverables (source):* an ARF defining structural and functional aspects of the EUDI Wallet ecosystem and its components' interactions.
*Available inputs:* Commission Implementing Regulations, ISO/IEC 18013 series, JSON-based credential formats.
*Atomic requirements:* `O03-R1` [S] the ARF defines the structural and functional aspects of the EUDI Wallet ecosystem, detailing key components and their interactions. `O03-R2` [S] it provides a technical foundation to ensure interoperability, security and privacy. `O03-R3` [S] Implementing Regulations cover wallet integrity and core functionalities, PID and attestations, certification, protocols and interfaces, relying-party registration, the list of certified wallets, and trust services. `O03-R4` [S] the PID is issued in two formats: the ISO/IEC 18013 series format required for proximity scenarios, and a JSON-based format better suited to online scenarios. `O03-R5` [S] Wallet Unit attestation, and re-issuance/batch issuance of PIDs and attestations, are active specification topics. `O03-R6` [I] dual PID formats mean equivalence between the two representations is itself a requirement, not an implementation detail.
*Component relationships:* PID provider → wallet unit → relying party → registrar. What passes: attestations, wallet unit attestation, relying-party registration status. Must stay consistent: the holder's identity binding across both PID formats, and revocation state.
*Whole-product constraints:* privacy (unlinkability across presentations) competes with accountability (relying-party registration); irreversibility — a disclosed attribute cannot be undisclosed.
*Acceptance criteria:* `O03-AC1` **(source)** PID is available in both the proximity and online formats. `O03-AC2` **(proposed — requires acceptance)** two presentations of the same attestation to different relying parties are not correlatable by those parties, while remaining revocable by the issuer.
*Assumptions/unknowns:* ARF version currency and the batch-issuance resolution are open.

### P — Education

**CB-P04 · CEDS v11 Domain Entity Schema + CEDS-IDS · ISIC P · FORD 5**
*Beneficiary/situation:* A state analyst asked whether early-learning participation predicts postsecondary completion, whose data live in four agencies with four vocabularies. *Source:* CEDS / US Dept of Education, Domain Entity Schema https://ceds.ed.gov/domainentityschema.aspx; CEDS-IDS https://github.com/CEDStandards/CEDS-IDS — **READ-P** (IDS README) + **SNIPPET** (DES).
*Required deliverables (source):* the Domain Entity Schema as an index of domains, entities, categories and properties; the Integrated Data Store as a normalised logical model.
*Available inputs:* agency source systems, CEDS element definitions.
*Atomic requirements:* `P04-R1` [S] CEDS v11 includes 264 context updates in the DES supporting new use cases involving 166 new and updated elements. `P04-R2` [S] the DES provides a hierarchy of domains, entities, categories and properties, intended primarily for people as an index to search, map and organise properties. `P04-R3` [S] entities are persons, places, events, objects or concepts about which data can be collected, and an entity provides context for a data element; there are over 70 entities in the DES. `P04-R4` [S] the IDS factors the complete set of CEDS elements, entities and attributes with standard technical syntax and third normal form database normalisation. `P04-R5` [S] the IDS models the most current view of data available to the enterprise, including some historical data, for P-20W systems. `P04-R6` [S] releases occur annually, typically around January/February.
*Component relationships:* source systems → element mapping → DES entities → IDS tables → analyses. What passes: mapped elements and entity keys. Must stay consistent: the person identity across sectors, which is exactly the linkage that is legally constrained.
*Whole-product constraints:* divided authority across agencies; annual version changes; longitudinal claims require historical stability the "most current view" does not guarantee.
*Acceptance criteria:* `P04-AC1` **(source)** every element maps to a DES property under an entity with stated context. `P04-AC2` **(source)** the store is in third normal form. `P04-AC3` **(proposed — requires acceptance)** a cohort query rerun after an annual release either reproduces prior results or reports which element changes caused the difference.
*Assumptions/unknowns:* the current IDS version and the person-linkage mechanism are unverified.

**CB-P02 · Ed-Fi Data Standard · ISIC P**
*Beneficiary/situation:* A district that buys an assessment product, an attendance product and an early-warning product, and needs them to agree on who a student is and which section they are in. *Source:* Ed-Fi Alliance, Ed-Fi-Standard, https://github.com/Ed-Fi-Alliance-OSS/Ed-Fi-Standard — **READ-P** (README only).
*Required deliverables (source):* a data standard serving as the foundation for interoperability among secure education data systems.
*Available inputs:* SIS records, vendor APIs, the Ed-Fi project tracker and documentation.
*Atomic requirements:* `P02-R1` [S] the Ed-Fi Data Standard serves as the foundation for enabling interoperability among secure education data systems. `P02-R2` [S] it is published by the Ed-Fi Alliance, LLC. `P02-R3` [I] "secure" is in the stated purpose, so access control is in scope of the standard's promise, not merely of deployments. `P02-R4` [H] district-level use requires stable student, school, section and school-year identifiers for the standard to deliver the stated interoperability.
*Component relationships:* SIS (system of record) → Ed-Fi API → consuming applications. What passes: student/section/school identifiers and enrolment associations. Must stay consistent: identifiers across the school year and across vendor changes.
*Whole-product constraints:* student data protection; vendors change but records persist.
*Acceptance criteria:* `P02-AC1` **(source)** independently built systems exchange data without bespoke mapping. `P02-AC2` **(proposed — requires acceptance)** replacing one vendor leaves all identifiers and historical associations intact.
*Assumptions/unknowns:* **Recorded shortage** — the README carried no version, no data-model detail and no API or descriptor requirements. This brief is thin by source, not by choice.

### Q — Human health and social work activities

**CB-Q04 · HL7 FHIR · ISIC Q · FORD 3**
*Beneficiary/situation:* A patient moving between a hospital, a community pharmacy and a national record, whose allergy list must arrive intact and be recognisably the same list. *Source:* HL7 International, https://github.com/HL7/fhir; specification published at http://hl7.org/fhir and http://build.fhir.org — **READ-P** (build README only; the specification body was not fetched).
*Required deliverables (source):* the published FHIR specification, built from spreadsheet source data in the project.
*Available inputs:* the repository's source data, master and R4B branches, the build toolchain.
*Atomic requirements:* `Q04-R1` [S] the repository builds and publishes the FHIR specification from contained spreadsheet data. `Q04-R2` [S] the repository maintains distinct branches including master and R4B. `Q04-R3` [S] the build has substantial resource requirements (16 GB RAM minimum stated). `Q04-R4` [I] because the specification is generated, the spreadsheet source is the normative artefact for change control — a fact that matters to anyone proposing changes. `Q04-R5` [H] implementers need the resource definitions, profiles and conformance rules from the published specification, not from this repository.
*Component relationships:* source data → publication toolchain → specification → implementation guides → conformance resources. What passes: resource definitions and profiles. Must stay consistent: version identity (R4B vs. R5) between a profile and the base it constrains.
*Whole-product constraints:* multiple live versions in production simultaneously; national programmes constrain the base differently.
*Acceptance criteria:* `Q04-AC1` **(source)** the specification builds and publishes from the repository source. `Q04-AC2` **(proposed — requires acceptance)** an instance validates against its declared profile *and* the base version that profile names.
*Assumptions/unknowns:* **Recorded shortage** — no version, date or implementer requirement was present in the fetched file. The substantive FHIR conformance rules are unread here.

**CB-Q02 · OMOP Common Data Model v5.5 · ISIC Q · FORD 3**
*Beneficiary/situation:* A pharmacoepidemiologist running one study protocol across five hospital databases in three countries, without moving patient-level data. *Source:* OHDSI, CommonDataModel, https://github.com/OHDSI/CommonDataModel — **READ-P**.
*Required deliverables (source):* the CDM structure as CSV specifications from which DDL is generated, plus ETL conventions.
*Available inputs:* source EHR/claims data, OHDSI standardised vocabularies, the DDL generator.
*Atomic requirements:* `Q02-R1` [S] v5.5 is the primary supported version. `Q02-R2` [S] v6.0 is not fully supported by the OHDSI suite of tools and methods. `Q02-R3` [S] changes to the model structure should be made in the representative CSV files. `Q02-R4` [S] the structure is set up so that the CSV files are the ground truth. `Q02-R5` [S] ETL conventions are not currently tracked by CDM version unless they are conventions specific to new fields (example given: CONDITION_STATUS added in v5.3). `Q02-R6` [I] `Q02-R5` is the reproducibility hazard: two sites on the same CDM version can differ in convention, so a study's results depend on unversioned decisions.
*Component relationships:* source data → ETL → CDM tables → analysis package → aggregate results. What passes: standardised concept IDs and the analysis code, not patient records. Must stay consistent: vocabulary version and ETL conventions across sites.
*Whole-product constraints:* federation — code travels, data does not; tool-chain compatibility constrains which CDM version is usable regardless of which is newest.
*Acceptance criteria:* `Q02-AC1` **(source)** the DDL generated from the CSV ground truth instantiates the tables. `Q02-AC2` **(proposed — requires acceptance)** each site publishes its ETL conventions and vocabulary version alongside results, and a discrepancy in either is reportable as a study-level limitation.
*Assumptions/unknowns:* the table-level conventions and vocabulary constraints were not read.

### R — Arts, entertainment and recreation

**CB-R02 · MovieLabs Ontology for Media Creation v2.8 · ISIC R**
*Beneficiary/situation:* A production coordinator tracking one costume across a script breakdown, a shooting schedule, a VFX vendor and a distribution master, where every department names it differently. *Source:* Motion Picture Laboratories, OMC, https://github.com/MovieLabs/OMC — **READ**.
*Required deliverables (source):* three interconnected RDF files: `omc.ttl` (production), `cw.ttl` (Creative Works), `omd.ttl` (distribution).
*Available inputs:* Part 7: Relationships; the earlier Creative Works Ontology (2018).
*Atomic requirements:* `R02-R1` [S] the OMC covers the production process for Creative Works as part of a larger framework modelling creative works and their creation and distribution. `R02-R2` [S] the current version is 2.8. `R02-R3` [S] the ontology comprises `omc.ttl`, `cw.ttl` (uniquely identified productions) and `omd.ttl` (distribution, built on OMC). `R02-R4` [S] relationships — the predicates in the RDF triples — are taken from Part 7: Relationships. `R02-R5` [S] the model includes class hierarchies (e.g. "Anything with a Name") and relationship properties. `R02-R6` [S] copyright 2021–2025; the original Creative Works Ontology dates to 2018.
*Component relationships:* creative work → production entities → distribution entities, joined by Part 7 predicates. What passes: entity identifiers and typed relationships. Must stay consistent: the identity of a work and its assets from pre-production through distribution — the single thread the ontology exists to hold.
*Whole-product constraints:* three files must stay mutually consistent; vendors join and leave mid-production, so identifiers must be assignable by parties who do not control the whole graph.
*Acceptance criteria:* `R02-AC1` **(source)** production, creative-work and distribution facts are expressed using the three ontology files with Part 7 predicates. `R02-AC2` **(proposed — requires acceptance)** an asset created by a vendor and delivered to distribution resolves to the same work identity as its script-breakdown origin.
*Assumptions/unknowns:* the enumerated required entities are in external documentation not read here.

**CB-R04 · OpenActive data models · ISIC R**
*Beneficiary/situation:* A person looking for a beginners' swimming session near them tonight, and the leisure trust whose sessions are invisible outside its own booking system. *Source:* OpenActive, data-models, https://github.com/openactive/data-models; modelling guidance at https://openactive.io/modelling-opportunity-data/ — **READ-P** (the fetched README documents the validator library, not the model).
*Required deliverables (source):* data models that drive the OpenActive validator and developer documentation.
*Available inputs:* schema.org vocabulary, JSON-LD, the `MODELS.md` listing, model version 2.0.
*Atomic requirements:* `R04-R1` [S] the repository holds data models used to drive the OpenActive validator and developer documentation. `R04-R2` [S] the models use JSON-LD and schema.org vocabulary, with model version 2.0 noted. `R04-R3` [S] an `Event` model exists, with properties including `meetingPoint`. `R04-R4` [I] because a validator is generated from the models, publisher conformance is machine-checkable — which is what makes an open activity-data ecosystem feasible for small providers.
*Component relationships:* publisher feed → validator → aggregator/booking client. What passes: opportunity descriptions (what, when, where, for whom). Must stay consistent: opportunity identity across updates and cancellations.
*Whole-product constraints:* small providers with little technical capacity; sessions change and cancel at short notice, so freshness and cancellation semantics carry the user experience.
*Acceptance criteria:* `R04-AC1` **(source)** a published feed passes the OpenActive validator built from these models. `R04-AC2` **(proposed — requires acceptance)** a cancelled session disappears from consumer surfaces within a stated interval and the cancellation is distinguishable from a feed error.
*Assumptions/unknowns:* **Recorded shortage** — publisher requirements and the model list were not in the fetched file.

### S — Other service activities

**CB-S01 · Open Repair Data Standard + community-repair impact methodology · ISIC S · FORD 2**
*Beneficiary/situation:* A repair café volunteer logging a fixed kettle, and the coalition that wants to tell a city council how much waste and carbon community repair actually avoids. *Source:* Open Repair Alliance / The Restart Project, environmental-impact-data, https://github.com/TheRestartProject/environmental-impact-data (`methodology.md`) — **READ**.
*Required deliverables (source):* a documented method producing approximate CO2e values per repair, plus a data-quality rating.
*Available inputs:* ORDS-categorised repair records, manufacturer/study footprint data, proxy assumptions.
