*Atomic requirements:* `S01-R1` [S] CO2e emissions prevented by repair (kg) = CO2e emissions prior to usage (kg) × rate of displacement of a new purchase. `S01-R2` [S] the displacement rate used is 50%, on the assumption that repair extends device life by half its intended duration. `S01-R3` [S] scope covers the "most relevant" device categories per the Open Repair Data Standard, plus unpowered products. `S01-R4` [S] pre-use CO2e footprint data comes from manufacturers, studies, or proxy assumptions, with documented sources and years. `S01-R5` [S] stated assumptions: repair material consumption negligible; energy-efficiency gains from upgrades negligible; regional distribution differences insignificant; average displacement applies uniformly. `S01-R6` [S] data quality is rated on representativeness, age and reliability via a Data Quality Index matrix. `S01-R7` [S] the method yields approximate CO2e values intended as an educational tool rather than exact measurements, because LCA reporting complexity prevents precise product comparisons.
*Component relationships:* repair record (ORDS category) → footprint lookup → displacement → CO2e → aggregate. What passes: device category, footprint value, source and year, quality rating. Must stay consistent: ORDS category definitions between the log and the footprint table.
*Whole-product constraints:* the "educational tool, not exact measurement" caveat must travel with every number — it constrains how outputs may be used in advocacy or reporting.
*Acceptance criteria:* `S01-AC1` **(source)** each CO2e figure is computed by the stated formula with a documented footprint source and year. `S01-AC2` **(source)** each figure carries a Data Quality Index rating. `S01-AC3` **(proposed — requires acceptance)** any published aggregate reproduces from the underlying records and displays the approximation caveat adjacent to the number.
*Assumptions/unknowns:* version/date and the organisational attribution were not in the fetched file. Unresolved: whether the 50% displacement rate is defensible per category — a single global rate is the method's largest exposure.

**CB-S03 · iFixit Repairability Scoring Rubric v2.0 · ISIC S**
*Beneficiary/situation:* A buyer choosing between two phones on how repairable they will be in year three, and a repair technician needing to know which components are realistically replaceable. *Source:* iFixit, Repairability Scoring Rubric v2.0, https://www.ifixit.com/Wiki/Repairability_Scoring_Rubric_v2.0; method explainer https://www.ifixit.com/News/75533/how-ifixit-scores-repairability — **SNIPPET**.
*Required deliverables (source):* a repeatable score from 0 to 10 per device, with a per-category component set and weights.
*Available inputs:* teardown observations, the proxy-time table, tool scaling factors.
*Atomic requirements:* `S03-R1` [S] devices are scored from zero (unrepairable) to ten (easiest to repair). `S03-R2` [S] for each critical component, trace the replacement path, note each disassembly and reassembly action, multiply its proxy time by the scaling factor for any required tool, and sum the products. `S03-R3` [S] the resulting sum falls in a predefined range corresponding to a score, which is multiplied by the component's weight. `S03-R4` [S] the sum of individual component scores represents 80% of the overall score. `S03-R5` [S] consumables such as batteries are weighted more heavily than mechanical buttons. `S03-R6` [S] the selection of critical components and their weights is unique per product category, so all smartphones are evaluated on the same terms. `S03-R7` [S] an extensive table of preassigned proxy times keeps scores consistent between devices. `S03-R8` [S] criteria are updated at least once annually in response to new designs, materials, technologies and obstacles.
*Component relationships:* teardown → action list → proxy times → component score → weighted total (+ the remaining 20%). What passes: action sequences and tool requirements. Must stay consistent: proxy times and category weights within a scoring generation.
*Whole-product constraints:* annual criteria revision means scores are only comparable within a generation; the remaining 20% (documentation, parts, software) is what makes design-only scoring insufficient.
*Acceptance criteria:* `S03-AC1` **(source)** the design component accounts for 80% of the score and follows the proxy-time procedure. `S03-AC2` **(proposed — requires acceptance)** two assessors independently scoring the same device with the same rubric generation agree within ±0.5.
*Assumptions/unknowns:* the composition of the remaining 20% was not confirmed; the proxy-time table was not read.

### T — Households as employers; own-use production

**CB-T02 · ILO Convention No. 189 (Domestic Workers) · ISIC T**
*Beneficiary/situation:* A live-in home attendant whose "off" hours are spent waiting to be needed, and a family employer who wants to be fair and has no template for what fair means. *Source:* ILO, Convention No. 189, https://www.un.org/en/development/desa/population/migration/generalassembly/docs/globalcompact/ILO_C_189.pdf; summary https://www.ilo.org/media/34541/download — **SNIPPET**.
*Required deliverables (source):* terms and conditions communicated to the worker, preferably in writing; equal treatment measures on working time.
*Available inputs:* national labour law, the household's schedule, the ILO summary of Articles 7 and 10.
*Atomic requirements:* `T02-R1` [S] domestic workers must be informed of their terms and conditions of employment in an easily understandable manner, preferably through a written contract (Article 7). `T02-R2` [S] measures aim to ensure equal treatment between domestic workers and workers generally regarding normal hours of work, overtime compensation, periods of daily and weekly rest, and annual paid leave (Article 10). `T02-R3` [S] stand-by hours — periods when the worker is not free to dispose of their time and must remain at the disposal of the household to respond to possible calls — are regulated (Article 10). `T02-R4` [S] weekly rest of at least 24 consecutive hours is required (Article 10). `T02-R5` [S] live-in domestic workers are not obliged to remain in the household or with household members during daily and weekly rest or annual leave.
*Component relationships:* agreement → schedule → stand-by classification → pay. What passes: hours by type (worked, stand-by, rest). Must stay consistent: the classification of an hour between the schedule and the payslip.
*Whole-product constraints:* the employer is a household, not an organisation — no HR function, no payroll department; the workplace is also someone's home, so privacy and supervision collide.
*Acceptance criteria:* `T02-AC1` **(source)** terms are communicated in an easily understandable manner, preferably written. `T02-AC2` **(source)** at least 24 consecutive hours of weekly rest, with no obligation to remain in the household during it. `T02-AC3` **(proposed — requires acceptance)** every hour in a pay period is classified as worked, stand-by or rest, and stand-by hours are visibly compensated.
*Assumptions/unknowns:* Convention text not read directly; national ratification and transposition determine actual obligations, which the Convention alone does not establish.

**CB-T03 · IRS Publication 926 (2026) · ISIC T**
*Beneficiary/situation:* A family paying a part-time cleaner who has just crossed a threshold they did not know existed, facing employment-tax obligations with no payroll system. *Source:* US IRS, Publication 926, Household Employer's Tax Guide (2026), https://www.irs.gov/publications/p926 — **SNIPPET**.
*Required deliverables (source):* correct withholding, payment and reporting of household employment taxes.
*Available inputs:* wage records, the calendar-year and quarterly thresholds, W-2 filing obligations.
*Atomic requirements:* `T03-R1` [S] Social Security and Medicare taxes apply to wages of household workers paid $3,000 or more in cash wages in 2026. `T03-R2` [S] the Social Security tax rate is 6.2% each for employee and employer. `T03-R3` [S] the Social Security wage base limit is $184,500. `T03-R4` [S] the Medicare rate is 1.45% each for employee and employer, with no wage base limit. `T03-R5` [S] federal unemployment tax is owed if $1,000 or more is paid to all household employees in any calendar quarter. `T03-R6` [S] Additional Medicare Tax withholding applies to wages paid in excess of the $200,000 withholding threshold.
*Component relationships:* wage ledger → threshold tests (annual per-worker, quarterly aggregate) → tax computation → filing. What passes: cash wages by worker by period. Must stay consistent: "cash wages" definition between the per-worker FICA test and the aggregate FUTA test — they use different bases and different periods.
*Whole-product constraints:* thresholds change annually; the FICA test is per worker per year while the FUTA test is all workers per quarter, so crossing one does not imply the other; retrospective liability if missed.
*Acceptance criteria:* `T03-AC1` **(source)** FICA applied for any worker at ≥$3,000 cash wages in 2026. `T03-AC2` **(source)** FUTA applied if any calendar quarter total across all household employees reaches $1,000. `T03-AC3` **(proposed — requires acceptance)** the household is warned *before* crossing either threshold, not after year end.
*Assumptions/unknowns:* figures are snippet-derived from a 2026 edition and must be re-verified against the published publication before use; state-level obligations are out of scope of this source.

### U — Extraterritorial organisations and bodies

**CB-U04 · Digital emblem (ICRC / IETF problem statement) · ISIC U**
*Beneficiary/situation:* A field hospital's IT team whose servers are legally protected under international humanitarian law but indistinguishable from any other server to an attacker who might respect the protection. *Source:* ICRC / IETF, "Problem Statement for a Digital Emblem", draft-linker-digital-emblem-02, https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-linker-digital-emblem-02; ICRC project page https://www.icrc.org/en/article/icrc-digital-emblem-project — **SNIPPET**.
*Required deliverables (source):* a means to signal IHL protection in cyberspace, marking digital assets including infrastructure of hospitals and humanitarian organisations.
*Available inputs:* existing infrastructure — TLS, DNS, UDP; Certificate Transparency.
*Atomic requirements:* `U04-R1` [S] the digital emblem has a unique combination of security requirements: authentication, accountability, and covert inspection. `U04-R2` [S] covert inspection means the emblem can be observed without arousing suspicion or alerting users that it is being perceived (also called undetectable validation). `U04-R3` [S] a digital emblem must always identify assets that only serve purposes enjoying protection under IHL. `U04-R4` [S] ADEM follows a decentralised trust model and uses existing infrastructure where possible, providing accountability through Certificate Transparency. `U04-R5` [S] ADEM is designed to be distributed over many channels and is not bound to one transport; distribution via TLS, DNS and UDP is foreseen. `U04-R6` [S] it is expected that an adopting IETF working group will refine ADEM for industry interoperability.
*Component relationships:* emblem issuer → asset marking → distribution channel → inspector → transparency log. What passes: signed emblem, asset identity, log inclusion proof. Must stay consistent: the binding between emblem and the set of assets it covers — `U04-R3` fails the moment a marked host also serves unprotected functions.
*Whole-product constraints:* the emblem is a target as much as a shield; covert inspection conflicts with ordinary observability; irreversible harm if misapplied.
*Acceptance criteria:* `U04-AC1` **(source)** an inspector can authenticate an emblem without the emblem holder detecting the inspection. `U04-AC2` **(source)** issuance is accountable via a transparency mechanism. `U04-AC3` **(proposed — requires acceptance)** an asset that begins serving a non-protected purpose can have its emblem withdrawn and the withdrawal is observable at next inspection.
*Assumptions/unknowns:* draft status; the ADEM specification itself was not read. Feasibility question retained: whether covert inspection is achievable against an adversary that controls the network path.

**CB-P05 · INEE Minimum Standards for Education 2024 · ISIC U (and P)**
*Beneficiary/situation:* A displaced 12-year-old whose schooling has stopped, and the education cluster coordinating ministry, UN agencies and NGOs who each hold part of the answer. *Source:* INEE, *Minimum Standards for Education: Preparedness, Response, Recovery*, 2024 edition (3rd), https://inee.org/sites/default/files/resources/INEE%20Minimum%20Standards%202024%20v2.2_EN.pdf — **SNIPPET**.
*Required deliverables (source):* 19 standards, each with key actions and guidance notes.
*Available inputs:* the 2024 handbook; more than 1,600 contributors across 35 countries.
*Atomic requirements:* `P05-R1` [S] the INEE MS contain 19 standards, each including key actions and guidance notes. `P05-R2` [S] the purpose is to improve the quality of educational preparedness, response and recovery; increase access to safe and relevant learning opportunities; and ensure the actors providing these services are held accountable. `P05-R3` [S] the standards are designed to apply to crisis response in many situations, including conflict, natural hazards such as those induced by climate change, and slow- and rapid-onset crises in rural and urban environments. `P05-R4` [S] education authorities prioritise the continuity and recovery of quality education, including free and inclusive access to learning. `P05-R5` [S] community members participate meaningfully, transparently and without discrimination in analysis, planning, design, implementation, monitoring and evaluation of the education response. `P05-R6` [S] education coordination mechanisms are in place to support stakeholders working to ensure access to and continuity of quality education.
*Component relationships:* coordination mechanism ↔ education authority ↔ community ↔ providers. What passes: needs analysis, plans, monitoring data, accountability reports. Must stay consistent: the learner cohort definition across agencies, or continuity cannot be measured.
*Whole-product constraints:* divided authority (state, cluster, NGOs); interruption is the normal operating condition; accountability runs to affected communities, not only donors.
*Acceptance criteria:* `P05-AC1` **(source)** each of the 19 standards is met through its key actions, contextualised via guidance notes. `P05-AC2` **(source)** community participation is evidenced at each of the six named stages. `P05-AC3` **(proposed — requires acceptance)** a learner's continuity is traceable across a provider handover without re-registration from zero.
*Assumptions/unknowns:* domain groupings and full standard list unverified.

### FORD 1 — Natural sciences

**CB-M08 · CMIP7 Data Request + DECK · FORD 1 (ISIC M)**
*Beneficiary/situation:* A modelling centre deciding which of thousands of possible outputs to write, and the impacts researcher three years later who needs a variable nobody archived. *Source:* WCRP CMIP Panel, CMIP7 Data Request, https://wcrp-cmip.org/cmip-phases/cmip7/cmip7-data-request/; preprint https://egusphere.copernicus.org/preprints/2026/egusphere-2026-1641/; CMIP7 description https://wcrp-cmip.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/09/25-07-17_GMD-template_CMIP7-description.pdf — **SNIPPET**.
*Required deliverables (source):* a Data Request specifying data requirements; DECK experiments; forcing datasets via input4MIPs.
*Available inputs:* CMIP6 experience, forcing task team outputs, thematic author team consultations.
*Atomic requirements:* `M08-R1` [S] CMIP7 DECK experiments include 1pctCO2, abrupt-4xCO2, amip, historical, piControl, piClim-Control, piClim-anthro and piClim-4xCO2. `M08-R2` [S] the historical experiment covers 175 years, 1850–2025. `M08-R3` [S] the CMIP Forcings Task Team works to resolve known forcing issues for DECK experiments; forcing data updates extend until at least December 2021. `M08-R4` [S] pre-release forcing versions were made publicly available through the input4MIPs ESGF project in mid-2024, with DECK datasets finalised and frozen in 2025. `M08-R5` [S] the Data Request was co-created by domain experts recruited into five Thematic Author Teams through wide consultation. `M08-R6` [S] the Data Request covers requirements for both use cases so centres supporting community MIPs can generate requested data efficiently.
*Component relationships:* forcing datasets → experiment protocol → model runs → requested variables → ESGF publication. What passes: forcing dataset versions, experiment identifiers, variable lists. Must stay consistent: the forcing version used across centres, or inter-model comparison compares forcings rather than models.
*Whole-product constraints:* compute scarcity forces prioritisation; freezing datasets is what makes comparison possible but blocks late corrections; concurrency across dozens of centres.
*Acceptance criteria:* `M08-AC1` **(source)** DECK simulations use the frozen CMIP7 forcing datasets published via input4MIPs. `M08-AC2` **(source)** the historical experiment spans 1850–2025. `M08-AC3` **(proposed — requires acceptance)** every published variable resolves to a Data Request entry and a forcing version, so a later user can tell what was and was not requested.
*Assumptions/unknowns:* Data Request version numbering (v1.2.2.3 seen in a preprint title) and current status unverified.

**CB-M05 · IUPAC InChI · FORD 1 (ISIC M)**
*Beneficiary/situation:* A curator merging three chemical databases where the same substance is drawn three ways, and a researcher who needs a citable identifier for a compound in a paper. *Source:* IUPAC / InChI Trust, InChI Technical Manual, https://www.inchi-trust.org/download/104/InChI_TechMan.pdf; overview https://link.springer.com/article/10.1186/s13321-015-0068-4 — **SNIPPET**.
*Required deliverables (source):* a non-proprietary identifier for chemical substances usable in printed and electronic data sources.
*Available inputs:* connection tables/structure drawings, the InChI software.
*Atomic requirements:* `M05-R1` [S] the aim was to establish a unique label, the InChI, as a non-proprietary identifier for chemical substances enabling linking of diverse data compilations and unambiguous identification. `M05-R2` [S] every InChI string starts with `InChI=` followed by the version number, and the letter S for standard InChIs. `M05-R3` [S] the rest is a sequence of layers and sub-layers separated by `/`, covering molecular connectivity, charge, stereochemistry, isotopic enrichment, position of hydrogen atoms, and bonding in metal compounds. `M05-R4` [S] as many as six varieties of normalisation rules are applied; steps 1–4 eliminate structure-drawing conventions, step 5 finds protons for variable protonation, step 6 handles tautomeric patterns and resonances. `M05-R5` [S] normalisation may modify the input structure by applying a consistent chemical model so that structures of the same compound drawn under different conventions become close if not identical. `M05-R6` [S] generation is a three-step process: normalisation, canonicalisation (unique atom numbering), serialisation.
*Component relationships:* structure input → normalisation → canonicalisation → serialisation → InChI/InChIKey. What passes: the normalised structure. Must stay consistent: the algorithm version, since `M05-R5` means the identifier is a function of the software, not only of the molecule.
*Whole-product constraints:* the identifier is algorithmically defined, so version pinning is a data-integrity requirement; standard vs. non-standard InChI must not be conflated.
*Acceptance criteria:* `M05-AC1` **(source)** the same compound drawn under different conventions yields the same standard InChI. `M05-AC2` **(proposed — requires acceptance)** every stored InChI records the generating software version, and a re-generation mismatch is surfaced rather than silently overwritten.
*Assumptions/unknowns:* technical manual not read directly.

### FORD 2 — Engineering and technology

**CB-C01 · MTConnect standard + C++ Agent · FORD 2 (ISIC C)**
*Beneficiary/situation:* A shop-floor supervisor who cannot say how much of the day the machines actually cut, because each controller speaks its own dialect. *Source:* MTConnect Institute, cppagent, https://github.com/mtconnect/cppagent — **READ-P**.
*Required deliverables (source):* an agent that collects machine data and exposes it in a consistent structured format.
*Available inputs:* vendor-specific machine signals; MTConnect XML/JSON schemas.
*Atomic requirements:* `C01-R1` [S] the agent is a C++ implementation of the MTConnect standard designed to reliably collect machine data from diverse industrial devices and expose it in a consistent, structured format. `C01-R2` [S] it transforms vendor-specific machine signals into semantic MTConnect XML/JSON outputs and acts as intermediary between raw machine data and higher-level software. `C01-R3` [S] v2.7 adds asset archetypes (Part, Process, Task). `C01-R4` [S] v2.6 added REST error response documents and `AssetChanged`/`AssetRemoved` events. `C01-R5` [S] v2.5 introduced validation support and WebSocket communication. `C01-R6` [S] the standard's model spans Devices (machine structure), Streams (buffered time-series samples), Assets (parts, processes, tasks) and Sampling with timestamps.
*Component relationships:* adapter → agent → client. What passes: device model, sampled observations with timestamps, asset documents. Must stay consistent: device/component identity between the Devices document and Streams, and clock alignment for timestamps.
*Whole-product constraints:* buffering means clients can miss data if they fall behind; read-only by design, which bounds what the product can be used for.
*Acceptance criteria:* `C01-AC1` **(source)** vendor signals are exposed as MTConnect XML/JSON with device structure, streams and assets. `C01-AC2` **(proposed — requires acceptance)** a client disconnected for longer than the buffer window learns it has a gap rather than receiving a silently truncated series.
*Assumptions/unknowns:* the standard's normative parts were not read; publisher and dates not in the fetched file.

**CB-D04 · OpenADR 3.x · FORD 2 (ISIC D)**
*Beneficiary/situation:* An aggregator that must curtail 4,000 heterogeneous devices — water heaters, batteries, EV chargers — within a utility's event window, and a utility that must know what actually responded. *Source:* OpenADR Alliance, https://openadr.org (3.1.0 announcement, 2025); Rust VTN/VEN implementation https://github.com/OpenLEADR/openleadr-rs — **SNIPPET**.
*Required deliverables (source):* an API-based VTN/VEN interface with a formal program construct.
*Available inputs:* DER telemetry, utility program definitions, TLS/JWT infrastructure.
*Atomic requirements:* `D04-R1` [S] OpenADR enables automatic exchange of demand response signals between energy providers, grid operators and consumers. `D04-R2` [S] interactions are between a server (VTN, Virtual Top Node) and a client (VEN, Virtual End Node); VTNs provide an API to manage VENs and their associated resources. `D04-R3` [S] OpenADR 3.0 introduces streamlined API-based communication between VTNs and VENs. `D04-R4` [S] it formalises the "program" construct to define and manage the metadata of demand response offerings, enabling consistent information exchange. `D04-R5` [S] it uses TLS encryption and JSON Web Tokens for secure authenticated communication, following OpenADR Alliance certification standards. `D04-R6` [S] it is designed to support utilities, operators, aggregators and customers managing DER including renewables, storage, EV batteries and charging infrastructure, and demand response resources such as commercial buildings and homes. `D04-R7` [S] the standard is downloadable free of charge.
*Component relationships:* program → event → VEN → resources → reports. What passes: program metadata, event schedules, opt-in/opt-out, delivered response. Must stay consistent: resource identity between the enrolment and the report, or settlement fails.
*Whole-product constraints:* concurrency at scale; devices may be offline at event time; grid events are time-critical and non-repeatable.
*Acceptance criteria:* `D04-AC1` **(source)** VTN–VEN exchange is over TLS with JWT authentication under the certification programme. `D04-AC2` **(source)** programs carry the metadata of the demand response offering. `D04-AC3` **(proposed — requires acceptance)** for every dispatched event, delivered response is attributable per resource and reconcilable to enrolment.
*Assumptions/unknowns:* 3.0 vs 3.1.0 differences unverified; the specification itself was not read.

### FORD 3 — Medical and health sciences

**CB-Q01 · WHO SMART Guidelines Digital Adaptation Kit (ANC) · FORD 3 (ISIC Q)**
*Beneficiary/situation:* A ministry digitising antenatal care that must implement WHO recommendations faithfully, and a software vendor who currently reinterprets a narrative guideline into code with no traceable path. *Source:* WHO, smart-anc, `input/pagecontent/index.md`, https://github.com/WorldHealthOrganization/smart-anc — **READ**.
*Required deliverables (source):* FHIR-based computable representations of the guideline's key components, structured as the DAK's eight components.
*Available inputs:* WHO ANC guidelines; the SMART IG tooling; L2 DAK and L3 FHIR authoring phases.
*Atomic requirements:* `Q01-R1` [S] the guide facilitates digital implementation of WHO ANC guidelines by providing FHIR-based computable representations of key components. `Q01-R2` [S] it is designed for implementers integrating WHO recommendations into digital systems. `Q01-R3` [S] the DAK contains eight components: Health Interventions & Recommendations; Generic Personas; User Scenarios; Business Processes & Workflows; Core Data Elements; Decision Support Logic; Indicators & Monitoring; Functional & Non-functional Requirements. `Q01-R4` [S] the content is draft material that has not yet been tested or validated for use in production environments. `Q01-R5` [S] it is a working draft in progress and subject to change without notice. `Q01-R6` [S] the authoring lifecycle distinguishes L2 DAK authoring from L3 FHIR authoring.
*Component relationships:* recommendation → persona → scenario → workflow → data element → decision logic → indicator → requirement. What passes: the recommendation identity threading through all eight components. Must stay consistent: a data element used in decision logic must be the same element counted in indicators, or reporting and care diverge.
*Whole-product constraints:* draft status forbids production claims; guidelines revise, so the L2→L3 chain must be re-derivable; national adaptation is expected, so the DAK must be modifiable without losing traceability to WHO.
*Acceptance criteria:* `Q01-AC1` **(source)** all eight components are present and computable representations are FHIR-based. `Q01-AC2` **(source)** the draft/non-production caveat is carried with the artefacts. `Q01-AC3` **(proposed — requires acceptance)** every decision-support rule traces to a numbered WHO recommendation, and a recommendation change lists the rules and indicators it invalidates.
*Assumptions/unknowns:* the L1–L5 layer definitions were sought in `smart-base` and not found in the files fetched — recorded as an unresolved gap; no version or date in the fetched page.

**CB-Q05 · IHE IT Infrastructure Technical Framework · FORD 3 (ISIC Q)**
*Beneficiary/situation:* A regional health information exchange procuring five systems that must actually connect, and a vendor who wants to state capability in terms a buyer can test. *Source:* IHE International, ITI Technical Framework Volume 1, https://profiles.ihe.net/ITI/TF/Volume1/ch-2.html; document conventions https://profiles.ihe.net/GeneralIntro/ch-7.html — **SNIPPET**.
*Required deliverables (source):* integration profiles defined in terms of actors, transactions and content modules.
*Available inputs:* underlying standards; the profile catalogue (XDS, XDR, XCA, DSUB…).
*Atomic requirements:* `Q05-R1` [S] Integration Profiles specify implementations of standards designed to meet identified clinical needs and enable users and vendors to state which IHE capabilities they require or provide by reference to the Technical Framework. `Q05-R2` [S] profiles are defined in terms of IHE actors, transactions and content modules. `Q05-R3` [S] actors are information systems or components that produce, manage or act on information associated with clinical and operational activities. `Q05-R4` [S] transactions are interactions between actors transferring required information through standards-based messages. `Q05-R5` [S] to comply with an actor in a profile, a system must perform all transactions required for that actor in that profile. `Q05-R6` [S] transactions labelled "R" are required; those labelled "O" are optional. `Q05-R7` [S] XDR provides document interchange using a reliable messaging system, permitting direct interchange between EHRs, PHRs and other systems in the absence of a document sharing infrastructure such as XDS Registry and Repositories.
*Component relationships:* actor ↔ transaction ↔ content module. What passes: standards-based messages carrying documents and metadata. Must stay consistent: patient and document identity across actors — the recurring failure point.
*Whole-product constraints:* the R/O labelling makes conformance claims precise and procurable; profiles compose, so a deployment inherits every constituent profile's requirements.
*Acceptance criteria:* `Q05-AC1` **(source)** a system claiming an actor performs all R transactions for that actor in that profile. `Q05-AC2` **(proposed — requires acceptance)** a procurement statement names profile, actor and options, and is testable at a connectathon-style event before contract.
*Assumptions/unknowns:* framework revision currency unverified.

### FORD 4 — Agricultural and veterinary sciences

**CB-A04 · GDST Core Normative Standard 1.2 · FORD 4 (ISIC A)**
*Beneficiary/situation:* A retailer's sourcing manager who must show that a tuna loin came from a legal vessel, and a processor who aggregates catches from dozens of boats into one lot. *Source:* GDST, The Standard, https://thegdst.org/resources/standard/; developer documentation https://developer.thegdst.org/ — **SNIPPET**.
*Required deliverables (source):* Key Data Elements captured at each Critical Tracking Event, exchangeable between interoperable traceability systems.
*Available inputs:* GS1 EPCIS as the technical basis; vessel and farm records.
*Atomic requirements:* `A04-R1` [S] the current version is the Core Normative Standard 1.2, managed by a non-profit foundation, covering wild-caught and aquaculture supply chains. `A04-R2` [S] the standard has two main elements: KDEs captured at each CTE, and the technical formats and nomenclatures for sharing data among interoperable traceability systems. `A04-R3` [S] the common language for interoperable traceability is based on GS1 EPCIS, enabling KDEs to be shared along GDST-capable supply chains. `A04-R4` [S] CTEs include fishing, on-vessel processing, transshipment, landing, aggregation/disaggregation, ship/receive and processing. `A04-R5` [I] aggregation/disaggregation is the load-bearing event: after it, provenance is a set, not a chain, and every downstream claim inherits the weakest member.
*Component relationships:* vessel/farm → CTE sequence → lot identity → buyer query. What passes: lot identifiers, event data, KDE values. Must stay consistent: lot identity across aggregation, and the EPCIS event vocabulary between trading partners.
*Whole-product constraints:* transshipment is where illegal catch enters; small vessels have limited data capability; claims are commercially and legally consequential.
*Acceptance criteria:* `A04-AC1` **(source)** the required KDEs are present for each CTE in scope. `A04-AC2` **(source)** data is exchanged in the GDST technical formats based on EPCIS. `A04-AC3` **(proposed — requires acceptance)** for any finished lot, the set of contributing vessel trips is enumerable and each carries its own KDE completeness state.
*Assumptions/unknowns:* the KDE list per CTE was not read; interaction with GS1 EPCIS versions is a dependency, not a shared origin.

**CB-A05 · Crop Ontology Guidelines v2.2 · FORD 4 (ISIC A)**
*Beneficiary/situation:* A breeding programme combining twenty years of trial data where "plant height" was measured three ways, and a curator building a trait dictionary others will reuse. *Source:* CGIAR / Alliance Bioversity-CIAT, *Guidelines for creating crop-specific ontologies to annotate phenotypic data*, v2.2, https://cgspace.cgiar.org/items/af4f3a25-8d7a-4afa-b5f3-998a556b1593 — **SNIPPET**.
*Required deliverables (source):* a high-quality Trait Dictionary of traits and variables produced with the Trait Dictionary Template v5.2, usable to annotate crop phenotypic data.
*Available inputs:* community-validated trait concepts, TD Template v5.2, existing crop ontology identifiers.
*Atomic requirements:* `A05-R1` [S] Crop Ontology compiles crop-related concepts — traits, methods and scales — validated by community members. `A05-R2` [S] a phenotypic variable is defined as a combination of a trait, a method and a scale. `A05-R3` [S] a standard variable equals one trait + one method + one scale, and a trait can be measured through different variables according to method or scale. `A05-R4` [S] the scale describes how the observation is expressed: the unit for quantitative values, or the predefined categories and their meanings for categorical values. `A05-R5` [S] when creating a new variable, if the scale or unit already exists in the crop ontology, it is mandatory to reuse the existing identifier. `A05-R6` [S] the TD Template is a structured format used to compile, curate and harmonise phenotypic variables and produce an ontology.
*Component relationships:* trait + method + scale → variable → observation. What passes: variable identifiers on every observation. Must stay consistent: identifier reuse (`A05-R5`) — the single rule that prevents the ontology from frag menting.
*Whole-product constraints:* community validation is the gate on new concepts, so curation authority is distributed; historical data must be re-annotatable without rewriting the observation values.
*Acceptance criteria:* `A05-AC1` **(source)** each variable resolves to exactly one trait, one method and one scale. `A05-AC2` **(source)** no new scale or unit identifier is minted where one already exists. `A05-AC3` **(proposed — requires acceptance)** two trials annotated by different curators for the same trait+method+scale carry the identical variable identifier.
*Assumptions/unknowns:* TD Template v5.2 field list not read; the validation workflow's authority structure is unverified.

### FORD 5 — Social sciences

**CB-U05 · HXL 1.1 tagging conventions · FORD 5 (ISIC U)**
*Beneficiary/situation:* An information management officer in week one of a response, receiving forty spreadsheets from forty agencies with forty header conventions, who must produce one 3W picture today. *Source:* UN OCHA / HXL, HXL tagging conventions v1.1, https://hxlstandard.org/standard/1-1final/tagging/; org https://github.com/HXLStandard — **SNIPPET**.
*Required deliverables (source):* a hashtag row added to existing spreadsheets, plus core hashtags/attributes reference and validation tooling.
*Available inputs:* existing spreadsheets and API outputs; HDX Data Check; the hashtag-chooser app.
*Atomic requirements:* `U05-R1` [S] HXL is a simple standard to make data easier to share and process, led by OCHA with partners. `U05-R2` [S] it works by adding a row of hashtags to a spreadsheet (or database API output) between the headers and the data rows. `U05-R3` [S] attributes follow the same syntactic rules as hashtags except they begin with `+` rather than `#`, and follow the hashtag with optional whitespace separating them. `U05-R4` [S] core HXL hashtags and attributes serve as the official reference source. `U05-R5` [S] HDX Data Check is a validation service for HXL-hashtagged documents. `U05-R6` [I] because the hashtag row is *added* rather than replacing headers, existing workflows keep working — this is the adoption mechanism, and any product must preserve it.
*Component relationships:* contributor spreadsheet → hashtag row → validator → aggregation. What passes: hashtag+attribute vocabulary. Must stay consistent: the tag vocabulary between contributors, which is the only thing making merge possible.
*Whole-product constraints:* contributors are under time pressure and have no tooling; the standard must survive being applied by hand in Excel mid-crisis.
*Acceptance criteria:* `U05-AC1` **(source)** the hashtag row sits between the header row and the data rows and validates in HDX Data Check. `U05-AC2` **(proposed — requires acceptance)** a file merged from three agencies preserves each source's provenance while resolving to one tag vocabulary.
*Assumptions/unknowns:* the core hashtag list was not read; v1.1 vs v1.0 differences unverified.

**CB-M01 · RO-Crate 1.1 · FORD 5 (ISIC M)**
*Beneficiary/situation:* A researcher depositing a dataset that must remain interpretable after she leaves the institution, and a repository that must ingest it without a bespoke agreement. *Source:* RO-Crate community / ResearchObject, specification, https://www.researchobject.org/ro-crate/specification/1.0/index.html; 1.1 context https://w3id.org/ro/crate/1.1/context — **SNIPPET**.
*Required deliverables (source):* a directory containing `ro-crate-metadata.json` describing data entities and contextual entities.
*Available inputs:* schema.org vocabulary, JSON-LD, ORCID, licences, persistent identifiers.
*Atomic requirements:* `M01-R1` [S] RO-Crate is a community-driven specification for packaging research data together with rich machine-actionable metadata. `M01-R2` [S] the current stable version is 1.1, identified by the context URI `https://w3id.org/ro/crate/1.1/context`. `M01-R3` [S] an RO-Crate is a directory containing an `ro-crate-metadata.json` file. `M01-R4` [S] it describes data entities — files and directories, either stored within the crate or referenced by persistent identifiers/URLs. `M01-R5` [S] it describes contextual entities — people (identified by ORCID), organisations, licences, places and others providing context. `M01-R6` [S] each entity is described with at least a type and a name. `M01-R7` [S] common requirements it addresses include contact information, descriptive information including abstract, spatial and temporal coverage, associated publications, funding relationships, and provenance of who and what created or contributed to the data set and individual files.
*Component relationships:* crate directory → metadata file → entity graph → repository ingest. What passes: entity types, names, identifiers, relations. Must stay consistent: the context URI (it declares the version) and the entity identifiers between the metadata and the files on disk.
*Whole-product constraints:* files may be referenced rather than contained, so a crate can be valid and unresolvable; the packaging must survive being copied by ordinary file tools.
*Acceptance criteria:* `M01-AC1` **(source)** `ro-crate-metadata.json` is present and every entity has at least a type and a name. `M01-AC2` **(source)** the 1.1 context URI is declared. `M01-AC3` **(proposed — requires acceptance)** every referenced (non-contained) data entity resolves at deposit time, and unresolved references are reported rather than accepted silently.
*Assumptions/unknowns:* the 1.1 specification text itself was not read (the 1.0 page and the 1.1 context URI were the visible sources).

### FORD 6 — Humanities and the arts

**CB-R07 · CIDOC CRM 7.1.3 / ISO 21127 · FORD 6 (ISIC R)**
*Beneficiary/situation:* A museum documentation officer integrating an excavation archive, an object catalogue and a conservation record, where the same pot is an event participant, a physical thing and a subject of study. *Source:* CIDOC CRM SIG (ICOM), version 7.1.3, https://cidoc-crm.org/sites/default/files/Documents/cidoc_crm_version_7.1.3.html; ISO 21127:2023 record https://standards.iteh.ai/catalog/standards/iso/0eaa1c44-5546-496e-bf81-246def3c8063/iso-21127-2023 — **SNIPPET**.
*Required deliverables (source):* a formal ontology providing definitions and a formal structure for describing concepts and relationships used in cultural heritage documentation.
*Available inputs:* existing database schemata and structured documents; the class/property set.
*Atomic requirements:* `R07-R1` [S] CIDOC CRM is a formal ontology intended to facilitate the integration, mediation and interchange of heterogeneous cultural heritage information and similar information from other domains. `R07-R2` [S] it defines the underlying semantics of database schemata and structured documents used in documenting cultural heritage and scientific activities, particularly the study of the past and current state, as characteristic for museums and other cultural heritage institutions. `R07-R3` [S] it provides definitions and a formal structure for describing implicit and explicit concepts and relationships used in cultural heritage documentation. `R07-R4` [S] version 7.1.3 is based on ISO 21127:2014; the standard status was renewed as ISO 21127:2023. `R07-R5` [S] the related stable release 7.1.1 consists of 81 classes and 160 unique properties.
*Component relationships:* source schema → CRM class mapping → event-centric graph. What passes: class and property assignments. Must stay consistent: event identity, because CRM models history as events and two mappings that split one event produce two pasts.
*Whole-product constraints:* the ontology is deliberately event-centric, which is a modelling commitment institutions must accept wholesale; ISO version drift between 2014 and 2023 affects citation.
*Acceptance criteria:* `R07-AC1` **(source)** source records are expressed using CRM classes and properties with their published definitions. `R07-AC2` **(proposed — requires acceptance)** two institutions mapping the same excavation independently produce graphs that merge on the event without duplicating it.
*Assumptions/unknowns:* the 7.1.3 class/property counts and the exact ISO alignment are unverified.

**CB-R08 · Europeana Data Model · FORD 6 (ISIC R)**
*Beneficiary/situation:* A small regional archive that wants its digitised photographs findable in a European portal, and an aggregator that must accept data from hundreds of such providers without editing each by hand. *Source:* Europeana Foundation, EDM Mapping Guidelines v2.3 (Nov 2016) and EDM Definition v5.2.8 (Oct 2017), https://pro.europeana.eu/files/Europeana_Professional/Share_your_data/Technical_requirements/EDM_Documentation/EDM_Mapping_Guidelines_v2.3_112016.pdf — **SNIPPET**.
*Required deliverables (source):* provider data mapped to EDM, with property obligation levels observed.
*Available inputs:* provider catalogues, the mapping guidelines with worked examples and diagrams.
*Atomic requirements:* `R08-R1` [S] EDM is a theoretical data model allowing data to be presented in different ways according to the practices of the various domains contributing to Europeana. `R08-R2` [S] the Mapping Guidelines define properties, the data types usable as values, and the obligation level of each property. `R08-R3` [S] `edm:isShownAt` or `edm:isShownBy` are mandatory properties. `R08-R4` [S] the Provided Cultural Heritage Object (`edm:ProvidedCHO`) denotes the original object — physical (painting, book) or born-digital (3D model) — which is the focus of description and search. `R08-R5` [S] Aggregations aggregate resources; `ore:aggregates` expresses that the object resource is a member of the set of aggregated resources of the subject Aggregation. `R08-R6` [S] documentation includes original data, its EDM conversion, and diagrams of property distribution across classes, alongside content and metadata tier examples.
*Component relationships:* provider record → ProvidedCHO + Aggregation + WebResource → portal. What passes: the CHO description and the links to the digital representation. Must stay consistent: the separation between the object and its digital surrogate — collapsing them is the classic aggregation error.
*Whole-product constraints:* providers have unequal capability and the model must degrade gracefully across metadata tiers; links to provider sites break over time, and `edm:isShownAt` is exactly such a link.
*Acceptance criteria:* `R08-AC1` **(source)** every record carries `edm:isShownAt` or `edm:isShownBy`. `R08-AC2` **(source)** the ProvidedCHO is distinguished from its aggregation and web resources. `R08-AC3` **(proposed — requires acceptance)** link rot in `edm:isShownAt` is detected and reported to the provider rather than silently degrading the portal.
*Assumptions/unknowns:* guideline versions are from 2016/2017; a newer EDM revision may exist and was not located.

---

## 3. PROPOSAL COLLECTION — invented constructions (separately labelled)

Everything in §3 is **invented by me**. None of it is required by any source. Each entry names the case it serves, the beneficiary, the product form, and why it is materially different from its siblings — not merely a rename. Grouped so that each cluster shows *several materially different constructions for one need*.

### 3.1 Cluster: identity of a thing across parties (CB-A02, CB-M05, CB-A05, CB-L05)

- **P-001 · Tolerance Atlas (research instrument).** For CB-A02. A published empirical study and open dataset measuring how far two independent field-boundary tracings of the same parcel diverge, by capture method (walked GPS, satellite delineation, cadastral import) and by terrain. Beneficiary: registry designers. Value: the GeoID matching tolerance is currently a design guess; this converts it into a measured parameter. Different in kind from the registry itself — it is evidence, not software.
- **P-002 · Boundary Reconciliation Clinic (service + operating procedure).** For CB-A02. A field procedure a co-op runs quarterly: farmers bring conflicting boundary claims, a facilitator walks disputed edges with two devices, and the session ends with either a merged GeoID or a *recorded disagreement* with both geometries retained. Value: the registry treats disagreement as an error; the clinic treats it as a state to be documented, which is what land conflicts actually require.
- **P-003 · "Same-Thing" Ledger (coordinated system).** Across CB-A02, CB-M05, CB-A05. A small, boring service whose only job is to record *assertions of sameness* between identifiers from different schemes ("this GeoID = this cadastral parcel = this traceability lot"), each assertion signed, dated and revocable. Value: every one of these standards mints identifiers and none of them owns the cross-walk; today the cross-walk lives in spreadsheets. Materially different from a registry: it never mints identity, only claims about identity.
- **P-004 · Algorithm-Version Tripwire (maintenance tool).** For CB-M05. A background job that re-generates the InChI for every stored structure with the current software version and raises a diff when the identifier changes. Value: `M05-R5` makes the identifier a function of the software; nobody notices when the function changes.
- **P-005 · Variable Minting Referee (generator + governance procedure).** For CB-A05. An interactive tool that, before allowing a new Crop Ontology variable, searches existing scales/units and forces the curator to either reuse or write a justification that goes to the community validation queue. Value: enforces `A05-R5` at the moment of temptation rather than at review.

### 3.2 Cluster: freshness, staleness and the honest gap (CB-H02, CB-H01, CB-C01, CB-R04)

- **P-010 · Staleness Ribbon (interface component).** For CB-H02/CB-R04. A reusable UI element that renders `ttl` and `last_updated` as a visible confidence band on any vehicle/session listing, degrading from "live" to "last seen 4 min ago" to "feed silent". Value: GBFS bounds staleness at 5 minutes but consumers render every record identically; the rider cannot tell.
- **P-011 · Gap Certificate (data product).** For CB-C01/CB-H02. A signed, machine-readable statement issued by a consumer that says "between 14:02 and 14:19 I could not read this feed" — attached to any analysis derived from that window. Value: `C01-AC2`'s buffer-overrun problem and feed outages currently vanish into averages. Different from monitoring: the certificate travels *with the analysis*, not with the infrastructure.
- **P-012 · Cancellation Semantics Conformance Suite (test artefact).** For CB-R04. A published set of feed fixtures that a publisher must handle correctly: session cancelled, session moved, session full, feed error. Value: distinguishes "no session" from "no data", which is the difference between a wasted journey and an informed choice.
- **P-013 · Feed Obituary (operating procedure).** For CB-H01/CB-H02. A written procedure for retiring a feed: a mandatory notice period, a terminal `gbfs.json`/`feed_info` state that says "this feed is discontinued", and a redirect record. Value: dead feeds currently 404 or, worse, serve stale data forever.

### 3.3 Cluster: authority, consent and revocation (CB-D05, CB-G03, CB-O03, CB-N01, CB-B05)

- **P-020 · Revocation Propagation Test Harness (research instrument + procurement artefact).** Across CB-D05, CB-G03, CB-N01, CB-O03. A standard test that measures, in wall-clock time, how long after a consent withdrawal a downstream party can still use the data or access. Beneficiary: regulators and buyers. Value: every one of these specifications defines granting well and revocation vaguely; a common measurement makes the weak link visible and comparable across sectors.
- **P-021 · Two-Key Access Card for garages (physical object + credential).** For CB-G03. A per-employee credential whose validity is bound to *both* the accredited operator approval and the individual authorisation, physically issued and physically returnable on leaving employment. Value: `G03-R6`'s two-layer authority is currently a login; a returnable artefact makes off-boarding a physical act.
- **P-022 · Disclosure Safety Review (operating procedure).** For CB-B05. A documented pre-publication step in which the MSG assesses physical-risk consequences of naming a beneficial owner, records the assessment, and either publishes, publishes with delay, or publishes a redaction notice stating that a redaction exists. Value: the requirement is irreversible in effect; there is presently no defined stopping mechanism short of non-compliance.
- **P-023 · Consent Receipt for household employment (document + template).** For CB-N01 combined with CB-T02/T04. A one-page artefact given to a worker listing exactly what data about them went to which agency and for what purpose. Value: transfers a corporate-scale concept to a household-scale employer, where it currently does not exist at all.

### 3.4 Cluster: irreversibility and compensating action (CB-K02, CB-C05, CB-E05, CB-B02)

- **P-030 · Compensation Rehearsal Kit (training product + simulation).** For CB-K02. A sandbox scheme where operations staff practise resolving mis-sent payments *only* by compensating transfers, with a scoring rubric. Value: `K02-R4` forbids revocation; staff trained on reversible systems reach for the wrong instinct at 3am.
- **P-031 · Ownership Transfer Rehearsal (procedure + hardware fixture).** For CB-C05. A documented, repeatable bench procedure that performs an OpenTitan ownership transfer on a spare device before doing it in the field, with a checklist of what becomes impossible afterwards. Value: lifecycle transitions are one-way; the failure mode is discovering the consequence after the fuse blows.
- **P-032 · Classification Second-Reader (operating procedure).** For CB-E05. A rule that any consignment classified as *non-waste* — the classification that avoids PIC — requires a second independent classifier, while waste classifications need only one. Value: asymmetric review matched to asymmetric harm. Materially different from generic QA: the asymmetry is the idea.
- **P-033 · Consequence Class Drill (coordinated exercise).** For CB-B02. A scheduled multi-party exercise for tailings facilities in the "Extreme"/"Very high" classes, run with downstream communities present, whose output is a corrected emergency plan. Value: GISTM's consequence classification is a rating; a drill turns it into a rehearsed relationship.

### 3.5 Cluster: making a principle-based standard concrete (CB-I01, CB-P05, CB-U03, CB-S05, CB-Q05)

- **P-040 · Twenty-Seat HACCP (product: a filled-in worked example, not a template).** For CB-I01. A complete, published food safety plan for a specific small kitchen — real process flow, real limits, real records — released as a worked reference that another small operator can read and adapt. Value: templates ask small operators to supply the expertise they lack; a worked example supplies judgement, not blanks.
- **P-041 · Standard-to-Test Translator (generator).** For CB-Q05 and CB-I01. A tool that turns "actor + profile + options" or "CCP + limit + frequency" into a machine-checkable test plan. Value: converts conformance language into procurement leverage.
- **P-042 · Participation Ledger (record-keeping instrument).** For CB-P05/CB-U03. A simple register recording, per decision, which community members participated at which of the six stages named in `P05-R5`, kept by the community rather than the agency. Value: accountability commitments are currently evidenced by the accountable party. Shifting who holds the record is the material change.
- **P-043 · Cooperative Identity Self-Audit (interactive tool).** For CB-S05. A structured self-assessment that walks a co-op through the seven principles with the 2016 Guidance Notes, producing a public statement of where it does and does not currently meet them. Value: identity claims are usually binary and unexamined; a public partial answer is more useful than an unexamined whole one.

### 3.6 Cluster: scoring, comparability and the limits of a number (CB-S03, CB-S01, CB-S02, CB-F05)

- **P-050 · Score Generation Marker (metadata convention + badge).** For CB-S03/CB-S02. Every published repairability score carries the rubric generation and date, and comparison interfaces refuse to rank scores from different generations without a visible warning. Value: `S03-R8` revises criteria annually; league tables silently mix generations.
- **P-051 · Displacement Rate Field Study (research instrument).** For CB-S01. A longitudinal study following repaired devices to establish whether the 50% displacement assumption holds, by category. Value: this single number carries the entire impact claim; it is currently an assumption applied uniformly by the method's own admission.
- **P-052 · Repair Impact Statement Generator (generator; see §4 G-3).** For CB-S01. Produces a council-ready one-page impact statement from a repair-event log, with the data-quality rating and the "educational tool, not exact measurement" caveat rendered as part of the headline, not a footnote.
- **P-053 · Measured-Not-Modelled Ledger (data product).** For CB-F05. A public register pairing each Passive House certified building's PHPP-modelled demand with its subsequent metered consumption. Value: certification is a design-stage promise verified by blower-door test; the register makes the promise checkable in operation without weakening the certification itself.

### 3.7 Cluster: the household as an organisation with no organisation (CB-T02, CB-T03, CB-T04, CB-T01, CB-T05)

- **P-060 · Threshold Watch (service).** For CB-T03. A minimal service that tracks cumulative household wages against both the per-worker annual FICA threshold and the all-workers quarterly FUTA threshold, and warns *before* either is crossed. Value: the two tests use different bases and periods; households discover the obligation retrospectively.
- **P-061 · Hour Classification Card (physical object).** For CB-T02/T04. A pocket card, co-signed weekly, on which each day's hours are marked as worked / stand-by / rest. Value: the stand-by concept (`T02-R3`) is the least understood and most disputed category; a shared physical artefact makes it a conversation rather than a later dispute.
- **P-062 · Kitchen-Wall Canning Chart (printed reference).** For CB-T01. A laminated chart keyed to one household's altitude band, showing only the pressures and times that apply there, with the "altitude changes pressure, never time" rule as its heading. Value: the general tables invite the exact substitution error that causes botulism risk; localising the artefact removes the choice.
- **P-063 · Point-of-Use Verification Kit (product + procedure).** For CB-T05. A household-scale test procedure that checks whether a water treatment device *still* performs at its certified log-reduction class after months of use. Value: the WHO Scheme evaluates products at type-test; households live with them after degradation.
- **P-064 · Household Employer Handbook Generator (generator).** Combining CB-T02, CB-T03, CB-T04. Produces one document per household: a written work agreement, an hours-classification schedule, and a tax-threshold calendar. Value: three separate obligations from three separate authorities currently arrive as three separate documents nobody assembles.

### 3.8 Cluster: professional judgement made visible (CB-F04, CB-M04, CB-Q02, CB-L03)

- **P-070 · Surface Determination Slip (document + procedure).** For CB-F04. A one-page record produced *before* access, naming the surface, the determination, who made it, and the systems selected per activity — posted at the access point. Value: `F04-R3`/`R4` is a precondition that leaves no artefact today; making it a posted slip binds it to the physical access.
- **P-071 · Protocol Item Concordance (document component).** For CB-M04. A required front-matter table mapping each of the 33 SPIRIT items to its protocol location, updated with every amendment and showing which items an amendment touched. Value: reviewers reconstruct this by hand; amendments are where outcome-switching hides.
- **P-072 · Convention Disclosure Block (metadata convention).** For CB-Q02. A short machine-readable block published alongside every federated study result stating vocabulary version and site ETL conventions. Value: `Q02-R5` says conventions are not version-tracked; this makes the untracked thing explicit at the point of use.
- **P-073 · Version-Boundary Annotation (analysis practice).** For CB-L03/CB-P04. Any longitudinal count crossing a standards effective date carries a visible boundary marker and a statement of which elements changed. Value: annual standards revisions create artificial trend breaks that get reported as findings.

### 3.9 Cluster: cross-domain transplants (deliberately unfamiliar combinations)

- **P-080 · HACCP for sanitation, in reverse: CCP thinking for data pipelines.** From CB-E03/CB-I01 into CB-Q02/CB-U05. Treat a humanitarian or federated-research data pipeline as a service chain with hazard events (mis-merge, identity collision, silent truncation), critical control points, operational limits and corrective actions defined *before* breakthrough. Value: data quality is usually inspected at the end; sanitation safety planning is an established discipline for controlling a chain you cannot re-run.
- **P-081 · Consequence classification for software systems.** From CB-B02 into CB-J05/CB-Q04. Classify integrations by potential consequence (Low → Extreme) using downstream-harm criteria rather than technical criticality, and set review depth and drill frequency from the class. Value: tailings dam practice ranks by what happens downstream, not by asset value — an inversion most software risk registers do not make.
- **P-082 · Covert inspection applied to safeguarding.** From CB-U04 into CB-Q03/CB-L03. A mechanism letting an authorised party verify a service's protected/registered status without the service learning it was checked. Value: in homelessness and human-services contexts, the act of checking can itself expose someone.
- **P-083 · Proxy-time scoring applied to bureaucratic burden.** From CB-S03 into CB-T03/CB-O04. Score a public process from 0–10 using preassigned proxy times for each required action and a scaling factor for each required tool or document, weighted by how unavoidable the step is. Value: administrative burden is discussed anecdotally; iFixit's method is a proven way to make a subjective difficulty comparable across designs.
- **P-084 · Golden thread applied to a research protocol.** From CB-F03 into CB-M04. A single accountable, digital, handover-obligated record of everything affecting the safety of trial participants, transferred with criminal-liability-grade formality when the sponsor or PI changes. Value: building safety solved the custody-of-safety-information problem after a disaster; trials transfer custody routinely with far less ceremony.
- **P-085 · Event-centric documentation applied to machine maintenance.** From CB-R07 into CB-C01/CB-B01. Model a machine's life as CRM-style events (installation, modification, failure, repair) rather than as a current-state record. Value: MTConnect gives the present; museums learned long ago that provenance is a chain of events, and maintenance history is provenance.
- **P-086 · Trait/method/scale applied to social indicators.** From CB-A05 into CB-N03/CB-P04. Require that every reported social or education indicator be a declared triple of concept + measurement method + scale, with mandatory identifier reuse. Value: crop science solved "plant height measured three ways" with a structural rule; education and labour statistics still argue about it in prose.
- **G-analogy note.** These transplants are the ones I judged non-trivial: each moves a *mechanism*, not a vocabulary.

### 3.10 Cluster: physical, printed and human-scale constructions

- **P-090 · Repair Café Wall Chart (printed object).** For CB-S01/CB-S02/CB-S03. A poster showing, for the ten most-brought device categories, the French index criteria, the iFixit weights, and the ORDS category name — so a volunteer logging a repair, a visitor choosing a next purchase and an advocate quoting impact all read the same wall.
- **P-091 · Site Fall-Protection Sequence Board (physical object).** For CB-F04. A magnetic board at site entry showing today's surfaces, today's determinations and today's systems, updated at shift change.
- **P-092 · Ward Data Element Card (physical object).** For CB-Q01. A pocket card for ANC clinicians listing the DAK core data elements that feed decision support *and* indicators, marked so staff can see which entries change patient care versus which only feed reporting.
- **P-093 · Emblem Inspection Drill (human procedure).** For CB-U04. A field exercise in which an organisation attempts to verify its own emblem from outside its network and records what an adversary would learn from the attempt.
- **P-094 · Boundary Walk (community practice).** For CB-L05/CB-A02. A scheduled, witnessed walk of a contested boundary with both claimants and a neutral recorder, producing a party–relationship–spatial-unit record including the disagreement.

---

## 4. COMPOSITION — larger useful wholes

Each system names its parts, what passes between them, what must stay consistent, and which requirements it inherits. Recursive composition is used: several systems take an earlier system as a component.

**S-1 · Repair Evidence System** — parts: repair-event log (CB-S01 ORDS), impact method (CB-S01), repairability scores (CB-S03, CB-S02), P-052 generator, P-090 wall chart.
*What passes:* device category, fault, outcome, footprint value + source year, quality rating, score generation.
*Must stay consistent:* ORDS category definitions between log and footprint table; rubric generation between any two compared scores.
*Emergent outcome:* a repair group can answer a council's "what does this achieve?" and a resident's "which one should I buy?" from one dataset.
*Inherited requirements:* `S01-R1`–`R7` in full, including the approximation caveat (`S01-R7`) which must appear on generator output; `S03-R6`/`R8` category-specific weights and annual revision.

**S-2 · Field Identity Fabric** — parts: AgStack registry (CB-A02), P-001 Tolerance Atlas, P-003 Same-Thing Ledger, GDST lot events (CB-A04), STDM/LADM tenure records (CB-L02/L05), P-094 Boundary Walk.
*What passes:* geometry → GeoID; GeoID ↔ lot identity ↔ tenure record, as signed sameness assertions.
*Must stay consistent:* geometry-to-GeoID determinism (`A02-R3`); the party–relationship–spatial-unit shape (`L05-R2`/`R3`) must not be flattened into ownership when joined to a commercial lot.
*Emergent outcome:* a buyer can verify a sourcing claim to a parcel while the parcel's tenure remains describable in non-ownership terms — the two systems currently force a choice.
*Inherited requirements:* `A02-R2` (only the GeoID returns — so the fabric must not leak identity), `A04-R3` EPCIS basis, `L05-R3` common structure.

**S-3 · Chain-of-Custody Stack for seafood** — recursive: takes S-2 as a component. Parts: GDST CTEs (CB-A04), GS1 EPCIS/CBV (CB-G02), Peppol invoice (CB-G01), P-032 second-reader logic adapted to aggregation events.
*What passes:* CTE events with KDEs; lot identity into the commercial invoice line.
*Must stay consistent:* lot identity across aggregation/disaggregation (`A04-R4`); the EPCIS event vocabulary between partners.
*Emergent outcome:* the commercial document and the traceability record refer to the same lot, so a claim can be audited from the invoice backwards.
*Inherited requirements:* `G01-R5` routing endpoint, `G01-R1` EN 16931 compliance, `A04-R2` KDE-at-CTE.

**S-4 · Grid-Edge Settlement Loop** — parts: OCPI (CB-D01), OpenADR programs/events (CB-D04), CSIP/IEEE 2030.5 device control (CB-D03), Green Button consented usage (CB-D05), CalTRACK savings method (CB-D02), P-020 revocation harness.
*What passes:* enrolment (resource identity), dispatch event, delivered response, metered usage, computed savings, settlement record.
*Must stay consistent:* resource/meter identity from enrolment through report to settlement; the tariff or baseline in force at event time.
*Emergent outcome:* a flexibility payment can be traced from the grid signal to the meter to the money, with the customer able to revoke data sharing without breaking settlement of past events.
*Inherited requirements:* `D01-R3` module set and CDR reconciliation, `D04-R4` program metadata, `D05-R2` regulator qualification of third parties, `D02` baseline method's own data-sufficiency rules (unread — flagged).

**S-5 · Building Life Record** — parts: IDS requirement specifications (CB-F01), Brick operational model (CB-F02), golden thread custody obligations (CB-F03), Passive House criteria and blower-door verification (CB-F05), P-053 measured-not-modelled ledger, P-070 surface determination slips (CB-F04) archived into the same record.
*What passes:* required information (IDS) at handover → operational graph (Brick) → in-use measurements; custody transfer records.
*Must stay consistent:* asset/equipment identity between the IFC model, the Brick graph and the maintenance record; the accountable person across transfers.
*Emergent outcome:* handover stops being a document dump: what was specified, what was built, what is running and who is responsible are one chain — which is exactly what `F03` requires and what nothing currently supplies end to end.
*Inherited requirements:* `F01` applicability/requirements facet structure; `F02-R3` minimal relationship set; `F03` criminal-liability transfer duty; `F05` numeric criteria as design-stage claims only.

**S-6 · Continuity of Care and Learning across Displacement** — parts: WHO DAK (CB-Q01), FHIR (CB-Q04), IHE actor/transaction conformance (CB-Q05), HSDS service directory (CB-Q03), INEE standards (CB-P05), HXL for the coordination layer (CB-U05), P-042 participation ledger, P-082 covert inspection.
*What passes:* a person's care and learning continuity records; service directory entries; coordination datasets.
*Must stay consistent:* the person's identity — the hardest and most dangerous element — and the learner/patient cohort definitions across agencies.
*Emergent outcome:* a displaced family arriving in a new district can be re-connected to health and schooling without starting from zero, while the identity that makes that possible is not a surveillance asset.
*Inherited requirements:* `Q01-R4` draft/non-production caveat, `Q05-R5` full-R-transaction conformance, `P05-R5` community participation at all six stages, `U05-R2` hashtag-row placement so agencies' existing spreadsheets keep working.

**S-7 · Procurement-to-Delivery Transparency Line** — parts: OCDS (CB-O02), Akoma Ntoso for the enabling instrument (CB-O05), OSCAL for the security controls of the delivered system (CB-O01), IATI where the funder is a donor (CB-U01), Peppol invoices (CB-G01), Open311 for the service the contract delivers (CB-O04).
*What passes:* contracting process identity; the legal basis; control implementation statements; disbursement records; invoices; resulting service requests.
*Must stay consistent:* the contracting process identifier from planning through invoice; the point-in-time version of the legal instrument that authorised it.
*Emergent outcome:* "was this contract legal, funded, delivered, and did the service actually improve?" becomes one traversal instead of four investigations.
*Inherited requirements:* `O05-R5` version-in-force resolution, `O01` OSCAL model separation, `U01` SSOT schemas/codelists/rulesets, `G01-R6` mandatory buyer reference.

**S-8 · Workforce Signal System** — parts: ESCO (CB-N03), JobTech (CB-N02), O*NET (CB-N04), HR Open payloads (CB-N01), Open Badges (CB-P03), CEDS/Ed-Fi education records (CB-P04/CB-P02), P-086 triple-declared indicators.
*What passes:* occupation and skill concepts; candidate and assessment payloads; verifiable achievement credentials.
*Must stay consistent:* concept identity and version across national vocabularies; the learner-to-worker identity, held by the person via credentials rather than by an intermediary.
*Emergent outcome:* a person's learning produces credentials that are matchable against vacancies in three vocabularies without any employer holding the linking record.
*Inherited requirements:* `N03` ISCO-08 mapping (one ISCO code per occupation), `N02-R5` version recording for auditability, `N01-R6` consent scope for non-résumé data.

**S-9 · Scientific Reproducibility Envelope** — parts: RO-Crate (CB-M01), DataCite DOIs (CB-M02), SPIRIT protocol concordance (CB-M04 + P-071), OMOP convention disclosure (CB-Q02 + P-072), Darwin Core / InChI / IVOA / CMIP7 domain identifiers (CB-M06, CB-M05, CB-M07, CB-M08).
*What passes:* a crate containing protocol, code, conventions, identifiers and results.
*Must stay consistent:* the identifier-generating software versions (`M05-R5`), the vocabulary/forcing versions (`Q02`, `M08-R4`), and the crate context URI (`M01-R2`).
*Emergent outcome:* a result arrives with everything needed to say *why* a re-run differs, rather than only that it differs.
*Inherited requirements:* DataCite's six mandatory properties, `M01-R6` type-and-name minimum, `M04-R2` all 33 items.

**S-10 · Household Employment Companion** — parts: P-064 handbook generator, P-060 threshold watch, P-061 hour cards, ILO C189 terms (CB-T02), IRS thresholds (CB-T03), Hand in Hand agreements (CB-T04), P-023 consent receipt.
*What passes:* hours by classification, wages by worker by period, agreement terms.
*Must stay consistent:* the definition of "cash wages" between the FICA and FUTA tests; the hour classification between card, agreement and pay.
*Emergent outcome:* a household behaves like a competent small employer without acquiring an HR function.
*Inherited requirements:* `T02-R1` easily-understandable terms, `T02-R4` 24 consecutive hours' rest, `T03-R1`/`R5` both thresholds, `T02-R5` no obligation to remain in the household during rest.

**S-11 · Cultural Heritage Presentation Chain** — parts: CIDOC CRM (CB-R07), EDM (CB-R08), IIIF Presentation API (CB-R01), TEI (CB-R06), MEI (CB-R05).
*What passes:* the event-centric graph → aggregation records → manifests → annotated texts/scores.
*Must stay consistent:* the distinction between the object and its digital surrogate (`R08-R4`) as it becomes a IIIF Canvas; Canvas URIs must be HTTP(S) and fragment-free (`R01`).
*Emergent outcome:* an object's history, its aggregation, its viewable surrogate and its transcribed text form one addressable whole across institutions.
*Inherited requirements:* `R01` Manifest `id` dereferenceable, painting-motivation annotations; `R08-R3` mandatory `isShownAt`/`isShownBy`; `R06` mandatory `teiHeader` with `titleStmt`, `publicationStmt`, `sourceDesc`.

**S-12 · Production-to-Distribution Thread** — parts: MovieLabs OMC (CB-R02), OpenTimelineIO (CB-R03), C2PA content credentials (CB-J01), OpenActive-style public listing where the output is a live event (CB-R04).
*What passes:* work identity → asset identity → timeline references → provenance manifests.
*Must stay consistent:* asset identity between the production ontology and the editorial timeline; provenance claims must survive the transformations OTIO describes.
*Emergent outcome:* a delivered master can be traced to the shoot day and the vendor that produced each element, and its provenance is assertable to audiences.
*Inherited requirements:* `R02-R4` Part 7 predicates, `R03` adapter/schema plugin model, C2PA 2.4 manifest requirements (unread — flagged).

### Generators and configurators (each with the concrete product it must produce)

- **G-1 · DAK-to-Implementation Kit generator.** *Generator:* takes a WHO DAK's eight components (CB-Q01) and a country's adaptation decisions, emits an implementation bundle. *Concrete product it must produce:* for the ANC DAK, a bundle containing (a) a decision-support rule set with each rule tagged to its numbered recommendation, (b) an indicator specification whose data elements are the same elements the rules read, (c) a change report listing rules and indicators invalidated by a given guideline revision. Must carry `Q01-R4`'s draft/non-production caveat on every artefact.
- **G-2 · Feed Conformance Pack generator.** *Generator:* takes a mobility feed URL and produces a conformance pack. *Concrete product:* for a GBFS v3.x feed, a report asserting HTTPS, UTF-8, valid JSON, no 404 on required files, `ttl` present on every response, maximum observed staleness of `station_status`/`vehicle_status` over 24h against the 5-minute bound, and referential integrity of station/vehicle IDs — plus a signed Gap Certificate (P-011) for any interval it could not read.
- **G-3 · Repair Impact Statement generator.** *Generator:* takes an ORDS event log plus the footprint table. *Concrete product:* a one-page council statement giving devices repaired by category, kg CO2e avoided computed as footprint × 0.5 displacement, the Data Quality Index rating per category, the source and year of each footprint, and the "approximate, educational tool" caveat in the headline block.
- **G-4 · Household Employer Handbook generator (P-064).** *Concrete product:* for one US household employing one part-time cleaner and one part-time nanny: a written work agreement per worker in plain language, an hours-classification schedule distinguishing worked/stand-by/rest, a threshold calendar marking the per-worker $3,000 FICA point and the all-workers $1,000 quarterly FUTA point for 2026, and a W-2 filing checklist.
- **G-5 · Conformance Test Plan generator (P-041).** *Concrete product:* given "IHE ITI XDS.b, Document Consumer actor, no options", a test plan listing every R transaction for that actor with a pass/fail procedure, formatted as a procurement annex.
- **G-6 · Localised Canning Chart generator (P-062).** *Concrete product:* for altitude band 4,001–6,000 ft and a dial-gauge canner: a single-page chart showing 13 psi for that band, per-food processing times unchanged from the USDA tables, and the rule "altitude changes pressure, never time" as the page heading — with all other altitude bands omitted.

---

## 5. SITUATIONAL VARIATION — how each variation changes the product

- **V-1 Interruption (CB-P05, CB-Q01).** When schooling or care is interrupted rather than continuous, the product must be *resumable from partial state*: cohort definitions become person-level continuity records, and re-registration from zero becomes a defect rather than an inconvenience.
- **V-2 Divided authority (CB-G03, CB-B05, CB-O03, CB-L03).** Where approval sits with one body and delivery with another, revocation becomes the design centre: the product needs a propagation path and a measurable latency (P-020), not just a grant path.
- **V-3 Irreversible effects (CB-K02, CB-C05, CB-E05).** Error handling must be compensating rather than corrective, which changes the interface: there is no edit affordance, only a new authorised counter-action with a traceable link.
- **V-4 Concurrency (CB-F04, CB-D04, CB-K02).** Several parties act on one object at once: the product must attribute duty and effect per actor per moment — the fall-protection duty attaches to *each employee on a surface*, not to the surface.
- **V-5 Scarce resources (CB-M08, CB-T05, CB-E02).** Compute, test capacity or field staff are limited: the product's job shifts from "capture everything" to "decide what not to capture, visibly" — which is precisely what a Data Request is.
- **V-6 Changing evidence (CB-L03, CB-P04, CB-S03, CB-M05).** Definitions revise on a schedule: every output needs a version stamp and every cross-boundary comparison a visible warning; trend analysis acquires a mandatory boundary annotation.
- **V-7 Competing needs (CB-O03, CB-U04, CB-B05).** Privacy against accountability, protection against targetability: the product cannot optimise one; it must expose the trade-off as a configurable, reviewable decision with a named decision-maker.
- **V-8 Uncertainty in the number itself (CB-S01, CB-S03).** Where the headline figure rests on an assumption the source itself calls approximate, the caveat becomes a rendering requirement, not documentation.
- **V-9 The beneficiary has no organisation (CB-T01–T05).** Products must be single-artefact, physical or one-page, and must not assume a system of record. This is why §3.7 favours cards, charts and one-off documents over software.
- **V-10 The producer has no capability (CB-R04, CB-U05, CB-I02).** Adoption mechanics dominate: a standard applied by hand in a spreadsheet mid-crisis (`U05-R2` adds a row rather than replacing headers) beats a better model requiring tooling.
- **V-11 Discovery vs. expression (CB-R01, CB-R08 vs. CB-R05, CB-R06).** Discovery products optimise for aggregation and minimum mandatory metadata; expression products optimise for encoding nuance. Combining them in S-11 requires that the discovery layer never becomes the ceiling for the expression layer.
- **V-12 Care vs. coordination (CB-Q01 vs. CB-P05).** A data element that changes a clinical decision has a different error cost from one that feeds a monthly indicator; P-092 makes that distinction visible at the point of entry, which no shared data dictionary does by itself.
