- **V-13 Maintenance as the primary situation (CB-C01, CB-F02, CB-S03).** When the product's purpose is keeping something working, event history outranks current state (P-085), and repairability outranks initial quality.
- **V-14 Creation under constraint (CB-C03, CB-R02).** Where the maker is also the maintainer (GVCS), modularity and documentation for multiple audiences are functional requirements, not niceties: `C03-R2` and `C03-R3` exist because the same person builds, fixes and teaches.

---

## 6. SAMPLING RECORD

- **Inventory frozen:** 112 cases, deduplicated by origin cluster, frozen before any seed was drawn. Ordered inventory = the 112 case IDs sorted lexicographically (`CB-A01 … CB-U05`), exactly as listed in §1.
- **Randomness tool:** Linux kernel CSPRNG via `getrandom(2)`, accessed as `python3 -c "import secrets; secrets.randbits(64)"` on `Linux-6.18.44-fc-v24-x86_64-with-glibc2.39`. (random.org and other external randomness services are unreachable from this environment; the OS CSPRNG is the actual randomness source used.)
- **Seed:** `1158297115365469046` (hex `0x10131916e9b7eb76`).
- **Runtime of draw:** `2026-09-09T20:48:16.903153+00:00` (seed generation); sampling executed in the same session immediately after.
- **Algorithm:** `random.Random(seed)`; categories processed in fixed order A,B,…,U, then F1…F6. For each category: eligible = cases tagged with that category and not already selected anywhere in the draw (global without-replacement); `random.shuffle` (Fisher–Yates) applied to the eligible list; the first two entries with **distinct origin clusters** taken. Deterministic and reproducible from the seed plus the frozen ordered inventory.
- **Result:** 54 selected, 2 per category, **no shortages**. Selections are listed as the headings of §2 and reproduced here in draw order:

A: CB-A03, CB-A02 · B: CB-B01, CB-B05 · C: CB-C03, CB-C05 · D: CB-D05, CB-D01 · E: CB-E05, CB-E03 · F: CB-F02, CB-F04 · G: CB-G03, CB-G01 · H: CB-H02, CB-H01 · I: CB-I01, CB-G05 · J: CB-J05, CB-J03 · K: CB-K02, CB-K04 · L: CB-L05, CB-L03 · M: CB-M06, CB-M04 · N: CB-N02, CB-N01 · O: CB-O05, CB-O03 · P: CB-P04, CB-P02 · Q: CB-Q04, CB-Q02 · R: CB-R02, CB-R04 · S: CB-S01, CB-S03 · T: CB-T02, CB-T03 · U: CB-U04, CB-P05 · F1: CB-M08, CB-M05 · F2: CB-C01, CB-D04 · F3: CB-Q01, CB-Q05 · F4: CB-A04, CB-A05 · F5: CB-U05, CB-M01 · F6: CB-R07, CB-R08

- **Post-draw caveat (recorded, not corrected):** section H drew GTFS and GBFS, which the inventory recorded as distinct origin clusters. They are distinct specifications with distinct origins but a single present-day steward (MobilityData). Recorded rather than redrawn, because the inventory and seed were frozen first.
- **Requirements freeze:** the atomic requirements in §2 are frozen as written above. §7 varies conditions only; it deletes no inherited requirement.

---

## 7. STRESS COLLECTION — one hypothetical variation per selected case

Every entry is **hypothetical**. Each states the changed condition and its consequences; inherited requirements remain in force unless the entry says a requirement is *contradicted*, in which case the conflict is named rather than resolved silently.

1. **CB-A03-X.** *Changed:* a herd runs three AMS brands and one brand's ID reader degrades to 94%. *Consequence:* `A03-R3` fails at device level while `A03-R1`, `R2`, `R4`, `R5` remain in force; the herd's records are still linkable but the certification claim is not. The product must be able to publish *partial* certification with a per-device read-rate disclosure — which the current pass/fail gate cannot express.
2. **CB-A02-X.** *Changed:* two co-ops register overlapping polygons for the same field after a boundary dispute. *Consequence:* `A02-R3` determinism does not adjudicate; the registry must hold two GeoIDs and a recorded overlap, and downstream products (S-2) must refuse to silently pick one. P-094 becomes a required, not optional, component.
3. **CB-B01-X.** *Changed:* a block model exceeds container size limits and must be delivered in parts. *Consequence:* `B01-R1`/`R2` element support is unchanged, but round-trip attribute binding (`B01-AC2`) now spans files; a part-manifest and a completeness assertion become necessary, and the "single interchange file" assumption of `B01-R5` is contradicted.
4. **CB-B05-X.** *Changed:* a named beneficial owner faces credible threats after publication. *Consequence:* `B05-R2` (PEP disclosure regardless of level) and the public-knowledge objective `B05-R1` collide with duty of care. P-022's safety review becomes mandatory; the unresolved question — whether any redaction is permissible under the Standard — is retained, not answered.
5. **CB-C03-X.** *Changed:* a module is redesigned for a different hydraulic standard in one region. *Consequence:* `C03-R2` parallel development survives only if interface compatibility is versioned; `C03-R4`'s 8× cost target may be met regionally but not globally, so cost claims must become region-scoped.
6. **CB-C05-X.** *Changed:* a device's owner ceases to exist (company dissolved) with ownership never transferred. *Consequence:* `C05-R1` ownership transfer has no counterparty; the device is functional but permanently unupdatable. Trademark conformance (`C05-R3`) is unaffected, which reveals that conformance says nothing about end-of-life custody.
7. **CB-D05-X.** *Changed:* the regulator withdraws a third party's qualification while active grants exist. *Consequence:* `D05-R2` is retrospectively violated by ongoing delivery; `D05-R1`'s custodian duty now requires proactive termination. The reference stack's module separation (`D05-R4`) helps only if the authorization server subscribes to qualification status — which is not stated.
8. **CB-D01-X.** *Changed:* a tariff changes while a session is in progress. *Consequence:* `D01-R3`'s Tariffs and CDRs modules remain required, but `D01-AC2` (CDR recomputes from the tariff referenced at session start) becomes ambiguous. The unresolved question from the brief is now load-bearing and must be answered by scheme rules outside OCPI.
9. **CB-E05-X.** *Changed:* a consignment is 60% functional units, 40% untested. *Consequence:* `E05-R2`/`R3` give no mixed category; `E05-R5` PIC applies to the waste fraction. Either the consignment is split before movement or it is classified wholly as waste — the guidelines' silence on mixed lots is the gap, and P-032's second-reader applies to the non-waste fraction only.
10. **CB-E03-X.** *Changed:* informal emptiers, who are outside the utility's authority, are the dominant conveyance step. *Consequence:* `E03-R1` requires the whole chain in scope, but `E03-R3`/`R5` monitoring and correction presume managerial authority. The plan must substitute incentive and access mechanisms for control, and the acceptance criterion `E03-AC3` becomes unachievable for that step unless the exposure group is protected by other means.
11. **CB-F02-X.** *Changed:* an application needs a point type Brick does not define. *Consequence:* `F02-R3`'s minimal relationship set and `F02-R6` backwards compatibility hold, but portability (`F02-AC2`) breaks for that application. A local extension must be declared as an extension, or the model silently becomes non-portable while still validating.
12. **CB-F04-X.** *Changed:* three employers' crews occupy one surface simultaneously. *Consequence:* `F04-R1` duty attaches per employee per employer; `F04-R3` determination is a single physical fact but a triplicated legal duty. P-070's posted slip must name all three employers' determinations, or one employer's compliance conceals another's gap.
13. **CB-G03-X.** *Changed:* an accredited entity's accreditation lapses. *Consequence:* every employee authorisation it issued is of uncertain status; `G03-R6`'s two-layer requirement gives no cascade rule. Independent operators lose security-feature access through no act of their own, and `G03-AC1`'s unrestricted non-security access must be shown to survive the lapse.
14. **CB-G01-X.** *Changed:* a buyer's electronic address changes mid-dispute. *Consequence:* `G01-R5` routing requirement is satisfiable for new invoices but historical invoices reference a dead endpoint; `G01-R6` buyer reference remains the correlation key. Credit notes must carry both old and new endpoints, which the CIUS does not describe.
15. **CB-H02-X.** *Changed:* the operator's telemetry backend is down but its web server still serves stale files. *Consequence:* `H02-R5` (no 404) is satisfied while `H02-R7` (≤5 minutes) is silently violated — the worst combination. `H02-R8`'s `ttl` becomes the only detectable signal, so P-010/P-011 move from nice-to-have to necessary.
16. **CB-H01-X.** *Changed:* an agency runs fixed routes and demand-responsive zones in the same feed. *Consequence:* `H01-R2` makes `stops.txt` conditionally excusable only for the DRT part; the feed needs both. `H01-R3`/`R4` remain, and `H01-AC3` must be restated: presence of DRT zones does not license omission of stops used by fixed routes.
17. **CB-I01-X.** *Changed:* the kitchen switches to a cook-chill process mid-year. *Consequence:* `I01-R2` (all seven principles) and `I01-R4` (GHP foundation) hold, but the process flow used for hazard analysis is now wrong — `I01-AC3`'s CCPs no longer correspond to the operation. Re-analysis is required before the change, not after, which the principle-based text implies but does not schedule.
18. **CB-G05-X.** *Changed:* a manufacturer reformulates without changing the barcode. *Consequence:* `G05-R1`'s barcode-as-key is now ambiguous across time; `G05-R2`/`R3` derived scores may describe a product no longer on shelf. `G05-AC2`'s absent-vs-none distinction must extend to a third state: "data may predate reformulation".
19. **CB-J05-X.** *Changed:* an OAD's referenced document declares a different `openapi` version. *Consequence:* `J05-R2` is satisfied per document but the description is internally inconsistent; `J05-R3` and `J05-R4` are evaluated per document, so a validator can pass each file and still produce an unusable client. Version homogeneity across an OAD must be asserted explicitly.
20. **CB-J03-X.** *Changed:* a page conforms to WCAG 2.2 AA using a technique that a widely used assistive technology does not support. *Consequence:* `J03-R4`'s normative/non-normative boundary means conformance is technically claimable while the user is still blocked. The proposed acceptance criterion `J03-AC2` must add an accessibility-supported determination naming the technologies relied upon.
21. **CB-K02-X.** *Changed:* a payee's account is closed between quote and transfer. *Consequence:* `K02-R4` irrevocability attaches only after both parties agree, so this is a pre-agreement failure — but `K02-R5` forbids intermediaries modifying the payload, so the switch cannot substitute an alternative account. A compensating flow is unnecessary; a clean abort path is, and it must be distinguishable from a timeout.
22. **CB-K04-X.** *Changed:* the two directions of a session negotiate different guarantees and the recoverable direction fails during recovery of the idempotent direction. *Consequence:* `K04-R3`'s asymmetry and `K04-R4`'s flow pair mean recovery state is per-direction; a shared re-establishment procedure risks resequencing one direction against the other. The unread flow definitions are exactly where this must be resolved — the gap is retained.
23. **CB-L05-X.** *Changed:* a customary group and a statutory titleholder both claim one spatial unit. *Consequence:* `L05-R2`/`R3` accommodate two parties and two relationship types on one spatial unit; `L05-AC2` becomes the primary acceptance test rather than a proposed extra. What the model cannot do — and must not pretend to — is rank the claims.
24. **CB-L03-X.** *Changed:* a client declines to provide a demographic element that changed definition in FY2024. *Consequence:* `L03-R2` requires UDE completion while client consent is voluntary; `L03-R4`'s deduplication and chronic-homelessness determination must degrade gracefully. `L03-AC3`'s version annotation now must also distinguish "not collected" from "declined".
25. **CB-M06-X.** *Changed:* an eDNA sample indicates presence at a location where no specimen or observation exists. *Consequence:* `M06-R3` covers samples, and `M06-R5`/`AC2`'s basis-of-record becomes decisive: a consumer filtering to specimens would report absence where the evidence says presence. The term set survives; the interpretation layer must not.
26. **CB-M04-X.** *Changed:* a trial amends its primary outcome after enrolment begins. *Consequence:* `M04-R1`/`R2` still require all 33 items; `M04-R4`'s "describe what is planned" now has two plans. P-071's concordance must show both, with dates, and the registry entry must be updated — the checklist alone does not compel that linkage.
27. **CB-N02-X.** *Changed:* a concept is retired and merged into another between two matching runs. *Consequence:* `N02-R1`/`R2` continue to serve current concepts, but `N02-AC2`'s replayability fails unless the retired identifier still resolves. Deprecation-with-tombstone becomes a hard requirement rather than good practice.
28. **CB-N01-X.** *Changed:* a candidate withdraws consent after their assessment result has been shared with an LMS. *Consequence:* `N01-R4`/`R5` describe the forward flow only; `N01-R6`'s non-résumé data makes the withdrawal consequential. `N01-AC2`'s propagation requirement is unmet by the specification and must be added contractually.
29. **CB-O05-X.** *Changed:* an amendment is itself later annulled by a court. *Consequence:* `O05-R4` machine-readability and `O05-R5` versioning hold, but the consolidated text must now represent a version that was in force and then retroactively was not. `O05-AC2`'s point-in-time resolution needs a distinction between *as enacted*, *as in force*, and *as later treated* — three states, not two.
30. **CB-O03-X.** *Changed:* a relying party requests an attribute in a jurisdiction where its registration does not extend. *Consequence:* `O03-R3` registration and `O03-R2` privacy both apply; the wallet must refuse and explain, which means refusal messaging becomes part of the interoperability surface. `O03-AC2` unlinkability must survive the refusal, which itself leaks a signal.
31. **CB-P04-X.** *Changed:* an agency withdraws from the linkage agreement mid-year. *Consequence:* `P04-R4`/`R5` IDS structure is unaffected, but the P-20W cohort loses a sector; `P04-AC3`'s reproducibility now requires distinguishing "element changed" from "source withdrawn" — a different explanation for the same numeric discontinuity.
32. **CB-P02-X.** *Changed:* a district changes SIS vendors mid-school-year. *Consequence:* `P02-R1` interoperability is precisely the promise under test; `P02-AC2`'s identifier and history preservation becomes the acceptance test. The thin source means the standard's own guarantees here are unverified — the shortage recorded in the brief becomes operationally material.
33. **CB-Q04-X.** *Changed:* a national programme profiles R4B while a partner has moved to a later version. *Consequence:* `Q04-R2`'s branch separation reflects a real production split; `Q04-AC2` requires an instance to validate against both its profile and the named base, so cross-version exchange needs an explicit mapping artefact, not a version negotiation.
34. **CB-Q02-X.** *Changed:* one site upgrades its vocabulary release mid-study. *Consequence:* `Q02-R1`/`R2` version support is unchanged, but `Q02-R5`'s untracked ETL conventions plus a vocabulary shift make the site's before/after results non-comparable. P-072's disclosure block is the minimum; `Q02-AC2` must be enforced at result submission, not at study design.
35. **CB-R02-X.** *Changed:* a vendor is replaced mid-production and assigns its own identifiers to delivered assets. *Consequence:* `R02-R4` Part 7 predicates still express relationships, but `R02-AC2`'s work-identity resolution fails without a sameness assertion. P-003 enters S-12 as a required component.
36. **CB-R04-X.** *Changed:* a provider's booking system is down while the opportunity feed remains live. *Consequence:* `R04-R1`'s validator-driven conformance passes; the user reaches a dead booking. `R04-AC2`'s cancellation distinction must extend to "listed but unbookable", a third state neither the feed nor the validator currently expresses.
37. **CB-S01-X.** *Changed:* a device category's footprint source is superseded by a study with a materially different value. *Consequence:* `S01-R4`'s documented sources and years and `S01-R6`'s quality rating make the change visible; every previously published aggregate becomes inconsistent. `S01-AC3`'s reproducibility requires that restatements be possible and labelled, not that history be rewritten.
38. **CB-S03-X.** *Changed:* a manufacturer ships a design that the current rubric's proxy-time table has no action for. *Consequence:* `S03-R7`'s consistency mechanism has a hole; `S03-R8`'s annual revision is the sanctioned fix but leaves an interim period. Scores issued in the interim must carry a rubric-coverage flag, extending P-050.
39. **CB-T02-X.** *Changed:* a live-in worker is asked to remain in the household during weekly rest because of a family emergency. *Consequence:* `T02-R4` and `T02-R5` are directly engaged; the hour is not rest. `T02-AC3`'s classification must reclassify it as stand-by or worked and compensate, and P-061's card is where that reclassification has to happen contemporaneously.
40. **CB-T03-X.** *Changed:* a worker is employed by two households that each pay below the per-worker threshold. *Consequence:* `T03-R1` applies per employer, so neither household owes FICA while the worker accrues no coverage. `T03-R5` FUTA is likewise per employer. The gap is in the statute's structure, not in the household's conduct — P-060 should surface it rather than reassure.
41. **CB-U04-X.** *Changed:* a marked hospital server is repurposed for a mixed-use function during a surge. *Consequence:* `U04-R3` — the emblem must always identify assets serving only protected purposes — is violated the moment the repurposing happens. `U04-AC3`'s withdrawal observability becomes the safety-critical path, and `U04-R2` covert inspection means the holder cannot know whether a stale emblem has already been relied upon.
42. **CB-P05-X.** *Changed:* an education response hands over from an NGO to the ministry mid-year. *Consequence:* `P05-R4` continuity and `P05-R6` coordination hold; `P05-R5` participation must be re-established with the new authority. `P05-AC3`'s traceability across handover is the test that most handovers currently fail.
43. **CB-M08-X.** *Changed:* a forcing dataset error is discovered after the freeze and after runs have begun. *Consequence:* `M08-R3`/`R4` freezing is what makes comparison possible and what blocks the fix. Either the ensemble carries a known error consistently, or it fragments. `M08-AC3`'s per-variable forcing-version record is what lets a later user tell which runs are affected.
44. **CB-M05-X.** *Changed:* an InChI software release changes a tautomer normalisation rule. *Consequence:* `M05-R4`/`R5` make the identifier software-dependent by design; stored identifiers silently disagree with regenerated ones. P-004's tripwire and `M05-AC2`'s version recording are the only defences; `M05-AC1`'s convention-independence still holds *within* a version.
45. **CB-C01-X.** *Changed:* an adapter's clock drifts by 90 seconds. *Consequence:* `C01-R6` timestamped sampling is intact in form and wrong in fact; utilisation analyses shift. Nothing in the fetched requirements detects it — a clock-agreement assertion must be added, and `C01-AC2`'s gap detection does not cover skew.
46. **CB-D04-X.** *Changed:* 30% of enrolled VENs are offline at event dispatch. *Consequence:* `D04-R2`'s VTN/VEN model and `D04-R4`'s program metadata hold; delivered response falls short with no per-resource attribution unless `D04-AC3` is implemented. The grid consequence is immediate and the event is not repeatable — V-3 and V-4 combine.
47. **CB-Q01-X.** *Changed:* WHO revises an ANC recommendation after national adaptation. *Consequence:* `Q01-R3`'s eight components must be re-derived; `Q01-R6`'s L2/L3 separation is what makes partial re-derivation possible. `Q01-AC3`'s invalidation list becomes the primary deliverable of the change, and `Q01-R5`'s "subject to change without notice" means the national implementer needs a subscription, not a download.
48. **CB-Q05-X.** *Changed:* a vendor claims an actor but implements one R transaction only for a subset of message types. *Consequence:* `Q05-R5`/`R6` make this non-conformant outright; `Q05-AC1` is binary. The stress reveals that procurement language must name profile, actor *and* options (`Q05-AC2`), because partial R implementation is otherwise invisible until integration.
49. **CB-A04-X.** *Changed:* a processor aggregates a legal catch with one of unknown provenance. *Consequence:* `A04-R4`'s aggregation CTE is recorded correctly while the resulting lot's claim is unsupportable; `A04-R5`'s weakest-member logic bites. `A04-AC3`'s enumerable contributing trips with per-trip completeness is the only construction that keeps the record honest instead of laundering it.
50. **CB-A05-X.** *Changed:* two curators independently create variables for one trait using the same method but scales differing only in decimal precision. *Consequence:* `A05-R5`'s reuse mandate is arguably satisfied (the scales differ) while `A05-AC3`'s convergence fails. The standard needs a rule on when a precision difference constitutes a different scale — retained as unresolved.
51. **CB-U05-X.** *Changed:* two agencies use the same hashtag with different implicit units. *Consequence:* `U05-R3` attribute syntax permits disambiguation but does not compel it; `U05-R5` validation checks syntax, not semantics. The merge in `U05-AC2` produces a plausible, wrong total — the most dangerous failure in the whole collection because it is invisible.
52. **CB-M01-X.** *Changed:* a crate references data by URL and the host disappears. *Consequence:* `M01-R4` explicitly permits referenced entities; the crate remains valid and the research becomes irreproducible. `M01-AC3`'s deposit-time resolution check plus periodic re-checking is the mitigation; the specification's permissiveness is not a defect but it is a hazard.
53. **CB-R07-X.** *Changed:* two institutions model one excavation with different event granularity — one season-level, one context-level. *Consequence:* `R07-R1`'s integration purpose and `R07-R3`'s formal structure hold, but `R07-AC2`'s merge fails: the graphs do not duplicate the event, they disagree about how many events there were. Granularity agreement is a prerequisite the ontology does not enforce.
54. **CB-R08-X.** *Changed:* a provider's `edm:isShownAt` target moves behind a paywall. *Consequence:* `R08-R3`'s mandatory property is satisfied; the user's access is not. `R08-AC3`'s link-rot detection must extend to access-state change, and the portal must be able to represent "described here, viewable elsewhere, not freely" — a state the mandatory property alone conflates with availability.

---

## 8. ASSUMPTIONS, MISSING INFORMATION, UNRESOLVED MEANINGS, OPEN FEASIBILITY QUESTIONS

**Assumptions recorded**
- ISIC section letters A–U are treated as stable across Rev. 4 and Rev. 5 for case assignment.
- FORD assignment is by the R&D field a case's *knowledge content* belongs to, not by the industry that funds it; this is why standards bodies' engineering specifications land in F2 and research-data specifications in F1/F5.
- CB-T05 (WHO HWT Scheme) is assigned to ISIC T on the reading that a household treating its own drinking water is own-use production of services. A defensible alternative reading places it in ISIC E or Q; I implemented the T reading and did not build the alternatives.
- "Distinct originating projects or organizations" was implemented as distinct *project-level* clusters, with shared parents (WHO, European Commission, MobilityData) recorded rather than merged.

**Missing information (recorded shortages, all source-access, none case-count)**
- ISO texts (ISO 19152, ISO 21127, ISO 18541) are unreachable in this environment; CB-L05 in particular rests entirely on secondary description and is labelled as such.
- The WHO SMART Guidelines L1–L5 knowledge-layer definitions were searched for in `smart-base` and not found in the files fetched; CB-Q01 therefore describes the eight DAK components (which were read) and not the layer model.
- CB-P02 (Ed-Fi) and CB-R04 (OpenActive) briefs are thin because the fetched files were a licence-bearing README and a validator-library README respectively.
- CB-Q04 (FHIR) rests on the build repository's README; no FHIR conformance requirement is verified here.
- CB-K04 (FIXP) flow-type definitions, CB-A02 (AgStack) matching semantics, CB-S03 (iFixit) proxy-time table, and CB-A04 (GDST) per-CTE KDE lists were all sought and not obtained.
- CB-C04 and CB-G04 rest partly on secondary commentary and are marked `SNIPPET-SECONDARY`; their primary instruments (Regulation (EU) 2024/1781 and 2023/1542; the ARTS/OMG specifications) were not read.
- 70 of 112 cases are SNIPPET-only. Every requirement drawn from them is unverified against the original text, and the numeric values in CB-T03 (2026 thresholds), CB-F05 (15 kWh/m²a, 0.6 ACH50), CB-S01 (50% displacement) and CB-T05 (log-reduction targets) should be re-verified against the publishers before any operational use.

**Unresolved meanings**
- CB-A03: is the 98% ID read rate measured on first read or after retries?
- CB-B05: is a 10%-or-lower beneficial-ownership threshold required or recommended?
- CB-D01: how are mid-session tariff changes to be reflected in the CDR?
- CB-E05: how are mixed functional/untested consignments classified?
- CB-A05: does a difference in decimal precision constitute a different scale?
- CB-O05: does the vocabulary distinguish *as enacted*, *as in force*, and *as later treated by a court*?
- CB-F02: where is the boundary between Brick's "minimal, principled" relationship set and application-specific modelling?

**Open feasibility questions retained**
- Is deterministic geospatial identity achievable under real capture noise (CB-A02), or is tolerance matching the only viable construction?
- Is covert inspection achievable against an adversary controlling the network path (CB-U04)? If not, the digital emblem's security-requirement triple is unsatisfiable as stated.
- Does a single 50% displacement rate survive per-category scrutiny (CB-S01)? The entire community-repair impact claim rests on it.
- Can asymmetrical per-direction delivery guarantees (CB-K04) be recovered correctly in a way implementers can test, or does asymmetry push correctness beyond practical verification?
- Can the 8× cost target (CB-C03) hold once safety-critical subsystems are included?

## Halt of the blind-agent phase after iteration 3 (partial)

The user stopped the blind-agent research loop after iteration 3's response arrived and before its mapping was complete. Iteration 3's verbatim response is retained at `coverage/iterations/03.raw.md` (54 briefs, 112-entry inventory, 70 of 112 snippet-only, seed 1158297115365469046, prompt hash identical to iterations 1 and 2). The 22 cases mapped before the halt are retained at `coverage/iterations/03.partial.jsonl`; they are not counted in the cumulative tally, which stands at iterations 1 and 2 (108 of 108 COVERED, 107 of 108 stresses expressed). All 22 partial mappings were COVERED with expressed stresses; the strongest observations from the partially mapped set are recorded here so they are not lost: beneficial-ownership disclosure (EITI) is anchored unusually well by P202, P253, P254 and P291; Basel's non-binding waste classification with binding consequences is a second data point for the modality-gradient watch item; the digital emblem's undetectable validation (not yet mapped) is the strongest strain candidate seen so far, because the boundary contract presumes a handoff both parties know about. Iteration 4's agent was launched and then stopped without a response. The next phase is procedural generation at volume, recorded in a new section when it starts.
